# Improved Keybindings — Code-Investigation Answers (adversarially verified)

**Date:** 2026-06-13
**Status:** Phase-1 answers to the 63 `code-investigation` design-readiness questions
**Companion to:** [`2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-design-readiness-questions.md`](./2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-design-readiness-questions.md) (the questions) and [`.understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md`](../../.understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md)

## Method

Each question was answered by a hybrid investigator using the `/understand-chat` knowledge-graph method (grep the graph, follow edges/layers, cite code) **plus** direct reads of `app/` — which the graph does not cover (it spans only `warpui_core`, `editor`, `vim`). Every answer was then handed to an independent **adversarial verifier** that re-opened the cited code and tried to refute it, producing a verdict:

- **CONFIRMED** — the answer held under independent code re-check.
- **CORRECTED** — mostly right; the verifier fixed specifics (line refs, scope, counts).
- **REFUTED** — materially wrong; the final answer is the verifier’s.
- **UNVERIFIABLE** — could not be confirmed from code alone.

Confidence is the verifier’s final call. `residual_unknowns` flags any decision/product residue that code cannot settle (those route back to the question doc’s `user-product-decision` / `cross-team-decision` / `prototype-spike` items).

## Summary

- Questions answered + verified: **63 / 63**
- Verdicts: **49** confirmed · **14** corrected
- Final confidence: **63** high

---

## Axis 1 — Config as portable, live data

### A1-Q3 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** On each reload, do we diff (apply only deltas vs the last-applied set) or nuke-and-reapply (remove_custom_trigger every previously-applied name, then re-set the whole file)?

**A.** Code facts decisively favor DIFF. Both set_custom_trigger and remove_custom_trigger call self.pending.clear() (matcher.rs:138 and 150) which drops any in-flight multi-keystroke chord on EVERY call, and nuke-and-reapply issues ~2x the calls (remove-all then set-all) plus a momentary revert-to-default window. update_custom_trigger is an idempotent per-binding field write (keymap.rs:422-433), so a diff that only emits mutations for changed entries is safe and minimizes chord-buffer clobbering. Neither approach exists today (load runs once at launch, lib.rs:2557, and never removes; the file watcher in native.rs:74-134 has no keybindings.yaml branch at all); both require a NEW persistent last-applied map keyed by name, so diff is not more expensive in bookkeeping.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. Every cited file:line was independently opened and matches the assertion (off-by-at-most-a-line cited ranges still encompass the referenced code). The recommendation (DIFF) is grounded in verifiable code facts.

Current state (greenfield): There is no reload path at all. keyboard::load_custom_keybindings runs exactly once at launch (app/src/lib.rs:2557 — confirmed: line 2557 is `keyboard::load_custom_keybindings(ctx);`), iterates the whole file's flat HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> (keyboard.rs:169), and calls app.set_custom_trigger for each entry (Trigger::Empty for "none" at keyboard.rs:44, Trigger::Keystrokes otherwise at keyboard.rs:47); it NEVER calls remove_custom_trigger (keyboard.rs:37-57). I additionally opened the un-cited file watcher handler native.rs handle_warp_managed_paths_event (74-134): it has branches for themes, workflows, launch_configs, tab_configs, and settings.toml, but NO keybindings.yaml branch — independently confirming that no reload is wired up today. A grep for last_applied across app/ and crates/ returns nothing. So neither "diff" nor "nuke-and-reapply" is implemented; A1-Q3 chooses the shape of code that does not yet exist.

Why nuke-and-reapply is the more costly shape (the two concrete code costs):
1. pending.clear() on every mutator call. Matcher.set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-141) and Matcher.remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:146-152) BOTH call self.pending.clear() (lines 138 and 150 — confirmed verbatim) before delegating to keymap.update_custom_trigger. pending is HashMap<EntityId, Pending> (matcher.rs:15) where Pending { keystrokes: Vec<Keystroke>, context } (matcher.rs:32-36) is the in-flight multi-keystroke chord buffer. So each call discards any partially-typed chord. Nuke-and-reapply makes N (remove) + M (set) calls per reload; diff makes only |delta| calls. More calls = strictly more chances to clobber the chord buffer. This is the decisive, directly-observable code cost.
2. Intra-reload revert window. update_custom_trigger(name, None) sets custom_trigger = None = revert to default (keymap.rs:422-433). Nuke-and-reapply first reverts every previously-applied name, then re-applies, so each binding passes through its default state mid-reload. Its observability depends on whether keystroke matching can interleave with the synchronous &mut AppContext reload handler (per A1-Q8 it generally cannot within one handler invocation), so this is mostly a state-model artifact while the pending.clear() drop is the concrete per-call harm — the answer correctly hedges this rather than overclaiming visible flicker.

Why diff is safe and cheap: update_custom_trigger is idempotent at the field level — it writes binding.custom_trigger for every binding whose name matches (keymap.rs:422-433). One precision worth recording (does NOT change the conclusion): the write iterates TWO collections, editable_custom_action_bindings (423-429) and editable_bindings (430-432), each filtered by `b.name == name`; it does not look up indices via editable_bindings_by_name (that index map, populated at 414-417, is consumed by get_binding_by_name at 379). The substance — re-setting a name to its current value changes nothing except the pending.clear() side effect, and the write hits all bindings with that name — is exactly right.

Bookkeeping required either way: The Matcher struct (matcher.rs:13-30) stores only pending, keymap, default_binding_validator, binding_validators, and two conversion fns — there is NO persistent set of applied names. The applied override lives only in EditableBinding.custom_trigger: Option<Trigger> (keymap.rs:300), and a name maps to a Vec of binding indices, so there is no clean name-keyed applied-map. (Nuance found: get_binding_by_name at keymap.rs:379 returns a lens exposing original_trigger: Option<&Trigger> which indirectly reveals whether a name is overridden — so "NO read-back API" is slightly strong — but it yields no applied-map and cannot distinguish file-applied overrides from in-app edits, so the conclusion that a new last_applied record is needed still holds.) Therefore both shapes need a new persistent last_applied: HashMap<String, AppliedKind> (Keystrokes | Empty | absent), seeded from the launch load, and diff is not the more expensive option.

Recommendation: DIFF, with a persistent last_applied seeded at launch. It avoids the intra-reload revert window for unchanged bindings, minimizes pending.clear() chord-buffer drops, exploits update_custom_trigger idempotency, and costs no more bookkeeping than nuke-and-reapply.

_Evidence:_
- set_custom_trigger clears the in-flight chord buffer (pending.clear() at line 138) before applying Some(trigger); remove_custom_trigger does the same at line 150 before applying None — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-152` _(code)_
- pending is the per-entity in-flight chord buffer: HashMap<EntityId, Pending{keystrokes: Vec<Keystroke>, context}>; clearing it drops any partially-typed multi-keystroke chord — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:15,32-36` _(code)_
- update_custom_trigger is an idempotent per-binding field write (Some=override, None=revert); it writes custom_trigger for every binding matching the name, iterating BOTH editable_custom_action_bindings (423-429) and editable_bindings (430-432) filtered by b.name==name (not via the index map) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(both)_
- The Matcher struct has no persistent applied-names map; it stores only pending, keymap, two validators, and two conversion fns — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:13-30` _(both)_
- The only storage of an applied override is EditableBinding.custom_trigger: Option<Trigger>; a name maps to a Vec of binding indices (editable_bindings_by_name, populated at 414-417). get_binding_by_name (379) exposes original_trigger as a partial read-back but yields no applied-map — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:300,379,414-417` _(code)_
- No reload exists today: load_custom_keybindings applies the whole flat HashMap with set_custom_trigger only (Trigger::Empty for 'none' at :44, Keystrokes at :47), never remove_custom_trigger — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57` _(code)_
- load_custom_keybindings is invoked a single time during app launch (line 2557 is the only non-test call site) — `app/src/lib.rs:2557` _(code)_
- AppContext::set_custom_trigger (delegates at :1638) and remove_custom_trigger (delegates at :1660) are thin wrappers over keystroke_matcher where pending.clear() lives — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1637-1638,1654-1660` _(code)_
- The file watcher handler has branches for themes, workflows, launch_configs, tab_configs, and settings.toml but NO keybindings.yaml branch, independently confirming no keybindings reload path exists; grep for last_applied returns nothing — `app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc commit to a diff-based reload with a concrete last_applied: HashMap<String, AppliedKind> (Keystrokes | Empty | absent) seeded at launch — sizing Axis 1 as the watcher+bookkeeping quick win, and giving Q2/Q15 a fixed bookkeeping shape and Q4/Q7/Q8 the state structure they each consume.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The remaining residue is a product/UX decision, not a code fact: whether the intra-reload revert-to-default window of nuke-and-reapply is acceptable. The decisive per-call harm is pending.clear() dropping an in-flight chord (matcher.rs:138,150); the 'momentary revert to default' is real in the state model but its visible observability depends on whether keystroke matching can interleave with the synchronous &mut AppContext reload handler (per the settings pattern in A1-Q8 it generally cannot within one handler invocation) — so no visible flicker is asserted, only the chord-drop. Owned by sibling questions: the exact tri-state apply policy for absent vs 'none' (A1-Q4), where the last_applied map is owned/seeded (A1-Q8), echo-suppression so an in-app self-write does not trigger a spurious diff (A1-Q6), and re-applying overrides to bindings registered after reload since the matcher has no re-apply-on-registration hook (A1-Q7). The applied-kind enum (Keystrokes | Empty | absent) is sufficient input for whichever Q4 policy is chosen. Separately, the file watcher branch that would need to ADD a keybindings.yaml case is native.rs:74-134 — currently absent — which is where Axis 1 wiring must land.
- _Verification:_ I opened all cited files plus the un-cited watcher handler (native.rs:74-134) and re-ran the grep. All evidence claims hold. Line citations are accurate (lib.rs:2557 is exact; app.rs delegations are at 1638/1660 within the cited ranges; matcher pending.clear() is at 138/150 exactly). Two non-material nuances I independently found and corrected in the prose: (1) update_custom_trigger does NOT drive its write off editable_bindings_by_name — it iterates two collections (editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings) filtering by b.name==name; editable_bindings_by_name is consumed by get_binding_by_name instead. The answer's substantive claim ('hits all bindings with that name', 'idempotent field write') is correct; only the implied mechanism was loose. (2) 'NO name-keyed read-back API' is slightly strong: get_binding_by_name (keymap.rs:379) returns a lens with original_trigger that indirectly reveals whether a name is overridden — but it yields no applied-map and can't distinguish file-applied overrides from in-app edits, so the conclusion that a new last_applied record is required still holds. I also confirmed remove_custom_trigger IS called in non-test code at util/bindings.rs:512, but only for in-app single-binding edits, NOT in any reload path, so the load-path claim ('never removes') is intact. None of these affect the verdict: the DIFF recommendation rests on pending.clear()-per-call (verified), update_custom_trigger idempotency (verified), and absence of any reload/bookkeeping today (verified), all of which are solid.

### A1-Q4 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** On hot-reload, how do we reconcile the three file states — name-present-with-keys, name-present-with-'none', and name-absent — given that the launch loader maps 'none' to set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) (an explicit unbind, keyboard.rs:43-44) but the report reset story (R1) assumes remove_custom_trigger reverting to default (matcher.rs:146-151 then keymap.rs:422)? Does an ABSENT name revert to default while an explicit 'none' STAYS an unbind?

**A.** Confirmed. In the engine, set_custom_trigger(Empty) (custom_trigger=Some(Empty)) and remove_custom_trigger (custom_trigger=None) are genuinely different live outcomes: Empty is an unmatchable unbind that preserves the default as original_trigger (as_lens, keymap.rs:741-747), while None reverts to the default. So 'none' must stay an unbind and an ABSENT name must revert via remove_custom_trigger. But the current loader (keyboard.rs:39-55) NEVER calls remove_custom_trigger, there is NO hot-reload (native.rs:74-134 has no keybindings branch) and NO applied-names ledger (grep = 0 hits), so today absent vs reverted is not reconciled at all — the "removed entries revert" leaf is only reachable from the in-app reset button, not the file loader. Every cited line ref is exact.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened all ten cited locations plus the one the answer omitted (the in-app WRITE side and the watcher's directory registration), and every evidence claim's file:line says exactly what the answer asserts. No shifted line refs, no app/-vs-crates scope confusion, no overgeneralization, and the grep counts reproduce.

WHAT I VERIFIED LINE-BY-LINE:
1) matcher.rs:137-152 — set_custom_trigger calls update_custom_trigger(name, Some(trigger)) (137-140); remove_custom_trigger calls update_custom_trigger(name, None) (146-151). Exact.
2) keymap.rs:422-433 — update_custom_trigger writes binding.custom_trigger = trigger (Some=override, None=revert) over BOTH editable_custom_action_bindings (423-429) and editable_bindings (430-432). Exact.
3) keymap.rs:741-759 — as_lens: if custom_trigger is Some -> (trigger=custom, original_trigger=Some(&self.trigger)); else (trigger=&self.trigger, original_trigger=None). Exact. This is the real proof that Some(Empty) is a tracked unbind and None is a true revert.
4) keymap.rs:48 — Trigger::Empty // empty trigger (cannot actually be matched). Exact.
5) keyboard.rs:37-57 — loop is over file entries only (39); Removed -> set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) (43-44); Keystrokes -> set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Keystrokes) (46-47); no remove_custom_trigger anywhere in keyboard.rs (grep over app/src shows keyboard.rs only has set_custom_trigger at 44 and 47). Exact. "none" = REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION (15, 192). HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> at 169, TryFrom 189-213, Vec1<Keystroke> 22. All exact.
6) lib.rs:2557 — keyboard::load_custom_keybindings(ctx) inside fn launch; grep confirms this is the ONLY non-test caller (definitions at keyboard.rs:37 non-test and 145 test). Exact.
7) native.rs:74-134 — handle_warp_managed_paths_event has branches for themes (81), workflows (92), launch_configs (103), tab_configs (114, flag-gated), and settings/user_preferences.toml (129-132, flag-gated). NO keybindings.yaml branch. Exact — no hot-reload.
8) grep -rni 'applied_names|applied_trigger|applied_custom' app/src crates/warpui_core/src -> ZERO hits (reproduced). Exact.
9) bindings.rs:511-512 — reset_keybinding_to_default calls ctx.remove_custom_trigger(binding_name) (the R1 revert). settings_view/keybindings.rs:592 — remove_keystroke calls ctx.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) (the in-app unbind). Exact, and they are genuinely different functions, proving revert and unbind are distinct primitives at runtime.
10) keymap.rs:405-419 — register_editable_bindings just extends editable_bindings and builds the by-name index; it consults no stored-override map, so newly registered bindings start at custom_trigger=None and any prior override is not re-applied. Exact. Supporting: custom_trigger field at keymap.rs:300; EditableBinding constructor sets custom_trigger: None at 661 (every freshly registered binding starts clean).

INDEPENDENT CHECK THE ANSWER DID NOT MAKE (corroborating, not contradicting): The app's WRITE side already encodes the tri-state the answer prescribes. In-app unbind (settings_view/keybindings.rs:592) pairs the runtime set_custom_trigger(Empty) with persisting 'none' to the file, while in-app reset (bindings.rs:512-513) pairs remove_custom_trigger with remove_custom_keybinding, which DELETES the file entry (makes the name ABSENT). So the file's "absent vs 'none'" distinction is not hypothetical — Warp's own writers already produce it: unbind => 'none', reset => absent. This strengthens the answer: the required reconciliation (keys=>override, 'none'=>stay-unbound, absent=>revert) is the exact inverse of what the writers already emit; only the reload READER is missing. I also grepped every reference to keybindings.yaml/keybinding_file_path/KEYBINDINGS_FILE_NAME outside keyboard.rs — all hits are tests, a one-time preview-config migration, an AI-skill path display, and a doc comment (user_config/mod.rs:81 listing the file as warp-managed). None is a runtime hot-reload watcher, so "no hot-reload exists" holds even though the directory itself is watched (the watcher fires but native.rs simply has no keybindings branch to act on it).

CONCLUSION: The answer is accurate in every material respect. 'none' (Some(Empty)) and absent (None) are distinct live states; the loader only ever emits Empty/Keystrokes and never reverts; there is no reload and no applied-names ledger; the revert leaf is reachable only from the in-app reset path. A correct future reload handler must diff against an owned previously-applied-names set (ideally with per-name kind) and call remove_custom_trigger for names in old_applied \ new_file.

_Evidence:_
- set_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger(name, Some(trigger)); remove_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger(name, None). Verified exact. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-152` _(both)_
- update_custom_trigger writes binding.custom_trigger = trigger (Some=override, None=revert) over both editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings. Verified exact. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- as_lens: Some(custom)=use custom + keep default as original_trigger=Some(&self.trigger); None=use default, original_trigger=None. Verified exact — this is what makes Empty a tracked unbind vs None a true revert. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:741-747` _(code)_
- Trigger::Empty is 'empty trigger (cannot actually be matched)', an unbind not a default. Verified exact. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:48` _(code)_
- Launch loader: loop over file entries only (39); Removed->set_custom_trigger(Empty) (43-44); Keystrokes->set_custom_trigger(Keystrokes) (46-47); absent names never iterated; no remove_custom_trigger in keyboard.rs. Verified exact. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57` _(both)_
- load_custom_keybindings is called once at launch only; grep confirms lib.rs:2557 is the sole non-test caller. Verified. — `app/src/lib.rs:2557` _(both)_
- handle_warp_managed_paths_event has themes/workflows/launch_config/tab_config/settings branches but NO keybindings.yaml branch — no hot-reload. Verified exact. — `app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134` _(code)_
- No applied-names ledger anywhere: grep -rni 'applied_names|applied_trigger|applied_custom' app/src crates/warpui_core/src returned zero hits. Reproduced. — `grep app/src crates/warpui_core/src — 0 hits` _(code)_
- In-app revert (reset_keybinding_to_default) uses remove_custom_trigger AND deletes the file entry (remove_custom_keybinding, line 513 => name becomes absent); in-app unbind (remove_keystroke) uses set_custom_trigger(Empty) AND persists 'none'. Distinct primitives; write side already encodes the tri-state. Verified exact. — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:511-513 and app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:592` _(both)_
- register_editable_bindings appends bindings and builds the by-name index but never re-applies any stored override; custom_trigger field (keymap.rs:300) defaults to None at construction (keymap.rs:661). Verified exact. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:405-419,300,661` _(code)_
- No watcher reloads keybindings.yaml at runtime: every keybinding_file_path/keybindings.yaml reference outside keyboard.rs is tests, one-time preview-config migration, AI-skill path display, or a doc comment (user_config/mod.rs:81). Independently grepped. — `grep keybindings.yaml|keybinding_file_path|KEYBINDINGS_FILE_NAME (app/src,crates)` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design commit to a concrete tri-state reconciliation in the (not-yet-existing) reload handler: keys->set_custom_trigger(Keystrokes), 'none'->set_custom_trigger(Empty) (stays unbound), absent->remove_custom_trigger (revert) — gated on diffing against an owned previously-applied-names set (with per-name kind) that must be added alongside the watcher branch in native.rs and seeded from the launch load.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The PRODUCT decision of whether 'none' should stay an unbind across reloads (vs. be folded into a plain revert) is not settleable from code: the engine supports both primitives (Empty=tracked unbind via original_trigger; None=revert) but no policy selects between them for the file loader. Where the applied-kind ledger should live (app-side keyboard state vs. promoted into the Matcher so register_editable_bindings can re-apply overrides for bindings registered after a reload — the A1-Q7 concern) is a design choice, not a fact derivable from current code, since neither a ledger nor a reload handler exists yet.
- _Verification:_ I opened all ten cited files at the exact offsets and confirmed each evidence claim verbatim; no line refs were shifted. I re-ran both greps: set/remove/update_custom_trigger across warpui_core and app/src (keyboard.rs has only set_custom_trigger at 44 and 47, no remove — confirmed), and the applied-ledger grep (zero hits — confirmed). I checked the one location the answer did not cite: the WRITE side. It corroborates rather than refutes — in-app unbind persists 'none' (keybindings.rs:592) while in-app reset DELETES the file entry making the name absent (bindings.rs:513 remove_custom_keybinding), so Warp's own writers already produce the absent-vs-'none' distinction the answer says the reader must honor. I also independently grepped for any runtime watcher on the keybindings file; all out-of-keyboard.rs references are tests, a one-time migration, an AI-skill path display, and a doc comment listing it as a managed file — none triggers a reload, so "no hot-reload exists" holds even though the directory is watched (the watcher fires but native.rs has no keybindings branch). One minor nuance I noted but it does not change correctness: update_custom_trigger iterates both editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings (keymap.rs:423-432), while register_editable_bindings only pushes Custom-trigger bindings into editable_custom_action_bindings (410) — irrelevant to the unbind-vs-revert distinction. The answer is accurate in every material respect.

### A1-Q5 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** When a hand-edit leaves the file transiently malformed (syntax error mid-typing, or the watcher fires during a non-atomic truncating write), read_custom_keybindings returns None (keyboard.rs:128-138) — the SAME value as an absent/empty file. Should None mean 'keep current applied bindings' rather than 'empty file, revert ALL custom bindings to default'?

**A.** Substantively confirmed, with one fixed nuance. read_custom_keybindings (keyboard.rs:128-138) collapses absent-file (File::open Err, :129) and parse-failure (serde Err, :134-137) into the same None, and the save path is a non-atomic truncating write (util/file.rs:143 → fs::File::create), so the mid-write/typo race is real. CORRECTION to the original answer's evidence #2: a zero-byte/whitespace-only "empty" file does NOT parse to Some(empty) — serde_yaml 0.8.26 returns Err(end_of_stream) (de.rs:110-112) → None, so absent, empty (zero-byte), AND unparseable all collapse to None; only an explicit `{}` literal yields Some(empty map). That makes the doc's "None == absent/empty" framing correct for the common empty case and broadens the conflation. With the current single-None API the proposed "None = keep current" policy is not implementable without splitting the reader (File::open Err = absent → revert vs serde Err = unparseable → hold). There is no keybindings hot-reload today, but the conflation already causes a silent on-disk data-loss bug in the in-app WRITE path (keyboard.rs:71,89 read_custom_keybindings().unwrap_or_default()).

VERDICT: corrected. Every code fact in the answer was independently opened and verified; one evidence item (the empty-file nuance) was backwards and is fixed below. The fix strengthens the answer's conclusion rather than undermining it.

CONFIRMED:
1. None conflates absent-file and parse-failure. read_custom_keybindings (app/src/keyboard.rs:128-138): :129 `std::fs::File::open(keybinding_file_path()).ok()?` turns any open error into None; :132-137 `serde_yaml::from_reader` Ok→Some, Err→log::warn!+None. Indistinguishable to the caller. Verified verbatim.

2. CORRECTION to original evidence #2. The original answer claimed "a valid empty file parses to Some(empty map), not None" and called the doc's "None==absent/empty" framing imprecise. That is wrong for the normal empty-file case. The workspace uses serde_yaml 0.8 (root Cargo.toml:272; app/Cargo.toml:190 serde_yaml.workspace=true; on disk serde_yaml-0.8.26). In that version, Deserializer::de returns Err(error::end_of_stream()) when loader.events.is_empty() (de.rs:110-112), and on_event drops StreamStart/StreamEnd/DocumentStart/DocumentEnd (de.rs:461-467) so a zero-byte/whitespace-only file produces zero content events → end_of_stream Err → read_custom_keybindings returns None. So a zero-byte empty file behaves exactly like an absent file (None). Only a file containing an explicit empty map literal `{}` deserializes to Some(empty). Net effect: absent + zero-byte-empty + unparseable ALL collapse to None — the conflation is BROADER than the original answer stated, and the doc's original "None == absent/empty" wording is actually accurate for the common empty case. This does not change the overall answer; it reinforces it.

3. Non-atomic truncating write — race is real. save_custom_keybindings (keyboard.rs:103-122) opens via crate::util::file::create_file(keybinding_file_path()) (:106), which is fs::File::create(path) after fs::create_dir_all (app/src/util/file.rs:132-145, :143). File::create truncates in place; YAML is then streamed via serde_yaml::to_writer over a BufWriter (:113-115). A watcher firing mid-write observes a truncated/half-written file → serde Err → None. Verified.

4. No keybindings hot-reload today. handle_warp_managed_paths_event (app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134) has branches for themes (:81), workflows (:92), launch_configs (:103), tab_configs (:114, flag-gated) and settings/user_preferences.toml (:129-133, emits WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Settings) — NO branch for keybindings.yaml. Grep confirms native.rs contains no read_custom_keybindings/load_custom_keybindings reference at all. load_custom_keybindings has exactly one (non-test) call site, app/src/lib.rs:2557. At launch None is benign: keyboard.rs:38 `if let Some(...)` applies nothing on None and there is no prior applied state to wipe. The "revert ALL to default" hazard is a FUTURE-design risk only if a hot-reload handler reuses this None-returning reader.

5. Conflation already causes a real silent on-disk data-loss path in the WRITE code. write_custom_keybinding (keyboard.rs:64-75, :71) and remove_custom_keybinding (keyboard.rs:78-93, :89) both do read_custom_keybindings().unwrap_or_default() before mutating and re-serializing the whole map. If an in-app rebind's read-modify-write races a transiently malformed/half-written/empty file → None → unwrap_or_default() = EMPTY map → save writes back only the single new entry, clobbering every other persisted binding. Verified.

6. Settings precedent for "hold last-good on parse error" exists and works because the settings reader returns a typed Result. UserPreferences::reload_from_disk returns Result<(), Error> (crates/settings/src/lib.rs:101). handle_warp_config_change (app/src/settings/init.rs:225-256): :236 `if let Err(err) = prefs.reload_from_disk()` → logs, emits SettingsFileError::FileParseFailed (:239-241), and returns early (:243) WITHOUT calling reload_all_public_settings — in-memory state HELD at last-good and error surfaced. Only on Ok does it reconcile (reload_all_public_settings, :245-246), where absent keys reset to default and present keys load (crates/settings/src/manager.rs:323-325; fn at :327). Keybindings cannot replicate this because read_custom_keybindings throws away the Ok/Err distinction. Verified.

ANSWER TO THE CODE-VERIFIABLE PART: Yes — the conflation is real (keyboard.rs:129 vs :134-137) and broader than first stated (zero-byte empty also → None via de.rs:110-112), the truncating-write race is real (util/file.rs:143), and the single-None API makes the proposed "keep current on parse error" policy unimplementable without splitting the reader (e.g. Result<Option<CustomKeybindings>, ParseError>: absent/empty → revert/reconcile; unparseable → hold-last-good + surface error per Q11) and switching save to atomic temp+rename to shrink the race window. The "should we" is a product/UX choice, but the code evidence favors "keep current": settings already does exactly this, and the conflation already produces a silent data-loss bug in the in-app write path today.

_Evidence:_
- read_custom_keybindings returns None for BOTH absent-file (File::open(...).ok()?, :129) and serde parse failure (logged + None, :134-137), conflating the two — `app/src/keyboard.rs:128-138` _(code)_
- CORRECTED: a zero-byte/whitespace-only empty file does NOT parse to Some(empty) — serde_yaml 0.8.26 returns Err(end_of_stream) when loader.events.is_empty() (Stream/Document events dropped in on_event), so it maps to None just like an absent file; only an explicit `{}` literal yields Some(empty map). The doc's 'None==absent/empty' is therefore accurate for the common empty case. — `/home/mhb/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/serde_yaml-0.8.26/src/de.rs:110-112,461-467` _(code)_
- Workspace pins serde_yaml 0.8 (the version whose empty-input behavior was tested) — `Cargo.toml:272; app/Cargo.toml:190` _(code)_
- Save uses a non-atomic in-place truncating write: save_custom_keybindings -> create_file -> fs::File::create over a BufWriter — `app/src/keyboard.rs:106,113-115` _(code)_
- create_file is fs::File::create(path) (after create_dir_all), truncates in place, no temp+rename — `app/src/util/file.rs:132-145` _(code)_
- No keybindings hot-reload: handle_warp_managed_paths_event has branches for themes/workflows/launch_configs/tab_configs/settings(user_preferences.toml) but none for keybindings.yaml; native.rs has no read/load_custom_keybindings reference — `app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134` _(both)_
- load_custom_keybindings has exactly one non-test call site, at app init; None benign there (no prior applied state to wipe) — `app/src/lib.rs:2557; app/src/keyboard.rs:38` _(both)_
- Write/remove use read_custom_keybindings().unwrap_or_default(), so parse-failure/empty during an in-app read-modify-write silently clobbers all other persisted bindings — `app/src/keyboard.rs:71,89` _(code)_
- Settings reader returns a typed Result (reload_from_disk -> Result<(), Error>), enabling hold-last-good — `crates/settings/src/lib.rs:101` _(code)_
- Settings reload subscriber returns early on Err (emits FileParseFailed, does NOT reconcile), holding last-good; reconciles only on Ok — `app/src/settings/init.rs:236-246` _(code)_
- On Ok reconcile, absent keys reset to default and present keys load with explicitly_set=true — `crates/settings/src/manager.rs:323-325` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design specify a tri-state-safe reload contract: split read_custom_keybindings so absent-file (File::open Err) maps to the revert/reconcile path while a parse failure (serde Err) holds the last-good applied bindings and surfaces an error (mirroring app/src/settings/init.rs:236-244), and switch save_custom_keybindings to atomic temp+rename — fixing both the future hot-reload flicker AND the already-present silent clobber in the in-app write path (keyboard.rs:71,89).
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The "should None mean keep-current vs revert-all" remains a product/UX policy decision, not a code fact. Minor residual on the serde nuance: yaml-rust behavior for borderline inputs (a bare `~`/`null` document, or a single comment line) deserializing into a HashMap newtype was not exhaustively tested — a YAML null scalar would error (invalid type, unit/Map) and thus also map to None, but I confirmed only the zero-byte/empty-events path directly; this does not affect the conclusion since all of these still collapse to None. Open design choices for the doc (unchanged from original): (1) exact split return type for read_custom_keybindings and whether absent-vs-empty should both revert or whether empty should also hold; (2) how parse errors surface to the user (Q11; settings emits SettingsFileError::FileParseFailed, keybindings has no analogue); (3) whether to adopt atomic temp+rename writes and whether the watcher then needs rename-event handling; (4) interaction with the nonexistent hot-reload handler (Q8) and self-write echo loop (Q6) — the reload plumbing that would consume a split reader does not exist in code yet.
- _Verification:_ I opened all cited files plus serde_yaml-0.8.26 source (which the original answer did NOT cite) to test the empty-file claim. CONFIRMED: keyboard.rs:128-138 (None conflation, both branches verbatim), keyboard.rs:106/113-115 + util/file.rs:132-145 (fs::File::create truncating write), native.rs:74-134 (branches: themes :81, workflows :92, launch_configs :103, tab_configs :114, settings/user_preferences.toml :129-133; NO keybindings branch — also grep-confirmed native.rs has zero read/load_custom_keybindings references), lib.rs:2557 (sole call site, grep-confirmed), keyboard.rs:71/89 (unwrap_or_default clobber), settings/lib.rs:101 + init.rs:236-246 + manager.rs:323-325 (hold-last-good precedent). FOUND ONE ERROR: evidence #2 of the original answer claimed "a valid empty file parses to Some(empty map), not None" and dinged the doc as imprecise. Reading serde_yaml 0.8.26 de.rs:110-112 (events.is_empty() -> Err(end_of_stream)) and de.rs:461-467 (on_event discards Stream/Document events), a zero-byte/whitespace-only file yields zero content events -> Err -> None, i.e. it behaves identically to an absent file; only an explicit `{}` literal yields Some(empty). So the original answer's correction was backwards: the doc's "None==absent/empty" is accurate for the normal empty case, and the conflation is actually broader (absent + zero-byte-empty + unparseable all -> None). This reinforces the answer's overall conclusion. No line refs were shifted; no app/-vs-crates scope confusion; counts (one load_custom_keybindings call site; no keybindings reload branch) re-verified by grep.

### A1-Q7 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Does an override survive bindings registered AFTER a reload? The Matcher stores no persistent name-to-trigger map; set_custom_trigger/update_custom_trigger mutate only bindings already in editable_bindings, and register_editable_bindings never re-applies a stored override. Is register_editable_bindings called once at init or per view-instance/lazily? Must the hot-reload design add a persistent applied map plus a re-apply hook on registration?

**A.** CONFIRMED. At the engine level there is no persistent override store: the Matcher struct holds none (matcher.rs:13-31), set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger only delegate to keymap.update_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-152), and update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) mutates custom_trigger ONLY on bindings already present in editable_bindings/editable_custom_action_bindings filtered by name — a silent no-op if the name is not yet registered. register_editable_bindings (keymap.rs:405-419; AppContext wrapper app.rs:1627-1632 just forwards to the matcher) appends new bindings and never consults any stored override, so a binding registered after an override evaporates that override. BUT the app-level "registered after" premise is false today: every editable binding is registered exactly once during initialize_app's central init block (lib.rs:1648-1703, "Register initial keybindings prior to creating menus"), and load_custom_keybindings runs later inside launch (lib.rs:2557), which initialize_app precedes (called at lib.rs:1065 vs launch at lib.rs:1077). I independently reproduced the call-site tally (109 sites: 99 in `init`, 7 in notebooks/editor/view.rs that the awk heuristic mislabels as the nested `debug_notebooks_enabled` but are actually inside `pub fn init` at :90, plus 3 helpers all invoked synchronously from `init`), and an enclosing-fn scan found ZERO register_editable_bindings in any render/new/constructor/paint/layout path. So no override is lost to late registration today. A persistent applied-names map is still required for hot-reload RESET/diff semantics; a re-apply-on-registration hook is defensive/future-proofing only, not a fix for a live bug.

I opened every cited file plus several the answer did not lean on, and re-ran the grep tally myself. All twelve evidence claims hold; I found only immaterial citation nuances that do not change the conclusion.

ENGINE LEVEL (crates/warpui_core, all verified directly):
1. Matcher struct (matcher.rs:13-31) has fields pending, keymap, default_binding_validator, binding_validators, custom_trigger_to_keystroke_fn, default_keystroke_trigger_for_custom_action — NO name->trigger applied/override map. Confirmed verbatim.
2. set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-141) -> keymap.update_custom_trigger(name, Some(trigger)); remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:146-152) -> keymap.update_custom_trigger(name, None). Confirmed.
3. update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) iterates editable_custom_action_bindings then editable_bindings, filters `b.name == name`, writes binding.custom_trigger = trigger.clone(). Touches only already-registered bindings; if the name is absent it is a silent no-op recording nothing. Confirmed.
4. register_editable_bindings (keymap.rs:405-419) extends editable_bindings with Tracked::new(action), pushes Custom-trigger bindings to editable_custom_action_bindings, and builds editable_bindings_by_name — it never reads any stored override. The AppContext wrapper (app.rs:1627-1632) and Matcher wrapper (matcher.rs:112-132) both just forward; neither re-applies overrides. Confirmed. (Minor: the answer's phrasing that register "seeds custom_trigger to default None" is loose — it preserves whatever custom_trigger the constructed EditableBinding carried, normally None — but this is immaterial to the no-re-apply claim.)

So the adversarial engine claim is correct: with no persistent store, an override set before a binding exists is lost for that binding.

APP LEVEL (app/, read directly; graph does not cover app/):
5. load_custom_keybindings (keyboard.rs:37-57) reads the on-disk map via read_custom_keybindings and, per entry, calls app.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) for Removed (line 44) or Trigger::Keystrokes (line 47). It keeps no in-memory applied map. Confirmed.
6. load_custom_keybindings is called once at lib.rs:2557, inside `fn launch` (defined at lib.rs:2552). Confirmed.
7. Ordering: the app_builder.run closure (lib.rs:1030-1078) calls initialize_app at lib.rs:1065 (the answer cited 1086, which is actually the fn DEFINITION line — the CALL is 1065; immaterial), then launch at lib.rs:1077. initialize_app contains the central block at lib.rs:1648-1703 with the comment "Register initial keybindings prior to creating menus" invoking ai::init, workspace::init (1654), terminal::init (1656), editor::init (1658), etc. So all editable bindings register before load_custom_keybindings applies any override. Confirmed.
8. I reproduced the tally from /home/mhb/warp: 109 total register_editable_bindings call sites (excluding _tests.rs and the trait def). Enclosing-fn breakdown: 99 in `init`, 7 attributed by the awk heuristic to the nested helper `debug_notebooks_enabled` (notebooks/editor/view.rs sites 299/364/396/483/581/657/695) — verified these sit textually inside `pub fn init` at notebooks/editor/view.rs:90 (the nested fn at :100 returns bool and closes before :299), 1 in register_input_mode_bindings, 1 in add_overflow_menu_items_as_editable_binding, 1 in add_open_setting_pages_as_editable_binding. Exact match to the answer.
9. The 3 helpers are all called synchronously from `init`: register_input_mode_bindings (def terminal/view/init.rs:1150) is called at init.rs:88 inside `pub fn init` (:80); add_open_setting_pages_as_editable_binding (def :1344) and add_overflow_menu_items_as_editable_binding (def :1489) are called at workspace/mod.rs:1329-1330, which lie inside the single `pub fn init` at workspace/mod.rs:78 — an awk scan found NO intervening fn signature between :78 and :1344, so those calls are inside init. Confirmed.
10. I additionally ran an enclosing-fn scan for render|new|build_view|element|paint|layout across all register_editable_bindings sites and got ZERO hits — independently confirming no registration sits in a View constructor/render/runtime path (a location the answer asserted but did not directly grep). 
11. code::init/notebooks::init are reached transitively via workspace::init (workspace/mod.rs:107 notebooks::init, :108 code::init), and workspace::init is in the startup block (lib.rs:1654). Confirmed.
12. editor/view/mod.rs register sites (518/908/928/936/953/961) use ctx.register_editable_bindings and are inside `pub fn init(ctx: &mut AppContext)` at editor/view/mod.rs:185 — ctx is the AppContext, not a per-view ModelContext. Confirmed.

CONCLUSION (unchanged from the answer): No editable binding registers lazily/per-view-instance; all register exactly once during initialize_app, before load_custom_keybindings runs in launch. Therefore neither launch nor a runtime hot-reload loses overrides to late registration today. A re-apply-on-registration hook is NOT required for correctness now (defensive only, e.g. for future plugin/runtime-contributed editable bindings). A persistent applied-names map IS still required for hot-reload RESET/diff semantics (to know which names to remove_custom_trigger back to default) and must be seeded from the launch-time load at lib.rs:2557.

_Evidence:_
- Matcher struct has no persistent name->trigger override store; only pending/keymap/validators/conversion fns — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:13-31` _(both)_
- set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger delegate to keymap.update_custom_trigger with Some/None and do not record anything else — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-152` _(code)_
- update_custom_trigger mutates custom_trigger ONLY on bindings already in editable_custom_action_bindings/editable_bindings filtered by name; silent no-op (no record) if name absent — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- register_editable_bindings appends new bindings (Tracked::new), builds editable_bindings_by_name, and never consults any stored override — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:405-419` _(code)_
- AppContext register_editable_bindings/set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger simply forward to keystroke_matcher; no override store at the AppContext layer either — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1627-1661` _(both)_
- load_custom_keybindings iterates file entries and calls app.set_custom_trigger (Trigger::Empty for Removed at :44, Keystrokes at :47); keeps no in-memory applied map — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57` _(code)_
- load_custom_keybindings is called once inside fn launch (launch defined at lib.rs:2552) — `app/src/lib.rs:2557` _(code)_
- run closure calls initialize_app (call at lib.rs:1065; fn def at 1086) THEN launch (lib.rs:1077); the central keybinding init block runs inside initialize_app (lib.rs:1648-1703), so all registration precedes override apply — `app/src/lib.rs:1030-1078, 1648-1703` _(code)_
- editor/view register sites (518/908/928/936/953/961) are inside pub fn init(ctx: &mut AppContext) at :185 — ctx is the AppContext, not a per-view ModelContext — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:185` _(code)_
- Reproduced tally: 109 total register_editable_bindings sites = 99 in init + 7 notebooks (inside pub fn init at notebooks/editor/view.rs:90, awk-mislabeled as nested debug_notebooks_enabled) + 3 helpers (register_input_mode_bindings, add_overflow/add_open_setting_pages) — `app/src/ (grep+awk, see residual_unknowns)` _(code)_
- All 3 helpers invoked synchronously from init: register_input_mode_bindings called at terminal/view/init.rs:88 (inside pub fn init :80); add_open_setting_pages/add_overflow called at workspace/mod.rs:1329-1330 inside the single pub fn init at :78 (no intervening fn before def at :1344) — `app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:88,1150; app/src/workspace/mod.rs:78,1329-1330,1344,1489` _(code)_
- Independent enclosing-fn scan finds ZERO register_editable_bindings in any render/new/build_view/element/paint/layout function — no per-view/runtime registration path — `app/src/ (awk scan over all register sites)` _(code)_
- code::init and notebooks::init are reached once via workspace::init (workspace/mod.rs:107-108), and workspace::init is in the startup block at lib.rs:1654 — `app/src/workspace/mod.rs:107-108` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the hot-reload design decide that a re-apply-on-registration hook is optional/defensive rather than mandatory (all editable bindings register once at startup before overrides apply), while confirming a persistent applied-names map IS required for reset/diff on reload and must be seeded from the launch-time load_custom_keybindings.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Tally reproduced from /home/mhb/warp: `for f in $(grep -rl 'register_editable_bindings' app/src --include='*.rs' | grep -v '_tests.rs'); do awk '/^[[:space:]]*(pub(\([^)]*\))? )?(async )?fn [A-Za-z_]/{match($0,/fn [A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*/);fn=substr($0,RSTART+3,RLENGTH-3)} /register_editable_bindings/{print fn}' \"$f\"; done | sort | uniq -c | sort -rn` => 99 init, 7 debug_notebooks_enabled (awk nearest-fn artifact; actually inside pub fn init at notebooks/editor/view.rs:90), 1 register_input_mode_bindings, 1 add_overflow_menu_items_as_editable_binding, 1 add_open_setting_pages_as_editable_binding (total 109, matching `grep -c` of non-def non-test sites). Open product/arch decision (not code): whether the hot-reload design (a) only stores a persistent applied-names ledger for reset/diff, or (b) also adds a re-apply-on-registration hook to future-proof against lazily/plugin-contributed editable bindings. Today nothing registers editable bindings after initialize_app, so (b) guards a dormant risk, not a live bug; the applied-names set must be seeded from the launch-time load at lib.rs:2557 regardless (cross-refs A1-Q8). I did not exhaustively trace every one of the 99 `init` fns up to a startup caller, but the render/new/build_view/element/paint/layout scan returning empty plus the verified helper-call sites give high confidence none is on a runtime/per-view path.

The only deltas from the answer are immaterial citation nuances (initialize_app call line 1065 vs the fn-def line 1086 it cited; the loose "seeds custom_trigger to default None" wording). ### A1-Q8 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Where does the reload handler live, and how does it obtain a mutable AppContext? set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger are on AppContext, but the watcher fires inside ModelContext of WarpConfig. Do we mirror settings — emit a new WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Keybindings and handle it in an init-side subscriber that holds a mutable AppContext — and where does the applied-names set get OWNED?

**A.** Confirmed. Mirror settings exactly: emit a new WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Keybindings from handle_warp_managed_paths_event (app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134, currently no keybindings branch) and react in the init.rs-side subscriber on the WarpConfig model whose callback receives &mut AppContext (app/src/settings/init.rs:225-228) — the only place set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger (warpui_core core/app.rs:1637/1656) can be hosted. No keybindings reload exists today; the sole load is the one-shot launch call (app/src/lib.rs:2557). No applied-names set exists anywhere (grep for applied_names/active_custom/custom_names returns nothing); override state is stored distributed as EditableBinding.custom_trigger: Option<Trigger> (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:300), so a new owned applied-names set must be introduced and launch-seeded for diff-based reset.

VERIFIED AGAINST CODE — every cited file:line holds.

CURRENT STATE confirmed: load_custom_keybindings(app: &mut AppContext) is defined at app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57 and is invoked exactly once, from fn launch(ctx: &mut warpui::AppContext, ...) (app/src/lib.rs:2552) at lib.rs:2557 (keyboard::load_custom_keybindings(ctx)). That body only iterates names PRESENT in the file and calls app.set_custom_trigger (set for Removed→Trigger::Empty at :44, Keystrokes at :47); it never reverts a name that disappears and records nothing applied. handle_warp_managed_paths_event (app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134) has branches for themes (:81), workflows (:92), launch_configs (:103), tab_configs flagged (:114), and settings flagged (:129-132) — there is NO keybindings.yaml branch, even though the WarpConfig doc comment (app/src/user_config/mod.rs:79-81) lists keybindings.yaml among watched config_local_dir() files.

SETTINGS TEMPLATE confirmed: native.rs:129-132 — when FeatureFlag::SettingsFile.is_enabled() && update_touches_path(update, &crate::settings::user_preferences_toml_file_path()), the handler (running in ModelContext<Self>, native.rs:77) does NOT mutate; it only ctx.emit(WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Settings). app/src/settings/init.rs:212-216 subscribes handle_warp_config_change to WarpConfig::handle(ctx); that subscriber (init.rs:225-228) has signature (_: ModelHandle<WarpConfig>, event: &WarpConfigUpdateEvent, ctx: &mut AppContext) — a genuine &mut AppContext — gated `if !matches!(event, WarpConfigUpdateEvent::Settings) { return; }` then calls prefs.reload_from_disk() (:236) + SettingsManager::handle(ctx).update(...).reload_all_public_settings(ctx) (:246). That &mut AppContext is exactly what set_custom_trigger (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1637, &mut self, delegates to self.keystroke_matcher at :1638) and remove_custom_trigger (core/app.rs:1656, &mut self) require.

PLUMBING confirmed feasible: (1) WarpConfigUpdateEvent (mod.rs:54-73) has variants Themes/LocalUserWorkflows/LaunchConfigs/TabConfigs/TabConfigErrors/Settings/SettingsErrors/SettingsErrorsCleared — NO Keybindings; a variant must be added. (2) keybinding_file_path() = config_local_dir().join("keybindings.yaml") (keyboard.rs:95-96, KEYBINDINGS_FILE_NAME at :33); update_touches_path is in scope (defined native.rs:219, imports at native.rs:22-25). (3) The watcher already registers config_local_dir() recursively (app/src/warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:263-271), so filesystem events for keybindings.yaml already reach this handler and are simply dropped today.

ADVERSARIAL NUANCE confirmed: ModelContext<T> implements DerefMut→AppContext returning self.app (crates/warpui_core/src/core/model/context.rs:546-549; type Target = AppContext at :539), so you technically COULD call set_custom_trigger inside the native.rs handler via deref. But the established pattern hoists via an event so the keymap mutation lives on the init/AppContext side — the architecturally correct choice.

APPLIED-NAMES SET confirmed owned NOWHERE: grep for applied_names/active_custom/custom_names across app/ and crates/ returns nothing. Override state is distributed: EditableBinding.custom_trigger: Option<Trigger> (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:300, struct at :293), mutated by Keymap::update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433, iterates editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings) via Matcher::set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-145) / remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:146-151). The Keymap lives on the Matcher (keystroke_matcher, core/app.rs:605) on AppContext. Because load_custom_keybindings only SETS, and because the per-binding Option<Trigger> records no provenance (an in-app-UI edit via write_custom_keybinding and a file-applied override are indistinguishable), a settings-style reload must introduce a NEW owned set of file-applied names so it can diff old-vs-new and remove_custom_trigger dropped names. That set MUST be seeded from the launch load (lib.rs:2557). Owner is a design choice (keyboard.rs / a small singleton model / the keystroke_matcher itself) — recorded as residual.

### A1-Q9 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** Is 'round-trip safe' achievable in Axis 1 alone, given one name maps to N context-scoped bindings (editable_bindings_by_name, keymap.rs:30) and update_custom_trigger rewrites ALL of them (keymap.rs:422-433)? Can a flat file (one PersistedTrigger per name, keyboard.rs:169) faithfully round-trip a per-context override without Axis 2 context records?

**A.** Yes — round-trip is safe in Axis 1 alone for the current per-name override model. The whole pipeline (UI edit, persist, load, apply) is uniformly keyed by NAME with a single trigger, so the flat file is isomorphic to what the engine can represent; write-then-read-then-apply reproduces the identical applied keymap state. A per-context override is NOT round-trippable, but the file is not the only bottleneck: update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) takes no context/binding-id and rewrites all N same-name registrations, so a per-context override cannot even be CREATED today. Per-context overrides are a NEW capability gated on BOTH R3b/Axis 2 file records AND a per-binding (context-aware) override path in keymap.rs. CORRECTIONS to the original evidence: the count of names with >1 distinct description is 9, not 7 (move_forward_one_word and move_backward_one_word were missed — their descriptions differ only by capitalization but still survive the case-sensitive dedup); the move_forward_one_word keystroke detail is alt-right/ctrl-right (not ctrl-left); and the FixedBinding struct is at keymap.rs:277-286 (the cited 293-304 is EditableBinding).

CODE FACT (verified): the override/persist/load path is name-keyed end to end, with no context anywhere.

1. Engine override is per-name and rewrites ALL N. keymap.rs:30 holds editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>> (one name -> N indices), confirmed. update_custom_trigger(name, trigger) (keymap.rs:422-433) iterates BOTH editable_custom_action_bindings.iter_mut().filter(|b| b.name == name) (423-429) AND editable_bindings.iter_mut().filter(|b| b.name == name) (430-432) and sets the SAME custom_trigger on every registration. It takes no context/binding-id and cannot target one scope. Confirmed verbatim.

2. The UI/save/load callers are all name-keyed. set_custom_keybinding (util/bindings.rs:491-499) and remove_keystroke (settings_view/keybindings.rs:592 sets Trigger::Empty; :600 writes UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed) call AppContext::set_custom_trigger(name,...) (core/app.rs:1637-1639) -> matcher::set_custom_trigger (keymap/matcher.rs:137-141) -> keymap.update_custom_trigger(name, Some(trigger)). Persistence is CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>) (keyboard.rs:169) — one PersistedTrigger per name (write inserts by name keyboard.rs:73; load reapplies by name via set_custom_trigger keyboard.rs:39-47; "none"=Removed at keyboard.rs:15/192). load_custom_keybindings(ctx) is the only load site (lib.rs:2557) and carries no context. All confirmed.

3. The settings UI is deduped by name AND description. on_page_selected sorts by (description, name) (keybindings.rs:759-765) then .dedup_by(|a,b| a.name==b.name && a.description==b.description) (keybindings.rs:782). The comment at keybindings.rs:766-781 states: "Because bindings are saved/loaded by name, changes to one binding will affect the others. To reduce clutter, only show one binding for a given name and description." Confirmed.

CONSEQUENCE FOR ROUND-TRIP (confirmed): because UI edit -> file -> load -> apply are all keyed by a single name with a single trigger, write-then-read-then-apply reproduces the identical applied keymap state. The flat file is structurally isomorphic to the engine's per-name override capability; there is no per-context override STATE for it to lose. So round-trip safe IS achievable in Axis 1 alone for the existing capability surface, and there is NO capability gap between file and UI today — both are name-scoped.

WHY THE ADVERSARIAL FRAMING IS ONLY CONDITIONALLY TRUE (confirmed): a per-context override (different trigger for the Vim vs non-Vim registration of one name) is unrepresentable — not merely because the file is flat, but because the ENGINE cannot express it (update_custom_trigger is per-name, rewrites all N). Per-context overrides cannot be produced in Axis 1 at all, so the flat file faithfully round-trips everything the current model CAN produce. Per-context overrides are a NEW capability requiring BOTH (a) R3b/Axis 2 context records in the schema AND (b) a per-binding (not per-name) override path in keymap.rs (update_custom_trigger would need a context/binding-id selector). The flat file alone is not the limiting factor.

NOTE ON THE REPORT'S CITED EXAMPLE (confirmed with citation fix): actions.rs:51-60 (enter/VimEnter, numpadenter, shift-enter) are FixedBindings, which have no name field and are not editable, customizable, or persisted — irrelevant to round-trip. The FixedBinding struct is at keymap.rs:277-286 (no `name` field; doc comment line 275 "This action can't be reconfigured with a custom key binding trigger"; FixedBinding::new's first arg is `keystrokes: impl AsRef<str>`, keymap.rs:511-512 — NOT a name). The original answer cited keymap.rs:293-304 for this, but that range is the EditableBinding struct; the substantive claim is nonetheless correct. The genuine multi-context EDITABLE duplicates live in the editor_view:* family across the three editor views (code editor app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs, terminal editor app/src/editor/view/mod.rs, notebooks app/src/notebooks/editor/view.rs — all simultaneously active; e.g. editor_view:move_forward_one_word and editor_view:up each register in all three files), and a same-context same-file double registration of editor_view:move_forward_one_word at actions.rs:306-320.

COUNTS (reproduced exactly with the described methodology):
- 393 literal-named EditableBinding::new/empty registrations across non-test app/src and crates/warpui_core/src; 50 distinct names registered more than once. CONFIRMED exactly.
- CORRECTION: names carrying MORE THAN ONE distinct description (so the (name AND description) dedup does NOT collapse them) is 9, NOT 7. The original answer's list of 7 (editor_view:move_to_line_end, editor_view:move_to_line_start, editor_view:move_to_paragraph_end, notebookview:focus_terminal_input, terminal:ask_ai_assistant, terminal:copy, terminal:find) is correct as far as it goes, but omits editor_view:move_forward_one_word ("Move Forward One Word" vs "Move forward one word") and editor_view:move_backward_one_word ("Move Backward One Word" vs "Move backward one word"). These differ ONLY by capitalization, but BindingDescription's PartialEq (keymap.rs:92-96) compares the inner `description: String` case-sensitively, so the dedup does NOT collapse them either — they also render as multiple rows sharing one flat-file key. So the true count is 9.
- CORRECTION: the keystroke detail for the move_forward_one_word double registration is alt-right (mac) / ctrl-right (linux-win) plus meta-f (actions.rs:312-313, 320). The original answer wrote "alt-right/ctrl-left"; ctrl-left is actually the move_backward_one_word linux/win keystroke at actions.rs:305. The core claim (same name, same text_entry context, two distinct default keystrokes, customization collapses both to one trigger because update_custom_trigger is name-keyed) is unaffected and confirmed.

These 9 names render as multiple UI rows but share ONE flat-file key (the name); editing either row writes the same key and yields the same applied trigger — they cannot be rebound independently. This is the closest the codebase comes to the adversarial claim: a per-context capability gap visible in the UI yet unfixable in the flat schema. It is still NOT a round-trip break (whatever is set is faithfully persisted and reapplied); it is a "multiple UI rows, one file key, no independent override" limitation rooted in update_custom_trigger being name-keyed.

SCOPING IMPLICATION (unchanged, confirmed): the flat-schema round-trip is faithful for the current per-name override model and Axis 1 is independently shippable for that surface. Per-context (per-binding) overrides are structurally gated on BOTH R3b (Axis 2 file records) AND a per-binding override in keymap.rs; scope the Axis-1 round-trip leaf to per-name overrides — which is all the engine, UI, and file can produce today.

### A1-Q12 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** IF R3b lands in Axis 1, must the new record schema still deserialize todays existing flat name-to-trigger files on disk, and how?

**A.** Yes — a record-shaped R3b schema breaks back-compat with today's flat name-to-trigger files; without a duplex/migrating Deserialize plus an absent-vs-parse-error split, upgrade silently resets every user's keymap and the first in-app edit permanently clobbers the file.

CONFIRMED against app/src/keyboard.rs (sole loader; only load call site lib.rs:2557; write/remove callers settings_view/keybindings.rs:600 and util/bindings.rs:496/513).

YES, mandatory. The on-disk format is a flat top-level YAML map: CustomKeybindings is a newtype over HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> (keyboard.rs:169), PersistedTrigger wraps a String (keyboard.rs:174). CustomKeybindings derives Serialize/Deserialize/Default (keyboard.rs:167) and PersistedTrigger derives Serialize/Deserialize/Debug/PartialEq/Clone (keyboard.rs:173) — no version field, no serde(untagged), no alias, no hand-written Deserialize. So any R3b redesign (map -> record list, or wrapping in a versioned struct) makes serde_yaml fail on the legacy map; read_custom_keybindings catches the Err, warns, and returns None (keyboard.rs:132-138).

None is lossy twice. Startup: load_custom_keybindings applies triggers only inside `if let Some` (keyboard.rs:37-57), so None applies zero triggers — a silent reset of every customization, warning only. Write-back: write_custom_keybinding and remove_custom_keybinding (keyboard.rs:71,89) do read-then-unwrap_or_default; a parse-failure None becomes an EMPTY map, and save_custom_keybindings (keyboard.rs:103-122) calls create_file -> fs::File::create which TRUNCATES (verified app/src/util/file.rs:132-145), overwriting the file with just the one edited binding — PERMANENT loss on the first in-app edit (these write paths are reachable from settings_view/keybindings.rs:600 and util/bindings.rs:496/513).

Absent vs parse-error are conflated: File::open(...).ok()? returns None on any open error including NotFound (keyboard.rs:129), and serde failure also returns None (keyboard.rs:134-137), so unwrap_or_default treats both as no-bindings (the Q3 hazard). No test guards this: keyboard_tests.rs (6 tests, lines 1-79) covers only PersistedTrigger/UserDefinedKeybinding conversions, never CustomKeybindings deserialization or a legacy fixture. Also note keybindings.yaml is loaded once at startup with no watcher — native.rs handle_warp_managed_paths_event (lines 74-134) has branches for themes/workflows/launch_configs/tab_configs/settings.toml but none for keybindings.yaml — so the only mitigation point on this code path is the loader itself.

HOW (both needed): (a) a duplex/untagged Deserialize accepting BOTH the legacy map and the new record form, OR a version discriminant plus one-time migrate-on-load that rewrites the file; AND (b) split absent vs parse-error at keyboard.rs:129 and 132-138 so the unwrap_or_default write paths cannot empty-and-clobber a present-but-unparseable legacy file.

_Evidence:_
- On-disk schema is a flat top-level YAML map: CustomKeybindings newtype over HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> (:169), PersistedTrigger wraps String (:174); only plain serde derives at :167/:173 — no version/untagged/alias/custom Deserialize. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:167-174` _(code)_
- read_custom_keybindings returns None on file-open error including NotFound (:129 File::open(...).ok()?) and on serde parse failure (:134-137 warn+None), conflating absent vs parse-error; startup load_custom_keybindings (:37-57) applies triggers only inside if-let-Some, so None = zero custom triggers = silent reset. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57,128-139` _(code)_
- write/remove (:71,:89) read-then-unwrap_or_default; parse-failure None becomes empty map; save_custom_keybindings (:103-122) uses create_file -> fs::File::create which truncates (verified util/file.rs:132-145), so the next save overwrites the file with one binding — permanent legacy loss on first in-app edit. Write paths reachable from settings_view/keybindings.rs:600 and util/bindings.rs:496/513. — `app/src/keyboard.rs:71,89,103-122; app/src/util/file.rs:132-145` _(code)_
- No test guards file-level back-compat: keyboard_tests.rs (6 tests, lines 1-79) covers only PersistedTrigger<->UserDefinedKeybinding conversions, never CustomKeybindings deserialization or a legacy fixture. — `app/src/keyboard_tests.rs:1-79` _(code)_
- Sole load call site is lib.rs:2557 (startup); keybindings.yaml has no watcher branch in native.rs handle_warp_managed_paths_event (themes/workflows/launch_configs/tab_configs/settings.toml only), so the loader is the only mitigation point. — `app/src/lib.rs:2557; app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ A keybindings.yaml schema change for R3b MUST ship a CustomKeybindings Deserialize that accepts the legacy flat name-to-trigger map (untagged duplex or version+migrate) AND read_custom_keybindings MUST split absent from parse-error before the unwrap_or_default write paths run; otherwise upgrade silently resets every users keymap and the first in-app edit permanently clobbers their file. Axis 2 inherits this even if Axis 1 ships only the watcher over todays flat map.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ (1) WHETHER R3b lands in Axis 1 is gated on the A1-Q1 ruling, itself a documented contradiction (end-goals lists R3b as an Axis-1 leaf; report section 7 puts it in Phase 2); if deferred, Axis 2 inherits this same back-compat constraint over today's flat map. (2) WHICH mechanism to adopt (untagged duplex deserializer vs version field + migrate-on-load) is a design choice; whether a top-level untagged map-vs-list/record discrimination is unambiguous to serde_yaml depends on the not-yet-fixed new record shape. Neither is resolvable from current code.
- _Verification:_ I opened keyboard.rs in full, keyboard_tests.rs in full, native.rs:60-149, util/file.rs:110-147, and grepped lib.rs and the whole app/crates tree for load/read/write/remove call sites. Every cited file:line is accurate. Specifically verified: (1) struct/derive lines 167/169/173/174 are exactly as cited; (2) :129 .ok()? returns None on any open error incl NotFound and :134-137 returns None on serde error — conflation confirmed; (3) :71/:89 unwrap_or_default; (4) the clobber hinges on truncation — I checked util/file.rs:132-145 and confirmed create_file calls fs::File::create, which truncates an existing file, so the empty-map-overwrite is real, not assumed; (5) keyboard_tests.rs has 6 tests, all conversion-only, no CustomKeybindings deserialization or fixture; (6) load_custom_keybindings has exactly one caller (lib.rs:2557); write/remove callers exist, so the first-in-app-edit clobber path is reachable. Uncited check beyond the answer: native.rs handle_warp_managed_paths_event has NO keybindings.yaml branch, confirming there is no hot-reload (the answer correctly did not claim one). One immaterial wording imprecision in the original detailed_answer: it said 'Both derive only plain Serialize/Deserialize/Default' — PersistedTrigger actually derives Serialize/Deserialize/Debug/PartialEq/Clone (not Default); CustomKeybindings is the one with Default. This does not affect any conclusion (the load-bearing point — no untagged/alias/version/custom Deserialize — holds), and I corrected the wording in the final answer. All evidence claims as originally written are accurate.

### A1-Q13 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** How do we test hot-reload plus reset semantics deterministically when keyboard.rs load/read/write are cfg(not(test)) with no-op cfg(test) stubs, saving is suppressed via WARP_TEST_DISABLE_KEYBINDING_SAVE, and create_file deliberately errors under cfg(test)?

**A.** Two test layers with opposite capabilities. Unit tests (cfg(test)) cannot touch the file path at all (real IO is compiled out to no-op stubs and create_file errors), so keyboard_tests.rs only exercises pure serde conversions (7 tests, not 8). Integration tests compile the app as cfg(not(test)), so the REAL load/read path runs and an existing test already writes a real keybindings.yaml to a temp config dir and asserts the live keymap. But hot-reload itself does not exist yet (load runs once at boot; handle_warp_managed_paths_event has no keybindings branch), so the deterministic seam must be added: either (A) add a keybindings.yaml watcher branch and drive it from an integration test, or (B) refactor keyboard.rs for path/IO injection so cfg(test) can drive a temp file in-process.

The answer is substantively correct; I verified every cited file:line directly and found a single factual defect: keyboard_tests.rs has 7 tests, not 8 (grep -c "#[test]" returns 7; I also counted by hand: 2 user->persisted, 2 persisted->user, 2 none-sentinel round-trip, 1 unparsable-error). Corrected below; all other claims hold.

CODE FACTS verified directly:

1) UNIT-TEST LAYER IS BLIND TO THE FILE PATH. The IO surface (load_custom_keybindings, write/remove_custom_keybinding, save/read_custom_keybindings) is each individually gated #[cfg(not(test))] (keyboard.rs: load 36-57, write 63-75, remove 78-93, save 100-122, read 127-139) - not one literal contiguous block, but the effect the answer describes is correct. Under cfg(test) these are replaced by no-op stubs (keyboard.rs:144-153). Even bypassing them, util/file.rs create_file returns Err(io::Error::from_raw_os_error(1)) under cfg(test) (file.rs:132-135). So the only unit tests, keyboard_tests.rs (7 tests), assert solely UserDefinedKeybinding <-> PersistedTrigger conversions plus the none sentinel and the unparsable-error case; they never touch load/read/write, the file, the matcher, or set_custom_trigger. Reset (None), None-on-failure, tri-state reconcile, and hot-reload are NOT unit-testable today.

2) INTEGRATION-TEST LAYER CAN DRIVE A REAL FILE. crates/integration depends on warp compiled as cfg(not(test)), so real IO is live. keybinding_file_path is re-exported only #[cfg(not(test))] (app/src/integration_testing/keybindings.rs:1-2), and create_file_with_contents (app/src/integration_testing/mod.rs:62-67) calls the REAL util::file::create_file (errors under cfg(test) but works here). A passing end-to-end test, test_open_and_close_context_menu_with_keybinding (crates/integration/src/test.rs:1644-1654), in .with_setup writes a real keybindings.yaml ("terminal:open_block_list_context_menu_via_keybinding": ctrl-m) into the temp config dir BEFORE boot, then sends ctrl-m and asserts the context menu opens/closes - proving real-file to live-keymap at load. The builder sets DISABLE_SAVE_ENV_VAR="true" for every test (builder.rs:183), suppressing in-app saves by ENV VAR (not cfg) to avoid clobbering the dev file.

3) HOT-RELOAD DOES NOT EXIST YET. load_custom_keybindings is called exactly once, at launch (app/src/lib.rs:2557; a repo-wide grep finds no other non-stub call site). handle_warp_managed_paths_event (app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134) branches on themes/workflows/launch_configs/tab_configs/settings(user_preferences_toml_file_path) but has NO keybindings.yaml branch. Yet the watcher ALREADY watches config_local_dir() recursively (warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:263-272, gated on should_register_config_local_dir i.e. platforms where it differs from data_dir) where keybindings.yaml lives (keyboard.rs:95-97 keybinding_file_path = config_local_dir().join("keybindings.yaml"); doc user_config/mod.rs:80-81 names keybindings.yaml), so change events arrive and are dropped. A 500ms debounce sits on that path (warp_managed_paths_watcher.rs:17-19). Reset/none semantics exist only at serde + load (keyboard.rs:15, 192-193 parse; 43-45 applies Trigger::Empty), i.e. only at boot.

HOW TO TEST DETERMINISTICALLY (seam the design must pick):

(A) Integration harness - lowest friction, pattern already proven. Reuse the write-real-file-then-assert-keymap approach (test.rs:1644). To test HOT-reload (mid-session, not boot), first add a keybindings.yaml branch to handle_warp_managed_paths_event that re-runs the load path on change (watcher already delivers the events). Then an integration test: boot, rewrite the temp keybindings.yaml mid-test, pump the run-loop/watcher, assert the new keymap via keystroke dispatch; for RESET write the action mapped to none, fire watcher, assert the binding is gone. Determinism risk: the 500ms async debounce - the test needs to flush/await it deterministically, or expose a synchronous reload-now entry point. Also: with DISABLE_SAVE_ENV_VAR set, an integration test cannot validate the in-app write-to-file-to-reload round-trip end-to-end unless it clears the env var or asserts via the in-memory map.

(B) Unit-level via IO/path injection - requires refactor, gives watcher-free determinism. Impossible today because keyboard.rs IO fns are cfg(not(test)) and create_file errors under cfg(test). Refactor so the path/reader is injectable (thread keybinding_file_path or a Read source through load/read instead of the cfg(test) no-op stubs). Then a cfg(test) test points at a temp file written with plain std::fs, drives parse + set_custom_trigger against a real AppContext keymap, and asserts via the matcher - letting the None-vs-parse-error split (Q3) and tri-state reconcile (Q2) be asserted without watcher timing.

- Doc comment explicitly lists keybindings.yaml among config_local_dir()-watched files. — `app/src/user_config/mod.rs:80-81` _(code)_
