### A1-Q16 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Is per-reload cost acceptable? update_custom_trigger linearly scans editable_custom_action_bindings AND all editable_bindings per name (keymap.rs:423-432), ignoring the editable_bindings_by_name index (keymap.rs:30) that get_binding_by_name already uses (keymap.rs:380), so reapplying N file entries over ~437 editable bindings is O(N*437) on every 500ms-debounced save. Do we route the override path through the existing name index?

**A.** Confirmed. update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) does two un-indexed linear .filter(b.name==name) scans over editable_custom_action_bindings then editable_bindings, bypassing the editable_bindings_by_name index (keymap.rs:30, used only by get_binding_by_name at :380-385). A full file load is O(N*M) with M≈435 non-test editable bindings. But that reapply-all runs ONCE at app launch (lib.rs:2557, the only real call site); there is no 500ms-debounced reapply and no live hot-reload of keybindings.yaml — handle_warp_managed_paths_event (native.rs:74-134) has no keybindings branch and WarpConfigUpdateEvent (mod.rs:54-73) has no Keybindings variant. So absolute cost is ~10^4 &str comparisons, sub-millisecond, one-time — acceptable today. Routing through the existing name index is a low-effort cleanup, not yet done, and is a design choice. The question's "500ms-debounced save" premise is incorrect.

I opened every cited file and re-ran the counts. All material claims hold.\n\n1) update_custom_trigger bypasses the index — CONFIRMED. crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433: the function body is exactly two un-indexed scans, `editable_custom_action_bindings.iter_mut().filter(|b| b.name == name)` (lines 423-429) then `editable_bindings.iter_mut().filter(|b| b.name == name)` (lines 430-432). It never consults editable_bindings_by_name. (Question cited :423-432 for the scans, answer cited :422-433 for the function; both accurate.)\n\n2) Index exists, populated, and used elsewhere — CONFIRMED. `editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>` declared at keymap.rs:30; populated in register_editable_bindings at keymap.rs:414-417; consumed by get_binding_by_name at keymap.rs:380-385 (`self.editable_bindings_by_name.get(name)?` then index lookup). So the index demonstrably exists and is the established lookup path — update_custom_trigger is the outlier.\n\n3) Routing — CONFIRMED. matcher.rs:137-141 set_custom_trigger → keymap.update_custom_trigger(.., Some(trigger)); matcher.rs:146-152 remove_custom_trigger → update_custom_trigger(.., None). Both go straight in.\n\n4) O(N*M) load — CONFIRMED. app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57 load_custom_keybindings loops `for (name, trigger) in keybindings.0` and calls app.set_custom_trigger per entry, so N entries × O(M) scan = O(N*M).\n\n5) One-time launch caller — CONFIRMED. grep shows the only real definition+call: keyboard.rs:37 (cfg(not(test)) impl), keyboard.rs:145 (cfg(test) no-op stub), and exactly one call site app/src/lib.rs:2557, inside fn launch between APP_LAUNCHED and KEYBINDINGS_LOADED interval marks. No per-save or runtime caller.\n\n6) No hot-reload — CONFIRMED. app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134 handle_warp_managed_paths_event has branches only for themes_dir, workflows_dir, launch_configs_dir, tab_configs_dir (flag-gated), and settings.toml (user_preferences_toml_file_path). No keybindings.yaml branch. app/src/user_config/mod.rs:54-73 WarpConfigUpdateEvent variants are Themes, LocalUserWorkflows, LaunchConfigs, TabConfigs, TabConfigErrors, Settings, SettingsErrors, SettingsErrorsCleared — no Keybindings. So editing keybindings.yaml needs a restart. The write path is also synchronous and undebounced: write_custom_keybinding (keyboard.rs:64-75) → save_custom_keybindings (keyboard.rs:103-122) writes via serde_yaml::to_writer/BufWriter with no async spawn or debounce.\n\n7) UI edit is single O(M), not O(N*M) — CONFIRMED. app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-506 set_custom_keybinding calls ctx.set_custom_trigger once then write_custom_keybinding once; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:590-602 remove_keystroke calls ctx.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) once then write_custom_keybinding once. One O(M) scan per edit.\n\n8) M≈435 — REPRODUCED EXACTLY. `grep -rn 'EditableBinding::new' crates/ app/ | grep -v 'tests.rs|test.rs|_tests' | wc -l` = 435 (451 including tests). It is a static call-site count and thus an approximation of runtime editable_bindings length, which the answer correctly hedges.\n\n9) Separate cloned custom-action vector — CONFIRMED. keymap.rs:409-417: editable_custom_action_bindings receives `Tracked::new((*binding).clone())`, a separate copy; the name index only covers editable_bindings, so routing the override path through the index would still leave the custom-action copy linear unless it gets its own index.\n\nCOST VERDICT holds: N = entries in CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger>) at keyboard.rs:169 (one per override, realistically tens), M≈435, so launch reapply ≈ 10^4 string comparisons, sub-millisecond, once. The question's \"every 500ms-debounced save\" premise is materially wrong (no debounce, no live reapply) and the answer correctly refutes it. The index reuse is a real but optional/unimplemented cleanup.

_Evidence:_
- update_custom_trigger does two un-indexed linear .filter(b.name==name) scans over editable_custom_action_bindings then editable_bindings, never consulting editable_bindings_by_name — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- editable_bindings_by_name index declared at :30, populated in register_editable_bindings at :414-417, and consumed by get_binding_by_name at :380-385 — establishing it as the standard lookup path that update_custom_trigger bypasses — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:30,380-385,414-417` _(both)_
- set_custom_trigger and remove_custom_trigger route straight into keymap.update_custom_trigger — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-152` _(code)_
- load_custom_keybindings iterates all N file entries calling app.set_custom_trigger, giving O(N*M) per full load — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57` _(code)_
- Only real caller of load_custom_keybindings is the one-time launch path; grep shows lib.rs:2557 plus a cfg(test) no-op stub at keyboard.rs:145, no per-save caller — `app/src/lib.rs:2557` _(code)_
- handle_warp_managed_paths_event branches cover themes/workflows/launch_configs/tab_configs/settings.toml ONLY — no keybindings.yaml branch, so no runtime re-read — `app/src/user_config/native.rs:74-134` _(code)_
- WarpConfigUpdateEvent has no Keybindings variant (Themes/LocalUserWorkflows/LaunchConfigs/TabConfigs/TabConfigErrors/Settings/SettingsErrors/SettingsErrorsCleared) — `app/src/user_config/mod.rs:54-73` _(code)_
- A single UI edit calls set_custom_trigger once then writes once (O(M) per edit, not O(N*M)) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-506; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:590-602` _(code)_
- M reproduces exactly: grep -rn 'EditableBinding::new' crates/ app/ | grep -v 'tests.rs|test.rs|_tests' | wc -l = 435 (451 with tests) — static, an approximation of runtime length — `crates/ + app/ (grep)` _(code)_
- editable_custom_action_bindings is a separate cloned vector (Tracked::new((*binding).clone())) and the index covers only editable_bindings, so it stays linear without its own index — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:409-417` _(code)_
- Keybinding write path is synchronous and undebounced: save_custom_keybindings writes via serde_yaml::to_writer/BufWriter with no async spawn — `app/src/keyboard.rs:64-75,103-122` _(code)_
- N = entries in CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger>), one per override — `app/src/keyboard.rs:169` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc drop the O(N*M)-per-save performance worry: the quadratic reapply runs once at launch at negligible absolute cost, and there is no debounced or live reapply to amortize. It reframes the real Axis-1 work as (1) deciding whether to add live reload for keybindings.yaml (today it requires restart), and (2) an optional low-effort cleanup to route update_custom_trigger through the existing name index (plus a parallel index for the custom-action copy) if/when live reload makes the override path hot.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Exact runtime length of editable_bindings (435 is a static EditableBinding::new call-site count; bulk/loop/macro registration could shift it, but it confirms M is in the few-hundreds). Realistic worst-case N (largest plausible keybindings.yaml) was not measured. Whether to route the override path through editable_bindings_by_name (and add a parallel index for the cloned editable_custom_action_bindings copy) is a design decision, currently NOT done. Separately, the larger Axis-1 liveness gap surfaced here is non-code: keybindings.yaml is not hot-reloaded at all (requires app restart), unlike settings.toml/themes/workflows which have watcher branches.
- _Verification:_ I independently opened all 8 cited source locations plus locations the answer leaned on but did not pin: save_custom_keybindings/read_custom_keybindings (keyboard.rs:99-135) to confirm the write path is synchronous with no debounce, and a full repo grep for 'keybindings.yaml|KEYBINDINGS_FILE_NAME|keybindings_file' to confirm no other watcher/reload path exists (only the loader, the AI-skill path-template, and a doc comment). Every evidence claim's file:line says what the answer asserts. I re-ran the M grep myself: 435 non-test / 451 with tests — exactly matching the answer's number (the question's 'roughly 437' is the loose figure; the answer's 435 is the precise non-test count). Verified update_custom_trigger truly never touches the index (read the whole function body). Verified the two scanned vectors are distinct (one a clone). Verified the only call site of load_custom_keybindings is the launch path; the apparent 'second definition' at keyboard.rs:145 is a cfg(test) no-op, not an extra runtime caller. Checked for app/-vs-crates scope confusion: the index/scan code is in crates/warpui_core, the loader/watcher/write code is in app/ — the answer keeps these correctly separated. No overgeneralization found: claims are scoped to editable bindings, not fixed bindings, and the cost analysis is honest. The only soft spot is the static-vs-runtime M approximation, which the answer already flags as a residual unknown. No material error.

## Axis 2 — Scope & context control

### A2-Q7 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Do config-authored predicates evaluate against a SINGLE per-view Context or a merged chain context? contexts_from_responder_chain (core/app.rs:1839-1864) builds a Vec<Context> with no merge loop (Context::extend at context.rs:90-97 is unused); dispatch_keystroke/push_keystroke (app.rs:2005-2041; matcher.rs:307-346) eval each predicate against one per-view ctx. The architecture report (line 32) wrongly claims context is "merged up the responder chain." Do we (a) keep per-view eval and forbid cross-level ANDs in the documented vocabulary, or (b) build a merged-context variant for predicate eval?

**A.** Per-view, decisively. Predicates (config-authored or compiled) are evaluated against exactly ONE view's Context at a time. The engine never merges the responder chain: contexts_from_responder_chain builds a Vec<Context> by pushing each view's keymap_context separately (only IMEOpen is inserted), and every matcher entry point evals binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx) against a single ctx. The keymap Context::extend exists but has no callers on a keymap Context. The architecture report's "merged up the responder chain" claim (line 32) is wrong.

Independently verified against the code; the original answer holds in full.

1) No merge building the chain. contexts_from_responder_chain (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1839-1862) does `let mut context = view.keymap_context(self); ... context_chain.push(context);` per view. The ONLY mutation is `context.set.insert("IMEOpen")` (app.rs:1853); push at 1855. No extend/fold/union across views. Confirmed.

2) Eval is always against a single per-view ctx. dispatch_keystroke (app.rs:2005) iterates `context_chain.iter_mut().enumerate().rev()` (leaf->root, 2014) and calls push_keystroke with ONE ctx (2018); the action target is `responder_chain[0..=i]` (2030). push_keystroke (matcher.rs:307) checks `binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx)` (327) against that single ctx; match_standard (350, eval 353) and match_custom (363, eval 366/371) do the same. The editable_bindings_for_view path (app.rs:1832-1834) is also per-view: `contexts.iter().any(|ctx| action.in_context(ctx))` — each view's Context tested independently, never a union.

3) ContextPredicate::eval (context.rs:100-119) takes exactly one `&Context`; And/Or recurse on the same ctx (115-116). And(left,right) requires BOTH atoms satisfied by ONE view's set/map. There is no FromStr/parser for ContextPredicate anywhere (rg over crates/ and app/ returned none) — predicates are Rust-only via macros/operators.

4) Context::extend (context.rs:90-97) is dead in the keymap path. The turbofish grep `Context::extend` returns nothing, AND the only method-style `context.extend(...)` in the repo (app/src/ai/blocklist/controller/input_context.rs:93) operates on a `Vec<AIAgentContext>`, not keymap::Context — so the keymap extend genuinely has zero callers. The architecture report at .understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:32 literally states contexts are "built per-view by keymap_context (core/mod.rs:252) and merged up the responder chain (app.rs:1846-1864)" — the "merged" half is false; that range is exactly the push-without-merge loop.

CONSEQUENCE: A predicate ANDing flags owned by DIFFERENT chain levels silently never matches. The question's `CodeEditorView & Workspace_SingleTab` is a real instance: Workspace_SingleTab is inserted only into the Workspace view's own Context (app/src/workspace/view.rs:25011; sibling flags PaneDragging 24974, TextOpen 24985, CloseWindow 25003, MultipleTabs 25014), while the code editor's Context (app/src/code/editor/view.rs:2388-2410) is built purely from its own state (NON_EDITABLE_KEYMAP_CONTEXT, Vim/VimNormalMode/VimVisualMode, FindBarAvailable) and never contains Workspace_* flags.

How "cross-level"-looking ANDs work today (both NOT engine merge):
- Register on the SAME view that owns all flags. Workspace-level bindings (app/src/workspace/mod.rs:557 `Workspace & Workspace_MultipleTabs & !Workspace_PaneDragging`; 831-834 `Workspace & Workspace_MultipleTabs & !Workspace_LeftmostTabActive & !Workspace_PaneDragging`) work because all atoms live in the single Workspace Context and dispatch at the Workspace level — intra-view.
- Imperative injection into a leaf's single Context. The TERMINAL editor exposes `keymap_context_modifier` (app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:8703-8705: `if let Some(modifier) = &self.keymap_context_modifier { modifier(&mut context, ctx); }`); terminal input wires one (app/src/terminal/input.rs:2846+) injecting TERMINAL_INPUT_PAGE_KEYS_HANDLED_BY_INPUT (2849), CTRL_ENTER_ACCEPTS_PROMPT_SUGGESTION (2864), AGENT_VIEW_ENABLED (2868). Still single-context eval, just merged-into-one-Context before eval.

CODE EDITOR vs TERMINAL EDITOR differ: terminal editor HAS the modifier hook; code editor (view.rs:2388-2410) has NO such hook, so cross-level ANDs are strictly impossible for code-editor bindings.

The (a) vs (b) eval-boundary choice remains a design decision (residual_unknowns).

This gates the predicate vocabulary/grammar (which atoms may be combined) and the validation policy — either document/forbid cross-level ANDs (option a) or scope a merged-context eval variant with a precedence + absent-key audit (option b).

(b) a merged-context variant changes matching semantics, not just capability: a merged ctx makes ancestor flags visible to leaf bindings, so existing leaf predicates relying on negation/absent-key semantics (e.g. NOT seeing an ancestor's Workspace_PaneDragging) could start/stop matching — every such predicate must be audited (compounds with Q9's absent-key asymmetry). An undocumented third state already exists in code: the terminal editor's keymap_context_modifier imperative injection (terminal only; code editor lacks it), so any 'no cross-level ANDs' rule the doc states is already partially violated by injection and would need to bless or deprecate that hook. Whether to keep/generalize/remove keymap_context_modifier is itself a product/architecture decision.

### A2-Q8 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** How do config-parsed predicate atoms (runtime Strings) evaluate against a Context/ContextPredicate that store static-str (context.rs 3-18)? Intern/leak to static-str (a), switch to Cow/interned IDs per R3a (b), or eval via a side table (c)?

**A.** Substantively correct. No config-side predicate exists today: there is no FromStr/parser for ContextPredicate, and config (keyboard.rs:169) is a flat HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> with UserDefinedKeybinding being only Keystrokes or Removed (keyboard.rs:20-25) — no when/context field. Context.set/map and every ContextPredicate variant are &'static str (context.rs:5-6,11-13), and eval (context.rs:101-119) keys on &'static str via set.contains/map.get. A runtime String cannot fill those slots, so a future parser must choose (a) leak/intern to &'static str (lowest churn, existing codebase pattern), (b) Cow/interned IDs per report R3a (wide refactor), or (c) a side lookup table (indirection). ### A2-Q9 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** Do we keep the asymmetric absent-key semantics for config-authored predicates, where ContextPredicate::NotEqual returns TRUE when the key is absent (unwrap_or(true), context.rs:113) and Not(Identifier) returns true when absent, while Equal/Identifier return false when absent (context.rs:103-108)? So a config predicate like mode != "insert" MATCHES in every view that has no mode key at all?

**A.** Code-verified and the core answer is exactly right: the absent-key asymmetry is real. Positive atoms fail-closed on a missing key (Identifier→false via set.contains, context.rs:103; Equal→false via unwrap_or(false), :104-108), and negative atoms fail-open (NotEqual→true via unwrap_or(true), :109-113; Not(...)→!eval, :114). So a bare `!=`/`!` predicate matches against every per-view Context that lacks the key. Per-view eval is confirmed (app.rs:1844-1863 builds one independent Context per view; app.rs:1873-1874 + matcher.rs:285/:327 eval each independently). No FromStr/parser exists yet, so config-authored predicates don't exist today; KEEPING the semantics is a product decision, not a code fact. CORRECTION: the answer's claim of "ZERO ne!/NotEqual constructions in product code; only assert_ne! hits" is FALSE — there are 12 real `ne!("TerminalView_BlockSelectionCardinality","None")` predicates in app/src/terminal/view/init.rs (385,393,439,450,577,586,595,604,613,670,679,765), each ANDed with the positive gate id!("Terminal"). The deeper conclusion (every negative atom is gated by a positive scope id, so no BARE negatives ship today) still holds.

VERDICT: corrected. The answer to the actual question (the asymmetric absent-key semantics) is fully verified and correct; one supporting evidence claim is materially wrong and self-contradictory, and I fixed it. Confidence high.

VERIFIED EXACTLY AS STATED:
1) Asymmetry — crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:101-119. Identifier(name)=>ctx.set.contains(*name) (FALSE when absent, :103); Equal=>map.get(l).map(==).unwrap_or(false) (:104-108, FALSE when absent); NotEqual=>map.get(l).map(!=).unwrap_or(true) (:109-113, TRUE when absent); Not(p)=>!p.eval (:114). So positives fail-closed and negatives fail-open on a missing key — exactly as A2-Q9 asserts. Not(Equal(k,v)) over-matches identically to NotEqual(k,v) when k is absent.
2) Single test — context_tests.rs:4-26 is the only #[test] fn; :22-23 assert `(!id!("a")).eval(&Context::default())` == true. Matches the spec's "How to answer" note that coverage is near-zero (~1 test fn).
3) Per-view eval — app.rs:1839-1863 contexts_from_responder_chain pushes one Context per responder view into a Vec<Context> (and at :1853 inserts "IMEOpen" per view); app.rs:1872-1874 iterates context_chain.iter_mut().rev() and matches each entry independently; matcher.rs:282-286 bindings_for_context filters via context_predicate.eval(&context); matcher.rs:324-334 push_keystroke evals binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx) against the current per-view ctx. Cross-view merge does not happen; Context::extend (context.rs:89-98) merges set+map only within one view. So a bare negative predicate is tested against every view's Context and matches wherever the key is absent — confirmed.

The only product map/Equal key is "TerminalView_BlockSelectionCardinality" (terminal/view.rs:27525, the sole non-test `.map.insert` in app/src). CORRECTION (material): The answer's point 4 states "there are ZERO bare ne!/NotEqual predicate constructions in product code (the only ne! hits are assert_ne! in tests)" and reports the repro `grep -rn "ne!(" crates/warpui_core crates/editor crates/vim app/src | grep -v assert_ne → (no output; zero real ne! predicates)`. Re-running that EXACT command yields 12 real predicate constructions in app/src/terminal/view/init.rs at lines 385, 393, 439, 450, 577, 586, 595, 604, 613, 670, 679, 765 — each `ne!("TerminalView_BlockSelectionCardinality", "None")`. This also self-contradicts the answer's own point 5, which acknowledges that exact map key. So "zero ne! predicates / only assert_ne!" is false. HOWEVER, all 12 are ANDed with the positive gate `id!("Terminal")` (the continuation-line cases at :450 and :765 are part of AND-chains beginning `id!("Terminal") & ...`). Because Identifier fail-closes when "Terminal" is absent, the whole &-chain is masked outside Terminal views — so the answer's deeper conclusion ("every negative atom is gated by a positive scope identifier; no BARE negatives ship; the footgun is contained by construction, not by eval") STILL HOLDS and is in fact better supported: even the ne! over the one map key is positively gated.

NET: The product/design question ("do we KEEP it for config authors") remains non-code-decidable; the code fact (the asymmetry + per-view eval + no parser) is settled and matches the answer. Only the "zero ne! constructions" evidence needed fixing.

### A2-Q11 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** A2-Q11. How does context-scoped REMOVAL work, given today's `Removed` maps to `set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty)` (keyboard.rs:44) which is name-wide and overloads `Trigger::Empty` ('cannot actually be matched', keymap.rs:48, with no real `Unbound`)? Can a user remove a binding in context X while keeping it in context Y, and does that pull in the Axis-3 `Unbound` work (L4/R2)?

**A.** No. Context-scoped removal does not work today. Removal funnels through the name-only `update_custom_trigger`, which sets `custom_trigger = Some(Trigger::Empty)` on EVERY editable binding sharing that name regardless of each binding's `context_predicate` (keymap.rs:422-433) — so removing name N disables it in all contexts at once. There is no context argument anywhere in the write path, no context field in the on-disk schema, and no real `Unbound` variant (only Keystrokes/Standard/Custom/Empty, keymap.rs:44-48). Expressing "remove in context X, keep in context Y" needs the same name+context/binding-id targeting Axis 2's scoped override needs; and truly suppressing an underlying default (or a Fixed per-context fork) pulls in Axis 3's Unbound/precedence (R2) and Fixed-remappability work, because Empty is shadow-by-non-participation, not suppression. Verified against the cited code; all line refs accurate.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened every cited file plus uncited corroborating locations (context.rs predicate/eval, match_custom). All line numbers are accurate and the conclusion holds.\n\nREMOVAL PATH (re-traced, both file-load and in-app UI):\n- Config load: `UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed` -> `app.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty)` (app/src/keyboard.rs:43-44). On disk this is the flat sentinel `\"none\"` (keyboard.rs:15 const; serialized at keyboard.rs:182-184).\n- In-app UI remove (`remove_keystroke`): `ctx.set_custom_trigger(row.binding.name.clone(), Trigger::Empty)` (app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:592) then `write_custom_keybinding(row.binding.name.clone(), UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed)` (keybindings.rs:600). util/bindings.rs `set_custom_keybinding` (:491-492) and `reset_keybinding_to_default` (:511-512) likewise pass only a name.\n- `AppContext::set_custom_trigger(name: String, trigger: Trigger)` (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1637-1639) — signature carries NO context — delegates to `Matcher::set_custom_trigger` (matcher.rs:137-141), which calls `keymap.update_custom_trigger(name.as_str(), Some(trigger))`. `remove_custom_trigger` (matcher.rs:146-152) is the same with `None` (used by the reset-to-default path, distinct from removal).\n- `Keymap::update_custom_trigger` (keymap.rs:422-433) iterates `editable_custom_action_bindings` and `editable_bindings`, FILTERING ONLY BY `b.name == name`, writing `binding.custom_trigger = trigger.clone()` to each. The binding's own `context_predicate` is never consulted. `editable_bindings_by_name` maps one name -> Vec<usize> (keymap.rs:28-30), so a single name routinely covers multiple same-name bindings with DIFFERENT context predicates, and removal hits all of them.\n\nWHY THIS BLOCKS SCOPED REMOVAL:\nEach `EditableBinding` already stores its own `context_predicate` (keymap.rs:297) AND its own `custom_trigger` (keymap.rs:300), and `as_lens` (keymap.rs:741-747) lets the custom trigger override the default — so per-binding storage COULD hold a scoped removal. What is missing is (a) any way to TARGET a single same-name binding (`set_custom_trigger`/`update_custom_trigger` are name-keyed, no context arg) and (b) any context field in the on-disk schema: `CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>)` is a flat name->string map (keyboard.rs:169) and `PersistedTrigger` is a bare `String` (keyboard.rs:174). Reinforcing point I verified that the answer did not cite: `ContextPredicate` and `Context` use `&'static str` everywhere and have NO `FromStr` (context.rs:3-18, :100-120) — a context literally cannot be parsed from config today, so the schema gap is structural, not just a missing field.\n\nOVERLOADED `Trigger::Empty` IS SHADOW, NOT SUPPRESS:\n`Trigger` has exactly four variants — Keystrokes, Standard, Custom, Empty — and NO `Unbound` (keymap.rs:44-48; comment :48 \"empty trigger (cannot actually be matched)\"; a repo-wide grep found only unrelated `Unbounded` offset-positioning variants, no `Unbound` trigger). Empty is inert in keystroke matching because `push_keystroke` matches solely `if let Trigger::Keystrokes(...)` and CONTINUES scanning all other bindings (matcher.rs:324-337); any other same-keystroke binding whose `context_predicate.eval(ctx)` is true still fires. So Empty means \"this binding does not participate,\" not \"this key is suppressed.\" It cannot disable an underlying default/Fixed binding — exactly Axis 3's end state (\"a user can truly disable a default, not just shadow it\", questions doc line 432), needing a real Unbound suppression in the precedence model (R2).\n\nNUANCE I FOUND (does not refute; strengthens): for CUSTOM-ACTION (macOS menu) bindings, `match_custom` additionally checks `binding.original_trigger` (matcher.rs:366-377), so a custom_trigger=Empty does NOT even suppress via the menu path — the action still resolves through original_trigger. This is an even stronger demonstration that Empty is not true suppression; the keystroke-removal conclusion is unaffected.\n\nWHERE IT PULLS IN AXIS 3:\n1. Fixed targets: `update_custom_trigger` touches only `editable_bindings`/`editable_custom_action_bindings`, NEVER `fixed_bindings` (keymap.rs:26 field; :422-433). The flagship per-context Vim forks (enter/backspace) are FixedBindings, so scoped removal of those is unreachable without Axis 3's Fixed-remappable work.\n2. True suppression: even for two disjoint-context editable bindings, Empty works only because nothing else binds that key in X; once a more-general or Fixed binding underlies it, the key still fires — requiring a real Unbound (R2).\n\nNET: A user CANNOT remove a binding in context X while keeping it in context Y. Minimum fix = the name+context/binding-id targeting Axis 2 already needs, plus a context field (and a parseable predicate) in the schema. Headline cases additionally require Axis 3 (Unbound suppression R2 and Fixed remappability).

_Evidence:_
- Removal config path: Removed maps to set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty), name-only, no context; line 43 match arm, line 44 the call — `app/src/keyboard.rs:43-44` _(code)_
- On-disk schema is flat HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>(:169) with PersistedTrigger=bare String(:174), removal sentinel const 'none'(:15) serialized at :182-184; no context field anywhere — `app/src/keyboard.rs:15,169,174,182-184` _(code)_

- util/bindings set_custom_keybinding(:491-492) and reset_keybinding_to_default(:511-512) also pass only a name (reset uses remove_custom_trigger=None, the default path, distinct from removal) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-492,511-512` _(code)_
- AppContext::set_custom_trigger(name: String, trigger: Trigger) carries NO context; delegates to keystroke_matcher — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1637-1639` _(both)_
- Matcher::set_custom_trigger(:137-141) and remove_custom_trigger(:146-152) both call keymap.update_custom_trigger with no context arg — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-152` _(code)_
- update_custom_trigger filters ONLY by b.name==name over editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings, writing custom_trigger to each; context_predicate never read; fixed_bindings never touched — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(both)_
- editable_bindings_by_name(:30) maps one name -> Vec<usize>; fixed_bindings field exists at :26 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:26,28-30` _(code)_
- EditableBinding stores its own context_predicate(:297) AND custom_trigger(:300); as_lens lets custom_trigger override default(:741-747) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:293-304,741-747` _(code)_
- Trigger has exactly Keystrokes/Standard/Custom/Empty; Empty 'cannot actually be matched'(:48); repo-wide grep finds NO Unbound trigger (only unrelated Unbounded offset variants) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:44-48` _(both)_
- push_keystroke matches only `if let Trigger::Keystrokes` and continues the loop, so Empty is shadow-by-non-participation; other same-key context-matching bindings still fire — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-337` _(code)_
- NUANCE (uncited by answer): match_custom additionally checks binding.original_trigger, so Empty does not suppress custom-action/menu bindings either — strengthens, does not refute — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:366-377` _(code)_
- ContextPredicate/Context use &'static str only and have NO FromStr (Identifier/Equal/NotEqual literals), so a context cannot even be parsed from config today — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:3-18,100-120` _(code)_
- Axis 3 end state requires truly disabling a default, not shadowing it — `docs/specs/2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-design-readiness-questions.md:432` _(code)_
- Per-view context produced by keymap_context trait method; the removal write path bypasses it entirely — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/mod.rs:252` _(code)_

### A2-Q12 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** How does a context-scoped CHORD override behave when the per-view context changes BETWEEN keystrokes of the chord? If a chord's first keystroke triggers a mode switch (e.g. enters VimNormalMode) so the second keystroke sees a different Context, does the scoped chord still complete, silently reset, or mis-fire?

**A.** CONFIRMED. A context-scoped chord SILENTLY RESETS and can MIS-FIRE when the per-view Context changes between keystrokes; it can never complete across a context change. push_keystroke (matcher.rs:315-319) clears the pending buffer on ANY full-Context-struct inequality (pending_ctx != ctx), not just predicate failure, then re-evaluates the trailing keystroke fresh against the new ctx (matcher.rs:324-338, eval only at line 327) — so the leftover key fires whatever single-key binding matches in the new context instead of completing the chord. Proven by existing unit tests matcher_tests.rs:58-66 (FixedBinding) and :150-158 (EditableBinding), where both contexts satisfy the chord predicate yet the chord still resets and the trailing key mis-fires to the single-key action.

Independently verified — the answer is accurate and well-evidenced. All cited file:line references say what the answer claims.

Mechanism (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:307-346):
1. Per view_id, `pending` (HashMap<EntityId, Pending>) holds a `keystrokes: Vec<Keystroke>` buffer plus a cached `context: Option<Context>` (matcher.rs:33-36, confirmed). The cache is written ONLY when a partial chord match occurs, to the current ctx (matcher.rs:334: `pending.context = Some(ctx.clone())` inside the else branch).
2. On entry, if `pending.context` is Some and `pending_ctx != ctx`, the keystrokes buffer is cleared (matcher.rs:315-319). This compares the whole `Context` struct (HashSet `set` + HashMap `map`, both with derived `Eq`/`PartialEq`, context.rs:3-7), NOT whether the chord's predicate still holds. Any change to the per-view Context — even one irrelevant to the chord's predicate — wipes chord progress.
3. The new keystroke is pushed (matcher.rs:321), then ALL keystroke-trigger bindings are checked with `keystrokes.starts_with(&pending.keystrokes) && binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx)` against the CURRENT ctx only (matcher.rs:324-338; eval at 327). There is no entry-context notion; ContextPredicate::eval takes a single Context (context.rs:100-120).

Per-keystroke context rebuild: dispatch_keystroke (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:2005-2041) calls contexts_from_responder_chain (app.rs:1839-1864) which calls `view.keymap_context(self)` per view (app.rs:1851) on every keystroke event, and pushes into the matcher keyed by `responder_chain[i]` (app.rs:2020). The EditorView's keymap_context inserts "VimNormalMode" into its context set when vim mode is Normal (app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:8692-8697). So a mode switch effected by keystroke 1 is reflected in the ctx seen by keystroke 2 for that same view, and `pending` is keyed by that same view_id — the premise holds.

Traced outcomes for a 2-key chord [k1,k2]:
- Chord scoped to the OLD context (predicate true at k1): k1 → Pending, pending.context = ctx_old. Mode switches. k2 arrives in ctx_new != ctx_old → buffer cleared to [] then [k2]. The chord [k1,k2] does not start_with [k2] (k1!=k2), so it never completes. The chord is silently reset; k2 is re-evaluated fresh in ctx_new. If a standalone binding for k2 matches in ctx_new it MIS-FIRES; otherwise None (silent drop).
- Chord scoped to the NEW context (e.g. predicate = VimNormalMode) but k1 pressed before the switch: at k1, predicate.eval(ctx_old) is false (line 327), so the binding never matches and the chord never becomes pending.
Either way a scoped chord whose first keystroke changes the Context cannot complete.

Direct proof in existing tests: test_matcher (matcher_tests.rs:13-66) registers chord "a b" with predicate `id!("a") | id!("b")` — satisfied by BOTH ctx_a and ctx_b. Lines 58-66: press "a" in ctx_b → None (Pending); press "b" in ctx_a → asserts Action::B (the standalone "b" binding scoped `id!("a")`, which matches ctx_a), NOT the chord Action::AB. The comment is literally "Pending keystrokes are cleared when the context changes." test_editable_binding_matching (matcher_tests.rs:97-158) repeats this for EditableBindings: register "a b" → Action::AOrB with predicate `id!("a") | id!("b")`; lines 150-158 assert the same reset+mis-fire to Action::B. Both contexts satisfy the chord predicate, yet the chord still resets and the trailing key mis-fires — confirming the reset is driven by raw Context inequality, not predicate failure.

Minor corrections (non-material): the responder-chain helper starts at app.rs:1839 (the answer wrote 1838-1864 — off-by-one on the start, range still covers it). The view whose keymap_context injects "VimNormalMode" is the generic EditorView (app/src/editor/view/mod.rs), which the answer loosely calls the "terminal editor view"; vim mode applies to the editor, so the substance is unchanged.

Implication for the design doc: this composes with the prefix-shadowing/ambiguity problem — the trailing keystroke after a reset is matched against all bindings and may shadow into a single-key action. The intended policy (evaluate the scoped predicate against the entry-context and/or pin the Context for the chord's duration, vs. the current current-ctx eval + clear-on-any-context-delta) is a design decision not yet implemented.

_Evidence:_
- On entry, pending keystrokes are cleared whenever cached pending.context != current ctx; the comparison is full Context struct equality (pending_ctx != ctx), not predicate satisfaction — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:315-319` _(code)_
- Chord matching iterates ALL keystroke-trigger bindings and evaluates binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx) only against the CURRENT ctx (line 327), caching pending.context = Some(ctx.clone()) only on a partial match (line 334) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-338` _(code)_
- pending is a HashMap keyed per view_id (EntityId); Pending stores keystrokes: Vec<Keystroke> plus a single cached context: Option<Context> — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:33-36` _(code)_
- Existing test: chord 'a b' scoped to id!(a)|id!(b); press 'a' in ctx_b (None/Pending) then 'b' in ctx_a (context changed) yields Action::B (single-key mis-fire), not chord Action::AB — proving silent reset + mis-fire even though both contexts satisfy the predicate. Comment: 'Pending keystrokes are cleared when the context changes.' — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:58-66` _(code)_
- Same reset/mis-fire behavior verified for EditableBindings: chord 'a b'->Action::AOrB predicate id!(a)|id!(b); press 'a' in ctx_b then 'b' in ctx_a asserts Action::B — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:150-158` _(code)_
- Context is a struct of HashSet<&str> + HashMap<&str,&str> deriving Eq/PartialEq, so equality is structural — any context delta (even predicate-irrelevant) trips the clear at matcher.rs:316 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:3-7` _(code)_
- Context is rebuilt fresh per keystroke: dispatch_keystroke calls contexts_from_responder_chain and keys push_keystroke by responder_chain[i] (line 2020), so a mode switch from keystroke 1 changes the ctx seen by keystroke 2 for that view — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:2005-2041` _(code)_
- contexts_from_responder_chain calls view.keymap_context(self) per view (line 1851) to build each per-keystroke Context (function actually begins at line 1839, not 1838) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1839-1864` _(code)_
- The EditorView's keymap_context inserts 'VimNormalMode' into its keymap context set when vim_mode == VimMode::Normal, so a mode switch mutates the view's Context — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:8692-8697` _(code)_
- ContextPredicate::eval has no entry-context concept; it evaluates a single Context passed in (Identifier checks ctx.set.contains) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:100-120` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc specify chord-vs-context composition policy for modal submodes (Axis-2 leaf goal): the engine must either pin the entry-context for a pending chord or evaluate the scoped predicate against entry-context, because the current current-ctx eval + clear-on-any-context-delta makes mode-switching scoped chords un-completable and prone to mis-firing the trailing key.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The CODE FACT is settled and proven by existing tests: a scoped chord whose first keystroke changes the per-view Context resets (and may mis-fire the trailing key to a single-key binding), and can never complete across a context change. The residue is non-code/design: (a) whether the engine should evaluate the chord predicate against the ENTRY context vs the current per-keystroke context, and (b) whether to pin/freeze the Context for the duration of a pending chord so predicate-irrelevant context deltas (line 316 raw struct inequality) do not abort it. No new targeted unit test was added (the two existing tests already assert the exact behavior, so a new one would be confirmatory, not discriminating). Whether real in-use Vim chords trigger this in practice depends on whether any chord's first keystroke both leaves the chord pending and flips the view's keymap_context (e.g. an operator-then-motion chord that changes vim mode) — a product/UX question outside code scope.
- _Verification:_ I opened every cited file and verified line-by-line. matcher.rs:307-346 reads exactly as claimed: clear-on-inequality (315-319) uses `pending_ctx != ctx` over the whole Context struct; eval is against the current `ctx` only at 327; cache write `pending.context = Some(ctx.clone())` is at 334 inside the partial-match else branch; the loop at 324 iterates all bindings (self.keymap.bindings()), filtered to Trigger::Keystrokes. Pending struct at 33-36 confirmed. context.rs:3-7 confirms Context derives Eq/PartialEq over HashSet+HashMap (structural equality); eval at 100-120 takes a single Context (no entry-context). Both tests confirmed verbatim: matcher_tests.rs:13-17 registers the three FixedBindings (standalone 'b' scoped id!('a')), and 58-66 asserts the press-'a'-in-ctx_b then press-'b'-in-ctx_a → Action::B (mis-fire), with the exact comment quoted; the editable variant at 97-158 mirrors it (Action::AOrB chord, lines 150-158 → Action::B). I also checked the path question: app.rs is at crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs (not app/), dispatch_keystroke at 2005 (a second overload exists at 255), push_keystroke keyed by responder_chain[i] at 2020, view.keymap_context per view at 1851. The VimNormalMode insertion is in app/ (app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:8692-8697), read directly since the graph does not cover app/. I additionally read context.rs in full (1-125) to check for any alternate evaluation path or FromStr — there is none; eval is the sole predicate path. Found only two trivial imprecisions: the responder-chain helper begins at line 1839 (answer wrote 1838), and the view is the generic EditorView (answer loosely called it the 'terminal editor view'). Neither changes the conclusion. No overgeneralization (the loop genuinely checks all keystroke bindings, both fixed and editable), no app-vs-crates scope confusion, and the test assertions match. Verdict: confirmed.

### A2-Q13 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Of the per-context same-name bindings users actually want to rebind, how many are FixedBindings (unremappable, L9) vs EditableBindings? The flagship forks (enter/backspace/numpadenter in Vim vs not) are FixedBindings and update_custom_trigger only touches editable bindings. Does Axis 2 deliver any user-visible value without Axis 3's Fixed-remappable work?

**A.** Confirmed. Every canonical same-name, per-context behavioral fork (enter, numpadenter, shift-enter, backspace, shift-backspace, delete, tab, shift-tab in the code editor; backspace in the terminal editor; ctrl-r/Redo in Vim normal mode) is a FixedBinding. I independently reproduced the grep counts exactly: 19 FixedBinding sites carry a Vim-mode context predicate, 6 EditableBinding sites do (and those 6 use DISTINCT names per context, so zero same-name multi-context EditableBindings exist). FixedBinding (keymap.rs:277-286) has no custom_trigger field, and update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) mutates only editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings by name, never fixed_bindings. So Axis 2's context-scoped editable-override mechanism cannot reach any of its own motivating examples; making FixedBindings remappable (Axis 3 / the L9 "Magnitude of FixedBinding usage" audit at end-goals line 145) is a hard prerequisite for the headline use case.

I opened every cited file and at least one location the answer did not foreground (bindings()/custom_action_bindings() read paths, the move_forward_one_word duplicate), and re-ran the grep counts myself. The answer holds in full; only trivial line-range offsets needed correcting.

STRUCTURAL FACTS (verified):
1) keymap.rs:277-286 FixedBinding = {trigger, action, command_description, context_predicate, enabled_predicate, group, id} — NO custom_trigger. EditableBinding (293-304) adds custom_trigger: Option<Trigger>. So remappability is structural, not contextual. Confirmed verbatim.
2) update_custom_trigger: the answer cites keymap.rs:421-434; the actual function is 422-433 (line 421 is the doc comment). It filters .editable_custom_action_bindings and .editable_bindings by b.name == name and never references fixed_bindings/fixed_custom_action_bindings. Confirmed (1-line citation drift, immaterial).
3) register_fixed_bindings: answer cites 391-396; actual 391-398. fixed_bindings + fixed_custom_action_bindings are written only here and read-only afterward (matcher reads at bindings() 454-464 and custom_action_bindings() 474-484). There is no fixed-trigger override setter anywhere. Confirmed.
4) editable_bindings_by_name at :30 (name -> Vec<usize>). Confirmed.

FORK SITES (verified):
- code/editor/view/actions.rs:51-176 — 8 same-key Vim/non-Vim FixedBinding fork pairs: enter (51-60), numpadenter (61-70), shift-enter (71-80), backspace (81-90), shift-backspace (91-100), delete (101-110), tab (157-166), shift-tab (167-176). NUANCE: shift-backspace's non-Vim variant (91-95) is scoped just text_entry (no !Vim), so in Vim mode both it and VimBackspace match — still a same-key fork pair, so the count stands. ctrl-r->Redo gated VimNormalMode at 290-294. Confirmed.
- editor/view/mod.rs:197-206 — backspace Backspace/VimBackspace FixedBinding fork (Backspace under !Vim, VimBackspace under Vim). ctrl-r->Redo VimNormalMode at 968-972. Confirmed.

COUNTS (independently reproduced, exact):
- FixedBinding::new -A6 | id!("Vim => 19
- EditableBinding::new -A8 | id!("Vim => 6
- Total FixedBinding::{new,custom,standard,new_per_platform} sites = 409
- Total EditableBinding::new sites = 437
All four match the answer exactly.

DISTINCT-NAME EDITABLE VIM BINDINGS (verified the decisive nuance): editor_view:delete under text_entry & !VimNormalMode & !VimVisualMode (504-508) vs editor_view:vim_scroll_half_page_down under Vim (509-515) and vim_scroll_half_page_up (516-522), both on ctrl-d/ctrl-u; editor_view:vim_exit_insert_mode (mod.rs:961-967); editor_view:clear_lines !Vim-scoped (mod.rs:780-788). Each name resolves to a single context, so name-only update_custom_trigger handles each without a sibling clobber — they are NOT same-name multi-context forks. Therefore among the actual Axis-2 target (same-name multi-context forks), FixedBindings = all of them, EditableBindings = 0. Confirmed.

DOC CROSS-REFS: end-goals line 145 is exactly the "Magnitude of FixedBinding usage" open question tagged (Axis 3). The A2-Q13 question block lives at readiness-doc line 365 and matches the asked question. Confirmed.

ONE IMPRECISION (non-material, in residual caveats): the residual_unknowns cites editor_view:move_forward_one_word "registered twice under text_entry" (actions.rs:307/315) as latent Axis-2 value. Confirmed it is registered twice (306-313 alt/ctrl-right, 314-320 meta-f) — but BOTH use the same context (text_entry.clone()), so they are same-name SAME-context duplicates, not multi-context. Context scoping would not disambiguate these two; only the broader claim (same-name editable bindings exist that name-only override clobbers wholesale) holds. This does not touch the core thesis.

CONCLUSION: The adversarial thesis is code-correct. Axis 2's mechanism (context-scoped EDITABLE override) cannot reach 100% of the same-name per-context behavioral forks because they are all Fixed and Fixed bindings have no override path. ### A2-Q14 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** What is the back-compat contract for a context-LESS entry in an existing keybindings.yaml? Today a flat `name: trigger` entry clobbers EVERY same-name binding via name-only update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422). After the schema change, does a context-less entry preserve clobber-all semantics, or change meaning to 'default scope only'?

**A.** Code fact: today a context-less flat entry has clobber-all-by-name semantics — it (and the "none" removal) overwrites the custom_trigger on EVERY same-name binding regardless of context (keymap.rs:422-433 filters only on b.name == name and never inspects context_predicate). Whether a post-schema context-less entry stays clobber-all vs becomes "default scope only" is an undecided product/architecture policy the code does not dictate; but the current deserializer (PersistedTrigger newtype over String, whole file in one serde_yaml::from_reader that returns None on any error) cannot read a context-bearing shape at all, and ContextPredicate has no FromStr/Display/serde anywhere in the crate — so a deserializer shim plus net-new parse/serialize support is mandatory either way. Verified end-to-end; all cited line refs are accurate.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened every cited file plus uncited corroborating locations; every evidence claim's file:line says what the answer asserts. No material errors found.

TODAY'S BEHAVIOR (verified end-to-end, clobber-all by name):
- Flat name->string schema: `CustomKeybindings(std::collections::HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>)` (app/src/keyboard.rs:167-169) with `PersistedTrigger(String)` (keyboard.rs:173-174). No context field. `"none"` removal sentinel = REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION (keyboard.rs:15), decoded by `TryFrom<PersistedTrigger> for UserDefinedKeybinding` (keyboard.rs:189-213).
- `load_custom_keybindings` (keyboard.rs:37-57) iterates flat `(name, trigger)` pairs and calls `app.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty)` for removal (line 44) or `Trigger::Keystrokes(..)` (line 47).
- `app.set_custom_trigger` (core/app.rs:1637-1639) -> `keystroke_matcher.set_custom_trigger` (keymap/matcher.rs:137-141) -> `keymap.update_custom_trigger(name, Some(trigger))`. Removal: `remove_custom_trigger` (matcher.rs:146-152) -> `update_custom_trigger(name, None)`.
- `update_custom_trigger` (keymap.rs:422-433) filters purely on `b.name == name` and assigns `binding.custom_trigger = trigger.clone()` to EVERY match in BOTH `editable_custom_action_bindings` (423-429) and `editable_bindings` (430-432). It never inspects `context_predicate`. => clobber-all by name, including the removal (None) path. EXACT line match.

SAME-NAME MULTI-CONTEXT IS A REAL, REACHABLE STATE:
- `EditableBinding` carries `context_predicate: ContextPredicate` (keymap.rs:297), and `editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>` (keymap.rs:30) maps one name to a Vec of indices, so multiple same-name bindings with different predicates coexist and are all hit by the name-only filter. Corroborated (uncited): per-view `keymap_context` (core/mod.rs:252) and the responder-chain `context_chain` merge (app.rs:1846-1864) show contexts are dynamically composed at dispatch time, so distinct-context same-name bindings are evaluated against a merged context — a genuine runtime state.
- The only update_custom_trigger test, `test_custom_triggers` (keymap_tests.rs:253; body 255-333), uses two DISTINCT names "first"/"second" and never exercises same-name-multi-context — so the clobber-all-on-one-name path is untested (corroborates A2-Q17).

THE DESERIALIZER CANNOT READ OLD + NEW SHAPES SIMULTANEOUSLY (shim required):
- `PersistedTrigger(String)` is a `#[derive(Serialize, Deserialize)]` newtype over String (keyboard.rs:173-174); serde deserializes it as its inner scalar, so a mapping value (e.g. `{trigger: cmd-a, context: Vim}`) fails.
- The whole file is parsed in a single `serde_yaml::from_reader` inside `read_custom_keybindings` (keyboard.rs:128-139); on any error it logs and returns `None`, silently dropping ALL keybindings, not just the malformed one. So introducing a structured/context value with the current type is actively breaking. Accepting both shapes needs an untagged-enum shim (Flat-string vs Scoped-struct).

NO TEXT GRAMMAR FOR CONTEXT EXISTS YET (read OR write):
- Whole-crate grep confirms ContextPredicate has NO FromStr/from_str and NO Display/ToString and NO Serialize/Deserialize anywhere under crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/. context.rs defines only the `Not`/`BitAnd`/`BitOr` operator impls (lines 53-75, inside the `pub mod macros` block 20-87), the construction macros id!/eq!/ne!/always! (20-87), and the inherent `eval` (100-120). So a context-bearing entry can neither be parsed on read nor serialized on write today — a net-new read grammar (A2-Q19) and write serializer (A2-Q15) are prerequisites. (Note: the answer scoped this to context.rs:20-120; my crate-wide grep makes it strictly stronger — true everywhere.)

WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE CONTRACT (the part the code constrains):
- The code as written gives context-less = ALL-SCOPES (clobber-all). To make adding an optional `context` field non-breaking, a context-LESS entry must continue routing through the name-only apply path (keep clobber-all). If the design instead redefines context-less as "default scope only," EVERY existing on-disk file silently changes behavior on upgrade, and an explicit auto-migration is required. That choice is a product/architecture decision, not a code fact.

_Evidence:_
- update_custom_trigger filters only by b.name == name and overwrites custom_trigger on every same-name binding in both editable_custom_action_bindings (lines 423-429) and editable_bindings (lines 430-432); never reads context_predicate (clobber-all) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- EditableBinding has a context_predicate: ContextPredicate field (line 297), and editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>> maps one name to a Vec (line 30), so same-name multi-context bindings coexist and are all hit by the name-only filter — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:30,297` _(both)_
- Flat name->string schema CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>) (167-169) with PersistedTrigger(String) (173-174); no context field; REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION 'none' sentinel (15); TryFrom decodes it (189-213) — `app/src/keyboard.rs:15,167-169,173-174,189-213` _(code)_
- load_custom_keybindings iterates flat (name, trigger) pairs and calls app.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) for removal (44) and Trigger::Keystrokes (47) — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-57` _(code)_
- matcher.set_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger(name, Some) (137-141); remove_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger(name, None) (146-152) — removal is also name-only clobber-all — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-152` _(code)_

- Crate-wide: no FromStr/Display/Serialize/Deserialize for ContextPredicate; context.rs has only Not/BitAnd/BitOr operator impls (53-75, inside macros mod 20-87), construction macros, and inherent eval (100-120) — context cannot be read or written from the file today — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:1-125` _(both)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc commit to an explicit migration contract for context-less entries: it confirms today's semantics are clobber-all-by-name (so preserving them is the only non-breaking option), and proves that ANY context-aware schema needs a serde deserializer shim plus net-new ContextPredicate parse/serialize support before a context-less-vs-scoped distinction can be honored on load.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The actual back-compat CONTRACT choice is a product/architecture decision the code does not settle: (1) context-less = ALL-SCOPES (preserve today's clobber-all; non-breaking; keeps routing through the name-only apply path) vs (2) context-less = DEFAULT-SCOPE-ONLY (cleaner future semantics but silently changes behavior for every existing keybindings.yaml on upgrade and mandates explicit auto-migration with version stamping). ### A2-Q15 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** How is a `ContextPredicate` SERIALIZED back to text for the write path? `write_custom_keybinding` produces a `PersistedTrigger(String)` and there is NO `Display`/`ToString` impl for `ContextPredicate` today. In-app edits must write the scoped predicate to file — what canonical text form, and does FromStr(Display(p)) == p hold for round-trip safety?

**A.** Substantively confirmed: there is NO text serializer (Display/ToString), NO text parser (FromStr), and NO serde derive for `ContextPredicate` anywhere in the repo (verified by repo-wide grep returning nothing). The persisted schema carries no context — `CustomKeybindings` is `HashMap<binding-name, PersistedTrigger(String)>` where the string is only space-joined keystrokes or the "none" sentinel. So `FromStr(Display(p)) == p` cannot hold because neither side exists; both a serializer and a parser must be designed from scratch, facing the `&'static str` interning obstacle and an undefined canonical form for `Just(false)`. ONE correction: the answer's claim that `from_editable_lens` "reads EditableBindingLens.context" is wrong — it never reads `context` (a private cross-crate field) and only maps description/trigger/action/name/group/id; the predicate is dropped because it is never read, not because a read result is discarded. The conclusion (predicate dropped on UI-driven writes) is unchanged.

VERDICT: corrected (the answer is substantively right; one supporting evidence claim's mechanism wording is wrong and is fixed below).

INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED:

1) context.rs (read in full): `ContextPredicate` enum at :9-18; derive at :9 is exactly `#[derive(Debug, Clone, Eq, PartialEq)]` — no Serialize/Deserialize. Variants hold `&'static str` (Identifier :11, Equal :12, NotEqual :13), `Box<ContextPredicate>` (Not :14, And :15, Or :16), and `Just(bool)` :17. Impls on the type: `std::ops::Not` :53, `BitAnd` :61, `BitOr` :69, and `impl ContextPredicate { fn eval }` :100-120. NO Display, NO ToString, NO FromStr. Macros `id!/eq!/ne!/always!` at :20-87 are compile-time source-construction helpers over literals; `always!()` => `Just(true)` (:79-83); there is no macro/token for `Just(false)`. All as the answer states.

2) Repo-wide grep over crates/ and app/ for any Display/FromStr/Serialize impl mentioning `ContextPredicate` returns ZERO results. Confirms no serializer, parser, or serde path exists anywhere (not just in context.rs).

3) Persisted schema (app/src/keyboard.rs): `struct CustomKeybindings(std::collections::HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>)` :169 (name-keyed only), `struct PersistedTrigger(String)` :174. `From<UserDefinedKeybinding> for PersistedTrigger` :176-187 emits `keystrokes.iter().map(Keystroke::normalized).join(" ")` or `REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION` ("none", defined :15). `write_custom_keybinding(name: String, keybinding: UserDefinedKeybinding)` :64-75 inserts name -> PersistedTrigger.into(). No predicate is serialized. Confirmed.

4) In-app write path (app/src/util/bindings.rs): `set_custom_keybinding(binding_name, keystroke, ctx)` :491-499 calls `ctx.set_custom_trigger(binding_name.into(), Trigger::Keystrokes(vec![...]))` then `write_custom_keybinding(binding_name.into(), UserDefinedKeybinding::keystroke(...))` — name + keystroke only. Confirmed.

5) CORRECTION to evidence claim #8: `from_editable_lens(lens, ctx)` :762-771 constructs `Self { description, trigger, action, name, group, id }` — it does NOT read `lens.context`. Moreover `EditableBindingLens.context` (keymap.rs:311) is a PRIVATE field (`context: &'a ContextPredicate`, no `pub`), and `from_editable_lens` lives in a different crate (app/), so it cannot access `context` at all. The resulting `CommandBinding` has no context field (the struct literal lists all fields, none is context). Net effect — the predicate is dropped on UI-driven writes — is the same and correct; only the "reads context" mechanism description was inaccurate.

6) Override key is name-only end to end: `AppContext::set_custom_trigger(name: String, trigger)` (core/app.rs:1637) -> `self.keystroke_matcher.set_custom_trigger(...)` where keystroke_matcher is a `Matcher` (app.rs:605). `Matcher::set_custom_trigger` (keymap/matcher.rs:137) -> `self.keymap.update_custom_trigger(name.as_str(), Some(trigger))`. `Keymap::update_custom_trigger(name: &str, trigger)` (keymap.rs:422-433) mutates EVERY binding where `b.name == name` in BOTH `editable_custom_action_bindings` and `editable_bindings`, with no predicate discrimination. (The answer omitted the intermediate `Matcher` hop at matcher.rs:137 but its claim "set_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger, name-only" is otherwise correct.) `editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>` at keymap.rs:30 confirms one name maps to many indices.

ANSWER TO THE QUESTION AS ASKED:
- "What canonical text form?" None exists today; it must be designed (no Display, and the file format has no field for context).
- "Does FromStr(Display(p)) == p hold?" Vacuously undefined — neither FromStr nor Display exists, so round-trip safety is currently 0% (both halves absent).

ROUND-TRIP HAZARDS a future Display/FromStr pair must solve (all read from enum shape context.rs:9-18 + eval :100-120): (a) the `&'static str` lifetime trap — Identifier/Equal/NotEqual hold `'static` borrows that today always come from source literals; a runtime FromStr needs interning/leaking or a closed static vocabulary registry; (b) `Just(false)` has no canonical token (`always!` only yields `Just(true)`); (c) precedence/parenthesization for the And/Or/Not Box tree must be defined for a stable minimal-parenthesized form. These are correct.

The predicate is dropped because it is never read, not because a read value is discarded. This strengthens rather than weakens the conclusion. I also noted the answer skipped the intermediate Matcher hop (matcher.rs:137) between AppContext::set_custom_trigger and Keymap::update_custom_trigger, but that does not change the name-only result. Line numbers cited by the answer are all accurate. Marking corrected due to the from_editable_lens mechanism error; the question's answer is otherwise fully correct.

### A2-Q16 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Does the revert/reset key become `name+context` (from today's name-only), and how does a scoped override interact with `Tracked<EditableBinding>` live-apply notifications and Axis-1 hot-reload reset semantics? When hot-reload re-applies the whole file, how is a previously-applied scoped override on a SUBSET of same-name bindings reverted without touching its siblings?

**A.** Confirmed. Today the revert/reset path is strictly name-only and clobbers every same-name binding exactly like the apply path; both funnel through the single `update_custom_trigger(name, Some|None)` filter (keymap.rs:422-433, name-only, mutating BOTH `editable_custom_action_bindings` and `editable_bindings`), so they must be upgraded to name+context (or stable id) in lockstep. The `Tracked<EditableBinding>` notification mechanism is already per-binding (each has a unique `TrackedId`; field mutation via `DerefMut` fires `track_update(id)`), so a narrowed filter would re-render only the mutated subset — the gap is purely the name-only filter plus the name-keyed file schema, not the notification layer. Keybindings hot-reload does not exist (only settings.toml has a reload watcher; keybindings load once at startup), and the on-disk schema `HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>` (keyboard.rs:169) cannot express a scoped subset, so the "currently-applied custom names" reset set is an explicit Axis-1 deferred design item, not code.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I opened every cited file and re-ran the grep; all 14 evidence claims hold at the stated lines.

VERIFIED CODE FACTS

1) Revert/reset is name-only end to end. `reset_to_default_keystroke` (app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:617-619) calls `reset_keybinding_to_default(&row.binding.name, ctx)`; `reset_keybinding_to_default` (app/src/util/bindings.rs:511) calls `ctx.remove_custom_trigger(binding_name)` (bindings.rs:512). `AppContext::remove_custom_trigger<N: AsRef<str>>` (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1656-1661) forwards to `Matcher::remove_custom_trigger` (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:146-152) -> `self.keymap.update_custom_trigger(name.as_ref(), None)` (matcher.rs:151). `update_custom_trigger` (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433) filters purely `|b| b.name == name` and sets `custom_trigger = trigger.clone()` on ALL matching bindings in BOTH `editable_custom_action_bindings` (423-429) and `editable_bindings` (430-432). No Context/ContextPredicate/id anywhere in the chain. CONFIRMED.

2) Apply and revert share identical name-only routing. `set_custom_trigger(name: String, trigger)` (app.rs:1637-1639, matcher.rs:137-141 -> `update_custom_trigger(name, Some(trigger))`) and `remove_custom_trigger` (app.rs:1656-1661, matcher.rs:146-152 -> `update_custom_trigger(name, None)`) funnel into the same function. The two public API signatures are exactly as the answer states: `set_custom_trigger(name: String, trigger: Trigger)` and `remove_custom_trigger<N: AsRef<str>>(name: N)`. CONFIRMED.

3) Notification layer already per-binding. `Tracked<T>` (crates/warpui_core/src/core/autotracking/tracked.rs:18-33) holds a unique `id: TrackedId` assigned via `TrackedId::next()` (atomic, 74-81); `DerefMut::deref_mut` calls `track_update(self.id)` (50-55), firing one invalidation per mutated binding. So a context-narrowed filter would notify only the mutated subset; the per-binding granularity Q16 asks for already exists. The defect is the name-only FILTER. CONFIRMED.

4) Same-name multi-context bindings are real (3-way, not just 2-way). `editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>` (keymap.rs:30) maps one name to many indices; `get_binding_by_name` (keymap.rs:379-386) iterates them and returns the first enabled. I re-ran the duplicate grep and got exactly 4 names: "a", "editor_view:delete", "editor_view:end", "editor_view:up". I then enumerated each name's registration sites. BOTH editor_view:up and editor_view:delete are genuine 3-way collisions across distinct view modules:
  - editor_view:up: terminal editor app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:618, code editor app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:329 (multi-line form), notebook editor app/src/notebooks/editor/view.rs:427.
  - editor_view:delete: terminal mod.rs:769, code actions.rs:504, notebook view.rs:589.
The answer's citations actions.rs:329 (=editor_view:up) and :504 (=editor_view:delete) are both correct. CONFIRMED. (Note: line 329 is a multi-line `EditableBinding::new(\n  "editor_view:up"` so a naive single-line grep can miss it — see caveat below.)

5) BindingId exists but is unused for override/revert routing. `BindingId(pub usize)` (keymap.rs:262), `BindingId::new()` allocates from a global `AtomicUsize` (264-272), and `EditableBinding.id: BindingId` (keymap.rs:303). `update_custom_trigger` never consults it; routing is name-string only. CONFIRMED. (Its atomic-counter origin means ids are regenerated per registration/run, so stability as a persistence key is unproven — correctly flagged in residual_unknowns.)

6) Hot-reload reset set undesigned; file schema name-keyed. Persisted schema `CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>)` (app/src/keyboard.rs:169) where `PersistedTrigger(String)` (174) — no context field. `load_custom_keybindings` (keyboard.rs:37-57) iterates `(name, trigger)` and calls `app.set_custom_trigger(name, ...)` (Trigger::Empty for the Removed sentinel, 44). `remove_custom_keybinding` (keyboard.rs:79-93) does `map.0.remove(name.as_ref())`. Keybindings hot-reload is NOT implemented: I independently confirmed `load_custom_keybindings` is called exactly once at startup (app/src/lib.rs:2557) and found NO keybindings file watcher/subscribe; only settings.toml has a reload watcher (app/src/settings/init.rs:205-217, `handle_warp_config_change` -> `reload_all_public_settings` at 245-246). The "currently-applied custom names" set is an explicit Axis-1 deferred item (docs/specs/2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-end-goals-design.md:151-152). CONFIRMED.

7) No same-name multi-context revert test. keymap_tests.rs:255-319 registers two DIFFERENT names ("first"/"second"), overrides "first", asserts subset isolation, then reverts "first" via `update_custom_trigger("first", None)` (319). No test with two same-name bindings under different contexts asserting subset revert. CONFIRMED.

CORROBORATING DETAIL (uncited, supports answer): Context/ContextPredicate (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:3-18) are built entirely from `&'static str` (HashSet/HashMap of &'static str; `Identifier(&'static str)` etc.) via compile-time macros, with NO FromStr/Deserialize impl. This reinforces that the on-disk `HashMap<name,trigger>` schema not only lacks a context field but has no deserialization path to reconstruct a ContextPredicate from disk — strengthening residual_unknown (b).

The answer is materially correct in every load-bearing claim; the design conclusion (thread a context/id through update_custom_trigger + the two public APIs + the file schema, upgrading apply and revert in lockstep; Tracked layer needs no change) is sound.

### A2-Q17 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** What test harness proves a context-scoped override hits ONLY its scope, given context_tests.rs has ~1 test fn and the keymap tests don't exercise same-name multi-context bindings? Unit test directly on Matcher::push_keystroke/update_custom_trigger with synthetic contexts, or an integration test across real terminal vs code-editor views?

**A.** Confirmed in substance with one naming fix. No harness today registers two SAME-name editable bindings under different contexts, applies a scoped override, and asserts only the in-scope one changes. The gap is structural: Keymap::update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) is a pure name-only filter (no context arg), so the proof requires BOTH a new keymap-crate unit test (synthetic same-name/different-context editable bindings) AND a crates/integration terminal-vs-code-editor test, because per-view context only exists at the app/ responder-chain layer that keymap unit tests cannot construct. Correction: the matcher entry point is remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:146), NOT "clear_custom_trigger" as the original evidence labeled it.

CODE FACT — the override path is name-only, so "only its scope" is currently unprovable AND unimplemented:\n- Keymap::update_custom_trigger (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433) filters solely by `b.name == name` over BOTH `editable_custom_action_bindings` (the Trigger::Custom mirror, populated at keymap.rs:410-413) and `editable_bindings`; it never reads `context_predicate`. Signature `update_custom_trigger(&mut self, name: &str, trigger: Option<Trigger>)` has no context parameter, so an override applied to a name clobbers EVERY editable binding with that name across ALL contexts. All public entry points funnel here: Matcher::set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137) and Matcher::remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:146) — NOTE the original answer called the latter "clear_custom_trigger"; the actual method name is remove_custom_trigger — plus AppContext::set_custom_trigger (core/app.rs:1637-1638), which delegates to keystroke_matcher.set_custom_trigger.\n- The index `editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>` (keymap.rs:30, populated at register_editable_bindings keymap.rs:414-417) is many-indices-per-name with no context key.\n\nTEST INVENTORY — confirms the harness gap (re-ran grep -c '#[test]' on each):\n- context_tests.rs: exactly 1 test fn, test_context_predicate_eval (:4-26). Exercises only ContextPredicate::eval boolean logic on a hand-built Context; registers zero bindings, applies zero overrides. (grep count = 1, verified.)\n- matcher_tests.rs: 3 test fns. test_editable_binding_matching (:88-176) registers editable bindings WITH context predicates but DISTINCT names ("a","b","a_or_b", :100-108) and never calls update_custom_trigger (grep for update_custom_trigger in this file = empty). test_matcher (:5) / test_bindings_for_context (:179) apply no overrides.\n- keymap_tests.rs: 19 test fns (verified). The only override test, test_custom_triggers (:253-341), registers two editable bindings with DISTINCT names ("first","second", :266-269), no context predicate, overrides "first", asserts "second" unchanged — and checks both bindings() and editable_bindings(). Proves NAME isolation, not CONTEXT isolation.\n- core/mod_tests.rs test_editable_binding_getters (:2187-2327) is the closest multi-context test: builds a real Root>Container>{ViewA,ViewB} hierarchy and asserts editable_bindings_for_view (core/app.rs:1814) returns the correct per-context set. BUT (a) it gives the same-key/different-context bindings DISTINCT names — "b-container" id!("Container") and "b-leaf" id!("ViewA") (:2274-2277) — sidestepping the same-name case, and (b) applies no override; it tests visibility, not override scoping.\n=> Across all 4 modules no test combines: same name + different contexts + override applied + assert only-scoped-changes. The question's premise ("keymap tests don't exercise same-name multi-context bindings") is confirmed.\n\nWHAT THE HARNESS MUST BE (both required):\n1. Unit test (keymap crate): register two EditableBinding::new(<same name>, ...) with .with_context_predicate(id!("Vim")) and .with_context_predicate(!id!("Vim")), apply a scoped override, assert ONLY the in-scope Trigger changed — verifying via BOTH editable_bindings() (keymap.rs:441) and editable_custom_action_bindings() (keymap.rs:466) because update_custom_trigger writes to both vectors. With today's code this test cannot pass and cannot even be written, because update_custom_trigger takes no context/predicate arg; the harness and the engine change are co-dependent.\n2. Integration test (crates/integration; builder.rs, step.rs, test/ exist; keybinding writes guarded by DISABLE_SAVE_ENV_VAR set at builder.rs:183, comment at :181): drive terminal-EditorView vs code-editor view end-to-end. This is the ONLY layer that proves the cross-view distinction, because per-view context is assembled in app/: each view's keymap_context (core/mod.rs:252 trait method) is merged into a Vec<Context> context_chain in contexts_from_responder_chain (core/app.rs:1844-1864), which push_keystroke (matcher.rs:307, predicate eval :327) and bindings_for_context (matcher.rs:282, filter :285) evaluate per context. Keymap unit tests construct synthetic Context values by hand and cannot reproduce the real responder chain.\n\nREAL FORK TO ANCHOR ON: app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:197-206 has two FixedBinding::new("backspace", ...) split on id!("Vim") vs !id!("Vim") (Backspace vs VimBackspace) — the Fixed same-keystroke/different-context analog. But these are FixedBindings (in fixed_bindings, not keyed by an editable name), so update_custom_trigger never touches them. The editable forks the override path actually touches use distinct names today (b-container/b-leaf); the feature's same-name-multi-context editable case has no existing analog, which is why a new harness is required. ContextPredicate has no FromStr/parser (context.rs: grep for FromStr/from_str/parse empty; only Not/BitAnd/BitOr operator impls at :53+ and eval at :101-120), so a config-file string cannot yet name a context as a predicate.

_Evidence:_
- update_custom_trigger filters ONLY by b.name==name across editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings; no context parameter or predicate check, so an override hits all same-name bindings in every context — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- All override entry points funnel through update_custom_trigger: Matcher::set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137) and Matcher::remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:146 — NOT clear_custom_trigger as originally labeled), plus AppContext::set_custom_trigger (core/app.rs:1637-1638) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-151` _(code)_
- editable_bindings_by_name is HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>> keyed by name only (many indices per name, no context); populated at register_editable_bindings keymap.rs:414-417 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:30` _(both)_
- context_tests.rs has exactly 1 test fn (test_context_predicate_eval), which only tests ContextPredicate::eval and registers no bindings (grep -c '#[test]' = 1, verified) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context_tests.rs:4-26` _(code)_
- The only override unit test (test_custom_triggers) uses DISTINCT names 'first'/'second' with no context predicates, overrides 'first', asserts 'second' unchanged — proving name isolation not context isolation; verified across bindings() and editable_bindings() — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap_tests.rs:253-341` _(code)_
- test_editable_binding_matching registers context-predicated editable bindings but with distinct names ('a','b','a_or_b') and never applies an override (update_custom_trigger absent from file) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:88-176` _(code)_
- Closest multi-context test (test_editable_binding_getters) gives same-key/different-context bindings DISTINCT names (b-container id!(Container)/b-leaf id!(ViewA)) and applies no override; it asserts editable_bindings_for_view visibility, not override scoping — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/mod_tests.rs:2274-2277` _(code)_
- Per-keystroke matching evaluates context_predicate per view context in push_keystroke (eval at :327) and bindings_for_context filters by predicate (:285) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:282-327` _(code)_
- Per-view contexts are assembled into a Vec<Context> context_chain in contexts_from_responder_chain at the app/core layer — the only place terminal-vs-code-editor distinction exists; each view contributes via keymap_context (trait at core/mod.rs:252) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1844-1864` _(code)_
- Real same-keystroke/different-context fork is Fixed (two backspace FixedBindings split on id!(Vim) vs !id!(Vim): Backspace/VimBackspace), held in fixed_bindings, not keyed by an editable name, so not overridable via update_custom_trigger — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:197-206` _(code)_
- crates/integration provides the Builder/TestStep harness (builder.rs, step.rs, test/ exist) and guards keybinding writes via DISABLE_SAVE_ENV_VAR (set_env call at line 183; comment at 181) — `crates/integration/src/builder.rs:183` _(code)_
- No FromStr/parser exists for ContextPredicate (grep for FromStr/from_str/parse empty); predicates are only macro/operator-built (Not/BitAnd/BitOr at :53+, eval at :101-120), so a config-named context string cannot be turned into a predicate yet — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:53-120` _(code)_

Two engine prerequisites are decisions, not yet code: (1) update_custom_trigger must gain a context/predicate parameter before any same-name-scoped-override test can pass — today such a test cannot even be written against the name-only API; (2) no FromStr for ContextPredicate, so a config-file-driven scoped override cannot name a context as a string yet.
