Checked the code-editor view: app/src/code/editor/view/ exists (actions.rs, view_tests.rs, vim_handler.rs) and app/src/code/editor/mod.rs exists, but there is no view/mod.rs at the exact path the original probed — matching the original's honest residual note; the integration terminal-vs-code-editor premise is sound, only the precise registration anchor remains to be pinned.

### A2-Q19 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** What is the current registered binding count n, and at what override count does the linear matcher (`for binding in self.keymap.bindings()` with `context_predicate.eval(ctx)`, matcher.rs:324-327, run once per responder-chain entry, core/app.rs:2014) exceed the per-keystroke frame budget — i.e. is there a concrete binding-count threshold above which Axis 2 must pull R4's trie forward, and does that threshold change under the inject-new model (Q4)?

**A.** Confirmed. There is ONE global keymap/matcher; n today is ~800 bindings (static estimate, exact value runtime-only). The matcher is a linear O(n) scan over self.keymap.bindings() re-run once per responder-chain entry (cost = chain_depth × n), but context_predicate.eval is short-circuited behind keystrokes.starts_with(&pending) via &&, so eval does NOT run n times per keystroke. There is no push_keystroke microbenchmark and no frame-budget constant in the matcher path, so the concrete trie-now binding-count threshold is NOT a static code fact — it needs the prescribed microbenchmark. Overrides MUTATE in place today (update_custom_trigger, no Vec append → n constant), so the override-driven threshold is live only under Q4=inject. Minor count fix: the pure FixedBinding::new estimate is ~808, not 820 (the 820 double-counted the 13 new_per_platform); does not change any conclusion.

Verified against the code, the answer holds on every load-bearing claim.\n\n1) SINGLE GLOBAL MATCHER — confirmed. app.rs:605 `keystroke_matcher: Matcher`, app.rs:766 `keystroke_matcher: Default::default()`. All 24 access points route through `self.keystroke_matcher`; the only register entry points are register_fixed_bindings (app.rs:1620) and register_editable_bindings (app.rs:1631). No per-window/per-view matcher exists (other "Matcher" symbols in the tree are mockito test imports and lsp GlobMatcher, unrelated).\n\n2) FLAT STORAGE, NO TRIE — confirmed. keymap.rs:26-27 `fixed_bindings: Vec<FixedBinding>` + `editable_bindings: Vec<Tracked<EditableBinding>>`; keymap.rs:30 `editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>`. (There are also two custom-action cache Vecs at :36-37, but they are NOT on the push_keystroke path — the answer's "two flat Vecs" is accurate for the hot path.) keymap.rs:454-464 `bindings()` = editable_bindings().rev()-filter-enabled chained with fixed_bindings.rev()-filter-enabled. No index/trie.\n\n3) HOT PATH = chain_depth × n — confirmed. app.rs:1844-1855 builds one Context per responder-chain entry via view.keymap_context(self) (trait def core/mod.rs:252, impl :357 and view/mod.rs:109). app.rs:2014 loops the context vector in reverse and app.rs:2018 calls push_keystroke once per entry. push_keystroke (matcher.rs:324) iterates ALL bindings.\n\n4) eval SHORT-CIRCUITED — confirmed. matcher.rs:325-327: `if let Trigger::Keystrokes(keystrokes) = &binding.trigger { if keystrokes.starts_with(&pending.keystrokes) && binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx)`. && short-circuits with starts_with first, so eval runs only for prefix-matching bindings, not n. eval (context.rs:100-119) is a recursive walk over HashSet/HashMap membership — cheap. (Note the separate match_standard path at matcher.rs:350-358 DOES eval every Standard binding, but that is StandardAction dispatch, not the per-keystroke push_keystroke path the question asks about — correctly scoped.)\n\n5) MUTATE vs INJECT — confirmed. set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-141) → update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) filters existing editable bindings by name and sets `custom_trigger`; no Vec entry added, so n is CONSTANT under user overrides today. Only Q4=inject would append bindings and scale the O(n) scan, so the threshold question is live only under inject.\n\n6) THRESHOLD / FRAME BUDGET — confirmed NOT in code. push_keystroke has exactly one non-test caller (app.rs:2018) plus a test helper (matcher_tests.rs:236); no Matcher/push_keystroke benchmark exists. grep for frame-budget/fps in the matcher path finds nothing — the only fps constants live in integration/video_recorder.rs and capture_recorder.rs (recording, unrelated). So the concrete binding-count-at-which-budget-exceeded is a runtime measurement, not a static fact.\n\n7) INJECT CONSTRAINT — confirmed. No FromStr/parser for ContextPredicate (grep over keymap/ returns nothing); predicates are built via compile-time macros id!/eq!/ne!/always! (context.rs:20-87) and Identifier carries &'static str (context.rs:11). A serialized inject model cannot deserialize into ContextPredicate without new parsing + non-'static handling.\n\nCOUNT RECONCILIATION (minor, non-material): re-running the greps myself: bare `FixedBinding::new` (word-bounded) = 384 total / 360 non-test-non-example; `new_per_platform` = 13; `EditableBinding::new` = 451 total / 435 non-test-non-example; no FromStr. The answer's "397/373" were the substring greps that already INCLUDE the 13 new_per_platform, so the final sum "373+13+435≈820" double-counts the 13; the clean static estimate is 360+13+435 ≈ 808. This is inside the answer's own "estimate, exact n is runtime-only" framing and changes no conclusion: n is ~800, chain_depth single-digit, eval prefix-gated → a few thousand cheap ops per keystroke, far under a frame, and the trie is a scalability hedge not an immediate blocker under mutate.

_Evidence:_
- Single global matcher on App, default-constructed; all access via self.keystroke_matcher; registers via register_fixed/editable_bindings — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:605,766,1620,1631` _(code)_
- Hot-path bindings stored as two flat Vecs (+ two unrelated custom-action caches); name index is HashMap<&'static str,Vec<usize>>; bindings() chains editable.rev()++fixed.rev() with no trie — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:26-30,36-37,454-464` _(code)_
- push_keystroke linearly iterates ALL bindings; eval short-circuited because keystrokes.starts_with(&pending) is checked before && binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-338` _(code)_
- dispatch_keystroke builds one Context per responder-chain entry then loops the context vector in reverse calling push_keystroke once per entry -> cost = chain_depth × n — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1844-1855,2014-2034` _(code)_
- keymap_context is a per-View trait method, one Context built per view — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/mod.rs:252,357 ; crates/warpui_core/src/core/view/mod.rs:109` _(code)_
- eval recursively walks predicate over HashSet/HashMap membership (cheap) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:100-119` _(code)_
- Override MUTATES in place (n constant): set_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger sets custom_trigger on existing name-matched bindings; no Vec entry added — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-141 ; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- Re-run counts: FixedBinding::new=384 total/360 non-test-example; new_per_platform=13; EditableBinding::new=451/435 -> clean static estimate n ≈ 808 (answer's 820 double-counts the 13 new_per_platform); still ~800 — `grep -rn 'FixedBinding::new\b' / 'FixedBinding::new_per_platform' / 'EditableBinding::new\b' --include=*.rs app/ crates/` _(code)_
- No push_keystroke/Matcher benchmark (sole non-test caller app.rs:2018, test helper matcher_tests.rs:236) and no frame-budget constant in the matcher path (only video/capture recorder fps constants exist) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:2018 ; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:236 ; crates/warpui_core/src/integration/video_recorder.rs:181` _(code)_
- ContextPredicate has no FromStr/parser (grep over keymap/ empty); built via compile-time macros id!/eq!/ne!/always!; Identifier is &'static str — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9-18,20-87` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets Axis 2 decide whether scoped overrides can ship as O(n) linear matching or must co-require R4's trie. Code says: keep mutate-in-place and n stays constant so the trie is not forced; choose inject and n scales with (overrides × contexts) in a single global keymap scanned chain_depth × n per keystroke — but eval is short-circuited and ops are cheap, so the trie is a scalability hedge, not an immediate blocker. The actual trie-now threshold still needs the microbenchmark; everything else (n source, hot-path shape, mutate/inject mechanism, missing predicate parser) is settled.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The actual numeric threshold (binding count at which push_keystroke exceeds the per-keystroke frame budget) is not a static code fact and could not be determined: there is no push_keystroke microbenchmark and no frame-budget constant. Settling it requires (a) instrumenting register_*_bindings to read exact runtime n (static estimate ~808; platform conditionals leaving Empty triggers, and any dynamic/loop registration, can shift it), and (b) microbenchmarking push_keystroke with +500 synthetic scoped bindings at realistic responder-chain depth against an explicit frame target. Two design calls remain open and depend on Q4: mutate (n constant -> no override-driven threshold) vs inject (n scales with overrides × contexts -> threshold live); and the trie-now-vs-later (R4) decision, which the qualitative estimate suggests is non-urgent at n≈800-1300 but is a product/perf judgment, not a code fact. An inject model also carries an unmeasured extra cost: ContextPredicate has no deserialization path (no FromStr; &'static str identifiers). I did not independently re-verify the ~265 register call-site count, but it is not load-bearing for the conclusion.

Independently confirmed there is only ONE matcher (all 24 keystroke_matcher uses go through the single App field; other "Matcher" symbols are mockito/lsp GlobMatcher). Confirmed no FromStr for ContextPredicate (grep exit 1) and no frame-budget constant on the matcher path (fps constants are only in video/capture recorders). Confirmed push_keystroke has one production caller (app.rs:2018). The ONE discrepancy I found is numeric and immaterial: the answer's FixedBinding::new figures (397/373) are substring greps that already include the 13 new_per_platform, so the final sum 373+13+435≈820 double-counts the 13; the clean estimate is 360+13+435≈808. The answer explicitly frames n as a static estimate (runtime-only exact value), so this does not alter any decision-relevant claim. I also note the answer correctly scopes eval-short-circuit to the push_keystroke path and does not conflate it with match_standard (which does eval every Standard binding). ## Axis 3 — Layering, precedence & unbinding

### A3-Q1 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Of the 397 FixedBinding::new sites (all keystroke triggers), how many sit in a user-reachable view context a user would plausibly rebind (code-editor up/down/left/right/enter/tab/backspace/delete) versus genuinely internal/transient (modal-escape, onboarding callouts, lightbox nav), and which subset is in-scope for 'remappable' in this axis?

**A.** Confirmed. Of 397 FixedBinding::new sites (384 ::new + 13 ::new_per_platform, exact), 168 (~42%) sit under persistent, user-reachable editor contexts (CodeEditorView, EditorView, RichTextEditorView, Terminal, Input) and register the core nav/edit/selection keys (up/down/left/right/enter/tab/backspace/delete/home/end/word-move/select-*, with Vim forks). This empirically refutes the report's L9 (report.md:134) and line 234 claim that FixedBinding is "reserved for internal/transient." Genuinely internal/transient sites total ~92 (MODAL_TRANSIENT 68 + TEST_EXAMPLE 24), with ~48 semi-transient (BLOCKLIST_INLINE 44 + AUTH 4) and an 89-site app-chrome (OTHER_VIEW) gray zone whose remappable status is a product call. The full bucketing reproduces exactly (sum 397). Two minor refinements to supporting evidence, neither overturning a conclusion: (1) the EditableBinding-absence proof should grep .with_key_binding(...) not EditableBinding::new(...) (that call carries name+description, not keystrokes) — the proper check still shows no bare nav key is editable in the four editor files, though terminal/input.rs:1944 binds bare `tab` editable and actions.rs gives Delete an editable ctrl-d alias; (2) FixedBinding has no `name` but does carry a runtime `id: BindingId` (atomic counter), so "no stable handle" means no stable string name, not no id.

The cited line numbers are correct.)\n\n7) GRAY-ZONE spot-check confirmed: drive/index.rs has 9 FixedBinding and uses `id!("DriveIndex") & !id!("DisableDriveIndexVimKeybindings")` (lines 578, 583; toggle inserted at 5077) — vim-style nav, persistent app chrome.\n\nREFINEMENTS (supporting evidence, no conclusion changed)\n\nA) EditableBinding-absence method. `EditableBinding::new(name, description, action)` (keymap.rs:646) carries NO keystroke; the trigger attaches via `.with_key_binding("...")`. So grepping `EditableBinding::new` for key strings tests the wrong field (it matched the description "Delete" case-insensitively). The CORRECT check — grep `.with_key_binding("up|down|left|right|enter|numpadenter|backspace|delete|tab|home|end")` across the four editor files — returns NONE, so the conclusion (bare core nav keys are exclusively Fixed in those four files) HOLDS. Caveats the original framing missed: terminal/input.rs:1944 binds bare `tab` as an EditableBinding (Open completions) — input.rs is in EDITOR_CORE but not among the "four files"; and actions.rs:504-508 gives the Delete ACTION an editable `ctrl-d` alias while the bare `delete` key stays Fixed. FixedBinding (keymap.rs:277-286) has no `name` field (true; EditableBinding does, line 294), but it DOES have `id: BindingId` (line 285). BindingId is a runtime atomic counter (`NEXT_BINDING_ID.fetch_add`, keymap.rs:268-271), registration-order-dependent — not a stable, config-usable string handle. ### A3-Q3 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** How do fixed bindings acquire a stable, portable identity for audit/override given FixedBinding has no name field (keymap.rs:277-286), as_lens hardcodes name=Default (keymap.rs:633), and BindingId is an unstable per-run atomic counter (keymap.rs:264-272)?

**A.** Confirmed. Fixed bindings have NO stable, portable identity today: FixedBinding carries only an unstable per-run BindingId (no name; keymap.rs:277-286), as_lens surfaces name="" to every consumer (keymap.rs:633 + lens.name is &'a str at :243), and the entire override/audit/persistence path keys on the &'static str name that fixed bindings lack (update_custom_trigger only touches editable bindings, keymap.rs:422-433; UI lookup rejects empty names, keybindings.rs:939). The proposed "derive id from action TypeId + context predicate" is non-unique — at least 3 same-action+same-context FixedBinding pairs differing only by trigger collide in app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs, and the problem is in fact broader than 3 (see below) because CodeEditorViewAction is a single enum, so all its variants share one TypeId. All 15 evidence items verify exactly.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. Every cited file:line and every count was independently opened and re-run; all 15 evidence items hold exactly. One precision nuance (below) STRENGTHENS the answer rather than weakening it.

CODE FACTS (all re-verified):
1. FixedBinding has no name. Struct (keymap.rs:277-286) = trigger, action, command_description, context_predicate, enabled_predicate, group: Option<&'static str>, id: BindingId. The only string-ish field is the optional group, and grep shows 0 with_group calls across all 46 FixedBinding registrations in app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs, so group is None and is not an identity. EditableBinding (keymap.rs:293-304) carries name: &'static str — THE stable key.
2. as_lens erases identity to "". FixedBinding::as_lens (keymap.rs:631-642) sets name: Default::default(); BindingLens.name is &'a str (keymap.rs:243), so Default = "".
3. BindingId is a per-run, non-portable counter. One global NEXT_BINDING_ID: AtomicUsize (keymap.rs:264-272) fetch_add'd by every constructor (FixedBinding::new :531, empty :548, standard :568, custom :591, EditableBinding::new :662). grep BindingId + serial/persist/json across app/ and crates/ = 0 hits — never serialized.
4. The override/audit/persistence machinery keys exclusively on name, which fixed bindings lack. update_custom_trigger(name) filters editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings by b.name == name and NEVER touches fixed_bindings (keymap.rs:422-433). Settings UI keys on row.binding.name (keybindings.rs:592 set_custom_trigger; :674 set_custom_keybinding; persists/telemeters action: name at :606/:631/:683). The list lookup at keybindings.rs:939 is .find(|b| !name.is_empty() && b.name == name) — it explicitly rejects empty names, so empty-named fixed bindings are unaddressable. util/bindings.rs:491 set_custom_keybinding(binding_name: &str). Net: no handle exists to key an override/audit onto a fixed binding.
5. The proposed derived id (hash of action TypeId + serialized context predicate) is REFUTED:
 (a) Non-unique. Confirmed same-action + same-context FixedBinding pairs differing ONLY by trigger:
   - VimBackspace under text_entry & id!("Vim"): "backspace" (86-89) AND "shift-backspace" (96-99).
   - SelectBackwardsByWord under text_entry: "shift-alt-left"/"shift-ctrl-left" (189-196) AND "shift-meta-left" (197-201).
   - SelectForwardsByWord under text_entry: "shift-alt-right"/"shift-ctrl-right" (202-209) AND "shift-meta-right" (210-214).
   PRECISION NUANCE (strengthens the refutation): CodeEditorViewAction is a SINGLE enum (actions.rs:619), so every variant shares ONE TypeId via action.type_id() (ActionType wraps it, action.rs:43). Under a literal "action TypeId + context" id, the collision set is therefore FAR larger than 3 pairs — e.g. MoveUp/MoveDown/MoveLeft/MoveRight all sit under editable_state with the same TypeId and collapse to one id, and SelectUp/Down/Left/Right all sit under text_entry likewise. The enter fork (actions.rs:51-60) — Enter under editable_state & !id!("Vim") vs VimEnter under text_entry & id!("Vim") — remains DISTINGUISHABLE, but strictly by CONTEXT, NOT by action TypeId (Enter and VimEnter are variants of the same enum, hence identical TypeId and identical type_name()). The answer's phrasing "distinct action AND distinct context" is loose on the "distinct action" half; the operative conclusion (the fork is distinguishable; the three cited pairs are NOT) still holds and the three cited pairs are robust collisions under ANY id scheme (same variant, same context, only trigger differs).
 (b) Action identity isn't release-stable. The Action trait (action.rs:10-19) exposes only type_name() (= std::any::type_name, std-documented as not stable across compiler versions) and TypeId via Any (ActionType, action.rs:32-45) — both runtime-only.
 (c) ContextPredicate isn't serializable/hashable. The enum (context.rs:9-18) derives Debug/Clone/Eq/PartialEq but NOT Serialize/Deserialize and NOT Hash, so a "serialized context predicate" requires adding impls before the derived id is even expressible.

Editor scope note (unchanged): the audited collisions are in the CODE editor (app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs). The terminal editor (app/src/editor/view/) registers an analogous Fixed set and would need the same audit; the three concrete collisions cited are from the code editor only — the answer states this correctly and does not overgeneralize.

_Evidence:_
- FixedBinding has no name field; only group: Option<&'static str> and id: BindingId — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:277-286` _(code)_
- EditableBinding carries the stable name: &'static str key that overrides use — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:293-304` _(code)_
- as_lens sets name=Default::default(); BindingLens.name is &'a str (keymap.rs:243) so every fixed binding surfaces name="" — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:631-642 and :243` _(code)_
- BindingId is one global AtomicUsize counter bumped per binding by all constructors (531,548,568,591,662); grep BindingId+serial/persist/json = 0 hits — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:264-272` _(both)_
- update_custom_trigger filters editable + editable_custom_action bindings by name and never touches fixed_bindings — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- Settings UI keys overrides/persistence/telemetry on binding.name (set_custom_trigger :592, set_custom_keybinding :674, action: name :606/631/683) — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:592,606,631,683` _(code)_
- UI binding-list lookup explicitly rejects empty names, so empty-named fixed bindings are unaddressable — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:939` _(code)_
- Override write path is name-keyed (binding_name: &str) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491` _(code)_
- Synthetic (action+context) id collides: VimBackspace same context (text_entry & Vim), triggers backspace vs shift-backspace — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:86-89 and 96-99` _(code)_
- Collides: SelectBackwardsByWord same text_entry context, shift-alt/ctrl-left vs shift-meta-left — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:189-201` _(code)_
- Collides: SelectForwardsByWord same text_entry context, shift-alt/ctrl-right vs shift-meta-right — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:202-214` _(code)_
- enter fork is distinguishable BY CONTEXT (Enter under editable_state&!Vim vs VimEnter under text_entry&Vim); note both are same TypeId so action does NOT distinguish them — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:51-60` _(code)_
- CodeEditorViewAction is a single enum, so all variants share one TypeId — making a TypeId-based id collide far more broadly than the 3 cited pairs — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:619` _(code)_
- Action trait exposes only type_name() (std::any::type_name) and TypeId via Any (ActionType wraps action.type_id()); neither guaranteed stable across compiler versions — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/action.rs:10-19,32-45` _(code)_
- ContextPredicate derives Debug/Clone/Eq/PartialEq but NOT Serialize/Deserialize/Hash, so it can't be serialized/hashed for a derived id today — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9-18` _(code)_
- 397 FixedBinding::new sites repo-wide; 38 ::new in the code-editor actions file (46 total FixedBinding registrations there, 0 with_group) — `grep -rn FixedBinding::new app/ crates/ = 397; grep -c FixedBinding::new actions.rs = 38; grep -c FixedBinding:: = 46; grep -c with_group = 0` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc rule out "just expose fixed bindings in the UI / derive an id from action+context" as a free option, and forces an explicit identity decision: every remappable fixed binding needs an authored stable string key (add name or promote to EditableBinding), because no existing field (name is absent, lens name is \"\", BindingId is per-run and unpersisted, group is None) provides a portable handle, and a content-hash id collides on real multi-trigger-same-behavior bindings.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The CHOICE among {add a &'static str name at every Fixed site, promote Fixed to EditableBinding, derive a synthetic id} is a design decision, not a code fact; code only constrains it (the derive-an-id route is non-viable as-is: non-unique, TypeId/type_name not release-stable, ContextPredicate not Serialize/Hash). I did not enumerate how many of the 397 FixedBinding::new sites sit under truly user-facing contexts (A3-Q1/A3-Q6's job), nor did I audit the terminal editor (app/src/editor/view/) for its own same-action+same-context collisions — its Fixed set is structurally analogous but I did not enumerate concrete collisions there. The exact size of the TypeId-collapsed collision set in actions.rs was reasoned (MoveUp/Down/Left/Right; SelectUp/Down/Left/Right) but not exhaustively enumerated.

ContextPredicate derive line (context.rs:9) confirmed: Debug/Clone/Eq/PartialEq only — no Serialize/Deserialize/Hash. Action trait (action.rs:10-19,32-45) confirmed: type_name + TypeId only. This surfaced the one imprecision in the answer: because CodeEditorViewAction is a single enum, all its variants share ONE TypeId, so the proposal's "action TypeId" does NOT distinguish enum variants. Both points STRENGTHEN, not weaken, the answer's thesis, and the three cited pairs remain genuine collisions under any scheme. No evidence item was wrong; no shifted line refs; no app/-vs-crates scope confusion; no overgeneralization (the editor-scope caveat is correctly stated). Verdict therefore confirmed.

### A3-Q6 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Is binding registration order deterministic and stable across releases/platforms, given precedence falls back to 'recency' = registration order produced by ~265 register_* calls fired during view init across 109 files, and given editable_bindings_by_name resolves ties by first-enabled (keymap.rs:379-385)?

**A.** Registration order is fully deterministic for a fixed build+feature-flag+platform config: it is a single synchronous, source-ordered init sequence (app/src/lib.rs:1648-1693 inside the once-called, non-async initialize_app, lib.rs:1086, sole caller lib.rs:1065), NOT a function of lazy view creation or responder-chain timing. But it is NOT invariant across releases (source reordering), platforms (cfg gates, lib.rs:1652), or runtime feature-flag states (conditional init blocks, lib.rs:1691) — any of these silently shifts which binding wins by 'recency'. There is a real, code-confirmed asymmetry: keystroke/standard/custom matching walks bindings() in reverse (LIFO/last-registered wins, keymap.rs:441-447,454-464) while get_binding_by_name (the settings-UI path) uses forward order returning the earliest-registered enabled binding (keymap.rs:378-385, doc comment is explicit). Every load-bearing claim verified against code; only the approximate call-site COUNTS differ from my reproduction (262/106 non-test, 387 FixedBinding::new vs the answer's 248/102/373), a method-dependent gap the answer itself flagged as non-substantive.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened every cited file plus uncited locations (notebooks/editor/view.rs fn layout, the editor_view:delete trigger bodies) and re-ran the grep/classification. Every substantive claim holds; the only deltas are non-load-bearing count figures the answer already hedged.

CODE FACTS RE-VERIFIED:

1. Single synchronous source-ordered init chain. app/src/lib.rs:1648 is the comment "Register initial keybindings prior to creating menus", followed by a flat list of `module::init(ctx)` calls through :1693. This block is inside `pub(crate) fn initialize_app` (lib.rs:1086, non-async), whose ONLY caller is lib.rs:1065. Confirmed verbatim. Order = literal source order of init calls, then register_* order within each init, then array-literal order. Not tied to window/view creation.

2. No lazy per-view registration. I re-ran the enclosing-fn classification over all non-test register_fixed_bindings/register_editable_bindings call sites: 212 attributed to `fn init` (matches the answer exactly), and the only 5 attributed to `fn new` are ALL in crates/warpui_core/src/core/mod_tests.rs (test code), so production code has ZERO register_* in a constructor/render. NOTE: the 212 figure is itself a naive-classifier UNDERCOUNT — nested helper fns defined inside init (e.g. `fn debug_notebooks_enabled() -> bool` at notebooks/editor/view.rs:100) cause the heuristic to mis-bucket ~12 calls that actually live in `pub fn init` (notebooks/editor/view.rs:90). The real init-resident count is higher, which STRENGTHENS the "all registration funnels through init" thesis. The named helpers (init_actions_from_parent_view, register_conversation_list_view_bindings, init_overlapping_keybindings, add_*_as_editable_binding, register_input_mode_bindings, add_toggle_setting_action_pairs_as_bindings) are init-time helpers invoked transitively from the lib.rs chain (verified: workspace/mod.rs:101 register_conversation_list_view_bindings and :103 init_actions_from_parent_view sit inside fn init at workspace/mod.rs:78, called from lib.rs:1654).

3. Consumption order / precedence. keymap.rs:454-464 bindings() yields editable_bindings() first then fixed (.rev()). editable_bindings() (keymap.rs:441-447) iterates `.iter().rev()`, and the doc comment at :439-440 states "the most recently registered editable binding will have the highest precedence" — LIFO/recency confirmed. All three match paths walk the same iterator: push_keystroke (matcher.rs:324, early-return :329-331), match_standard (matcher.rs:351), match_custom (matcher.rs:363). editable_bindings_by_name is a HashMap (keymap.rs:30) storing Vec<usize> "in the order they were registered" (doc :28-30), pushed at keymap.rs:417 — used only for name lookup, not matching, so no HashMap-iteration nondeterminism enters tiebreaking.

4. Forward/reverse asymmetry (the sharp finding) — CONFIRMED. get_binding_by_name (keymap.rs:379-385) does `indices.iter().find_map(... is_enabled)` in FORWARD registration order; the doc comment at :378 literally reads "Returns the earliest-registered currently-enabled binding with the given name." This is the OPPOSITE of the matcher's reverse/recency order. The settings UI / palette read through this path: app/src/util/bindings.rs:471-473 keybinding_name_to_keystroke -> ctx.get_binding_by_name; app/src/search/command_palette/view.rs:267 uses ctx.get_binding_by_name. So displayed/edited precedence (first-registered) can diverge from dispatched precedence (last-registered) for editable same-name bindings with DIFFERING default triggers. A user override updates ALL same-name bindings (update_custom_trigger, keymap.rs:422-433 iterates both editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings filtering by name), so the asymmetry only bites for differing DEFAULT triggers.

5. Duplicate names — CONFIRMED at exact lines. editor_view:delete is registered by the code editor (app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:504, CodeEditorViewAction::Delete), terminal editor (app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:769, EditorAction::Delete), and notebooks (app/src/notebooks/editor/view.rs:589, EditorViewAction::Delete); editor_view:up at actions.rs:329 / notebooks:427 / mod.rs:618; editor_view:end at actions.rs:380 / notebooks:476 / mod.rs:659. Different actions and context predicates. NUANCE I checked that the answer did not: all three editor_view:delete sites use the SAME default key "ctrl-d", so this specific triplet would NOT produce a divergent UI-vs-dispatch DISPLAY (the answer correctly scoped this example to "real duplicate-name case" and flagged divergence as a residual unknown — no overclaim).

DETERMINISM CAVEATS — CONFIRMED:
- Across releases: any reorder/insert of an init line or a binding shifts Vec indices and thus reverse-order precedence. Nothing pins absolute order.
- Across platforms: init chain is cfg-gated, e.g. lib.rs:1652 `#[cfg(not(target_family = "wasm"))] code::editor::find::view::init`. Per-binding variants e.g. root_view.rs:457 `with_linux_or_windows_key_binding("f11")` and :467 `with_enabled(|| ...)` change which binding is enabled at match time.
- Across feature-flag states (same binary): lib.rs:1691 `if FeatureFlag::CodeReviewSaveChanges.is_enabled() { code_review::init(ctx); }` conditionally registers a whole block, shifting later indices.

CONCLUSION (unchanged): 'recency' is deterministic/reproducible for a given (build, platform, flag-state) tuple, but is an emergent artifact of source ordering + cfg/flag gating, so it silently changes across releases/platforms/flag flips, and is compounded by the forward(UI)/reverse(matcher) asymmetry. The design should replace implicit registration-order recency with an explicit stored ordering key and unify get_binding_by_name with the matcher's resolution order.

### A3-Q7 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** If user overrides move from in-place custom_trigger mutation (keymap.rs:300,422-433) to a separate layer, what drives the live re-render that Tracked<EditableBinding> provides today — keep per-binding Tracked, or move to a layer-level revision counter?

**A.** Today live re-render is driven by per-binding autotracking: each EditableBinding is a Tracked with its own TrackedId; render-time reads go Deref→track_read (registering the view as a dependent, mod.rs:272-287) and update_custom_trigger mutates custom_trigger via iter_mut → DerefMut→track_update, which pushes every dependent view into invalidations (≈ ctx.notify, mod.rs:290-296). A layer refactor preserves live-apply ONLY if the new override store is itself Tracked (or carries a Tracked revision counter) and render reads/writes flow through Deref/DerefMut; storing overrides as plain side data silently breaks the distributed keystroke-hint surfaces. Both granularities are mechanically supported (mod.rs:41-46): per-binding gives fine-grained single-hint invalidation; a layer-level counter is correct but over-invalidates. That choice is a design decision, not a code fact. Independently verified: all 10 evidence claims hold at the cited lines.

VERDICT: Confirmed. I opened every cited file (plus uncited locations: app.rs:1704 chain and tip_view.rs:550-570) and each evidence claim says what the answer asserts.

MECHANISM (verified):
- Tracked<T> assigns a unique TrackedId at construction via TrackedId::next() (atomic, tracked.rs:74-81). editable_bindings and editable_custom_action_bindings are both Vec<Tracked<EditableBinding>> (keymap.rs:27,37).
- Reads: Deref::deref calls track_read(self.id) (tracked.rs:41-48). track_read, only while cache.rendering_view is set, records the view as a dependent of that TrackedId (mod.rs:272-287). Doc confirms reads-during-render are cached as the view's deps (mod.rs:50-63).
- Writes: DerefMut::deref_mut calls track_update(self.id) (tracked.rs:50-55). track_update extends cache.invalidations with every view in value_dependencies[id] (mod.rs:290-296) — effectively ctx.notify per dependent view.
- Trigger of the write: Keymap::update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) iterates editable_custom_action_bindings.iter_mut().filter(...) then editable_bindings.iter_mut().filter(...) and assigns binding.custom_trigger = trigger.clone(); each field assignment on &mut Tracked goes through DerefMut→track_update for that binding's TrackedId. Reached from Matcher::set_custom_trigger/remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-152) ← app set_custom_keybinding/reset (util/bindings.rs:491+).

GRANULARITY = PER-BINDING (verified): one Tracked<EditableBinding> == one TrackedId. Single-binding render path get_binding_by_name (keymap.rs:379-385) → matcher.rs:274-275 → AppContext::get_binding_by_name (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1704) → keybinding_name_to_keystroke/_display_string (util/bindings.rs:471-487) derefs only the name-matching binding(s). Iterator readers editable_bindings()/bindings() (keymap.rs:441-464) deref EVERY binding via as_lens (741-759), so walkers depend on all. The keymap keeps TWO Tracked copies per custom-action binding with DIFFERENT ids (primary in editable_bindings + a separate Tracked::new((*binding).clone()) at keymap.rs:412); update_custom_trigger feeds both in lockstep — a layer model must keep both fed (A3-Q8 overlap).

KEY NUANCE (verified): the settings keybindings list does NOT rely on Tracked. on_page_selected (keybindings.rs:748, snapshot at 754-792) caches self.bindings/self.rows, and every edit handler (remove_keystroke 590-615, reset 617-641, cancel 643-667, confirm 669-699) refreshes via explicit update_binding_list + ctx.notify and mutates row.binding.trigger. render (keybindings.rs:734,1098) draws cached rows. So Tracked autotracking drives the DISTRIBUTED keystroke-hint surfaces (get_binding_by_name path), NOT the settings list, which survives via explicit notify.

DISPATCH independent of autotracking (verified): push_keystroke (matcher.rs:307-346, reads keymap.bindings() at :324, early-return :330-331), match_standard (:351), match_custom (:364) read at dispatch time, not under render tracking — matching always sees the fresh trigger; only on-screen display needs Tracked.

DESIGN-DECISION (code-verifiable constraints): mod.rs:41-46 explicitly supports both per-value (granular) and whole-model (coarse) tracking, so either "keep per-binding Tracked" or "layer-level Tracked revision counter" is viable. Invariant that MUST hold for the layer refactor: (1) the override/layer store is a Tracked field (or holds a Tracked revision counter), and (2) render-time hint readers read through Deref (track_read) while writes go through DerefMut (track_update). If overrides move to a plain non-Tracked side store (or are mutated without DerefMut), track_update never fires and distributed hints go stale — and because the settings list refreshes via explicit ctx.notify, that regression would be INVISIBLE in the settings page during testing. Trade-off: a layer-level counter is simplest but over-invalidates (any override bumps it, invalidating all single-hint views); per-binding keeps fine-grained single-hint invalidation.

_Evidence:_
- Deref::deref calls track_read; DerefMut::deref_mut calls track_update; each Tracked gets a unique TrackedId via TrackedId::next() (atomic counter) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/autotracking/tracked.rs:41-55,74-81` _(code)_
- track_read records the rendering view as a dependent of the TrackedId (only while cache.rendering_view is set); track_update extends cache.invalidations with every view in value_dependencies[id] (≈ ctx.notify) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/autotracking/mod.rs:272-296` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc state a concrete invariant for the layering refactor: overrides may move to a separate layer, but that layer must live under autotracking — store it as a Tracked field (per-binding Tracked to retain fine-grained single-hint invalidation, or a layer-level Tracked revision counter for simpler coarse invalidation) and ensure render-time hint readers (get_binding_by_name path) and the override write path keep flowing through Deref/DerefMut. It also flags the test-masking trap: the settings list refreshes via explicit ctx.notify, so a broken Tracked path would still look fine in the settings page while distributed keystroke hints silently go stale.

Open residue: (a) acceptable invalidation granularity vs simplicity (over-invalidating all hint views per override is likely fine perf-wise but unmeasured); (b) whether the new layer model collapses the two parallel Tracked copies (editable_bindings + editable_custom_action_bindings, keymap.rs:412) into one store or keeps per-layer shadows — overlaps A3-Q8; (c) whether the settings list should migrate onto autotracking (currently explicit ctx.notify), which changes what a Tracked regression makes visible. I did not exhaustively audit every app/ render path that draws a shortcut, but the get_binding_by_name/editable_bindings/keybinding_name_to_keystroke reader set is representative; I confirmed one distributed reader chain end-to-end (bindings.rs → core/app.rs:1704 → matcher → keymap:379).

### A3-Q8 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Does the layer refactor unify or shadow the parallel custom-action collections fixed_custom_action_bindings and editable_custom_action_bindings (keymap.rs:396,411), maintained alongside the main vecs and updated in lockstep by update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) and walked separately via custom_action_bindings() (keymap.rs:474)?

**A.** Confirmed. The shadow vecs (fixed_custom_action_bindings keymap.rs:36, editable_custom_action_bindings :37) are independent filtered clones of Custom-trigger bindings, kept only as the macOS menuNeedsUpdate optimization (documented :32-35). They are kept coherent by exactly three lockstep write sites (register_fixed_bindings :396, register_editable_bindings :411-412, update_custom_trigger :424) and read by exactly two consumers — neither on a keystroke hot path. keymap.rs:428/:431 are the only two custom_trigger writers in the codebase. A layer model can unify them into a single layered store plus a derived Custom index (mirroring editable_bindings_by_name :30), collapsing update_custom_trigger's dual write to one mutation; unify-vs-shadow is a design decision, not a code fact.

Every load-bearing claim was independently opened and verified; no material error found. The answer is precise on line refs and scope.

COLLECTIONS (keymap.rs:25-38): primary fixed_bindings (:26) and editable_bindings (:27); name index editable_bindings_by_name (:30); shadows fixed_custom_action_bindings (:36) and editable_custom_action_bindings (:37). The comment at :32-35 states the shadows exist to optimize Custom-action lookups for the macOS [WarpDelegate menuNeedsUpdate] selector. VERIFIED verbatim.

THREE LOCKSTEP WRITE SITES (verified, and grep confirms these are the ONLY writers of either shadow vec):
1. register_fixed_bindings (:391-399): extends fixed_bindings then pushes Custom-trigger entries' clone into fixed_custom_action_bindings (:396).
2. register_editable_bindings (:405-419): extends editable_bindings with Tracked wrappers, pushes an INDEPENDENT Tracked clone Tracked::new((*binding).clone()) of each Custom-trigger entry into editable_custom_action_bindings (:411-412), and updates editable_bindings_by_name (:414-417).
3. update_custom_trigger (:422-433): dual write — mutates custom_trigger on editable_custom_action_bindings filtered by name (:424-428) AND on editable_bindings filtered by name (:430-432).
The grep `\.custom_trigger\s*=` returns ONLY keymap.rs:428 and :431 across crates/ and app/ — confirming "the only two custom_trigger writers."

WHY LOCKSTEP IS REQUIRED: EditableBinding has #[derive(Clone)] at keymap.rs:292 (struct at :293), and the shadow holds a fresh Tracked over a cloned EditableBinding (:411-412), so custom_trigger is independently owned per copy. Primary and shadow drift unless update_custom_trigger writes both. VERIFIED.

READS — two distinct paths:
- Primary bindings() (:454-464): editable_bindings().rev/filter chained with fixed_bindings.iter().rev().filter(); does NOT touch shadows. Consumed by push_keystroke (matcher.rs:324), match_standard (:351), match_custom (:364), default_binding_for_custom_action (:233-234), bindings_for_context (:284), and the pass-through get_bindings (:304). All VERIFIED at the exact cited lines.
- Shadow custom_action_bindings() (:474-484): editable_custom_action_bindings().rev/filter chained with fixed_custom_action_bindings.iter().rev().filter().

SHADOW CONSUMERS — exactly two (verified by grep of custom_action_bindings call sites): keymap.custom_action_bindings() (:474) is called only from matcher.rs:224 (Matcher::custom_action_bindings wrapper, :223-225, reached only via app.rs:1911-1912 AppContext::custom_action_bindings → mac/app.rs:386 warp_app_has_custom_action_for_keystroke) and matcher.rs:252 (binding_for_custom_action_in_context, :246-263). app/ has ZERO references to custom_action_bindings — confirming app/ never touches the shadows directly; the macOS consumer lives in crates/warpui (read directly, outside graph scope).

SPEC-DOC INACCURACY CORRECTION — VERIFIED CORRECT: the spec's A3-Q8 "Why it matters" (line 493) claims the shadow is "used by default_binding_for_custom_action, matcher.rs:229-243." That is wrong: default_binding_for_custom_action walks self.keymap.bindings() at matcher.rs:233-234 (the PRIMARY path). The shadow's real consumers are binding_for_custom_action_in_context (:252) and the macOS menu path (:386). Consequence (correct): the shadow sits off every keystroke/standard/custom dispatch hot path, so unifying it has no matcher-correctness blast radius beyond the macOS menu lookup and binding_for_custom_action_in_context.

ENTRY POINTS: set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-141 → update_custom_trigger Some), remove_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:146-152 → update_custom_trigger None), AppContext::set_custom_trigger exposure at app.rs:1637. VERIFIED — every external mutation routes through the lockstep.

FEASIBILITY OF COLLAPSE: editable_bindings_by_name (:30) is an existing derived Vec<usize> index carrying no duplicated binding state — a valid precedent for replacing the two shadow clone-vecs with a derived Custom index, which would make custom_trigger single-source-of-truth and reduce update_custom_trigger to one mutation. This is a sound design inference; the answer correctly caveats unify-vs-shadow as a design decision in residual_unknowns, not a code fact.

NITPICK (immaterial): the answer says EditableBinding "derives Clone (keymap.rs:292)" — the #[derive(Clone)] attribute is at :292 exactly and the struct keyword at :293, so the cite is correct, not off by one.

_Evidence:_
- Four collections: primary fixed_bindings (:26) + editable_bindings (:27), name index editable_bindings_by_name (:30), and two shadow vecs (:36,:37) whose stated purpose (:32-35) is the macOS menuNeedsUpdate optimization — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:25-38` _(code)_

- Shadow consumer #2: macOS warp_app_has_custom_action_for_keystroke calls ctx.custom_action_bindings() at :386 (via app.rs:1911-1912 → matcher.rs:223-225); app/ has zero shadow references — `crates/warpui/src/platform/mac/app.rs:375-396` _(both)_
- set_custom_trigger (:137-141) and remove_custom_trigger (:146-152) both route through update_custom_trigger, so lockstep covers every external mutation — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-152` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc decide the keymap data structure for layering: replace the two cloned shadow vecs with a single layered store + a derived Custom index (mirroring editable_bindings_by_name), collapsing update_custom_trigger's dual write to one mutation and removing the primary/shadow desync class — while knowing the shadow only feeds binding_for_custom_action_in_context and the macOS menu path, not the keystroke/standard/custom match hot paths.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The unify-vs-shadow choice is a design decision, not a code fact: code shows it is feasible and low-risk to unify (shadow is off all dispatch hot paths; the editable_bindings_by_name derived-index precedent exists), but whether each layer/mode-pack carries its own Custom shadow or the keymap derives a single global Custom index is for the design doc. Also undetermined in code: whether a per-layer model retains the macOS menuNeedsUpdate perf optimization (the shadow's only motivation per keymap.rs:32-35) as a derived/cached index or accepts a full bindings() scan. ### A3-Q9 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Must a real Unbound state suppress the original_trigger fallback in match_custom (verified at matcher.rs:370-373), and does doing so break the intended 'rebind a menu action's keystroke but keep the menu item firing' behavior that the fallback was built for?

**A.** Yes — a real Unbound MUST suppress the original_trigger fallback, because today Trigger::Empty does NOT unbind a Custom-triggered binding (match_custom still matches via original_trigger at matcher.rs:370-373). And no, suppressing it need not break the legitimate rebind case: Rebound (trigger=Keystrokes) and Unbound (trigger=Empty) are already discriminable via Trigger::is_empty (keymap.rs:52-54), so the fallback can be gated on !trigger.is_empty() to suppress it only for genuine unbinds while preserving rebinds. The same fallback exists in two sibling resolvers (matcher.rs:240, :258), so the guard must be applied at all three Custom resolvers, not just match_custom.

Independently verified against the code; the colleague's answer is accurate on every material point.\n\n1) match_custom (matcher.rs:363-377) matches a Custom action two ways: (a) current trigger `if let Trigger::Custom(tag) = binding.trigger` (365-368), and (b) UNCONDITIONALLY the recorded `if let Some(Trigger::Custom(tag)) = binding.original_trigger` (370-373). There is no Empty/non-Empty guard before consulting original_trigger. Confirmed verbatim.\n\n2) 'none' flow: app/src/keyboard.rs:15 defines REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION="none"; TryFrom at keyboard.rs:192-193 decodes the literal "none" into UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed; the Removed arm at keyboard.rs:43-44 calls app.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty). set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-141) → keymap.update_custom_trigger(name, Some(Empty)) (keymap.rs:422-433), setting custom_trigger=Some(Empty). (Minor note: the answer compressed the two-step "none"→Removed→Empty into "line 44 maps 'none'"; line 44 actually maps Removed→Empty, with the literal-string decode at 192-193 — substantively correct.)\n\n3) Lens result: EditableBinding::as_lens (keymap.rs:741-747) — when custom_trigger is Some it returns (trigger=custom_trigger, original_trigger=Some(&self.trigger)); else (&self.trigger, None). So an 'unbound' menu/Custom editable binding yields trigger=Empty, original_trigger=Some(Custom(tag)). push_keystroke (matcher.rs:324-325) only matches Trigger::Keystrokes, so Empty correctly drops the KEYBOARD match; BUT match_custom (370-373) still matches original_trigger=Custom(tag) and returns the action. Net: menu/Custom dispatch is NOT unbound — the leak the report's L4 (report:129) describes ("cannot truly unbind a default except by overloading Trigger::Empty"). Confirmed.\n\n4) Rebound case: custom_trigger=Keystrokes([..]) (keyboard.rs:47) → trigger=Keystrokes (keyboard fires via push_keystroke 325) AND original_trigger=Custom(tag) (menu still fires via match_custom 370-373). This is the intended 'rebind a menu action's keystroke but keep the menu item firing' behavior. Confirmed.\n\n5) Disentangling Unbound vs Rebound needs NO new field: Rebound has trigger=Keystrokes (non-empty); Unbound has trigger=Empty (Trigger::is_empty, keymap.rs:52-54). Guarding the fallback with `!binding.trigger.is_empty()` suppresses original_trigger only when the current trigger is itself inert (Empty), making 'none' a true unbind for Custom triggers while leaving rebinds untouched. Confirmed sound.\n\n6) Scope: the identical `(Trigger::Custom(tag), _) | (_, Some(Trigger::Custom(tag)))` fallback appears in default_binding_for_custom_action (matcher.rs:237-242, :240) and binding_for_custom_action_in_context (matcher.rs:255-262, :258). Confirmed — a true Unbound state / is_empty guard must be applied at all three Custom resolvers (cross-refs A3-Q11).\n\n7) Fixed bindings are NOT affected: FixedBinding::as_lens hardcodes original_trigger:None (keymap.rs:638) and there is no custom_trigger override path for fixed bindings (register_fixed_bindings, keymap.rs:391-399). So the unbind leak is specific to editable Custom-triggered bindings — exactly the class menu items use. Confirmed.\n\nTEST GAP confirmed: keymap_tests.rs:319 (`update_custom_trigger("first", None)`) only exercises the remove-customization revert path with Keystrokes-default actions (assertions at 327-339), not the Empty/unbind path. A grep over matcher_tests.rs returned ZERO references to match_custom / Trigger::Empty / original_trigger, corroborating that no test asserts the current 'none on a Custom-triggered editable binding still fires via match_custom' behavior. A failing test should be added on a Custom-trigger editable binding with custom_trigger=Empty asserting match_custom currently returns Action (documents the leak) and the desired post-fix None.

_Evidence:_
- match_custom consults original_trigger unconditionally (no Empty guard), so an 'unbound' Custom binding still dispatches via the second branch — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:363-377 (esp. 370-373)` _(code)_
- 'none' constant decodes to UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed (TryFrom) which maps to Trigger::Empty via set_custom_trigger — `app/src/keyboard.rs:15,192-193,43-44` _(code)_
- set_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger sets custom_trigger=Some(Empty) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137-141; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(both)_
- as_lens: when custom_trigger=Some, trigger=custom_trigger and original_trigger=Some(default trigger); thus Empty override leaves original_trigger=Custom(tag) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:741-747` _(code)_
- Rebound case: custom_trigger=Keystrokes makes keyboard fire (push_keystroke only matches Keystrokes) while menu still fires via original_trigger — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-325,370-373; app/src/keyboard.rs:47` _(code)_
- Trigger::Empty exists and Trigger::is_empty discriminates Unbound(Empty) from Rebound(Keystrokes), enabling a !trigger.is_empty() fallback gate — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:43-49,52-54` _(code)_
- Same Custom-fallback leak exists in two sibling resolvers, so suppression must apply to all three resolvers — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:237-242(:240),255-262(:258)` _(code)_
- Fixed bindings have original_trigger:None and no custom_trigger override path, so the unbind leak is specific to editable Custom-triggered bindings — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:638,391-399` _(both)_
- Existing test only covers custom_trigger=None revert with Keystrokes defaults; grep of matcher_tests.rs finds no match_custom/Trigger::Empty/original_trigger test asserting the leak — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap_tests.rs:319-339; grep matcher_tests.rs (0 hits)` _(both)_
- Report L4/R2 corroborate: cannot truly unbind except by overloading Trigger::Empty; R2 calls for an explicit Unbound state — `.understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:129,140` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design define two distinct states — Rebound (keep original_trigger fallback) vs Unbound (suppress it) — and specify that Unbound suppression is implemented either by gating the original_trigger fallback on !trigger.is_empty() or by a dedicated Unbound/tombstone variant, applied uniformly across all three Custom-action resolvers, so 'none' actually unbinds menu/Custom dispatch without breaking legitimate keystroke rebinds of menu actions.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Product decision (not code-settleable): whether 'unbind keystroke' should also disable menu/Custom dispatch, or whether some actions must remain reachable from the menu even when keyboard-unbound — i.e., whether Unbound means 'no keystroke' or 'fully inert'. Design decision (cross-refs A3-Q10/Q12): whether to model Unbound as a distinct enum/layer tombstone vs. reuse Trigger::Empty + an is_empty() fallback guard; both are viable in code but interact with the conflict map and any settings 'state' badge. Either way the fix must be applied at all three Custom resolvers (match_custom, default_binding_for_custom_action, binding_for_custom_action_in_context). One small unverified runtime detail: I confirmed the data-flow statically but did not execute a test proving match_custom returns Action for a live Empty-override Custom binding — the recommended failing test would close that.
- _Verification:_ I opened every cited file and confirmed each line reference. matcher.rs:363-377 match_custom has the unconditional original_trigger second branch (370-373) with no is_empty guard — verified verbatim. matcher.rs:137-141, keymap.rs:422-433, keymap.rs:741-747, keymap.rs:43-55, keymap.rs:638, keymap.rs:391-399, matcher.rs:324-346, and the two sibling resolvers at matcher.rs:237-243/246-263 (fallback at :240 and :258) all say exactly what the answer asserts. I checked a location the answer under-cited: the actual 'none'->Removed string decode is in TryFrom at keyboard.rs:192-193, not literally at line 44 (line 44 is Removed->Trigger::Empty) — a harmless compression in the answer's prose; the substance and the constant at line 15 are correct, so I added 192-193 to the evidence. I independently grepped matcher_tests.rs (where match_custom lives, being a Matcher method) and found ZERO references to match_custom/Trigger::Empty/original_trigger, which strengthens (rather than weakens) the TEST GAP claim. Report lines 129 (L4) and 140 (R2) match the quoted text exactly. No overgeneralization found: the answer correctly scopes the leak to editable Custom-triggered bindings and excludes fixed bindings, and correctly notes app/ vs crates/ boundaries (keyboard.rs in app/, matcher/keymap in crates/warpui_core). No shifted line refs, no off counts, no graph-vs-code contradiction. The proposed !trigger.is_empty() gate is logically sound against the verified as_lens semantics.

### A3-Q10 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** In the new model, are Unbound bindings excluded from the precedence walk / catalog / conflict detection, or retained as tombstones — given EditableBinding::new defaults trigger=Empty (keymap.rs:660) and removal sets custom_trigger=Empty (keybindings.rs:592), both still yielded by bindings()?

**A.** Today there is no first-class Unbound/tombstone type. An "unbound" binding is just Trigger::Empty, which is STILL yielded by bindings() (the only filter on either editable or fixed iterators is is_enabled()/enabled_predicate — never trigger emptiness). It is RETAINED in the precedence walk (engine bindings()) and the UI catalog (rendered as trigger=None and kept as a resettable row), EXCLUDED from conflict detection (Empty→None is flattened out and has_conflict(None)=false), and acts as a de-facto tombstone for its OWN default in keystroke matching (push_keystroke/match_standard never read original_trigger). But it LEAKS for Custom/menu triggers because match_custom, default_binding_for_custom_action, and binding_for_custom_action_in_context all fall back to original_trigger==Custom. So Trigger::Empty is a within-the-same-binding override-nullification, not a cross-layer key-level block: in a multi-layer model it cannot suppress a SEPARATE lower-layer binding on the same key — the linear scan simply skips the Empty entry and continues. The new resolver needs an explicit Unbound entry recognized by all three match paths (and the Custom original_trigger fallback suppressed for genuine unbinds), while staying invisible to ConflictMap (already true).

CommandBinding.trigger is Option<Keystroke> (bindings.rs:534), built via trigger_to_keystroke in from_lens (bindings.rs:752) and from_editable_lens (bindings.rs:765). ANSWER (excluded vs tombstone): Neither cleanly — a hybrid with no first-class Unbound type. Unbound = Trigger::Empty: RETAINED in the precedence walk and the UI catalog (None row), EXCLUDED from conflict detection, tombstone for its OWN default in keystroke matching, but LEAKY for Custom/menu via original_trigger.

DESIGN IMPLICATION (correctly labeled as a decision, not a code fact): Trigger::Empty is a within-the-same-binding override-nullification, not a cross-layer key-level block. In the single-layer model that suffices (default and override are one object; custom_trigger overrides trigger in place). In a multi-layer model, a user-layer Unbound that must suppress a SEPARATE lower-layer (default/mode-pack) binding on the same keystroke is NOT achievable by Empty alone — push_keystroke's linear scan (matcher.rs:324-338) just skips the Empty entry and matches the lower-layer binding. So a real tombstone needs an explicit "block this key at this layer" entry recognized by all three paths, with the Custom original_trigger fallback suppressed for genuine unbinds (Q9/Q11), while remaining invisible to ConflictMap (already true).

### A3-Q11 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Does the (layer, recency) precedence and the new Unbound state apply uniformly across ALL THREE match paths that independently walk bindings() — push_keystroke for Keystrokes (matcher.rs:324), match_standard for Standard (matcher.rs:351), match_custom for Custom (matcher.rs:363) — or only to keystroke matching?

**A.** Confirmed. Precedence (layer + recency) IS uniform across all three match paths because all three walk the same Keymap::bindings() iterator and take the first match — ordering is centralized in bindings(), not in any per-path code. But the existing Unbound state (Trigger::Empty) is NOT uniform: it suppresses dispatch in push_keystroke and match_standard, yet match_custom's original_trigger fallback (matcher.rs:370-374) still matches an unbound Custom/menu binding, so unbinding a Custom action silently no-ops. The crux — is_enabled() (keymap.rs:782-784) filters only on enabled_predicate, never on the trigger, so an Empty-trigger binding stays in the iterator with original_trigger=Some(Custom(tag)) and re-matches. Verified independently.

PRECEDENCE — UNIFORM (verified). All three loops iterate the identical iterator self.keymap.bindings(): push_keystroke at matcher.rs:324 (filters Trigger::Keystrokes at :325, early-returns full match at :329-331), match_standard at matcher.rs:351 (filters Trigger::Standard at :352, returns first match at :354), match_custom at matcher.rs:364 (filters Trigger::Custom at :365, returns at :367). The (layer, recency) order lives entirely inside Keymap::bindings() (keymap.rs:454-464): editable_bindings() first (keymap.rs:441-447 — .rev() = reverse-registration/LIFO recency, filtered by is_enabled()), chained with fixed_bindings.iter().rev(). So layer = editable-before-fixed and recency = LIFO-within-group are both centralized in one place; any new (layer, recency) sort installed at bindings() is inherited by all three paths. The three dispatchers also share an identical structure — the answer's cited app.rs lines 1873/1988/2014 are EXACTLY the `for (i, ctx) in context_chain.iter_mut().enumerate().rev()` reverse responder/context-chain loops in dispatch_standard_action (fn at app.rs:1866), dispatch_custom_action_internal (fn at app.rs:1981) and dispatch_keystroke (fn at app.rs:2005), each immediately calling match_standard/match_custom/push_keystroke — so context-layering is uniform too. (Note the file is crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs, which the evidence path states correctly; the prose 'app.rs' is shorthand, and app/src/core/app.rs does not exist.)

UNBOUND STATE — NOT UNIFORM (the correctness hole, verified). Unbinding maps to custom_trigger = Some(Trigger::Empty): on-disk 'none'/UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed -> app.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) (keyboard.rs:15 REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION='none', :44), and the settings UI remove_keystroke does the same (settings_view/keybindings.rs:592); both route through Keymap::update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422). EditableBinding::as_lens (keymap.rs:742-746) then yields trigger=&Empty AND original_trigger=Some(&self.trigger) (the default, which for a Custom action is Trigger::Custom(tag)). Crucially, is_enabled() (keymap.rs:782-784) tests only self.enabled (the enabled_predicate), NOT the trigger, so an Empty-trigger binding is NOT filtered out of editable_bindings()/bindings(); it still appears in the iterator. Per path:
- push_keystroke (matcher.rs:325): only matches Trigger::Keystrokes on binding.trigger; Empty skipped -> unbind effective.
- match_standard (matcher.rs:352): only matches Trigger::Standard on binding.trigger; never consults original_trigger; Empty skipped -> unbind effective.
- match_custom (matcher.rs:364-374): checks Trigger::Custom on binding.trigger (Empty skipped) AND ALSO on binding.original_trigger (matcher.rs:370-374). For an unbound Custom binding original_trigger=Some(Trigger::Custom(tag)), so it STILL MATCHES and dispatches. Unbinding a Custom/menu trigger therefore silently no-ops. The Trigger::Empty enum comment 'empty trigger (cannot actually be matched)' (keymap.rs:48) reflects the author's assumption that Empty can never match — an assumption the original_trigger fallback silently violates for Custom triggers. FixedBinding (keymap.rs:277-286) has no name field and no custom_trigger, so this only affects editable Custom bindings (fixed/menu bindings cannot be unbound at all).

ASYMMETRY: match_standard ignores original_trigger entirely while match_custom consults it. The fallback is presumably intentional for REMAPPING (custom_trigger=Keystrokes keeps the menu's Custom dispatch alive), but it leaks for UNBINDING (custom_trigger=Empty). The two Trigger families have divergent original_trigger semantics today.

TEST COVERAGE: matcher_tests.rs has 0 references to match_standard/match_custom/Standard/Custom (verified by grep -c = 0); only push_keystroke is exercised, via the test_keystroke helper at matcher_tests.rs:229-236. The Standard/Custom paths and the unbind-of-Custom hole are untested in the matcher.

DESIGN IMPLICATION: To make precedence + Unbound apply uniformly, resolve layer order and Unbound/tombstone suppression once at the bindings()/resolver level (all three inherit it), AND make match_custom's original_trigger fallback tombstone-aware (skip when the current trigger is an Unbound tombstone). Otherwise re-layering/unbinding leaks dispatch for menu/Custom actions. Add Standard and Custom unbind/precedence fixtures (Q12). 103 non-test with_custom_action/with_standard_action registrations size the blast radius (reproduced exactly).

### A3-Q12 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** How does a user-layer Unbound relate to the THREE existing suppression mechanisms — context_predicate=Just(false), the zero-arg enabled_predicate closure (keymap.rs:626-627), and Trigger::Empty (keymap.rs:660) — so that 'is this key active in this context?' has a single, well-defined answer instead of four overlapping ones?

**A.** Architecture thesis is correct and well-anchored: the three suppression mechanisms live at three different layers and are never unified by any single is_active()/classify_state() (grep confirms none for bindings). enabled_predicate is a context-free, fn()->bool whole-binding filter applied at the bindings() iterator source (keymap.rs:446/461/471/481); context_predicate (incl. Just(false)) is evaluated per-call at each of the four consumers (matcher.rs:285,327,353,366/371), leaving the binding inside bindings(); Trigger::Empty is the implicit no-match fall-through and IS the existing unbind path (set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty), app/src/keyboard.rs:44 and settings_view/keybindings.rs:592). A user-layer Unbound is only a genuine fourth switch if it is a distinct tombstone rather than reusing Trigger::Empty. ONE correction: the answer says enabled_predicate feature-flag gating is "~30 app/ sites" — the actual count is 93 with_enabled call sites (83 closure-form + ~10 fn-pointer-form), ~3x higher.

VERDICT: corrected. Every structural/architectural claim was independently re-opened and holds at (or within one line of) the cited location. The only material defect is an undercounted statistic.

1) enabled_predicate — GLOBAL, context-free, whole-binding exclusion at the SOURCE. Type `EnabledPredicate = fn() -> bool` (keymap.rs:237). Read by is_enabled = `self.enabled_predicate.is_none_or(|predicate| predicate())` on both FixedBinding (keymap.rs:626-627) and EditableBindingLens (keymap.rs:782-783). APPLIED as `.filter(|b| b.is_enabled())` at the bindings() iterator: editable_bindings() keymap.rs:446, fixed branch :461, custom-action editable :471, custom-action fixed :481. A disabled binding is EXCLUDED from bindings() entirely — never reaches any matcher loop, never appears in bindings_for_context, and never reaches the settings list (which iterates ctx.editable_bindings() → keymap.editable_bindings(), already is_enabled-filtered, keybindings.rs:754). Doc-comment at keymap.rs:231-236 confirms the intent: feature-flag gating that re-evaluates at runtime. Semantics = UNAVAILABLE (binding does not exist in this build/runtime). CORRECTION: usage is broader than the answer claims — not "~30 app/ sites" but 93 `.with_enabled(` calls in app/src/ (83 closure-form like `.with_enabled(|| FeatureFlag::CodeFindReplace.is_enabled())`, plus ~10 fn-pointer-form like `.with_enabled(debug_notebooks_enabled)` and `.with_enabled(enabled_predicate)`); both code (app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:605) and terminal editor (app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:916 AgentMode, :923 !AgentMode) use it — so the same-mechanism, no-divergence claim still holds.

2) context_predicate — PER-CONTEXT, evaluated at each consumer, not at the source. ContextPredicate enum context.rs:10-18, variant Just(bool) at :17; default for editable bindings is Just(true) (keymap.rs:657). eval at context.rs:101-119; `Self::Just(val) => *val` at :117, so Just(false) returns false unconditionally. APPLIED per match-site, NOT at bindings(): bindings_for_context UI listing `.filter(move |binding| binding.context_predicate.eval(&context))` (matcher.rs:285), push_keystroke `binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx)` (matcher.rs:327, inside the Trigger::Keystrokes arm at :325 with early-return :329-331), match_standard (matcher.rs:353), match_custom (matcher.rs:366 and original_trigger path :371). The binding stays inside bindings(); it is rejected separately at each call. Just(false) = never matches in any context. Semantics = UNAVAILABLE in this context.

3) Trigger::Empty — IMPLICIT no-match fall-through and the EXISTING unbind path. Trigger::Empty keymap.rs:48 "empty trigger (cannot actually be matched)"; is_empty :52-53; default trigger of every freshly registered EditableBinding (keymap.rs:660). No consumer has a match arm for it: push_keystroke matches only Trigger::Keystrokes (matcher.rs:325), match_standard only Trigger::Standard (:352), match_custom only Trigger::Custom (:365/:370). An Empty-trigger binding falls through all three loops — present in bindings(), never matched. Unbinding TODAY = set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) (matcher.rs:137 → keymap.rs:422 update_custom_trigger, which iterates both editable_custom_action_bindings :430-ish and editable_bindings :430). Two callers: (a) app/src/keyboard.rs:44 — on-disk Removed sentinel → UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed → set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty); (b) app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:592 — UI remove_keystroke → set_custom_trigger(..., Trigger::Empty) plus write_custom_keybinding(..., Removed) at :600. The override resolves in as_lens (keymap.rs:742-747): custom_trigger=Some(Empty) makes the resolved trigger Empty while original_trigger holds the default. Semantics = USER-UNBOUND.

UNIFICATION STATUS (the crux): No single is_active(binding, ctx) and no classify_state() for bindings exists. Grep for is_active/classify/is_unbound across crates/warpui_core/src returns only windowing/fullscreen hits (is_active_window_fullscreen, app_is_active) — nothing about binding suppression; the settings UI files have zero hits. The composite "is this key active here?" is implicitly is_enabled() (at bindings()) AND context_predicate.eval(ctx) (at each loop) AND trigger is a matchable non-Empty variant — computed at three different places, never named or centralized. A user-layer Unbound is NOT automatically a fourth mechanism: if it reuses Trigger::Empty it collapses into mechanism 3; if it is a distinct layer tombstone it becomes a genuine fourth needing explicit ordering.

MISREPORT RISK (grounded in code): the settings UI models a row as a single Option<Keystroke> (trigger_to_keystroke maps Trigger::Empty → None at app/src/util/bindings.rs:256) and decides affordances purely from `if self.binding.trigger.is_some()` (keybindings.rs:397), consulting neither enabled_predicate nor context_predicate. Because the UI list (keybindings.rs:754 ctx.editable_bindings()) applies is_enabled but NO context filter, an enabled_predicate=false binding is silently absent while a context_predicate=false binding still appears identical to an active one. So the UI today cannot distinguish "unavailable (feature/context)" from "user-unbound (Empty)" from "default-no-key (Empty)" — exactly the four-overlapping-states ambiguity the question warns about.

_Evidence:_
- enabled_predicate is a zero-arg context-free closure (fn()->bool), read by is_enabled on FixedBinding — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:237,626-627` _(code)_
- is_enabled also exists on EditableBindingLens with identical is_none_or(|p| p()) body — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:782-783` _(code)_
- enabled_predicate applied as whole-binding filter at the bindings() iterator source (editable, fixed, and both custom-action branches), excluding disabled bindings before any matcher loop — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:446,461,471,481` _(code)_
- context_predicate (incl. Just(false)) is NOT filtered at bindings(); evaluated per-call. push_keystroke eval is at line 327 (Trigger::Keystrokes arm at 325), match_standard 353, match_custom 366 and original_trigger 371 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:325,327,353,366,371` _(both)_
- bindings_for_context (UI listing) applies the context filter separately — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:285` _(code)_
- ContextPredicate::Just(bool) variant; Just(val)=>*val evaluates to false unconditionally for Just(false); editable default is Just(true) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:17,117; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:657` _(code)_
- Trigger::Empty is default editable trigger; cannot be matched; no match arm in any dispatch loop (each matches only its own variant) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:48,660; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:325,352,365` _(code)_
- Unbinding implemented as set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) on the on-disk Removed load path — `app/src/keyboard.rs:44` _(code)_
- set_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger updates custom_trigger across BOTH editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings; as_lens resolves custom_trigger=Some(Empty) to a resolved Empty trigger with original_trigger holding the default — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:137; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422,430,742-747` _(code)_
- UI remove_keystroke sets Trigger::Empty and persists UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:592,600` _(code)_
- No unified binding-state is_active()/classify_state()/is_unbound(); only unrelated windowing is_active_* hits exist — `grep is_active|classify|is_unbound over crates/warpui_core/src (only windowing/state.rs, platform hits) and app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs, app/src/util/bindings.rs (no hits)` _(code)_
- Settings UI collapses state to single Option<Keystroke> (Trigger::Empty -> None) and affordances key off trigger.is_some(), ignoring enabled/context predicates; UI list (editable_bindings) applies no context filter — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:256; app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:397,754` _(code)_
- CORRECTION: enabled_predicate feature-flag gating is ~93 with_enabled call sites in app/src/ (83 closure-form + ~10 fn-pointer-form), not ~30; used in both code and terminal editors — `grep -rn '\.with_enabled(' app/src (93 total; 83 match '\.with_enabled(||'); app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:605; app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:916,923` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc specify a single is_active(binding, ctx, layers) predicate plus a classify_state() that orders the (now four) off-switches, deciding explicitly that enabled_predicate=false and context_predicate=false render as 'unavailable' while Trigger::Empty/user-layer Unbound render as 'user-unbound', so the matcher and the settings badge cannot disagree.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Pure design decisions (not code-derivable): (1) whether the new user-layer Unbound should be a distinct tombstone/layer entry or reuse Trigger::Empty — reuse keeps it mechanism 3, a tombstone makes it a genuine fourth needing explicit ordering; (2) canonical precedence among the four when more than one fires (e.g. enabled_predicate=false vs user-Unbound on the same binding); (3) how the settings state badge should render 'unavailable' (enabled/context predicate false) distinctly from 'user-unbound' vs 'default-no-key', which today all collapse to Option<Keystroke>::None or absence. Also not exhaustively classified: of the 93 with_enabled sites, the exact split between FeatureFlag-gated vs ContextFlag-gated vs debug-gated vs settings-view enable/disable pairs (sampled, not fully enumerated) — but all are binding-level enabled_predicate setters, which is the load-bearing point.

Verified verbatim: Trigger enum/Empty (keymap.rs:43-55), EnabledPredicate type + doc (231-237), FixedBinding is_enabled (626-628) and EditableBindingLens is_enabled (782-784), bindings()/editable_bindings() filters (441-484), EditableBinding::new defaults context Just(true)/enabled None/trigger Empty (653-664), with_enabled at 605 and 674, as_lens custom_trigger resolution (741-759), update_custom_trigger (422-430), ContextPredicate enum+eval (context.rs:9-120), matcher.rs push_keystroke/match_standard/match_custom/bindings_for_context (280-374), app/src/keyboard.rs:44, settings_view/keybindings.rs:397/592/600/754, util/bindings.rs:256.
