### A3-Q15 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** When a first-class Unbound replaces the overloaded Trigger::Empty, does the on-disk 'none' sentinel (keyboard.rs:15,44 to UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed) keep meaning 'unbind' and migrate silently vs via a schema bump — and since fixed bindings have no name, how does the file express disabling a nameless default key, especially given platform is baked at parse (keymap.rs:914-919) so a key-scoped disable is platform-specific?

**A.** The on-disk 'none' sentinel is a raw string keyed by editable-binding NAME, so it CAN keep meaning 'unbind' for free — only the in-memory mapping at keyboard.rs:44 would flip from Trigger::Empty to a hypothetical Unbound. But there is NO schema/version field in the file, so any reinterpretation is necessarily silent. Because the file is name-keyed and FixedBinding has no name field, the format physically cannot express disabling a nameless fixed default; a key-scoped disable would be a NEW concept that, given platform is baked at parse and normalized() emits resolved cmd-/ctrl-, would store a non-portable per-OS keystroke. Independently re-verified line-by-line — confirmed.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I opened every cited file (keyboard.rs, keymap.rs, matcher.rs, settings_view/keybindings.rs, editor/view/mod.rs, code/editor/view/actions.rs) plus locations the answer did not cite (Trigger enum keymap.rs:43-49, BindingId keymap.rs:264-272, grep for version/schema and BindingId in keyboard.rs) and re-ran all greps. Every evidence claim holds.\n\n(A) 'none' is name-scoped and token-stable. CustomKeybindings is HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> (keyboard.rs:167-169), keyed by editable-binding name (doc :155-166). REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION = \"none\" (keyboard.rs:15). From writes \"none\" for Removed (:182-184); TryFrom returns Removed iff string == \"none\" (:192-193). Load maps Removed to set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty) (:43-44). The Trigger enum (keymap.rs:43-49) currently has only Keystrokes/Standard/Custom/Empty — no Unbound — so the answer correctly frames Unbound as HYPOTHETICAL: replacing Empty with Unbound touches only the RHS at keyboard.rs:44, not the file token. So 'none' keeps meaning unbind with zero file changes. CONFIRMED.\n\n(B) Migration is silent by construction. grep for version/schema in keyboard.rs returns only two doc-comment hits about the 'normalized version' of keystrokes — no schema/version field exists. No versioning machinery gates a migration. CONFIRMED.\n\n(C) Fixed bindings have no name, so the name-keyed file cannot disable them. FixedBinding (keymap.rs:277-286) has fields trigger/action/command_description/context_predicate/enabled_predicate/group/id — NO name; only EditableBinding has name: &'static str (:294). Fixed bindings are identified by process-local BindingId (AtomicUsize, :264-272), never persisted (grep confirms no BindingId reference in keyboard.rs). FixedBinding::new's first arg is a KEYSTROKE str parsed by Keystroke::parse (:511-533, parse at :522). Settings UI lists editable bindings only — ctx.editable_bindings().collect() (keybindings.rs:754) — and unbinds by name: set_custom_trigger(row.binding.name, Trigger::Empty) (:592) plus write_custom_keybinding(name, Removed) (:600). Fixed defaults are neither shown nor disableable today. CONFIRMED.\n\n(D) Platform baked at parse → key-scoped disable is platform-specific. Keystroke::parse resolves cmdorctrl to cmd on Mac / ctrl elsewhere (keymap.rs:914-919); this runs from FixedBinding::new (:522) and the file loader (keyboard.rs:199). Persisted form is normalized() (keyboard.rs:180), which emits ctrl-/alt-/shift-/cmd-/meta- and NEVER re-emits cmdorctrl (keymap.rs:970-993). new_per_platform bakes platform at registration (:502-506; editor/view/mod.rs:212 is a concrete new_per_platform with mac:'cmd-enter'/linux_and_windows:'ctrl-shift-enter'). So a key-scoped disable storing a resolved keystroke is non-portable. CONFIRMED.\n\n(E) Reload distinguishes explicit unbind from untouched default — for NAMED editable bindings. load_custom_keybindings calls set_custom_trigger only for names present in the file (keyboard.rs:38-48); absent names keep their default. The matcher scans only Trigger::Keystrokes (matcher.rs:325, early-return :329-331), so an Empty/Unbound editable binding never matches. The answer correctly scopes this to named bindings. CONFIRMED.\n\nCOUNTS independently re-run: FixedBinding::new = 397; register_fixed_bindings = 150; register_editable_bindings = 123 — exact match. Per-file concentration: editor/view/mod.rs=49, terminal/view/init.rs=38, code/editor/view/actions.rs=38, notebooks/editor/view.rs=35 — exact match. editor/view/mod.rs:281 is verbatim FixedBinding::new(\"up\", EditorAction::Up, id!(\"EditorView\") & !id!(\"IMEOpen\")); code/editor/view/actions.rs:47 is id!(\"CodeEditorView\") & !id!(\"IMEOpen\"). All four heaviest files are user-facing editors. CONFIRMED.\n\nOnly cosmetic nit: the prose renders contexts as id!(EditorView)/id!(CodeEditorView) without the string-literal quotes the code uses (id!(\"EditorView\")); immaterial to every conclusion.

_Evidence:_
- On-disk unbind sentinel is the literal 'none', keyed by editable-binding name; CustomKeybindings is a bare HashMap with no version field — `app/src/keyboard.rs:15,167-169` _(code)_
- 'none' round-trips to UserDefinedKeybinding::Removed via raw string compare (From writes 'none', TryFrom returns Removed iff =='none'); token can stay unchanged across a hypothetical Unbound migration — `app/src/keyboard.rs:182-184,192-193` _(code)_
- Load maps Removed to set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Empty); replacing Empty with Unbound changes only this in-memory RHS, not the file token. Trigger enum currently has no Unbound (Keystrokes/Standard/Custom/Empty) — `app/src/keyboard.rs:43-44; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:43-49` _(code)_
- No version/schema field in keyboard.rs (only doc-comment 'normalized version' hits); no BindingId reference, confirming BindingId is never persisted — `app/src/keyboard.rs (grep version|schema|BindingId)` _(code)_
- FixedBinding struct has no name field (fields: trigger/action/command_description/context_predicate/enabled_predicate/group/id BindingId); EditableBinding has name: &'static str — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:277-286,293-304` _(both)_
- BindingId is a process-local auto-increment AtomicUsize, never written to disk — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:264-272` _(code)_
- FixedBinding::new first arg is a keystroke string parsed via Keystroke::parse, not a name — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:511-533 (parse at :522)` _(code)_
- Platform baked at parse: cmdorctrl resolves to cmd on Mac / ctrl elsewhere at Keystroke::parse — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:914-919` _(code)_
- Persisted form is normalized(), which emits resolved ctrl-/alt-/shift-/cmd-/meta- and never re-emits cmdorctrl; loader also parses via Keystroke::parse — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:970-993; app/src/keyboard.rs:180,199` _(code)_
- new_per_platform bakes platform at registration (mac vs linux_and_windows); concrete site is a new_per_platform mac:'cmd-enter'/linux_and_windows:'ctrl-shift-enter' — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:502-506; app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:212-215` _(code)_
- Settings UI lists editable bindings only (editable_bindings().collect()) and unbinds by name (set_custom_trigger + write_custom_keybinding Removed); fixed defaults are neither shown nor disableable — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:592,600,754` _(code)_
- Reload distinguishes explicit unbind (present 'none') from untouched default (absent name): set_custom_trigger only called for names in file — `app/src/keyboard.rs:38-48` _(code)_
- Matcher scans only Trigger::Keystrokes (early-return at :329-331), so an Empty/Unbound editable binding never matches — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-331` _(code)_
- 397 FixedBinding::new sites; 150 register_fixed_bindings; 123 register_editable_bindings. Heaviest under user-facing editors: editor/view/mod.rs=49, terminal/view/init.rs=38, code/editor/view/actions.rs=38 (under id!("CodeEditorView") at :47), notebooks=35 — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:281; app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:47` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design fix the back-compat/migration story for unbind: keep 'none' as a name-scoped unbind that maps to a first-class Unbound with no file change and no schema bump (silent, safe for existing files), and treat disabling nameless fixed defaults as a separate NEW schema feature — explicitly acknowledging it needs either a non-portable per-OS keystroke key or a platform-neutral default identity (R5), and that today's settings UI/file cannot even address fixed bindings.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Pure design decisions not settleable from code: (1) keep 'none' as name-scoped unbind for back-compat vs reinterpret it (code shows the token can be preserved for free, but with no versioning any change is silent); (2) whether a key-scoped disable for nameless fixed defaults stores a resolved per-OS keystroke (non-portable, per D) or a platform-neutral default identity (which needs a platform-in-data-model change, R5); (3) whether to add a schema-version field at all (none today). Also, an exact classification of how many of the 397 FixedBinding sites sit under user-facing contexts would require evaluating every ContextPredicate; I confirmed the top ~160 (the four editor files) are user-facing keystroke defaults but did not exhaustively classify all 397 — note actions.rs uses both text_entry (CodeEditorView) and a narrower editable_state predicate, so not all 38 are under the identical context, though all are user-facing editor keys.
- _Verification:_ I attacked every line ref and count rather than trusting them. (1) Re-ran all three greps myself: 397/150/123 — exact match to the answer. (2) Re-ran per-file concentration: 49/38/38/35 for the four editor files — exact match. (3) Opened keyboard.rs fully: :15, :167-169, :180-184, :192-193, :43-48, :199 all say exactly what is claimed. (4) Opened keymap.rs: FixedBinding (:277-286) genuinely has no name field; EditableBinding (:294) has name; new (:511-533) takes a keystroke str; cmdorctrl resolution (:914-919) and normalized() (:970-993) are verbatim as described; new_per_platform (:502-506) confirmed. (5) Verified the Trigger enum (:43-49) has NO Unbound today — the answer correctly frames Unbound as a hypothetical replacement, so it is not a false 'this exists' claim. (6) Verified matcher.rs:325 scans only Trigger::Keystrokes with early-return at :329-331. (7) settings keybindings.rs:592/600/754 confirmed (editable-only, name-scoped unbind). (8) Checked the two locations the answer did NOT explicitly cite for these sub-claims: grep for version/schema in keyboard.rs (none, only doc comments) and grep for BindingId in keyboard.rs (none) — both support claims (B) and the 'BindingId never persisted' point. ### A3-Q16 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** What is the golden-test harness for the new resolver, given matcher_tests.rs (251 lines) has exactly three tests (test_matcher, test_editable_binding_matching, test_bindings_for_context) and ZERO coverage of shadowing, chord-prefix, precedence ordering, or unbind?

**A.** There is no golden/table-driven/snapshot harness today. matcher_tests.rs is exactly 251 lines with exactly 3 imperative tests built on a test_keystroke(&str,view,&ctx)->Option<Action> helper, and a keyword scan confirms zero coverage of shadowing, chord-prefix conflict, editable-vs-fixed precedence, or unbind. There isn't even an Unbound outcome to assert against: MatchResult has only None|Pending|Action. The harness must be built from scratch; the two verified bugs are currently unpinned.

CODE FACT (decisive, independently verified): No resolver golden-test harness exists. crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs is exactly 251 lines (wc -l) and contains exactly three #[test] fns — test_matcher (:5), test_editable_binding_matching (:89), test_bindings_for_context (:179) — plus one non-test helper fn test_keystroke (impl Matcher, :230-241, no #[test] attr) that returns Option<Arc<dyn Action>> and collapses both MatchResult::None and MatchResult::Pending to None (the `_ => None` arm at :238). A grep of that file for shadow|precedence|unbind|unbound|disable|reset_to_default|custom_trigger returns NOTHING (exit 1).

WHY THE CONFLICT CASES ARE UNCOVERED:
1) short-shadows-chord (matcher.rs:329-331 early return): In test_matcher and test_editable_binding_matching the chord "a b" is only resolved in ctx_b, where the short "a" binding's context predicate id!("a") is FALSE (ctx_b holds only "b"), so the short binding is never an eligible competitor at the instant the chord fires — the chord's own predicate id!("a")|id!("b") is what evaluates true. The same-context case where a complete short binding shadows a prefix-sharing chord is never asserted.
2) editable-always-beats-fixed (keymap.rs:454-464): bindings() yields editable bindings first (LIFO via editable_bindings() at :441-447), then fixed in reverse, and push_keystroke (matcher.rs:324-338) linear-scans and returns the FIRST exact match (:329-331) — so an editable beats a same-key fixed. But test_matcher is fixed-only (FixedBinding::new, :13-17) and test_editable_binding_matching is editable-only (register_editable_bindings, :99-109); neither ever puts an editable and a fixed binding on the same trigger+context, so this precedence is never exercised through the resolver.
3) Unbind/Unbound: the concept does not exist in code. MatchResult (matcher.rs:49-53) is exactly {None, Pending, Action}; grep unbound|unbind across the entire keymap module is empty (exit 1). A broad warpui_core scan only surfaces unrelated UI DropShadow / layout-"unbounded" tokens. The "new Unbound outcome" the question proposes is genuinely new — there is no fixture, no variant, and no resolver path for it.

THE THREE RESOLVER PATHS to be covered (matcher.rs:324-346): exact-match -> Action (:329-331); longer-candidate present -> retain_pending -> Pending (:341); no candidate -> None (:344). The 3 existing tests are imperative keystroke sequences (basic match, multi-keystroke, failed-match isolation, context-change clearing, per-view pending) — they touch these paths only in non-conflicting contexts.

PARTIAL COVERAGE EXISTS, but only at the LIST level in a different file (keymap_tests.rs), never through the resolver: test_keymap_bindings_list (:123-238) pins bindings() iteration order (editable LIFO then fixed reverse: Idle,Close,Open,Paste,Copy) but with NO shared triggers (cmd-o/cmd-w/cmd-c/cmd-v/Empty, all distinct) and via map.bindings() not push_keystroke; test_disabled_bindings (:344-403) tests enabled-filter via with_enabled()/map.bindings() (enable/disable, NOT user-unbind); test_custom_triggers (:253) tests editable trigger override via update_custom_trigger. NONE of the keymap_tests.rs tests call push_keystroke. So even the broader suite has no shadow / chord-prefix / same-key precedence / Unbound coverage.

PRIMITIVES AVAILABLE TO BUILD THE TABLE-DRIVEN HARNESS: Matcher::test_keystroke (matcher_tests.rs:230-241) gives string-keystroke -> Option<Action>; the id!()/context macros (keymap::macros) build active contexts; Keymap::new(vec![FixedBinding...]) and register_editable_bindings seed mixed layers; get_bindings() (matcher.rs:303) and bindings_for_context (used in test_bindings_for_context, :200-226) enumerate winners for the A3-Q17 startup snapshot; the #[cfg(debug_assertions)] validate_bindings scaffolding (matcher.rs:181-203) is also present. A new suite would extend test_keystroke to also surface Pending/None (and a future Unbound) so (layers, context, keystroke-seq) -> expected MatchResult can be table-asserted, seeded with the two verified-bug fixtures.

SCOPE NOTE: The "what should the harness be" is a design choice (table schema, fixture set, snapshot vs unit) — see residual_unknowns. The code-verifiable answer is that no such harness exists yet and exactly which gaps/primitives are present.

_Evidence:_
- matcher_tests.rs is exactly 251 lines with exactly 3 #[test] fns (test_matcher, test_editable_binding_matching, test_bindings_for_context) plus a non-test test_keystroke helper that collapses Pending+None to None — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:1-251 (tests at :5,:89,:179; helper at :230-241, `_ => None` at :238)` _(code)_
- Keyword scan of matcher_tests.rs for shadow|precedence|unbind|unbound|disable|reset_to_default|custom_trigger returns NONE (grep exit 1) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs (grep -niE)` _(code)_
- No Unbound outcome exists: MatchResult has exactly None|Pending|Action, and grep unbound|unbind across the keymap module is empty (exit 1) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:49-53` _(both)_
- push_keystroke early-returns on the first exact match (len-equal :329-331) and only retains Pending for longer candidates (:332-335 -> :341); no-candidate -> None (:344); this is the short-shadows-chord path — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:324-346` _(code)_
- bindings() yields editable bindings first (LIFO via editable_bindings() :441-447) then fixed in reverse, so editable always beats a same-key fixed via the first-match early return — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:441-447,454-464` _(both)_
- In the two resolver tests the chord 'a b' is only resolved in ctx_b where the short 'a' binding's predicate id!("a") is false (ctx_b holds only "b"), so the same-context shadow conflict is never asserted — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:13-17,38-46,99-109,130-138` _(code)_

### A3-Q17 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** Beyond synthetic unit fixtures (Q12), is there a full-keymap resolution snapshot — captured at startup over the real ~265 registration sites as (context, keystroke)→action — that can be diffed before/after the refactor to prove no real binding changed winner unintentionally?

**A.** No. There is no full-keymap resolution snapshot anywhere in the codebase — nothing captures a (context, keystroke)→winning-action table at startup that could be diffed before/after the refactor. The only startup-time scaffolding over the real bindings is the debug-only validate_bindings() pass, which runs PER-BINDING yes/no validity predicates and panics on failure. (Correction to the original answer: it is NOT "just is_binding_cross_platform" — validate_bindings also runs context-registered is_binding_pty_compliant validators for RootView, PaneGroup, TerminalView and Workspace via register_binding_validator. Both kinds are validity predicates, not winner-resolution, so the conclusion stands and is reinforced.) It never resolves a winner per (context, keystroke) and emits no diffable table. The building blocks (get_bindings(), bindings_for_context(), the startup validate_bindings hook) exist; the snapshot harness itself does not.

CODE FACT — there is no characterization/golden snapshot of full-keymap resolution. Verdict: corrected (central conclusion fully confirmed; one supporting detail about the validator inventory fixed, plus a minor file-count nuance).

1) No snapshot infrastructure or test exists. Grepping snapshot/golden/characterization across app/ and crates/ yields nothing tied to keybinding resolution. The lone 'snapshot' hit (app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:1047 "Write current codebase index snapshot") is an unrelated codebase-index action description. `insta` is NOT a dependency — the only matches are the time crate `instant` (e.g. app/Cargo.toml:135) and `app-installation-detection`; the original answer correctly distinguished these. matcher_tests.rs is 251 lines with exactly three tests (test_matcher:5, test_editable_binding_matching:89, test_bindings_for_context:179) plus a test_keystroke helper (:230) — none enumerate contexts or assert a (context,keystroke)→action table.

2) The closest "over all real bindings at startup" mechanism is validate_bindings(), and it is NOT a resolution snapshot:
   - crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:181-203 (validate_bindings, #[cfg(debug_assertions)]) iterates bindings_for_context(context) for EACH registered (context, validator) pair (matcher.rs:184) and get_bindings() for the default validator, pushing any binding for which the validator returns IsBindingValid::No and panicking if any fail.
   - It is wired to run once after all registrations load: crates/warpui_core/src/platform/app.rs:85 calls ctx.validate_bindings() right after init; the App wrapper is crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1609-1611 (debug) / :1615-1616 (release no-op).
   - CORRECTION to the original answer's "today just is_binding_cross_platform": there are actually TWO families of registered validators. (a) The single DEFAULT validator is is_binding_cross_platform (app/src/lib.rs:1395 → app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933), asserting non-mac bindings don't use `cmd`. (b) FOUR context-scoped validators registered via register_binding_validator with is_binding_pty_compliant: app/src/root_view.rs:262 (RootView), app/src/pane_group/mod.rs:318 (PaneGroup), app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:83 (TerminalView), app/src/workspace/mod.rs:82 (Workspace). is_binding_pty_compliant (bindings.rs ~:910-913) is also a per-binding validity predicate (PTY-compliance of keystroke/action). Neither family picks a first-match per keystroke, enumerates the keystroke space, nor produces a stable serializable output to diff. So the additional validators do NOT change the conclusion — they reinforce it (all startup scaffolding is per-binding validity, none is resolution).

3) The resolution machinery a snapshot would have to capture (and which the refactor touches):
   - Winner ordering: keymap.rs:454-464 bindings() = editable bindings first (reverse-registration LIFO via editable_bindings() at :441-447, which calls .iter().rev()) then fixed bindings (.iter().rev() at :458-462). "Winner" = the FIRST match in this iteration.
   - Consumers that depend on first-match: push_keystroke (matcher.rs:307-346; early exact-match return at :329-331), match_standard (:350-358), match_custom (:363-369), bindings_for_context (:282-286), get_bindings (:303-305). A (layer, recency) reorder changes the head of this iterator, so any winner-flip is exactly what a snapshot would catch — and nothing asserts it today.

4) Scale of what's uncovered (counts re-run and confirmed): 266 register_* calls (146 register_fixed_bindings + 120 register_editable_bindings) plus 397 FixedBinding::new objects feed bindings(). 242 of 266 register_* calls live under app/ (fixed app/=132 crates/=14; editable app/=110 crates/=10), with 98 distinct files under app/. (Minor count nuance: the original answer's "108 files / 98 of 108 under app/" is slightly soft — re-running gives 98 app/ files and ~10-12 crates/ files depending on whether the wrapper/definition files in core/app.rs, matcher.rs and keymap.rs are counted; total ~110, vs the doc's stated "~265 sites / 109 files." Immaterial to the conclusion.) Because app/ holds the bulk of registrations and the knowledge graph is BLIND to app/, any snapshot built only from crates/warpui_core or from the graph would miss most production bindings; a real snapshot must boot/assemble the full Matcher after app-level registration.

5) Feasibility (for the design doc): a snapshot harness is buildable from existing parts but is net-new work. bindings_for_context(Context) (matcher.rs:282) returns bindings in precedence order filtered by a concrete Context, so per representative context you can take the first binding per distinct trigger to get the winner, then serialize a sorted (context, trigger)→action table and diff master vs refactor. Two gaps confirm it doesn't exist yet: (a) no enumeration of representative Contexts (predicates evaluate against concrete Context values that must be hand-constructed); (b) no keystroke-space enumeration nor stable Action serialization, so "winner per keystroke" reduces to "winner per registered trigger." The validate_bindings startup hook (matcher.rs:181-203 / platform/app.rs:85) is the natural anchor for such a dump.

Bottom line: the regression net the question asks about is absent; only per-binding validity checks exist (cross-platform + PTY-compliance). Whether/how to build the snapshot is a design choice.

_Evidence:_
- No keymap resolution snapshot/golden/characterization test or snapshot infra exists; the one 'snapshot' hit is an unrelated codebase-index action label; insta is not a dependency (only the time crate 'instant' and 'app-installation-detection' match). — `grep -rniE 'snapshot|golden|characterization' app/ crates/ → only app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:1047 (codebase index); insta absent from all Cargo.toml; app/Cargo.toml:135 instant` _(code)_
- matcher_tests.rs is 251 lines with exactly three tests (no context/keystroke table, no snapshot) plus a test_keystroke helper. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher_tests.rs:5,89,179,230 (251 lines)` _(code)_
- validate_bindings is a debug-only per-binding yes/no validity pass: it loops (context,validator) pairs over bindings_for_context and the default validator over get_bindings, panicking on failures — not a winner-resolution snapshot. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:181-203 (loop at :184-190)` _(code)_
- validate_bindings runs once at startup after all registrations are loaded (the natural snapshot hook point); App wrapper is debug-only with a release no-op. — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/app.rs:85; crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1609-1611,1615-1616` _(code)_
- CORRECTION: validate_bindings runs MORE than one validator. The only DEFAULT validator is is_binding_cross_platform (non-mac cmd check), but four context-scoped is_binding_pty_compliant validators are also registered. Both are per-binding validity predicates, not resolution comparisons. — `default: app/src/lib.rs:1395, app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933; context validators: app/src/root_view.rs:262, app/src/pane_group/mod.rs:318, app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:83, app/src/workspace/mod.rs:82 (is_binding_pty_compliant, bindings.rs:910-913)` _(code)_
- Winner ordering (what a snapshot must pin) = editable LIFO (.iter().rev()) then fixed reverse-registration (.iter().rev()), first-match wins; this is exactly what the refactor reorders. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:441-447,454-464` _(both)_
- Resolution consumers depend on first-match in bindings(): push_keystroke early-return at :329-331, match_standard, match_custom. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:307-346 (esp. 329-331),350-358,363-369` _(code)_
- bindings_for_context returns precedence-ordered bindings for a concrete Context (reusable to compute a per-context winner table); get_bindings is the unfiltered full ordering. — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:282-286,303-305` _(code)_
- 266 registration calls (146 register_fixed_bindings + 120 register_editable_bindings) feed bindings(); 397 FixedBinding::new objects — consistent with the doc's ~265 sites. — `grep -rn over app/ crates/ (excluding defs): fixed=146, editable=120, FixedBinding::new=397` _(code)_
- 242 of 266 register_* calls live under app/ (fixed app/=132 crates/=14; editable app/=110 crates/=10), across 98 app/ files; the graph is blind to app/, so a graph-only or crates-only snapshot would miss most production bindings. (File-count total ~110, vs the answer's stated 108 and the doc's 109 — immaterial to the conclusion.) — `grep -rn / grep -rln breakdown over app/ and crates/` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Confirms Axis-3's regression-net design must include building a net-new characterization snapshot harness (anchored on the startup validate_bindings hook, reusing bindings_for_context over hand-built representative contexts) rather than relying on anything that exists today — and that it must boot the full app-level keymap (242/266 sites live in app/, invisible to crates-only tests and the knowledge graph) to actually catch winner-flips from the fixed-vs-editable-LIFO → (layer, recency) reorder.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Whether to build the snapshot and its shape remain design/product decisions, not code facts: (a) which representative Contexts to enumerate (CodeEditorView, terminal EditorView, each Vim/modal mode) — no canonical context list exists; (b) how to reduce (context,keystroke)→action to a stable diffable key (Actions are Arc<dyn Action> with no guaranteed stable identity/serialization today — would need a name/debug projection); (c) per-trigger winners via bindings_for_context vs a true keystroke-space walk including chord/prefix and push_keystroke early-return semantics (matcher.rs:329-331); (d) whether to gate it behind the existing #[cfg(debug_assertions)] validate_bindings hook or a dedicated test binary. Not decidable from code alone. Also unverified at runtime: whether all 4 is_binding_pty_compliant registrations and the default validator are always active in a given build/feature-flag configuration (read statically, not executed).
- _Verification:_ Independently opened every cited file:line. CONFIRMED exact: matcher.rs:181-203 (validate_bindings), :282-286 (bindings_for_context), :303-305 (get_bindings), :307-346 with early-return :329-331 (push_keystroke), :350-358 (match_standard), :363-369 (match_custom); keymap.rs:441-447 (editable .iter().rev() LIFO) and :454-464 (bindings() = editable then fixed .iter().rev()); platform/app.rs:85 (validate_bindings call); core/app.rs:1609-1611/1615-1616 (debug/release wrapper); lib.rs:1395 (set_default_binding_validator(is_binding_cross_platform)); bindings.rs:918-933 (is_binding_cross_platform). matcher_tests.rs = 251 lines, three tests at 5/89/179 + test_keystroke at 230 — confirmed. Re-ran all grep counts myself: 146 fixed + 120 editable = 266 calls, 397 FixedBinding::new, app/=242 (132+110), crates/=24 (14+10), 98 app/ files — all match. ONE FIX (reason for 'corrected' not 'confirmed'): I checked a location the answer did not cite — grep for register_binding_validator — and found FOUR additional context-scoped validators (is_binding_pty_compliant for RootView/PaneGroup/TerminalView/Workspace at root_view.rs:262, pane_group/mod.rs:318, terminal/view/init.rs:83, workspace/mod.rs:82). So the short_answer's 'today just is_binding_cross_platform' understates the validator inventory; validate_bindings runs is_binding_cross_platform (default) PLUS is_binding_pty_compliant (4 contexts). This does NOT weaken the answer: both are per-binding validity predicates, not winner-resolution, so the central claim (no full-keymap resolution snapshot exists) is fully confirmed and reinforced. ## Axis 4 — Modality as a first-class, pluggable choice

### A4-Q4 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Entity carries one associated type Event (entity.rs:39-40); VimModel Event is VimEvent (vim.rs:1837), subscribe_to_model is typed on that event (model/context.rs:67). Should all engines share Entity Event = EditorCommand so ONE subscription + ONE blanket ModalEditExecutor impl (replacing vim.rs:1849) serves every mode -- and in that one executor, can Helix's new ExtendSelection(Motion) REUSE the 11-way vim navigate fan-out (vim.rs:1865-1883) or need a parallel fan-out? Or keep per-engine event types requiring N subscriptions in a ModalEngineModel enum?

**A.** Verified against code. The framework mechanically supports a shared-Event design: Entity::Event is a free associated type (entity.rs:39-40) and subscribe_to_model downcasts to <S as Entity>::Event monomorphically (context.rs:67-86), so one Entity emitting EditorCommand = exactly one subscription + one blanket ModalEditExecutor impl, generalizing today's single blanket `impl<T> VimSubscriber for T where T: VimHandler` (vim.rs:1849-1942). A per-engine-Entity enum does NOT collapse N: each variant is a distinct Entity with its own Event type, each needing its own subscribe_to_model. Helix ExtendSelection(Motion) CAN reuse the existing motion dispatch instead of a duplicated 11-way fan-out, because the model primitives already carry a keep_selection:bool extend/collapse flag (model.rs:2315-2321, tail logic 2410-2413) and the operator path already drives motions with extend semantics (vim_handler.rs:223-231). The only real cost is threading a keep_selection/extend parameter through the navigate trait methods (which today hardcode false). None of ModalEditExecutor/EditorCommand/ModalEngine/ExtendSelection exist yet (grep returns 0) -- all are design proposals.

I independently opened every cited file plus one location the answer flagged as unverified (the terminal navigate path). Every evidence claim holds at the cited line ranges.

CODE FACTS (re-verified):
1. Entity has exactly one associated type: `pub trait Entity: 'static { type Event; }` (entity.rs:39-40). No second event slot. CONFIRMED.
2. subscribe_to_model<S: Entity, F> binds the callback to S::Event and at dispatch downcasts the payload to `<S as Entity>::Event` (context.rs:67-86, downcast at 79-81). One call = one subscription bound to one concrete Event type; N distinct Entity/Event types => N subscriptions. CONFIRMED.
3. VimModel fixes Event=VimEvent: `impl Entity for VimModel { type Event = VimEvent; }` (vim.rs:1836-1838). VimEvent struct at vim.rs:524, VimEventType enum at vim.rs:540. CONFIRMED.
4. A single blanket dispatch impl already exists today: `impl<T> VimSubscriber for T where T: VimHandler` (vim.rs:1849-1942) is one big match over VimEventType. This "one subscription + one blanket impl per view" is the CURRENT shape, just nailed to VimEvent/VimHandler. CONFIRMED.
5. Exactly TWO concrete VimHandler impls: CodeEditorView (vim_handler.rs:23) and terminal EditorView (mod.rs:1959). Grep `impl VimHandler for` returns exactly these two. CONFIRMED.
6. The navigate fan-out (vim.rs:1865-1883) matches all 11 VimMotion variants and dispatches to navigate_char/word/line/... trait methods. VimMotion enum (vim.rs:302-319) has exactly 11 variants: Character, Word, Line, FirstNonWhitespace, FindChar, JumpToFirstLine, JumpToLastLine, JumpToLine, JumpToMatchingBracket, JumpToUnmatchedBracket, Paragraph. CONFIRMED.
7. NONE of the proposed types exist: grep -rE 'ExtendSelection|extend_selection_by_motion|ModalEditExecutor|ModalEngine|EditorCommand' over crates/ and app/ returns 0 hits. CONFIRMED.

SHARED-EVENT vs PER-ENGINE (code-verifiable portion):
- A shared Event is mechanically available; nothing constrains Entity::Event. A single Entity (e.g. a ModalEngineModel emitting EditorCommand) sets type Event = EditorCommand, is subscribed once, and dispatched by one blanket ModalEditExecutor impl -- a direct generalization of vim.rs:1849.
- The adversarial angle is sound: an enum of distinct per-engine Entities does NOT reduce N, because each variant is its own Entity with its own Event associated type needing its own subscribe_to_model. Only a single Entity with a shared Event collapses to one subscription.

EXTENDSELECTION REUSE OF THE FAN-OUT (strongly supported):
- The extend-vs-collapse switch already exists one layer below the fan-out: vim_move_horizontal_by_offset(.., keep_selection, ..) (model.rs:2315-2321) builds `SelectionOffsets { head, tail: if keep_selection { selection.tail } else { head } }` (model.rs:2410-2413). keep_selection=false = pure move; keep_selection=true = extend -- exactly Helix's "every motion extends".
- navigate_* impls hardcode keep_selection=false (vim_handler.rs:40,49). The VimHandler trait navigate methods (vim.rs:1949+) take (count, motion, ctx) with NO keep_selection param, so reuse requires adding that bool to the fan-out's trait-method signatures (or having the executor pass it). The model-layer selection algorithm itself is already shared.
- The operator-pending path already drives motions with extend semantics: vim_handler.rs:223-231 calls `model.vim_find_char(true /* keep_selection */, ..)`. There is a second, parallel motion fan-out (operator-select) calling distinct vim_select_for_char_motion (model.rs:2601), vim_select_for_word_motion (2665), vim_select_for_line_motion (2744), vim_find_char (3102). ExtendSelection slots into one of the two existing dispatch tables (most naturally navigate with keep_selection=true), not a brand-new third table.

VIEW WIRING: each view does one add_model + one subscribe_to_model for the vim model (view.rs:359-360; mod.rs:3015,3017). CONFIRMED.

INDEPENDENT CHECK BEYOND THE CITED LINES (terminal path, which the answer flagged as not line-verified): terminal EditorView::navigate_char (mod.rs:2032) calls `move_cursors_by_offset(count, &Direction::Backward, /* keep_selection */ false, /* stop_at_line_boundary */ true, ctx)`. This is a DIFFERENT method name than the code editor's vim_move_horizontal_by_offset, but it carries the SAME keep_selection extend/collapse flag and hardcodes false in navigate. This corroborates (does not refute) the answer's "model primitives carry keep_selection" claim across both editors -- the flag concept is shared even though the two editors use distinct model methods.

NITPICK (non-material): detailed_answer point 3 cites VimEventType at "vim.rs:522-599"; actual lines are struct VimEvent at 524 and enum VimEventType at 540. The evidence array correctly uses 524-599. No bearing on any conclusion.

_Evidence:_
- Entity has exactly one associated type Event: `pub trait Entity: 'static { type Event; }` — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/entity.rs:39-40` _(both)_
- subscribe_to_model<S: Entity, F> binds callback to S::Event and downcasts payload to <S as Entity>::Event -- one subscription per (model, concrete Event type) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/model/context.rs:67-86 (downcast at 79-81)` _(code)_
- VimModel fixes Entity::Event = VimEvent; VimEvent struct at line 524, VimEventType enum at line 540 — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1836-1838 (and struct 524 / enum 540)` _(code)_
- A single blanket dispatch impl already exists: impl<T> VimSubscriber for T where T: VimHandler, one match over VimEventType — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1849-1942` _(both)_
- The navigate fan-out matches all 11 VimMotion variants and dispatches to navigate_* trait methods — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1865-1883` _(code)_
- VimMotion has exactly 11 variants (Character, Word, Line, FirstNonWhitespace, FindChar, JumpToFirstLine, JumpToLastLine, JumpToLine, JumpToMatchingBracket, JumpToUnmatchedBracket, Paragraph) — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:302-319` _(code)_
- Exactly two concrete VimHandler impls exist: CodeEditorView and terminal EditorView (grep `impl VimHandler for` returns exactly these two) — `app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs:23 and app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:1959` _(both)_
- Motion model primitive carries keep_selection:bool; tail collapses to head when false, stays when true -- the extend/collapse switch — `app/src/code/editor/model.rs:2315-2321,2410-2413` _(code)_
- navigate_char impl hardcodes keep_selection=false (pure move); VimHandler trait navigate methods take (count, motion, ctx) with no keep_selection param — `app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs:40,49 and crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1949-1973` _(code)_
- Operator path already drives motions with extend semantics: vim_find_char(true /* keep_selection */) — `app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs:223-231` _(code)_
- A second, parallel operator-select motion fan-out exists via distinct model methods vim_select_for_char/word/line_motion and vim_find_char — `app/src/code/editor/model.rs:2601,2665,2744,3102` _(code)_
- Each view wires exactly one add_model + one subscribe_to_model for the vim model — `app/src/code/editor/view.rs:359-360 and app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:3015,3017` _(code)_
- ModalEditExecutor, EditorCommand, ModalEngine, ExtendSelection, extend_selection_by_motion do not exist in code (grep over crates/ and app/ returns 0 hits) — `grep -rE 'ExtendSelection|extend_selection_by_motion|ModalEditExecutor|ModalEngine|EditorCommand' crates/ app/ => 0` _(code)_
- Terminal EditorView::navigate_char uses move_cursors_by_offset(..,/* keep_selection */ false,..) -- a different method name than code editor's vim_move_horizontal_by_offset but the same keep_selection flag (corroborates shared selection-switch concept across both editors) — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:2032-2071` _(code)_

### A4-Q5 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** The reusable buffer algorithms take vim-namespaced value enums (vim_inner_word(.., WordType), vim_find_matching_bracket(.., BracketChar), vim_word_iterator_from_offset(.., WordType, WordBound)) and vim_find_char is NOT generic (concrete &str, find_char.rs:28). **A.** CONFIRMED. Today and as prescribed by the report, the IR carries the vim enums DIRECTLY — the proposed EditorCommand is explicitly "a crate-level superset of VimEventType" (report line 210) and the buffer algorithms are "reused as-is" (report line 221). No neutral motion/operator type and no translation shim exists anywhere in code (grep for EditorCommand/ModalEditExecutor returns 0 hits in app/ and crates/); the executor unpacks the IR's vim enums straight into the algorithms (model.rs:2898-2933, verbatim pass-through). The enum VALUES are mode-neutral editing primitives that Helix's analogous commands map onto without new variants, but the NAMING/vocabulary is vim, so "mode-agnostic" is partly a naming fiction — exactly the caveat the report flags at line 191.

I independently opened every cited file and re-ran the greps. The answer is accurate on all line numbers, enum bodies, signatures, executor behavior, grep counts, and report citations. Nothing material is wrong.

Every line matches.

The report's Step B adds only ExtendSelection(Motion) and ApplyOperator(Operator) as the two NEW primitives and maps vim events 1:1 — i.e. Option A (carry vim enums directly), not neutral types + shims.

### A4-Q6 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Verified the editor ALREADY applies operators across all selections (update_selections_internal iterates selections.iter().zip(...).map(...); vim operators map over Vec1 of SelectionOffsets) -- so per-selection apply is NOT the Helix gap. Which specific Helix selection-management commands (s regex-split, S, x line-split, % whole-file, keep/collapse, rotate-primary) require NEW model primitives, and does the IR/ModalEditExecutor own them or do they stay app-level?

**A.** Confirmed: the per-selection APPLY path is already fully multi-selection (selection.rs:642 returns Vec1; update_selections_internal merges then maps over the Vec1 at selection.rs:683,694-697; vim operators map over selection_offsets() e.g. vim_handler.rs:237,364-365 and write back via vim_set_selections -> BufferSelectAction::SetSelectionOffsets), so per-selection apply is NOT the gap. The real gap is selection-set CREATION/MANAGEMENT: of the Helix commands only `%` (SelectAll) has a primitive; `s`/`S`/`x`/keep/remove/`;`/`,`/rotate-primary have ZERO primitives, and there is no "primary selection" pointer (model exposes only first/last of the Vec1). These new primitives must land in the crates/editor selection model (they need buffer bytes + the regex DFA, both unreachable from the IR/executor); the IR/ModalEditExecutor can only carry intent and dispatch.

VERIFIED by opening every cited file plus the trait-default sites the answer did not pin precisely.

PART 1 — Per-selection APPLY is genuinely already multi-selection (the draft "operators assume a single primary selection" framing is false):
- `Selection::selections()` returns `Vec1<SelectionOffsets>` at crates/editor/src/selection.rs:642 (confirmed; answer's :642 exact).
- `update_selections_internal` first calls `selection_model.merge_overlapping_selections()` (selection.rs:683), then `selections.iter().zip(goal_xs.iter()).map(|(selection, old_goal_x)| selection_update(...)).unzip()` at selection.rs:694-697 (answer said 693-697 — off by one on the start, immaterial). Movement/extension flows through it: extend_selection:729, move_selection:764, navigate_character:822, navigate_word:835, navigate_line:868, navigate_line_boundary:922 (answer's "navigate_* 800-922" — first navigate is actually 822, not 800; immaterial range slip).
- Vim operators map over the whole set: vim_handler.rs:237 `current_selections.mapped(|selection| ...)` (JumpToLine, full block 232-250 confirmed), and the text-object path `model.buffer_selection_model().as_ref(ctx).selection_offsets().mapped(|selection| { let start = head.min(tail); ... })` at vim_handler.rs:364-365 then `model.vim_set_selections(starts, ...)` at :372. Write path `vim_set_selections` issues `BufferSelectAction::SetSelectionOffsets { selections }` at app/src/code/editor/model.rs:587/595. `add_cursor` exists at selection.rs:499; `add_cursor_at` exists as a trait method at crates/editor/src/model.rs:633 (NOTE: the answer's bare "model.rs:633" resolves to crates/editor/src/model.rs, not app/src/code/editor/model.rs whose :633 is the unrelated nav_diff_up — minor citation ambiguity, the method genuinely exists). Net: operator application over N selections is reusable today.

PART 2 — Selection-set CREATION/MANAGEMENT primitives: the true missing layer.
- The complete write substrate is `BufferSelectAction` (crates/editor/src/content/buffer.rs:164-197): MoveLeft/Right, ExtendLeft/Right, SelectAll, SetLastHead, SetLastSelection, AddCursorAt, AddSelection, UpdateSelectionOffsets, SetSelectionOffsets, plus `merge_overlapping_selections` (selection_model.rs:156). All low-level "write raw offsets". No pattern-compute command, no primary pointer.
- Reproducible grep RE-RUN by me across crates/editor crates/vim crates/warpui_core app/src for split_selection|split_on_regex|select_regex|select_all_matches|keep_selections|keep_matching|remove_selections|rotate_primary|keep_primary|remove_primary|collapse_selection|trim_selection|align_selection => EXACTLY 2 hits, both `collapse_selections_to_cursors` (app/src/notebooks/editor/model.rs:1221 + view.rs:2026). I read the impl: it takes `selection.selections(ctx).first()` and writes a single `vec1![SelectionOffsets{ head: first_selection.head, tail: first_selection.head }]` via SetSelectionOffsets — collapses ALL selections to ONE cursor at the first head, which is neither Helix `;` (collapse each) nor `,` (keep primary). Zero hits in crates/editor, crates/vim, or the code-editor/vim path.
- No "primary selection" concept: the model only exposes first/last of the Vec1; buffer.rs:1981 "non-primary selection models" is the shared-buffer-across-views primary model, and terminal `rotate_selection` (app/src/terminal/model/alt_screen.rs:341) is scroll — neither is the Helix multi-cursor primary pointer.
- Regex/search is single-match only: search.rs select_next_from_cursor:174, select_next_result:199, select_match_at_offset:245 each select ONE match; no "select all regex matches as multi-selection" and no split/keep-by-regex.
- The only multi-cursor CREATION features (`select_next_occurrence` app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:6981; add_cursor_above/add_cursor_below bindings mod.rs:938/945, impl `add_cursor` mod.rs:7099) live ONLY in the TERMINAL editor and are built on the DisplayMap/DisplayPoint model (verified at 7099: uses `editor_model.display_map(ctx)` and `to_display_point`), so they are NOT reusable by the code editor / vim path. grep confirms app/src/code/editor has none.

PART 3 — Helix command -> existing vs missing primitive:
- `%` whole file -> EXISTS: BufferSelectAction::SelectAll (buffer.rs:169, impl :1653), CodeEditorViewAction::SelectAll (actions.rs:639/914) -> trait default model.select_all (crates/editor/src/model.rs:545).
- `s`/`S`/keep/remove (regex select/split/keep) -> MISSING; need buffer text + regex DFA.
- `x` whole-lines/line-split -> only partial substrate (selected_lines selection_model.rs:89, navigate_line selection.rs:868); no `x` primitive.
- `;`/`,` collapse-each / keep-primary -> only the notebooks "collapse to one cursor" hack; no shared/correct primitive; no primary pointer.
- rotate selections/contents, remove-primary, `_` trim, `&` align, copy-selection-next/prev -> MISSING.

PART 4 — Ownership: the COMPUTE side of every missing command needs buffer bytes and/or the regex DFA, both living in crates/editor (find.rs/search.rs, buffer.rs) and unreachable from the IR (EditorCommand) or a ModalEditExecutor. So new primitives MUST be new crates/editor selection-model operations (new BufferSelectAction variants + Selection/SelectionModel methods: SelectRegexWithin, SplitOnRegex, KeepMatching/RemoveMatching, CollapseEach, RotatePrimary, plus new "primary index" model state since Vec1 has none). The IR/executor own only intent + dispatch. This corrects the report's "~90% shared" framing: per-selection apply is reusable; selection-set management is net-new model surface.

### A4-Q7 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** VimModel is built once (ctx.add_model) and subscribed once at view.rs:359-360; the AppEditorSettings subscription at view.rs:312 has an empty body; mode is only re-read live via vim_mode_enabled() (view.rs:1953). When a user changes editing_mode mid-session (Vim to Helix), what happens to in-flight FSA state (pending_action, pending_action_count, pending_visual_object, register, dot_repeat_event, current submode) and the existing single subscription, and is the engine Entity swapped or reset in place?

**A.** Confirmed. In-flight FSA state is untouched and the engine is neither swapped nor reset.

VERDICT confirmed. Code editor view.rs builds the VimModel Entity once at 359 and subscribes once at 360. The field at 271 is a concrete ModelHandle, no enum or boxed dyn, with supports_vim_mode captured once at 357. The only enable normalization is a one-time escape keypress during construction at 363-368 (brace 369), gated on supports_vim_mode and vim_mode_enabled, never re-run on a settings change. The AppEditorSettings observer at 312-314 is literally ctx.notify, no rebuild, reset, or re-subscription. The terminal editor mod.rs wires the model identically, add_model 3015 and subscribe 3017, and its settings handler 3380-3408 matches only cursor and autocomplete and autosuggestion events with a catch-all arm at 3406 swallowing any mode toggle. Mode is read live: vim_mode_enabled 1952-1953 is supports_vim_mode and the live AppEditorSettings boolean. keymap_context 2388-2405 inserts the Vim flags only when vim_mode is Some, gating the id-Vim and not-id-Vim forks in actions.rs, 16 occurrences across 54-175 plus 593, that route via vim_keystroke and vim_user_insert 2081-2102, calling vim_model.update with only an is_self_focused guard and no vim_mode_enabled guard. So toggling vim_mode off stops dispatch but never resets the single VimFSA in VimModel, vim.rs 1800-1802, and the fields at vim.rs 38-54 (mode, showcmd, pending_action, pending_action_count, pending_operand_count, pending_visual_object, last_find_motion, register, dot_repeat_event) persist and resume stale. The engine is neither swapped, a concrete ModelHandle with no dyn, nor reset in place: force_insert_mode and interrupt exist around vim.rs 1825 and 1831 but only terminal insert-mode actions call them, never on a settings change. No editing_mode, Helix, or Kakoune engine exists, only the boolean vim_mode at settings/editor.rs 202, default false, read at 285-286. Nit: escape brace at 369 not 368.

### A4-Q8 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** The report says registers "remain an app concern and generalize cleanly," but the FSA stores a vim-specific register: char (vim.rs:53), there is a vim-only app/src/vim_registers.rs, and a vim-only setting vim_unnamed_system_clipboard (editor.rs:211-220). Does the shared EditorCommand IR plus executor carry a single register model, and does vim_unnamed_system_clipboard become a generalized modal_* setting, stay Vim-only, or get a per-engine analog?

**A.** There is no "EditorCommand IR" today; the de-facto IR (VimEventType) and its executor carry one register model that is vim-specific to the bone: char-named registers all the way down (register_name: char on the events), a char-keyed HashMap store, vim's MotionType inside RegisterContent, and a single app-global VimRegisters singleton shared by both the code editor and the terminal command editor. vim_unnamed_system_clipboard is strictly Vim-only in code (name, gating, and a single functional consumer); whether it becomes modal_*, stays Vim-only, or gets a per-engine analog is an unmade design decision with no code basis yet.

I independently opened every cited file and re-ran the greps. The answer is accurate in all material respects.

1) No multi-engine IR. `grep -rn "EditorCommand" crates app/src` returns NOTHING — the type does not exist. There is no `modal_mode`/`editing_mode` editing-mode setting (the only `modal_` hits are `one_time_modal_model`, an unrelated launch-modal UI feature). `grep -rin "helix|kakoune"` over crates+app/src hits only non-engine code: external-editor command generation (app/src/util/file/external_editor/mod.rs:267-268 plus its tests), a BERT tokenizer vocab json, and a tree-sitter grammar README URL — none are modal-editing engines. So no Helix/Kakoune engine exists. The de-facto IR being generalized is VimEventType (crates/vim/src/vim.rs:539-599), executed by the VimHandler dispatch (~vim.rs:1981+). CONFIRMED.

2) Single, vim-specific register model — verified at every layer:
- FSA state `register: char` at vim.rs:52 (comment "Vim has char-named registers" at lines 50-51). Defaulted to the unnamed register '"' at construction (vim.rs:717) and reset to '"' in clear() (vim.rs:728); register chosen via the `"` command only if valid_register_name(c) (vim.rs:1132, 1608). CONFIRMED. (Note: the spec doc's own question cites vim.rs:53, off by one; the answer correctly uses 52.)
- IR vocabulary: `register_name: char` is hardcoded on the events — Paste (552), Operation (568), VisualOperator (582), VisualPaste read+write (586-587), and again in the handler dispatch (1986, 2014, 2020, 2021, 2034). `grep -c "register_name: char"` = 10. The "10 sites" count is EXACT.
- valid_register_name / BLACK_HOLE_REGISTER live in the shared vim crate (crates/vim/src/register.rs): black hole '_' at line 3, valid_register_name at lines 6-8 accepting only `a-zA-Z + * "` — pure vim vocabulary. CONFIRMED.
- Storage: one app-global VimRegisters SingletonEntity (app/src/vim_registers.rs): `registers: HashMap<char, RegisterContent>` (line 25), `RegisterContent { text: String, motion_type: vim::vim::MotionType }` (lines 12-15) — content payload keyed on a vim enum. CONFIRMED.
- Created once at app startup: `ctx.add_singleton_model(|_| VimRegisters::new());` at app/src/lib.rs:1707 (exact). Not per-engine/per-editor. CONFIRMED.
- Shared across BOTH editors: code editor app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs writes at 310/349/530/613 and reads at 593/692; terminal command editor reads at app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:2476/2731 and writes via app/src/editor/view/model/mod.rs:2806 — all on the SAME `VimRegisters::handle(ctx)` singleton. CONFIRMED.

Net: registers are NOT mode-neutral today; the IR carries vim register names (char) and vim paste semantics (MotionType). The report's "generalizes cleanly" is currently untested/contradicted by the char-typed IR fields.

3) vim_unnamed_system_clipboard is strictly Vim-only in code:
- Setting def app/src/settings/editor.rs:211-218: bool, default false, sync_to_cloud Globally, toml_path "text_editing.vim_unnamed_system_clipboard", description "Whether the Vim unnamed register uses the system clipboard." CONFIRMED.
- Functional consumption is a SINGLE site: the setting field `.vim_unnamed_system_clipboard` is read only inside VimRegisters::points_to_system_clipboard at app/src/vim_registers.rs:39 (that fn is the lone gate: '*'/'+' always hit the system clipboard; '"' only when the flag is on). Every other reference is UI plumbing (features_page toggle render/telemetry/dispatch) or the keymap flag. CONFIRMED.
- Surfaced as a UI toggle gated on VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG (app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:506-519, builds context `... & id!(flags::VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG)`), and published as keymap context flag VIM_UNNAMED_SYSTEM_CLIPBOARD nested inside the `if *vim_mode.value()` block (app/src/workspace/view.rs:22107-22111); flag string "Vim_Unnamed_System_Clipboard" at app/src/settings_view/mod.rs:456. CONFIRMED.

So: the executor carries one vim-shaped register model, and vim_unnamed_system_clipboard is today 100% Vim-scoped (name, gating flag, single functional consumer). Whether the setting evolves to modal_*, stays Vim-only, or gets per-engine analogs, and whether register_name is neutralized in a future IR, are forward-looking design decisions with no current code basis.

_Evidence:_
- register: char field at vim.rs:52 (comment 'Vim has char-named registers' at 50-51); defaulted to '"' at 717, reset to '"' at 728; register chosen via " only if valid_register_name at 1132 and 1608 — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:52,717,728,1132,1608` _(code)_

- vim_unnamed_system_clipboard is a Vim-named bool setting (default false, cloud-synced, toml 'text_editing.vim_unnamed_system_clipboard') — `app/src/settings/editor.rs:211-218` _(code)_
- The setting field is functionally read at exactly one site, VimRegisters::points_to_system_clipboard, which decides whether '"' routes to the system clipboard — `app/src/vim_registers.rs:37-44 (setting read at :39)` _(code)_
- Setting surfaced as a UI toggle gated on VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG and published as keymap context flag VIM_UNNAMED_SYSTEM_CLIPBOARD nested under the vim_mode block — `app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:506-519; app/src/workspace/view.rs:22107-22111; app/src/settings_view/mod.rs:456` _(code)_
- No EditorCommand type and no modal_/editing_mode editing-mode setting exist; helix/kakoune appear only in non-engine code (external_editor command gen, tokenizer vocab, grammar README) — `app/src/util/file/external_editor/mod.rs:267-268 (grep -rn EditorCommand returns nothing)` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc decide the register seam: today registers are a single vim-shaped model (char names in the IR, char-keyed singleton store, vim MotionType payload) shared across both editors, and vim_unnamed_system_clipboard is strictly Vim-scoped. The doc must therefore explicitly choose (1) whether to neutralize register_name in the IR vs keep it vim-typed with shims, and (2) the fate of the clipboard setting (rename to modal_*, keep Vim-gated, or per-engine), rather than assuming registers "generalize cleanly" as-is.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The product/architecture decisions are not settleable from code because the multi-engine layer does not exist: (a) whether a future shared IR keeps register_name: char or introduces a neutral RegisterRef with per-engine translation; (b) whether the store generalizes beyond HashMap<char, RegisterContent> to express Kakoune's per-selection register arrays and yank-register-on-delete semantics; (c) whether vim_unnamed_system_clipboard is renamed to a generalized modal_* setting, kept Vim-gated, or given per-engine analogs. RegisterContent already couples content to vim's MotionType, which would need neutralizing for non-vim paste models. These are unmade design choices, not current code facts.

### A4-Q9 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** The IR is already polluted with vim repeat semantics: VimEventType::Operation carries a replacement_text field kept empty except for Change when dot-repeated (vim.rs:565-572), and the FSA holds dot_repeat_event: Option<VimEvent> driven by for_dot_repeat (vim.rs:53-54). Vim dot, Helix dot, and Kakoune macros differ. Does the shared EditorCommand IR carry repeat/dot-repeat state, or does each engine own its repeat mechanism with the IR staying stateless?

**A.** Confirmed. There is no "EditorCommand" IR anywhere in the repo; the de-facto IR is VimEventType emitted by VimFSA into the VimHandler trait. The dot-repeat STATE MACHINE is fully per-engine FSA-internal state (dot_repeat_event + for_dot_repeat + insert tracking), never crossing the handler boundary. But the emitted IR is NOT repeat-neutral: Operation.replacement_text and InsertText.text are dot-repeat-derived payloads baked into the shared event/trait surface (VimHandler::operation even takes replacement_text: &str, threaded through BOTH the code-editor and terminal handlers). No macro (q/Q) recording exists anywhere, so Kakoune's model has zero representation. Every cited file:line was independently opened and verified; the only line-ref discrepancy (the spec's own 56-57 for dot_repeat_event) was already corrected by the answer to the accurate 53-54.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I opened every cited location plus uncited ones (full VimHandler trait, all InsertText producers, repo-wide EditorCommand/macro greps). All 14 evidence claims hold.

CURRENT STATE OF THE "SHARED IR":
- No type named EditorCommand exists. Repo-wide grep across BOTH crates/ and app/ returned zero hits (stronger than the answer's "graphed crates" qualifier). The "shared EditorCommand IR" is purely a report-proposed abstraction. The actual IR boundary: VimFSA emits VimEvent/VimEventType (struct at vim.rs:524-536, enum at vim.rs:538-599); the blanket impl<T: VimHandler> VimSubscriber for T (vim.rs:1849) dispatches those events into the VimHandler trait (vim.rs:1945-2064), the closest thing to a shared/abstract executor. App-level handlers implement VimHandler: app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs (code editor) and app/src/editor/view/mod.rs (terminal duplicate).

REPEAT STATE IS PER-ENGINE FSA-INTERNAL (stateless w.r.t. the handler boundary):
- dot_repeat_event: Option<VimEvent> is a private field of VimFSA (vim.rs:53-54 — NOTE the spec question itself mis-cited this as 56-57; the answer correctly uses 53-54), owned inside VimModel { fsa: VimFSA } (vim.rs:1800-1801).
- Set in typed_character via for_dot_repeat() (vim.rs:767-770); the mapping table for_dot_repeat (vim.rs:607-661) decides which events repeat and how (ChangeMode->Insert maps to InsertText at 627; ChangeMode->Replace maps to ReplaceChar at 638). NOTE: both Change AND Delete operators are dot-repeatable Operations (615-616), but only Change carries replacement_text — fully consistent with the answer's scoping.
- The '.' command is resolved entirely inside the FSA: handle_normal_nothing_pending clones dot_repeat_event.event_type and re-emits it (vim.rs:1038-1044). Dot count semantics live in compute_event_count (vim.rs:850-857).
- Insert-mode char accumulation for repeat is FSA-internal: dot_repeat_text_mut / handle_insert_char / handle_insert_mode_backspace / handle_insert_mode_delete (vim.rs:1683-1728), and count>1 insert replay in exit_insert_mode (vim.rs:865-892).
- None of this machinery is a handler parameter; it never leaves the FSA. The repeat MECHANISM is already per-engine and could stay so for Helix/Kakoune without touching the executor.

BUT THE EMITTED IR / EXECUTOR TRAIT IS NOT REPEAT-NEUTRAL (the report's "1:1, repeat-neutral" claim is wrong):
- VimEventType::Operation { operator, operand, register_name, replacement_text: String } (vim.rs:565-572). Doc comment states replacement_text is "kept empty for all operators except Change when it is dot-repeated." Produced empty by create_operation (vim.rs:1656-1663), back-filled only on the Change branch by dot_repeat_text_mut (vim.rs:1689-1697), then passed across the boundary into VimHandler::operation(..., replacement_text: &str, ...) (signature at vim.rs:1981-1989, param at 1987; emitted at vim.rs:1884-1896) and consumed in the handler at vim_handler.rs:325-326 (gated on operator==Change && !replacement_text.is_empty(), then model.insert(replacement_text,...)) with the terminal duplicate at app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:2199 (param) and 2325 (editor_model.insert(replacement_text, None, ctx)). So a vim-dot-repeat-only field rides inside the shared event AND the executor trait signature in BOTH handlers.
- VimEventType::InsertText { text, position } (vim.rs:574-577): I verified the ONLY construction sites that emit a fresh InsertText are for_dot_repeat's ChangeMode->Insert mapping (627) and exit_insert_mode's count>1 replay (886); normal typing emits InsertChar(c) per character (1707). So "this variant exists specifically to materialize dot-repeat/count-insert as a single replayed event" is accurate. Replayed via the handler at vim.rs:1903-1905.

MACRO MODEL (Kakoune q/Q): No macro recording exists at all. Grep for macro/record/replay/record_macro in crates/vim found nothing. vim.rs's only "register" is the char-named clipboard register field (vim.rs:50-52), set on the `"` prefix command (self.register = c at 1133 and 1609) and used for yank/paste, not macro recording. Kakoune's q/Q macro model has ZERO representation in the current IR or FSA.

BOTTOM LINE: The repeat machinery is already cleanly per-engine FSA state and can stay stateless at the IR/handler boundary EXCEPT that two emitted variants (Operation.replacement_text, InsertText.text) currently smuggle vim-dot-repeat payload through the shared event + the VimHandler::operation signature (threaded through both the code-editor and terminal handlers). Making the IR truly repeat-neutral requires extracting replacement_text off Operation (have the FSA emit a delete+InsertText pair, as it already does for the InsertText repeat path) so Helix/Kakoune don't inherit a Change-only field. Macro recording is a net-new surface with no existing plumbing.

_Evidence:_
- No EditorCommand type exists anywhere in the repo (crates AND app, not just graphed crates); the IR is VimEventType/VimEvent emitted by VimFSA into VimHandler — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:524-599` _(both)_
- dot_repeat_event is a private FSA field (answer correctly uses 53-54; the spec question mis-cited 56-57), not a handler parameter — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:53-54` _(code)_
- VimFSA is owned inside VimModel { fsa: VimFSA }, keeping repeat state engine-local — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1800-1801` _(code)_
- for_dot_repeat() is the FSA-internal mapping deciding repeatability and event remapping (ChangeMode->Insert->InsertText at 627, ChangeMode->Replace->ReplaceChar at 638; both Change and Delete repeatable as Operation at 615-616) — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:607-661` _(code)_
- dot_repeat_event set in typed_character via for_dot_repeat; never exposed to handler — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:767-770` _(code)_
- '.' is resolved inside the FSA by cloning dot_repeat_event.event_type and re-emitting — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1038-1044` _(code)_
- Operation.replacement_text is a dot-repeat-only field documented as empty except Change when dot-repeated — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:565-572` _(code)_
- replacement_text produced empty by create_operation, back-filled only on the Change branch of dot_repeat_text_mut — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1656-1663,1689-1697` _(code)_
- replacement_text crosses the executor boundary: VimHandler::operation takes replacement_text:&str (param at 1987); emitted at 1884-1896 — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1981-1989` _(code)_
- Code-editor handler consumes the dot-repeat replacement_text by inserting it, gated on Change && non-empty — `app/src/code/editor/view/vim_handler.rs:325-326` _(code)_
- Terminal duplicate handler also threads replacement_text through operation() (param 2199) and inserts it (2325) — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:2193-2325` _(code)_
- InsertText.text is accumulated via dot_repeat_text_mut/handle_insert_char; the only fresh InsertText producers are for_dot_repeat (627) and exit_insert_mode (886) — normal typing emits InsertChar per char (1707) — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1683-1716` _(code)_
- count>1 insert replay materializes InsertText from dot_repeat_event in exit_insert_mode; replayed via handler at 1903-1905 — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:865-892` _(code)_
- No macro (q/Q) recording mechanism exists in the vim crate; only the char-named clipboard register (set on " prefix at 1133/1609), used for yank/paste — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:50-52` _(both)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc decide the repeat/macro ownership boundary: keep repeat as per-engine FSA state (cheap, already true) while extracting the two vim-dot-repeat payload fields (Operation.replacement_text, InsertText.text) out of the shared event/executor trait so Helix/Kakoune don't inherit vim-shaped repeat fields, and scope macro recording (Kakoune q/Q) as net-new per-engine state rather than an IR concern.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Whether the future shared IR SHOULD carry repeat state or stay stateless is a design decision, not code-verifiable. The code shows the choice is open: the repeat machinery (dot_repeat_event, for_dot_repeat, insert tracking) is already per-engine FSA-internal and can remain so, but achieving a truly repeat-neutral IR requires removing replacement_text from VimEventType::Operation (and from the VimHandler::operation trait signature in BOTH handlers) and likely emitting a delete+InsertText pair instead — confirming the report's "VimEventType maps 1:1, repeat-neutral" is inaccurate on these two variants. Kakoune's q/Q macro model has no existing plumbing to extend; it is net-new. How Helix's "repeat last insert" (.) maps versus vim's broader dot-repeat (operations + inserts) is a semantic design choice the code does not settle.
- _Verification:_ I independently opened all cited locations and several uncited ones. Confirmed: VimFSA struct (37-55), VimEvent/VimEventType (522-599), for_dot_repeat (607-661), typed_character (749-772), compute_event_count dot path (845-860), exit_insert_mode (865-892), '.' resolution (1038-1044), create_operation/create_visual_operator (1656-1679), dot_repeat_text_mut + handle_insert_char/backspace/delete (1683-1728), VimModel (1795-1838), blanket VimSubscriber dispatch with Operation (1884-1896) and InsertText (1903-1905), and the FULL VimHandler trait incl. operation signature with replacement_text: &str at line 1987. ### A4-Q10 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Step D writes a dynamic format!('Modal:{engine}:{submode}') keymap context, but Context.set is HashSet of &'static str (context.rs:5) and every ContextPredicate holds &'static str (context.rs:11-17), with no FromStr/runtime-key path. Do we represent the per-engine submode cross-product as a CLOSED, compile-time-enumerated set of &'static str flags (same mechanism as today's VimNormalMode/VimVisualMode, view.rs:2388-2410), or does Axis 4 hard-require the Axis 2 interned/Cow context-string refactor (R3a) as a prerequisite?

**A.** Closed compile-time static-str flag table; the per-engine submode cross-product should be a compile-time-enumerated set of static-str flags, the same mechanism vim already uses; the Context type forbids non-static keys so the report dynamic-format keymap context is illustrative not insertable; the submode set is finite so a closed static table suffices and Axis 4 does not hard-require the Axis-2 interned or Cow refactor R3a.

Verified all cited lines accurate. Context holds static-str set and map; ContextPredicate variants hold static str; eval does static-str lookups; no FromStr leak or intern path; macros take only literals; mutators static-str-bound; escape-hatch modifier gets a mut Context. Code editor inserts Vim and VimNormalMode and collapses Visual to VimVisualMode; terminal inserts only Vim and VimNormalMode; consumer predicate uses id VimNormalMode. VimMode is a finite enum. All set-insert sites pass literal or const static str. SelectionSet exists. R3a is not a hard prerequisite unless keys come from arbitrary runtime user strings, which the report does not mandate.
