### A4-Q11 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** vim_mode: bool, sync_to_cloud: Globally(RespectUserSyncSetting::Yes), toml_path text_editing.vim_mode_enabled, no storage_key (editor.rs:202-210). Migrating to editing_mode/modal_mode: how do mixed-version cloud-synced clients coexist — dual-write legacy bool + new enum, migrate-on-read via vim_mode_enabled() shim (editor.rs:285), and which value wins when an OLD client toggles the bool while a NEW client has set Helix?

**A.** Cloud sync is keyed by storage_key, per-key, last-write-wins, with the value an opaque serialized string and NO field-merge/timestamp at this layer. vim_mode's sync key is "VimModeEnabled" (derived from the setting STRUCT name, not the toml path); its TOML key is "vim_mode_enabled". There is NO dual-write infrastructure anywhere; the only bool->enum precedent (KeepThinkingExpanded -> ThinkingDisplayMode, same Globally sync class) is one-time migrate-on-read-then-DELETE under a NEW distinct storage_key, which does not solve cross-version coexistence. vim_mode_enabled() (editor.rs:285) is a pure READ shim and does nothing for the write/sync path. "Which value wins" is purely last-write-wins per storage_key -- the code encodes no enum-vs-bool precedence -- so an old client toggling the bool can silently override a new client unless the new enum lives under its own key AND the runtime stops deriving mode from the bool. (Independently verified; every cited line reference holds.)

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened every cited file plus the un-cited startup-divergence path and the two usage sites; all line references are accurate and say what the answer asserts.

CODE FACTS (re-verified):

1) Sync identity = storage_key; per-key, last-write-wins, opaque string value. The cloud syncer keys every preference by storage_key. On a cloud change it OVERWRITES the local value for that key (maybe_sync_cloud_pref_to_local keys by storage_key at cloud_preferences_syncer.rs:832-835; the actual local overwrite is update_setting_with_storage_key at :926-930 gated on is_changing :910-920; runtime subscription :206-221 applies cloud->local ONLY after has_completed_initial_load :214). On a local change it debounce-pushes to cloud (:222-238). Startup direction is decided by a hash-divergence flag force_cloud_to_match_local / force_local_wins_on_startup (handle_initial_load :533-554), not a per-field merge. So effectively last-writer-wins per storage_key. Two cross-version guards confirmed: (a) a cloud value for a key whose syncing mode resolves to Never is ignored (cloud_syncing_mode_for_storage_key -> SyncToCloud::Never, :850-858); (b) a serialized cloud string that fails to deserialize into the client's expected type is dropped and local is kept (macros.rs:868-879 returns Err "Failed to parse updated value ... Not updating" when both from_file_value and serde fall through).

2) vim_mode's storage_key is "VimModeEnabled" (the SETTING STRUCT name), NOT "vim_mode_enabled". With a toml_path and no explicit storage_key, the macro convenience arm sets storage_key: stringify!($name) where $name is the setting struct (macros.rs:217-218), and the @base arm's storage_key() returns that (macros.rs:278-280). The toml_key()/storage_key trait fallback (lib.rs:254-256; macro override macros.rs:282-288) governs only the TOML file key, which is the last toml_path segment "vim_mode_enabled" under [text_editing]. Confirmed in use: VimModeEnabled::storage_key() at features_page.rs:6133 and the literal string "VimModeEnabled" inserted into a sync-key set at terminal/input.rs:15945. CursorDisplayType (editor.rs:192-201) carries an EXPLICIT storage_key "CursorDisplayType" differing from its toml last-segment "cursor_display_type" -- proving storage_key and toml key are independent and a new enum can/must choose its own unique storage_key. CursorDisplayType was born an enum, NOT a bool->enum migration, so it is not the precedent the question implies.

3) The real bool->enum precedent: KeepThinkingExpanded (bool) -> ThinkingDisplayMode (enum) at initializer.rs:121-171. Migrate-on-read, ONE-TIME, gated on "new key present" (read_value("ThinkingDisplayMode").is_some() -> return at :140-148), reads the old bool directly from prefs (:153-158), maps true->AlwaysShow (:160-164), then DELETES the old key remove_value("KeepThinkingExpanded") (:166-169). NOT dual-write. ThinkingDisplayMode is private:false, SyncToCloud::Globally(RespectUserSyncSetting::Yes) (ai.rs:361-369) -- the SAME sync class as vim_mode -- under a NEW distinct storage_key. Its "Remove ~6 weeks from 3/19/26" TODO (:133) shows it bets old clients churn out; it provides NO cross-version coexistence contract.

4) vim_mode_enabled() (editor.rs:285-287) is `*self.vim_mode.value()` -- a pure in-memory READ shim. It cannot mediate the WRITE/sync path across client versions; the adversarial angle (a read shim does nothing for sync) is correct.

5) No dual-write infrastructure exists in the settings layer; migrations are migrate-once-then-delete. schema_tests.rs enforce non-empty storage_keys (no_empty_storage_keys :28-35) and uniqueness (no_duplicate_storage_keys :62-84), so a new modal_mode enum cannot share "VimModeEnabled" while the old setting exists -- it must take its own key. NUANCE I VERIFIED: the duplicate-key assert is `#[cfg(not(test))]`-gated (:79), i.e. enforced in the real app build but skipped under cfg(test) because test fixtures intentionally overlap keys; this does not weaken the conclusion.

"WHICH VALUE WINS" (old client toggles bool, new client set Helix): the sync layer encodes ONLY last-write-wins per storage_key -- no automatic enum-vs-bool precedence or merge. Two concrete design paths and their code-determined outcomes:
- New enum under a NEW distinct key (e.g. "ModalMode"): the old client never reads/writes "ModalMode", so it cannot directly clobber the enum object. But the old client toggling "VimModeEnabled" pushes a bool to cloud -> the new client receives a CloudPreferencesUpdated for "VimModeEnabled" and overwrites its local bool (:206-221, :832-930). If the new runtime still derives effective mode from VimModeEnabled (as today's vim_mode_enabled() shim does), that stale bool silently flips the new client out of Helix even though "ModalMode" is untouched. Whichever value the runtime READS wins -- and that read precedence is NOT encoded today.
- New enum REUSING "VimModeEnabled" with an enum type: cross-version type collision on one cloud object -- old writes bool, new writes enum, last-write-wins with no merge; each version drops the other's value on deserialize failure (macros.rs:868-879), so the cloud object thrashes bool<->enum on every change. This is exactly the corruption mode the question warns about, and it is additionally illegal per the uniqueness test.

Conclusion on the code-verifiable part: a distinct storage_key for the enum is effectively mandatory (uniqueness + type-safety), no dual-write helper exists to reuse, and the only shipped migration shape is one-time migrate-on-read-then-delete. The "precedence" question (which is authoritative under simultaneous cross-version edits) is a design decision the code does not answer; a read shim does not provide it.

_Evidence:_
- vim_mode is bool, Globally(RespectUserSyncSetting::Yes), toml_path text_editing.vim_mode_enabled, struct name VimModeEnabled, no explicit storage_key field — `app/src/settings/editor.rs:202-210` _(code)_
- vim_mode_enabled() is a pure read shim returning *self.vim_mode.value() — `app/src/settings/editor.rs:285-287` _(code)_
- No-explicit-storage_key+toml_path macro arm sets storage_key: stringify!($name) (=setting struct name, so vim_mode -> VimModeEnabled); @base storage_key() returns it — `crates/settings/src/macros.rs:217-218,278-280` _(code)_
- toml_key() = last segment of toml_path (toml_path_storage_key) for settings with toml_path; trait-level fallback toml_key()=storage_key() only for settings without toml_path — `crates/settings/src/macros.rs:282-288 and crates/settings/src/lib.rs:254-256` _(code)_
- VimModeEnabled::storage_key() used as cloud sync identity in settings UI — `app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:6133` _(code)_
- Literal string "VimModeEnabled" inserted into a sync-key set, gated on vim_mode_enabled() — `app/src/terminal/input.rs:15945` _(code)_
- Cloud->local sync keys by storage_key; runtime applies cloud update only after has_completed_initial_load, per storage_key — `app/src/settings/cloud_preferences_syncer.rs:206-221` _(code)_
- maybe_sync_cloud_pref_to_local keys by storage_key; Never-mode cloud value ignored (:850-858); local overwritten with cloud value when is_changing (:910-930) = last-write-wins — `app/src/settings/cloud_preferences_syncer.rs:832-930` _(code)_
- Startup conflict direction decided by hash-divergence force_cloud_to_match_local / force_local_wins_on_startup, not field merge — `app/src/settings/cloud_preferences_syncer.rs:533-554` _(code)_
- Cloud value that fails to deserialize into expected type is dropped (Err 'Failed to parse updated value ... Not updating'), keeping local — `crates/settings/src/macros.rs:868-879` _(code)_
- Real bool->enum precedent KeepThinkingExpanded->ThinkingDisplayMode: one-time migrate-on-read, gated on new-key-present return, reads old bool, maps true->AlwaysShow, then deletes old key; NOT dual-write; TODO remove ~6 weeks — `app/src/settings/initializer.rs:121-171` _(code)_
- ThinkingDisplayMode is private:false, Globally(RespectUserSyncSetting::Yes) (same sync class as vim_mode), defined via implement_setting_for_enum with its own distinct storage_key — `app/src/settings/ai.rs:361-369` _(code)_
- CursorDisplayType has explicit storage_key "CursorDisplayType" distinct from toml last segment cursor_display_type; born an enum, not migrated from a bool — `app/src/settings/editor.rs:192-201` _(code)_
- Schema tests enforce non-empty (no_empty_storage_keys) and unique (no_duplicate_storage_keys) storage_keys; uniqueness assert is cfg(not(test))-gated so it fires in real app build — `crates/settings/src/schema_tests.rs:28-35,62-84` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design pick the migration contract for vim_mode -> modal_mode: (1) give the new enum its own unique storage_key (mandatory per schema uniqueness + type-safety; reusing VimModeEnabled causes bool<->enum thrash with silent drops); (2) reuse the proven one-time migrate-on-read-then-delete shape (KeepThinkingExpanded precedent) rather than inventing dual-write, while accepting it offers no cross-version coexistence; (3) recognize that vim_mode_enabled() as a read shim cannot mediate sync, so to stop an old client's bool toggle from flipping a new client out of Helix the runtime must stop deriving mode from the legacy bool once modal_mode is set (an explicit enum-wins read precedence the code must add).
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The actual coexistence CONTRACT is a design decision the code does not encode: (a) dual-write vs migrate-on-read -- no dual-write helper exists today, only one-time migrate-then-delete; (b) read precedence under simultaneous cross-version edits (enum authoritative vs bool authoritative) is unspecified in code; last-write-wins per storage_key is the only mechanism. The cloud backend's exact write semantics (server-side timestamp/versioning beyond the client's hash-divergence check and SyncQueue) were not traced into the server; client-side behavior is last-write-wins per key. I did not open the three lib.rs:415/580/623 supporting lines individually (substance confirmed via the syncer). Whether the team accepts the precedent's '~6 weeks old-client churn' bet for a public globally-synced vim_mode is a product/rollout choice, not settleable from code.
- _Verification:_ I attacked every load-bearing line reference by opening the file directly. All held: editor.rs:202-210/285-287, macros.rs:217-218/278-288/868-879, lib.rs:254-256, cloud_preferences_syncer.rs:206-221/832-930/533-554, initializer.rs:121-171, ai.rs:361-369, schema_tests.rs:28-84, features_page.rs:6133, terminal/input.rs:15945. The KeepThinkingExpanded precedent is one-time migrate-on-read-then-delete, not dual-write -- confirmed including the new-key-present early return and remove_value. ### A4-Q12 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Forked non-char vim keys (id(Vim)/!id(Vim), actions.rs:50-294) yield distinct named actions (VimEnter vs Enter), described as EDITABLE bindings a user can override/persist by name to keybindings.yaml. When Step D migrates them to id(ModalEditing)+ModalKeystroke (renaming/removing Vim-prefixed action names), does a user's existing persisted custom trigger silently orphan/no-op, or is there a name-migration map?

**A.** Confirmed. The spec question's premise is false and the answer correctly refutes it: the forked non-char Vim keys (VimEnter/VimBackspace/VimDelete/VimTab/VimShiftTab/VimShiftEnter) are FixedBinding (registered via app.register_fixed_bindings at actions.rs:50-287), NOT editable — they have no name, never appear in the settings UI, are never persistable, so renaming those action variants orphans nothing. Persistence is keyed by the EditableBinding NAME string (HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger>), not the Rust action variant. Exactly 3 distinct Vim-NAMED editable/persistable name strings exist: editor_view:vim_scroll_half_page_down, editor_view:vim_scroll_half_page_up, editor_view:vim_exit_insert_mode (the last shared by code + terminal editors). For those, dropping/renaming the name string silently orphans the persisted entry as a no-op (no warning, dead entry left in keybindings.yaml), and there is NO name-migration/alias map anywhere.

I independently opened every cited file plus uncited locations and re-ran the grep; all 12 evidence claims hold, with only trivial line-range generosity (noted below). The answer is CONFIRMED at high confidence.

1) PREMISE CORRECTION VERIFIED. actions.rs:44-287 is one app.register_fixed_bindings([...]) call (open line 50, close line 287). Every Vim fork inside is FixedBinding::new: enter/VimEnter (:56-60), numpadenter/VimEnter (:66-69), shift-enter/VimShiftEnter (:76-79), backspace/VimBackspace (:86-89), shift-backspace/VimBackspace (:96-99), delete/VimDelete (:106-109), tab/VimTab (:162-165), shift-tab/VimShiftTab (:172-175). The spec question text (line 699) itself asserts "These are EDITABLE bindings whose triggers a user can already override and persist" — the code directly contradicts that, exactly as the answer states.

2) FixedBinding is NOT reconfigurable / has NO name. keymap.rs:275 doc comment reads verbatim "This action can't be reconfigured with a custom key binding trigger"; the struct (277-286) has fields trigger/action/command_description/context_predicate/enabled_predicate/group/id — no name field. register_fixed_bindings (388-399) doc reads "These bindings are internal and cannot be changed once they are added". Confirmed.

3) Persistence keyed by EditableBinding NAME string. keyboard.rs:157 + 167-169: CustomKeybindings is HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> documented "(Editable Binding Name) -> Keybinding". load path (37-56) iterates (name, trigger) and calls app.set_custom_trigger(name, ...) at :44/:47; the ONLY log::warn (49-53) fires for an unparsable trigger string, never for an unknown name. write_custom_keybinding (64-74) only does map.0.insert(name, ...) — nothing prunes orphaned keys. Confirmed.

4) set_custom_trigger -> update_custom_trigger, exact-match by name, no fallback. matcher.rs:137-141 set_custom_trigger calls update_custom_trigger(name, Some(trigger)). keymap.rs update_custom_trigger (422-433) filters BOTH editable_custom_action_bindings (b.name == name, :423-428) AND editable_bindings (b.name == name, :430-431); it never touches fixed_bindings. An unknown/renamed name matches zero bindings -> silent no-op. Confirmed.

5) Settings UI lists only editable bindings. keybindings.rs:754 ctx.editable_bindings().collect(); subsequent dedup sorts by description then name (759-761). Fixed Vim forks (no name) can never appear here. Confirmed.

6) EXACTLY 3 distinct persistable Vim-named editable bindings. My app-wide grep returns only: actions.rs:510 editor_view:vim_scroll_half_page_down (ctrl-d, :515), actions.rs:517 editor_view:vim_scroll_half_page_up (ctrl-u, :522), actions.rs:589 editor_view:vim_exit_insert_mode (ctrl-[, :594), and mod.rs:962 editor_view:vim_exit_insert_mode (ctrl-[, terminal) — the SAME name string, so 3 distinct strings total. (Other grep hits are test fixtures referencing the external editor "vim", unrelated.) Terminal editor also forks VimBackspace as FixedBinding at mod.rs:202-206. Confirmed.

7) NO migration/alias map. grep migrat|alias|legacy|old_name|rename across keyboard.rs + keymap.rs + matcher.rs returns a single hit, keymap.rs:651, a constructor comment in EditableBinding::new: "Explicitly not supporting registering legacy actions, as they will be removed when the conversion to editable bindings is complete" — this is the OPPOSITE of a migration mechanism and reinforces the answer's conclusion. get_binding_by_name (keymap.rs:379-386) is exact-match on editable_bindings_by_name with no alias fallback. Confirmed.

CONCLUSION FOR DESIGN (unchanged): forked non-char modal keys kept as fixed bindings carry zero persistence-orphan risk. The only data-loss surface is the 3 editable Vim-named bindings; if their name strings are dropped/renamed during a ModalEditing migration, existing persisted custom triggers silently die with no warning and leave dead entries in keybindings.yaml. No alias/migration/rename pass exists today.

_Evidence:_
- actions.rs:44-287 is a single register_fixed_bindings call; all Vim forks (VimEnter/VimShiftEnter/VimBackspace/VimDelete/VimTab/VimShiftTab) are FixedBinding::new, not editable — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:50-287` _(code)_
- The spec question itself (line 699) wrongly asserts these forks are EDITABLE/persistable bindings — directly contradicted by the code — `docs/specs/2026-06-13-improved-keybindings-design-readiness-questions.md:699` _(code)_
- FixedBinding doc says 'This action can't be reconfigured with a custom key binding trigger' and the struct has NO name field; register_fixed_bindings is 'internal and cannot be changed once they are added' — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:275-286,388-399` _(both)_
- EditableBinding carries a name; register_editable_bindings doc states the name is used to override triggers via set_custom_trigger — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:293-304,401-419` _(code)_
- update_custom_trigger filters editable_custom_action_bindings AND editable_bindings by name==name only; never touches fixed_bindings; unknown name = silent no-op — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- Custom keybindings file is HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> documented '(Editable Binding Name) -> Keybinding', keyed on name not on Rust action variant — `app/src/keyboard.rs:155-169` _(code)_
- On load, each persisted (name,trigger) calls app.set_custom_trigger; only an unparsable trigger logs warn (49-53); an unknown name does not warn — `app/src/keyboard.rs:37-56` _(code)_
- write_custom_keybinding only map.0.insert(name,...); nothing prunes orphaned unknown names, so dead entries persist in keybindings.yaml — `app/src/keyboard.rs:64-74` _(code)_

- Settings keybindings UI lists only ctx.editable_bindings(), so fixed Vim forks (no name) never appear / can't be customized — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:754` _(code)_
- Exactly 3 distinct persistable Vim-named editable bindings: vim_scroll_half_page_down (:510), vim_scroll_half_page_up (:517), vim_exit_insert_mode (:589 code editor; :962 terminal, same name string) — `app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:510,517,589; app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:962` _(both)_
- Terminal editor also forks VimBackspace as FixedBinding, not editable — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:202-206` _(code)_
- No keybinding name migration/alias mechanism exists; grep migrat|alias|legacy|rename across keyboard.rs/keymap.rs/matcher.rs yields only keymap.rs:651, a comment refusing legacy-action support (reinforces no migration) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:651; app/src/keyboard.rs; crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs` _(both)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets Step D pick the migration seam safely: forked non-char keys can be renamed freely (never persistable), so the only data-loss guard needed is for the 3 editable Vim-named bindings — keep those name strings stable, add a name-alias map, or add a one-time rename pass in load_custom_keybindings, since today an unmatched name silently no-ops with no warning and leaves a dead entry in keybindings.yaml.

### A4-Q14 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Step E says surface editing_mode as a dropdown, but vim_mode today is a toggle (ToggleVimMode -> toggle_and_save_value) with two dependent toggles (vim_unnamed_system_clipboard, vim_status_bar) and vim_status_bar drives a runtime status-bar mode indicator. (a) Where does the dropdown live and what options show when VimCodeEditor-gated; (b) do the vim-only sub-settings become modal_*, stay Vim-gated, or get dropped; (c) what does the runtime status bar SHOW for Helix submodes / selection-count vs the vim mode string?

**A.** A reusable Dropdown<FeaturesPageAction> infra already exists (template: code_editor_line_number_mode_dropdown) and the vim control has TWO UI surfaces that must both be reworked: a command-palette ToggleSettingActionPair (features_page.rs:490-504) and the settings-page VimModeWidget (features_page.rs:6105), which sits in text_editing_widgets right beside the line-number dropdown widget — the natural home. (a) An editing_mode dropdown is mechanically straightforward to host there, but the only engine that exists in code is Vim (VimMode{Normal,Insert,Visual(MotionType),Replace}); there is NO Helix/Kakoune engine anywhere (in fact "kakoune" appears nowhere in the repo, and "helix" only in external-editor CLI parsing), so a VimCodeEditor-gated dropdown can today only offer {off, Vim}. (b) The two sub-settings are currently conditionally visible only when vim_mode_enabled (VimModeWidget:6154) / gated by VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG in the palette; they are Vim-named settings (storage keys VimUnnamedSystemClipboard/VimStatusBar via storage_key(), toml text_editing.vim_*) and whether they become modal_* vs dropped is a product decision code cannot settle. (c) The runtime indicator (render_vim_status, terminal/input/common.rs:46) renders only vim_state.showcmd + a per-VimMode icon via an exhaustive match (Visual(_) collapses all visual submodes to ONE icon); VimState carries only mode+showcmd so there is NO selection count and NO Helix submode concept, and it lives ONLY in the terminal input editor — the code editor (VimCodeEditor surface) renders no runtime status bar at all.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened every cited file and re-ran the greps. All line references are accurate (a few are 1-3 lines wider/narrower than stated but land on the asserted code). One cosmetic imprecision: the answer says the only "helix"/"kakoune" strings are in external-editor parsing, but "kakoune" appears NOWHERE in the repo (.rs); only "helix" appears (external_editor/mod.rs:268 `"hx" | "helix"`). That strengthens, not weakens, the "no other engine" conclusion.

CODE FACTS (decision is downstream of these):

DROPDOWN INFRA EXISTS (the template to copy). Four pieces, all present for code_editor_line_number_mode and all verified: (1) ViewHandle<Dropdown<FeaturesPageAction>> field (features_page.rs:1385); (2) update_code_editor_line_number_mode_dropdown maps enum variants -> DropdownItem carrying SetCodeEditorLineNumberMode(value) and selects current by index (features_page.rs:3014-3047); (3) SetCodeEditorLineNumberMode(CodeEditorLineNumberMode) action variant (features_page.rs:812) + handler calling .set_value() (features_page.rs:2109-2113); (4) CodeEditorLineNumberModeWidget::render (features_page.rs:5789-5820) renders via render_dropdown_item passing &view.code_editor_line_number_mode_dropdown. Converting vim_mode (bool) to an editing_mode enum dropdown is a well-trodden path.

(a) WHERE THE DROPDOWN LIVES + OPTIONS. Two independent UI surfaces, both gated identically and both needing rework:
  - Command-palette toggle: ToggleSettingActionPair for ToggleVimMode (features_page.rs:490-504), gated by flags::VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG (= "Vim_Mode_Enabled", settings_view/mod.rs:455; inserted into the active context at workspace/view.rs:22108 — verified exact line).
  - Settings-page widget: VimModeWidget (features_page.rs:6105-6213), registered into text_editing_widgets (features_page.rs:2833) right after CodeEditorLineNumberModeWidget (features_page.rs:2826) — the dropdown's natural home.
  The editing engine RUNS only when FeatureFlag::VimCodeEditor.is_enabled() && AppEditorSettings::vim_mode_enabled() (code editor: view.rs:357,363,1953; terminal editor: editor/view/mod.rs:4920-4921). The ONLY engine in code is Vim: closed enum VimMode { Normal, Insert, Visual(MotionType), Replace } (crates/vim/src/vim.rs:14-20) with MotionType { Charwise, Linewise } (vim.rs:325-327). A repo-wide grep finds NO Helix/Kakoune engine/FSA/handler; "kakoune" is absent entirely, "helix" appears only in external_editor file:line:column parsing (util/file/external_editor/mod.rs:267-268). So a VimCodeEditor-gated dropdown can TODAY only offer {Off/None, Vim}.

(b) FATE OF THE TWO SUB-SETTINGS (code constraints only; final choice is product). vim_unnamed_system_clipboard and vim_status_bar are CONDITIONALLY VISIBLE under vim: in VimModeWidget they render only inside `if vim_mode_enabled { ... }` as a subgroup (features_page.rs:6154-6209); in the palette they are gated `context & id!(flags::VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG)` (features_page.rs:512,528). They are Vim-named at persistence: type/storage-key identifiers VimUnnamedSystemClipboard / VimStatusBar (used via ::storage_key() at 6169/6193), toml paths text_editing.vim_unnamed_system_clipboard / text_editing.vim_status_bar (settings/editor.rs:211-228), each an independent bool (vim_status_bar default true) with its own toggle_and_save_value handler (features_page.rs:1903-1920). Constraints: vim_unnamed_system_clipboard is a vim-register concept (no register subsystem for any other engine), and vim_status_bar is consumed only by the terminal vim status indicator. Whether these become modal_*, stay Vim-gated, or get dropped is NOT code-decidable.

(c) RUNTIME STATUS BAR CONTENT. Indicator is render_vim_status (terminal/input/common.rs:46-87). It renders exactly two things: vim_state.showcmd and a single icon chosen by an EXHAUSTIVE match over the closed VimMode enum — Normal -> green, Insert -> blended, Visual(_) -> ONE blue icon for all visual submodes, Replace -> red (common.rs:49-61). VimState (crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1791-1794) has ONLY `mode` and `showcmd` — NO selection-count field. Gated `vim_state.is_some() && *vim_status_bar.value()` and wired ONLY into terminal input surfaces (classic.rs:96,191; universal.rs:48,105), with the agent surface explicitly passing show_vim_status=false (agent.rs:148,622). The CODE editor (the very surface the dropdown gates) renders NO runtime status bar: it reads vim_state()/vim_mode() (code/editor/view.rs:1942-1949) but a tree-wide grep confirms render_vim_status/add_vim_status_to_stack are called ONLY in terminal/input. Adding Helix submodes or a selection count would require widening the VimMode enum AND the exhaustive icon match (won't compile otherwise) — the status bar is structurally vim-shaped, not engine-agnostic.

_Evidence:_
- vim_mode is a bool toggle whose handler calls toggle_and_save_value; satellites vim_unnamed_system_clipboard and vim_status_bar have identical handlers — `app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:1896-1920` _(code)_
- vim_mode/vim_unnamed_system_clipboard/vim_status_bar are bool settings; vim_status_bar default true, others false; toml keys text_editing.vim_* — `app/src/settings/editor.rs:202-228` _(code)_
- Command-palette ToggleVimMode is a ToggleSettingActionPair gated by VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG; the two satellites are gated context & id!(VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG) — `app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:490-536` _(code)_
- VIM_MODE_CONTEXT_FLAG = 'Vim_Mode_Enabled' — `app/src/settings_view/mod.rs:455` _(code)_

- VimModeWidget is registered into text_editing_widgets right after CodeEditorLineNumberModeWidget — `app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:2820-2834` _(code)_
- Dropdown infra template fully present: field (1385), update fn mapping enum->DropdownItem with Set* selecting current by index (3014-3047), Set* action variant (812) + handler calling set_value (2109-2113), widget render via render_dropdown_item passing the dropdown handle (5789-5820) — `app/src/settings_view/features_page.rs:1385,812,2109-2113,3014-3047,5789-5820` _(code)_
- Only engine in code is Vim: VimMode{Normal,Insert,Visual(MotionType),Replace}; MotionType only Charwise/Linewise — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:14-20,325-327` _(both)_
- No Helix/Kakoune engine exists; 'kakoune' absent repo-wide, 'helix' only in external-editor CLI parsing — `app/src/util/file/external_editor/mod.rs:267-268` _(both)_
- Editing engine runs only when FeatureFlag::VimCodeEditor.is_enabled() && vim_mode_enabled() (code editor; terminal editor 4920-4921) — `app/src/code/editor/view.rs:357,363,1953` _(code)_
- render_vim_status renders only showcmd + a per-VimMode icon via exhaustive match; Visual(_) collapses to one icon; VimState has only mode+showcmd (no selection count) — `app/src/terminal/input/common.rs:46-87 + crates/vim/src/vim.rs:1791-1794` _(both)_
- Status bar gated vim_state.is_some() && vim_status_bar.value(); wired only into terminal input (classic/universal); agent passes show_vim_status=false; not called anywhere in code editor — `app/src/terminal/input/classic.rs:96,191;app/src/terminal/input/universal.rs:48,105;app/src/terminal/input/agent.rs:148,622` _(both)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets Step E scope the settings-UI rework precisely: (1) host an editing_mode dropdown in text_editing_widgets modeled on code_editor_line_number_mode_dropdown, replacing BOTH the VimModeWidget switch AND the command-palette ToggleSettingActionPair; (2) decide conditional-visibility of vim sub-settings per selected engine (today gated on a bool, easily generalized to 'engine == Vim'); (3) recognize the runtime indicator is a separate, terminal-only, vim-shaped surface (render_vim_status with an exhaustive VimMode match and no selection count) that must be widened/relocated — not a free side effect of the dropdown swap.

I did not exhaustively trace the legacy/agent input render paths beyond the cited gates, but the call-site grep confirms no code-editor status bar.

### A4-Q15 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Cursor shape is derived from vim_mode: Option<VimMode> on the render element (element.rs:203) while users also have an independent CursorDisplayType setting (element.rs:51, editor.rs:192). When a modal mode is active, does the mode's per-submode cursor (block in normal/visual, underline in replace, bar in insert) OVERRIDE the user CursorDisplayType, and does that ownership move into the Step D proposed ModalCursorState?

**A.** Yes — whenever a vim submode is active the per-submode cursor UNCONDITIONALLY overrides the user CursorDisplayType in both editors; the user setting is consulted only in the vim-OFF fallback. The two editors are asymmetric: the terminal EditorElement falls back to the user setting (gated by allow_user_cursor_preference, today set true only at app/src/terminal/input.rs:2840), while the code editor's RichTextElement hardcodes Bar and never threads the user setting in at all. Step D's ModalCursorState (active flag + CursorDisplayType) captures only the active-mode shape; it does NOT encode the precedence-vs-user-setting fallback, which today lives in the element's unwrap_or(...) arm. All independently verified.

CONFIRMED after opening every cited file:line plus uncited corroborating locations.

Two parallel cursor-derivation functions, structurally identical in the mode-active arm but DIVERGENT in the fallback arm — verified these are the ONLY two (grep for VimMode->CursorDisplayType mapping returns exactly element.rs:1537 and mod.rs:966):

CODE EDITOR — crates/editor/src/render/element/mod.rs:963-971 cursor_display_type(): self.vim_mode.map(|m| Normal|Visual(_)=>Block, Replace=>Underline, Insert=>Bar).unwrap_or(CursorDisplayType::Bar). VERIFIED verbatim. The RichTextElement struct (mod.rs:127) carries ONLY vim_mode: Option<VimMode>; its constructor (mod.rs:416-441) takes no cursor-preference param (verified param list: model, parent_view, display_options, display_state, vim_mode, vim_visual_tails). The code editor view passes only self.vim_mode(app) into RichTextElement::new (code/editor/view.rs:2191-2198, verified). So vim-on => mode mapping; vim-off => hardcoded Bar, user setting never reachable.

TERMINAL EDITOR — app/src/editor/view/element.rs:1534-1542 get_cursor_type(): same mode arms, .unwrap_or(self.preferred_cursor_type). VERIFIED. preferred_cursor_type field at element.rs:191; constructor param cursor_display_type at element.rs:229; assignment preferred_cursor_type: cursor_display_type.unwrap_or_default() at element.rs:257. All VERIFIED. The Option comes from self.cursor_display_override at view/mod.rs:8645, which is Some(*editor_settings.cursor_display_type) only when options.allow_user_cursor_preference else None (view/mod.rs:3028-3032). VERIFIED. allow_user_cursor_preference defaults false (view/mod.rs:1489 and 1583, two default builders); repo grep over app/src shows the ONLY true is app/src/terminal/input.rs:2840 (terminal command input). VERIFIED. Additionally (uncited) view/mod.rs:3389-3392 live-updates cursor_display_override from the user setting only when is_some() — corroborates, does not contradict.

CursorDisplayType is an independent user setting (app/src/settings/editor.rs:192-201) whose enum default is Bar (editor.rs:51-56, #[default] Bar). VERIFIED. So Insert==Bar coincidentally equals the default, masking the conflict for default users but silently overriding users who picked Block/Underline.

VimMode is a closed 4-variant enum (crates/vim/src/vim.rs:14-20: Normal, Insert [#[default]], Visual(MotionType), Replace) — NO Select submode. VERIFIED. The doc question's "block in normal/select" example is therefore aspirational; a Helix/Kakoune select row does not exist in code. vim_mode() returns None when vim disabled (view/mod.rs:7642-7650, vim_state gated on vim_mode_enabled), which makes unwrap_or the off-state branch. VERIFIED.

STEP D / ModalCursorState — report .understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:214 proposes changing element field vim_mode: Option<VimMode> to modal: Option<ModalCursorState> (active flag + CursorDisplayType), keeping the fork as if self.modal.is_some(); report:202 has state() return "active flag + submode + desired cursor shape". VERIFIED. This formalizes the is_some() (active) arm only; it does NOT, as written, own the unwrap_or(...) fallback nor declare a per-(engine,submode) precedence vs the user CursorDisplayType. The answer's three consequences (code-editor would change behavior if it began deferring to the user setting; terminal opt-in fallback must be preserved or it regresses; Insert==Bar masks the conflict) all follow correctly from the verified code.

The adversarial framing is correct: today the mode wins almost everywhere (active vim submode overrides user CursorDisplayType in both surfaces; with mode inactive only the terminal command input honors the setting; the code editor never does), and the precedence table is undecided in code.

Minor note on the QUESTION (not the answer): the doc question cites "element.rs:51" for CursorDisplayType, but the enum actually lives at settings/editor.rs:51-56 — the answer correctly resolved this to settings/editor.rs.

_Evidence:_
- Code editor derives cursor from vim_mode and hardcodes Bar when vim is off, never consulting user CursorDisplayType — `crates/editor/src/render/element/mod.rs:963-971` _(code)_
- Code editor RichTextElement struct carries only vim_mode: Option<VimMode> (mod.rs:127); constructor (mod.rs:416-441) takes no cursor-preference param — `crates/editor/src/render/element/mod.rs:127,416-441` _(both)_
- Terminal get_cursor_type maps vim submodes to cursor and falls back to preferred_cursor_type when vim is off — `app/src/editor/view/element.rs:1534-1542` _(code)_
- preferred_cursor_type field (191) is populated from cursor_display_type.unwrap_or_default() (257); constructor param at 229 — `app/src/editor/view/element.rs:191,229,257` _(code)_
- cursor_display_override is Some(user CursorDisplayType) only when allow_user_cursor_preference true, else None; passed into element at 8645 — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:3028-3032,8645` _(code)_
- allow_user_cursor_preference defaults false (1489,1583); repo grep over app/src shows the ONLY true is the terminal command input — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:1489,1583; app/src/terminal/input.rs:2840` _(both)_
- CursorDisplayType is an independent user setting with enum default Bar — `app/src/settings/editor.rs:51-56,192-201` _(code)_
- VimMode is a closed 4-variant enum (Normal, Insert[default], Visual(MotionType), Replace) with no Select submode — `crates/vim/src/vim.rs:13-20` _(both)_
- vim_mode() returns None when vim disabled (vim_state gated on vim_mode_enabled), making unwrap_or the off branch — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:7642-7650` _(code)_
- Code editor view passes only self.vim_mode(app) into RichTextElement::new (no cursor pref) — `app/src/code/editor/view.rs:2191-2198` _(code)_
- Step D proposes element field modal: Option<ModalCursorState> = active flag + CursorDisplayType, keeping is_some fork; state() returns active flag + submode + desired cursor shape — `.understand-anything/keybindings-architecture-report.md:202,214` _(graph)_
- Uncited corroboration: cursor_display_override is live-updated from the user setting only when is_some(), confirming only opted-in editors track CursorDisplayType — `app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:3389-3392` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design declare an explicit per-(engine, submode) cursor precedence table and decide whether Step D's ModalCursorState owns only the active-mode shape or also the user-setting fallback — preventing a silent regression in the terminal command input (which today honors CursorDisplayType when modal is inactive) and a silent behavior change in the code editor (which today never honors it).
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Non-code design decisions, unresolvable from current code: (1) the precedence table per (engine, submode) vs user CursorDisplayType — should any submode (e.g. helix/vim insert) DEFER to the user setting instead of forcing Bar? (2) Should ModalCursorState own only the active-mode shape (status quo) or also subsume the inactive fallback so behavior is unified — unifying would CHANGE the code editor (today never honors the user pref) and must preserve the terminal opt-in (allow_user_cursor_preference) or regress it. (3) Whether a Helix/Kakoune Select submode gets its own cursor row (none exists today). I did not exhaustively confirm that input.rs:2840 is literally the primary command-input editor's options block (file path and uniqueness of the true value make it overwhelmingly likely), and I did not grep crates/ for the field since the EditorViewOptions struct is app-defined.
- _Verification:_ Opened all 11 cited locations directly and confirmed each says exactly what the answer asserts: mod.rs:963-971 (verbatim unwrap_or(Bar)); mod.rs:127 + 416-441 (struct + 6-param constructor, no cursor pref); element.rs:1534-1542 (verbatim unwrap_or(preferred_cursor_type)); element.rs:191/229/257 (field/param/unwrap_or_default assignment); view/mod.rs:3028-3032 (override gated on allow_user_cursor_preference) + 8645 (passed into element); settings/editor.rs:51-56 (#[default] Bar) + 192-201 (setting); vim.rs:13-20 (4 variants, default Insert, no Select); view/mod.rs:7642-7650 (None when off); code/editor/view.rs:2191-2198 (only vim_mode threaded); report:202,214 (ModalCursorState). Re-ran the allow_user_cursor_preference grep over app/src myself: only one literal `true` (input.rs:2840); two default builders set false (1489,1583); 1524 passes options through. Independently checked COMPLETENESS via grep for VimMode->CursorDisplayType mapping and fn defs — exactly two derivation functions exist (element.rs:1534, mod.rs:963); no third path was missed. Inspected one location the answer did NOT cite (view/mod.rs:3389-3392) — it reinforces the model (only opted-in editors track the user setting). No shifted line refs, no app-vs-crates confusion, no overgeneralization, no off-by count. ## Axis 5 — Discoverability & truthful conflicts

### A5-Q2 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** scope

**A.** priv

CONFIRMED. Making a binding's scope visible/conflict-keyable is necessarily an engine change, not a UI-only or group-proxy change. Every premise in the answer is true in source: (1) EditableBindingLens.context is private at keymap.rs:311 (no pub keyword) while siblings name, description, action, trigger, original_trigger, group, id are pub; the backing EditableBinding.context_predicate (keymap.rs:297) and Lens.enabled (keymap.rs:312) are also private. (2) The only scope-shaped public API on the lens is in_context(&Context)->bool, a boolean point-eval via self.context.eval at keymap.rs:787-789; the predicate itself is never handed out (the only methods passing context — as_binding at keymap.rs:766 and as_lens at keymap.rs:741 — are private). (3) CommandBinding (app/src/util/bindings.rs:531-541) has fields name, description, trigger, action, group:Option<BindingGroup>, id — no scope/context field. (4) from_editable_lens (bindings.rs:762-771) copies description, trigger, action, name, group, id and never references lens.context; from_lens (bindings.rs:749-758) likewise discards it. (5) BindingGroup (bindings.rs:797-810) is exactly 12 SEMANTIC buckets (Settings, Close, Navigation, WarpAi, Workflow, Notebooks, Folders, KeyboardShortcuts, AutoUpdate, Notifications, EnvVarCollection, Terminal), and the lens group is an optional &'static str (keymap.rs:316) so many bindings are None and code-editor vs vim-normal are not separated by group — so group cannot key per-context collisions. (6) The conflict map confirms scope is absent end-to-end: ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke,usize> (keybindings.rs:104-107), populated from binding.trigger.clone() only (keybindings.rs:795-800), built from the editable list produced by from_editable_lens via ctx.editable_bindings() (keybindings.rs:754-758); filter_bindings_including_keystroke reads only binding.trigger and binding.description.in_context — NOT binding.name (bindings.rs:625-687). (7) ContextPredicate (keymap/context.rs:9-18) is an arbitrary 7-variant boolean tree (Identifier, Equal, NotEqual, Not, And, Or, Just) with no enumerable scope id. The editor distinction is real: app/src/editor (terminal) and app/src/code/editor both exist and differ only as different ContextPredicates on their bindings — exactly the scope being discarded. Additional independent check the answer did not cite: the sibling BindingLens.context_predicate (keymap.rs:246) is ALSO private, so there is no alternate public scope path on either lens — this strengthens, not weakens, the conclusion that Option A (thread real scope) requires an engine change (make context pub/add accessor at keymap.rs:311, add CommandBinding.scope, populate in from_editable_lens) and Option B (group proxy) is provably wrong. So context-aware conflicts (R6) and the catalog scope column (R9) are an engine refactor.

_Evidence:_
- EditableBindingLens.context is private (no pub) while sibling fields name/description/action/trigger/original_trigger/group/id are pub — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:311` _(code)_
- Lens exposes scope only as in_context(&Context)->bool point-eval via self.context.eval, not as a predicate or scope id — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:787` _(code)_
- CommandBinding has fields name/description/trigger/action/group/id and no scope/context field — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:531` _(code)_
- from_editable_lens copies description/trigger/action/name/group/id and never reads lens.context — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:762` _(code)_
- BindingGroup is exactly 12 semantic categories (Settings..Terminal), not context scopes — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:797` _(code)_
- ConflictMap is HashMap<Keystroke,usize> populated from binding.trigger only, built from from_editable_lens output — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104` _(code)_
- Conflict map populated from binding.trigger.clone() with no context — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:795` _(code)_
- filter_bindings_including_keystroke matches description and trigger only, does not read binding.name — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:625` _(code)_
- ContextPredicate is an arbitrary 7-variant boolean tree with no enumerable scope id — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/context.rs:9` _(code)_
- Sibling BindingLens.context_predicate is also private, closing any alternate public scope path; from_lens discards it — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:246` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ eng
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Which option to adopt (expose ContextPredicate raw and reduce to a comparable scope id — needing an overlap primitive per A5-Q5; vs expose a derived opaque scope id; vs rejected group proxy) is a non-code engine-ownership decision. Unaudited at runtime: how many distinct ContextPredicates exist across editable_bindings() and whether two real scopes share a predicate spelling (needs a startup walk). Fixed/custom-action binding context plumbing is A5-Q6. Note: BindingLens (non-editable path) also carries context_predicate but it is private and from_lens discards it; the settings page uses the editable path regardless, so this does not open a UI-only path.
- _Verification:_ Opened all cited files directly. keymap.rs:307-318 — context at 311 has no pub; name/description/action/trigger/original_trigger/group/id are pub; enabled (312) and backing EditableBinding.context_predicate (297) are private. keymap.rs:787-789 — in_context returns bool via self.context.eval; grep of the EditableBindingLens impl confirms in_context is the only context-touching public method (as_lens/as_binding are private). bindings.rs:531-541 — CommandBinding has exactly name/description/trigger/action/group:Option<BindingGroup>/id, no scope. bindings.rs:749-771 — both from_lens and from_editable_lens omit any context read. bindings.rs:797-810 — counted exactly 12 BindingGroup variants matching the listed names. bindings.rs:625-687 — filter_bindings reads binding.trigger (638) and binding.description.in_context (651) only; the anchor's hint "does it read name?" answered NO, consistent with the answer's "description text and trigger only". keybindings.rs:104-107 ConflictMap=HashMap<Keystroke,usize>; :795-800 populated from binding.trigger.clone(); :754-758 built from ctx.editable_bindings()+from_editable_lens. context.rs:9-18 — exactly 7 ContextPredicate variants. Verified both editor dirs exist (app/src/editor, app/src/code/editor). Checked a NON-cited location: BindingLens struct (keymap.rs:242-255) — its context_predicate (246) is also private, so no alternate public scope path exists on either lens; this reinforces the answer. No shifted line refs, no off counts, no app/-vs-crates confusion, no overgeneralization (answer correctly scopes to the editable-bindings settings path and notes editor distinction). Every grep re-run matched.

### A5-Q3 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** How do read-only FIXED bindings get a stable, persistable catalog id? They are enumerable via get_key_bindings(), but FixedBinding::as_lens hardcodes name: Default::default() and the only identifier is a process-local atomic BindingId assigned in registration order. Do we add a name/stable-id field to FixedBinding (~146 register sites) or synthesize one (e.g. from group+trigger+description)?

**A.** Confirmed core thesis with one count correction. Fixed bindings get NO stable, persistable catalog id today: FixedBinding has no name field, FixedBinding::as_lens hardcodes name: Default::default() (empty &str, keymap.rs:633), and the only id is BindingId(pub usize) minted from a process-local atomic counter (keymap.rs:262-272) that derives no Serialize/Deserialize and is never persisted. The entire edit/persist/editor layer is name-keyed (editable_bindings() only; set_custom_keybinding/update_custom_trigger match by name; UI guards !name.is_empty()), so an empty-name fixed binding cannot be anchored, and from_lens additionally drops every description-less fixed binding. CORRECTION: the constructor-site edit surface is 469, not 456 — the answer omitted FixedBinding::new_per_platform (13 sites, delegates to ::new so also description-less), so ~404 (not 391) fixed bindings would be dropped by from_lens; the 65 survivors (44 custom + 21 empty), the 93-file count, and the 146 register-call count are unchanged. Synthesis from current fields (group=1-of-12, platform/custom-mutated trigger, absent description, build-unstable type_name) is not viable.

VERDICT: corrected. The answer's substance is fully verified by direct reading of the cited files; I fix one undercount that enlarges (not contradicts) the blast radius.

WHAT I VERIFIED (all true):
1) FixedBinding has no name field — fields are trigger, action, command_description: Option<BindingDescription>, context_predicate, enabled_predicate: Option<EnabledPredicate>, group: Option<&'static str>, id: BindingId (keymap.rs:277-286). Constructors new(:511), empty(:536), standard(:553), custom(:573) take no name; all set group:None; new+standard set command_description:None, empty+custom set Some. No with_name builder.
2) as_lens hardcodes name: Default::default() (keymap.rs:633, inside 631-642); BindingLens.name is &str (keymap.rs:243), so Default is the empty string.
3) BindingId(pub usize) (keymap.rs:262) is minted from static NEXT_BINDING_ID.fetch_add (keymap.rs:264-272) in construction order; derive at :261 is Copy/Clone/Debug/Hash/PartialEq/Eq/PartialOrd/Ord — NO Serialize/Deserialize/FromStr. The Serialize import in this file is consumed by Keystroke at :320, not BindingId. Order-assigned => not stable across builds; not persisted anywhere.
4) Enumeration path confirmed: get_key_bindings (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1699 — evidence item 6's path is correct) -> KeyMatcher::get_bindings (matcher.rs:303) -> Keymap::bindings (keymap.rs:454-462) which yields editable lenses first, then fixed_bindings.iter().rev().filter(is_enabled).map(FixedBinding::as_lens). (Answer's snippet omitted the harmless .filter(is_enabled); immaterial.)
5) Name-keyed edit/persist model: editor list built only from ctx.editable_bindings() (keybindings.rs:754) — fixed bindings never enter. set_custom_keybinding -> ctx.set_custom_trigger(name) + write_custom_keybinding(name) (bindings.rs:491-505). update_custom_trigger filters editable_custom_action_bindings + editable_bindings by b.name == name (keymap.rs:422-433) — no fixed path. update_binding_list guards !name.is_empty() && binding.name == name (keybindings.rs:939).
6) from_lens (bindings.rs:749-758) — the only fixed-lens bridge — returns None when lens.description is None and otherwise copies name verbatim (lens.name.to_string() = "") plus the volatile id.
7) Synthesis ingredients weak: BindingGroup has exactly 12 variants (bindings.rs:797-810); trigger is cmd<->ctrl platform-resolved (keymap.rs:914-919) and custom->keystroke mutated at registration (matcher.rs:96); description absent for the description-less constructors; Action::type_name uses std::any::type_name (action.rs:16-18), build-unstable.

GREP COUNTS REPRODUCED EXACTLY: 456 (new 384 / standard 7 / custom 44 / empty 21), 146 register callers, 93 files, 0 .with_command_description.

CORRECTION (the one defect): the answer treats "456 FixedBinding constructor call sites" as the full field-addition surface, but there is a 5th constructor it ignores: FixedBinding::new_per_platform (keymap.rs:497), which has 13 call sites and delegates to Self::new (so command_description:None). Including it, the true constructor surface is 469 sites (still 93 files; the 13 sites are in already-counted files), and the from_lens-dropped (description-less) count is ~404 (391 + 13), not 391. Survivors (65 = custom 44 + empty 21), file count (93), and register-call count (146) are unchanged. This correction strictly enlarges the blast radius and reinforces the conclusion.

CONCLUSION (unchanged): fixed bindings are enumerable but nameless (empty name) with only a volatile, non-serializable BindingId; the name-keyed persistence/editor/conflict layer cannot anchor them; description-gated from_lens drops the vast majority. A stable catalog id requires a deliberate change — add an explicit name/stable-id field (~469 constructor sites / 146 register calls / 93 files) — because synthesis from existing fields is not stable+unique+persistable.

_Evidence:_
- FixedBinding has no name field; fields are trigger/action/command_description:Option/context_predicate/enabled_predicate:Option/group:Option<&'static str>/id:BindingId — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:277-286` _(code)_
- Four named constructors (new:511, empty:536, standard:553, custom:573) take no name; all set group:None; new+standard set command_description:None, empty+custom set Some — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:511-593` _(code)_
- FixedBinding::as_lens hardcodes name: Default::default() (empty &str) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:633` _(both)_
- BindingLens.name is &str, so Default::default() is the empty string — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:243` _(code)_
- BindingId(pub usize) minted from static NEXT_BINDING_ID.fetch_add in construction order; derive line lacks Serialize/Deserialize (Serialize import is used by Keystroke at :320, not BindingId) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:261-272` _(both)_
- Enumeration: Keymap::bindings yields editable lenses then fixed_bindings.iter().rev().filter(is_enabled).map(FixedBinding::as_lens) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:454-462` _(code)_
- get_key_bindings entry point is in warpui_core, returns BindingLens iterator — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1699` _(code)_
- get_bindings delegates to keymap.bindings() — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:303` _(code)_

- Persistence name-keyed: set_custom_keybinding -> set_custom_trigger(name) + write_custom_keybinding(name) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-505` _(code)_
- update_custom_trigger matches editable bindings by b.name == name; no fixed-binding path — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- UI guards !name.is_empty() && binding.name == name before mutating a binding — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:939` _(code)_

### A5-Q4 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Are the binding validators usable on the release-build edit path? is_binding_pty_compliant / is_binding_cross_platform run only through validate_bindings(), which (with register/set_default_binding_validator) is entirely #[cfg(debug_assertions)] (matcher.rs:159-203) and runs at registration, not on set_custom_trigger. Do we lift them into a release-safe AppContext::validate_trigger(name, trigger) reusing the registered validators, and is the inline warning advisory or blocking?

**A.** The validator PREDICATE FUNCTIONS (is_binding_pty_compliant at app/src/util/bindings.rs:894, is_binding_cross_platform at :918) are plain non-cfg-gated pub fns that compile and run in release, so their checking LOGIC is reusable. But the REGISTRY + DISPATCH around them (register_binding_validator / set_default_binding_validator / validate_bindings) is entirely #[cfg(debug_assertions)] at BOTH the AppContext layer (real impl + empty release no-op twin) and the Matcher layer (debug-only, no release twin), so in release the registry is never populated and validate_bindings() is a {} no-op. validate_bindings runs exactly once at startup (platform/app.rs:85) and PANICS on failure; it never runs on the edit path. The save path (confirm_keystroke_editing -> set_custom_keybinding -> set_custom_trigger) performs no validation, and there is no inline pty/cross-platform warning today and no AppContext::validate_trigger anywhere — so advisory-vs-blocking is an unimplemented product decision, and R10 is a real-but-bounded wire-up, not a no-op nor a from-scratch validator engine. Verified independently; the original answer holds.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened every cited file plus one uncited location and every materially load-bearing claim holds; line refs are exact. Only one cosmetic imprecision noted (does not change the verdict).

VERIFIED CODE FACTS:

1) Predicate fns are release-safe. is_binding_pty_compliant (app/src/util/bindings.rs:894) and is_binding_cross_platform (:918) are ordinary `pub fn`, no cfg gate on definition or body, taking `BindingLens` and returning `IsBindingValid`. Bodies are pure: read `binding.original_trigger.unwrap_or(binding.trigger)`, call `trigger_to_keystroke`; pty runs a regex (CONTROL_CHARACTER_KEY_REGEX.is_match) AND an allow-list check (is_pty_non_compliant_binding_allowed, bindings.rs:909); cross-platform returns Yes early when is_mac() (line 919) then checks `keystroke.cmd`. Compile and execute identically in release. IsBindingValid/BindingLens are imported from warpui_core (bindings.rs:13-14). So R10 does NOT need the checking LOGIC re-authored.

2) Registration + run machinery is debug-only at BOTH layers:
   - AppContext::register_binding_validator: real #[cfg(debug_assertions)] impl (crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1567-1575), empty #[cfg(not(debug_assertions))] twin (1577-1582).
   - AppContext::set_default_binding_validator: real (1587-1594) vs empty no-op (1599-1604).
   - AppContext::validate_bindings: real (1608-1611) vs `{}` no-op (1615-1616).
   - Matcher::register_binding_validator (matcher.rs:160, cfg at :159), set_default_binding_validator (:172, cfg :171), validate_bindings (:182, cfg :181) are each #[cfg(debug_assertions)] with NO release twin — they do not exist in release.
   - Matcher fields default_binding_validator (matcher.rs:19) and binding_validators (:22) are NOT cfg-gated (struct always has them), but because release registration is a no-op the registry is always EMPTY in release. Every validator registered at app-init is dead in release.

3) The registered validators are an assertion harness, not a UI warning. The ONLY call to validate_bindings() is at startup in initialize_app (crates/warpui_core/src/platform/app.rs:85), right after init_fn runs registration. It iterates every matching binding and PANICS on the first failure (matcher.rs:200-202: `panic!("Bindings failed validation ...")`). Registration-time, debug-only, fatal — not advisory, not on the edit path.

4) Registration call sites (re-ran grep; all app-init, all no-op in release): default cross-platform validator at app/src/lib.rs:1395; per-view pty-compliant validators at app/src/root_view.rs:262, app/src/pane_group/mod.rs:318, app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:83, app/src/workspace/mod.rs:82. Exactly 4 pty + 1 cross-platform, matching the answer.

5) Edit/save path does NO validation. confirm_keystroke_editing (app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:669-699): when `unsaved_binding` is Some it unconditionally calls set_custom_keybinding(&row.binding.name, &key, ctx) — no has_conflict gate, no validator. set_custom_keybinding (app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-506) calls ctx.set_custom_trigger(name, Trigger::Keystrokes(vec![keystroke])) -> Matcher::set_custom_trigger (matcher.rs:137-141) -> keymap.update_custom_trigger, and persists via write_custom_keybinding. No validator consulted. grep for `validate_trigger` across app/ and crates/ returns ZERO hits — that method does not exist.

6) No inline pty/cross-platform warning exists. The only edit-time feedback is the same-keystroke ConflictMap (keybindings.rs:104-133, HashMap<Keystroke,usize>); has_conflict (:123, count>1) is read only at keybindings.rs:1064 to compute has_conflicting_binding, which feeds render code (render_clicked :219, render_summary :221, conflict_warning :313). It never gates confirm_keystroke_editing. So even the conflict signal is purely advisory/non-blocking; pty/cross-platform validity is surfaced nowhere on save.

CONSEQUENCE FOR R10 (design decision, accurately scoped by the answer): the pure predicate fns are reusable as-is but have signature `fn(BindingLens) -> IsBindingValid`, so calling them on a candidate keystroke needs a tiny signature adaptation / a transient BindingLens. To instead reuse the per-view REGISTERED validators in release you must lift register_binding_validator / set_default_binding_validator out of #[cfg(debug_assertions)] (the struct fields already exist in release) and add a non-panicking per-trigger query (e.g. validate_trigger returning IsBindingValid) invoked in confirm_keystroke_editing before set_custom_keybinding. R10 logic = trivial; R10 wiring = real but bounded — not a free wire-up nor a from-scratch engine.

_Evidence:_
- is_binding_pty_compliant and is_binding_cross_platform are plain pub fns with no cfg gate; bodies pure (original_trigger.unwrap_or(trigger) + trigger_to_keystroke; pty=regex+allow-list, cross-platform=is_mac early-return then keystroke.cmd); run in release — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:894-933` _(code)_
- AppContext::register_binding_validator has a real #[cfg(debug_assertions)] impl and an empty #[cfg(not(debug_assertions))] no-op twin — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1567-1582` _(code)_

- validate_bindings() is invoked exactly once, at startup in initialize_app after init_fn runs registration; grep shows no other call site in app/ or crates/ — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/app.rs:85` _(both)_
- Registration sites are all app-init: cross-platform default at lib.rs:1395; per-view pty at root_view.rs:262, pane_group/mod.rs:318, terminal/view/init.rs:83, workspace/mod.rs:82 (re-ran grep: 4 pty + 1 cross-platform); all no-op in release — `app/src/lib.rs:1395; app/src/root_view.rs:262; app/src/pane_group/mod.rs:318; app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:83; app/src/workspace/mod.rs:82` _(code)_

- No AppContext::validate_trigger (or any validate_trigger) exists; grep across app/ and crates/ returns zero hits — `grep -rn validate_trigger app/ crates/ (no matches)` _(code)_
- Edit-time conflict feedback is the same-keystroke ConflictMap (HashMap<Keystroke,usize>); has_conflict (:123) is read only at keybindings.rs:1064 feeding render code, never gating confirm — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-133; :1064` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design size R10 ('validators on the edit path') correctly: the pty/cross-platform CHECKING LOGIC is already release-safe and reusable (small), but the validator DISPATCH/REGISTRY is compiled out in release and currently fatal-only at startup, so wiring a non-fatal, release-safe validate_trigger(name, trigger) into confirm_keystroke_editing is a real-but-bounded refactor (un-gate registration + add a non-panicking query + call it before set_custom_keybinding) rather than either a free wire-up or a from-scratch validator engine.

### A5-Q6 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Should conflict detection scan fixed bindings and custom-action bindings, not just editable ones? ConflictMap is built only from the editable list shown in the editor, so a user rebind that collides with a FixedBinding (get_key_bindings) or a custom-action binding (custom_action_bindings) reports 'no conflict' — the opposite of truthful.

**A.** Confirmed. Today the conflict map's universe is editable bindings ONLY. It is built and mutated exclusively from ctx.editable_bindings() (which iterates the keymap's editable_bindings Vec, then deduplicates by name+description). Fixed bindings (get_key_bindings) and custom-action bindings (custom_action_bindings) live in separate keymap collections that the conflict map never reads, so a rebind colliding with either reports has_conflict()==false. Whether it SHOULD scan them is a design decision; the code-verifiable part is that it currently does not, and fixed lenses cannot even name a collision because their lens name is empty (name: Default::default()).

I independently opened every cited file and re-ran the greps; all substantive claims hold.

The keymap stores FOUR disjoint binding collections (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:25-37): fixed_bindings (:26), editable_bindings (:27), fixed_custom_action_bindings (:36), editable_custom_action_bindings (:37). Three accessors expose three different universes:
1. editable only — editable_bindings() (keymap.rs:441-447) iterates ONLY self.editable_bindings, filtered to enabled. Reached via matcher.rs:294-296 and AppContext::editable_bindings (core/app.rs:1788-1790).
2. editable + fixed — bindings()/get_bindings (keymap.rs:454-464) chains editable_bindings() with fixed_bindings; exposed by get_key_bindings (core/app.rs:1699-1701 -> matcher.rs:303-305). FixedBinding is documented "This action can't be reconfigured with a custom key binding trigger" (keymap.rs:275-277).
3. custom-action — custom_action_bindings() (keymap.rs:474-484) chains editable+fixed custom action collections, exposed by core/app.rs:1911-1913.

The settings-view ConflictMap is fed from universe #1 only:
- Initial build: on_page_selected snapshots ctx.editable_bindings() (keybindings.rs:754) into self.bindings (after sort+dedup_by name/description at :759-783), then builds conflict_map from self.bindings ... binding.trigger.clone() (keybindings.rs:795-800) via the FromIterator<Option<Keystroke>> impl (keybindings.rs:135-146), which just counts identical Keystrokes.
- All incremental mutations pass only editable-row triggers: remove_keystroke (:596), reset_to_default_keystroke (:620-621), cancel_keystroke_editing (:647-650), set_temporary_keystroke_state (:717-718). conflict_map.update (:110-121) increments/decrements counts; has_conflict returns count>1 (:123-132).
- Consumed at render: has_conflict(&row.binding.trigger) (:1064), gating only the generic warning. A fixed or custom-action collision contributes 0 to the count for that keystroke, so the UI shows "no conflict."

Truthfulness/naming is doubly broken: (a) the universe is wrong, and (b) even extending the scan to fixed bindings cannot name the colliding action because FixedBinding::as_lens carries name: Default::default() i.e. empty string (keymap.rs:631-642, inside impl FixedBinding which begins at :277, before impl EditableBinding at :645). The warning is generic: SHORTCUT_CONFLICT_WARNING_TEXT = "This shortcut conflicts with other keybinds" (keybindings.rs:48).

No existing code does a cross-universe conflict scan: filter_bindings_including_keystroke (bindings.rs:625-687) only matches on binding.trigger (:638) and binding.description (:651) of whatever &CommandBinding iterator the caller supplies (the settings view supplies editable-only self.bindings); it is search, not conflict, and never reads name.

Magnitude (re-verified): grep counts reproduce exactly — 397 FixedBinding::new construction sites and 146 register_fixed_bindings call sites across app/+crates/. Spot-checked user-facing sites: modal.rs:102 (app.register_fixed_bindings(vec![FixedBinding::new(...)) and root_view.rs:426 (app.register_fixed_bindings([) confirmed; menu.rs FixedBinding::new at :1749 (the detailed answer's illustrative "menu.rs:1748" is off by one — not a hard evidence claim). Every one of these is invisible to conflict detection today.

Design residue: to be truthful, conflict detection must union all three universes and give fixed/custom lenses real identity (name/label) so the colliding action can be displayed.

_Evidence:_
- Keymap has 4 disjoint binding collections: fixed_bindings, editable_bindings, fixed_custom_action_bindings, editable_custom_action_bindings — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:25-37` _(code)_
- editable_bindings() iterates ONLY self.editable_bindings, filtered to enabled (matcher.rs:294-296 forwards it) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:441-447` _(both)_
- get_bindings/bindings() = editable_bindings() chained with fixed_bindings; this is what get_key_bindings exposes — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:454-464` _(code)_
- AppContext::get_key_bindings exposes editable+fixed via keystroke_matcher.get_bindings — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1699-1701` _(code)_
- AppContext::custom_action_bindings exposes the custom-action universe (editable+fixed custom) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1911-1913` _(code)_
- AppContext::editable_bindings only forwards the editable universe (used by settings view) — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1788-1790` _(code)_
- ConflictMap is built from self.bindings (sourced from ctx.editable_bindings() at :754, sort+dedup at :759-783) triggers only — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:754-800` _(code)_
- ConflictMap FromIterator just counts identical Keystrokes; has_conflict = count>1; update inc/dec counts — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-146` _(code)_
- All incremental conflict_map.update calls feed only editable-row triggers (remove :596, reset :620-621, cancel :647-650, temp edit :717-718) — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:596,620-621,647-650,717-718` _(code)_
- Conflict consulted only against editable row trigger at render: has_conflict(&row.binding.trigger) — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:1064` _(code)_
- FixedBinding (doc: 'can't be reconfigured', :275-277) lens has empty name (name: Default::default()), so a fixed collision cannot be named — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:631-642` _(code)_
- Conflict warning text is generic and names no action — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:48` _(code)_
- filter_bindings_including_keystroke matches only binding.trigger (:638) and binding.description (:651) of caller-supplied iterator; no cross-universe scan, never reads name — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:625-687` _(code)_
- 397 FixedBinding::new sites and 146 register_fixed_bindings call sites across app/+crates/; spot-checked user-facing modal.rs:102 and root_view.rs:426 register sites (menu.rs FixedBinding::new at :1749, detailed text's :1748 is off by one) — `grep -rn FixedBinding::new --include=*.rs app/ crates/ | wc -l => 397; grep -rn register_fixed_bindings --include=*.rs app/ crates/ | grep -v 'fn register_fixed_bindings' | wc -l => 146` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc fix the conflict-universe decision: conflict detection today is editable-only and silently passes rebinds that collide with fixed or custom-action bindings. To make conflicts truthful (Axis 5 / R6), the engine must union all three keymap collections AND attach displayable identity to fixed/custom lenses so the colliding action can be named — sequencing this as a prerequisite before any "names the colliding action" UI work.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The 'should' is a product/design decision, not a code fact: the design doc must decide (a) whether to union fixed and custom-action bindings into the conflict universe, and (b) whether collisions with non-editable bindings surface as hard conflicts vs. informational warnings (the user cannot rebind a FixedBinding — 'can't be reconfigured', keymap.rs:275). Truthful conflicts require a data-model change: fixed/custom lenses lack a name/label (FixedBinding::as_lens name is empty), so naming the colliding action is impossible without giving those bindings stable user-facing identity. - _Verification:_ I opened and independently verified every cited file:line. keymap.rs:25-37 (4 collections), :441-447 (editable-only iter), :454-464 (editable+fixed), :474-484 (custom-action), :275-277 (FixedBinding doc), :631-642 (FixedBinding::as_lens with name: Default::default() — confirmed this as_lens sits inside impl FixedBinding, since impl EditableBinding only begins at :645). app/core/app.rs:1699-1701, :1788-1790, :1911-1913 accessors all confirmed. matcher.rs:294-296/:303-305 forwarders confirmed (location the answer cited only indirectly — I opened it). Settings view: keybindings.rs:48 (warning text), :104-146 (ConflictMap impl), :754-800 (build from editable bindings + dedup), :596/:620-621/:647-650/:717-718 (all four incremental updates use row.binding.trigger), :1064 (render). bindings.rs:625-687 (filter reads trigger :638 and description :651, never name). One additional nuance I found that does NOT refute the answer: even within the editable universe, the conflict map is built from the deduplicated self.bindings (dedup_by name+description at :782), so the blind spot is if anything slightly larger than 'all editable', never smaller. No claim was overgeneralized to terminal-vs-code-editor scope; no app/-vs-crates confusion.
