### A5-Q7 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** How does conflict detection handle chords and prefix-shadowing once chord capture (G4/R8) lands? ConflictMap keys on a single Keystroke and update() takes one Keystroke, but chords are Vec<Keystroke> and the matcher is first-match-wins so a complete short binding silently shadows any longer chord sharing its prefix. Is prefix-shadow a 'conflict' the UI must surface, and how is ConflictMap rekeyed to Vec<Keystroke> + prefix relationships?

**A.** Confirmed. Today there is zero chord/prefix awareness in conflict handling: the settings-UI ConflictMap is keyed on a single Keystroke (HashMap<Keystroke, usize>, keybindings.rs:104-132) and is fed from CommandBinding.trigger, which collapses any chord to only its FIRST keystroke (trigger_to_keystroke -> keys.first().cloned(), bindings.rs:236). So once real chords exist the map is unsound both ways: distinct prefix-sibling chords (K-C vs K-D) collapse to the same key and are FALSELY flagged as conflicts, while genuine first-match prefix-shadow is never surfaced as a distinct relationship. There is no engine-level conflict/prefix detector at all (matcher only does first-match-wins via starts_with; validate_bindings runs per-binding, debug-only validators). Whether prefix-shadow is a hard conflict vs a warning is a product call; the code fact is the map must be rekeyed from Keystroke to the full Vec<Keystroke> trigger plus an explicit strict-prefix index mirroring the matcher's starts_with check (matcher.rs:326). All evidence line refs verified accurate.

VERDICT: confirmed. I independently opened every cited file:line plus uncited locations; all claims hold exactly.

CURRENT STRUCTURE (settings UI is the ONLY conflict detector in the codebase):
- ConflictMap is `map: HashMap<Keystroke, usize>` — a single Keystroke key (app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-107). `update(&mut self, old: &Option<Keystroke>, new: Option<Keystroke>)` decrements the old key and increments the new (110-121). `has_conflict` returns true iff the count for that exact Keystroke is > 1 (123-132, predicate at 128). Rebuilt at page open from `binding.trigger.clone()` via `FromIterator<Option<Keystroke>>` (135-146; rebuild loop 794-800) and rendered as a warn-colored border (270-276) / bold "shortcut conflict" text (313-324) when `has_conflict(&row.binding.trigger)` is true (1064).
- The model it keys on cannot hold a chord. `CommandBinding.trigger: Option<Keystroke>` is a SINGLE keystroke (app/src/util/bindings.rs:534). It is filled by `from_editable_lens` -> `trigger: trigger_to_keystroke(lens.trigger)` (bindings.rs:765). `trigger_to_keystroke` for `Trigger::Keystrokes(keys)` returns `keys.first().cloned()` (bindings.rs:236) — every keystroke after the first is DISCARDED.
- The engine's real chord type is `Trigger::Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>)` (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:45); `Keystroke` is one key + 5 modifier bools (keymap.rs:320-328). A chord lives in the engine as a Vec but is flattened to its head keystroke the moment it crosses into the settings model.
- The settings UI can also only CREATE single-keystroke triggers today: `set_custom_keybinding` always wraps in a 1-element vec `Trigger::Keystrokes(vec![keystroke.clone()])` (bindings.rs:492-494) and capture is a single `on_keydown` keystroke (keybindings.rs:294-300). Chord capture is the G4/R8 work that does not exist yet.

WHY IT BREAKS UNDER CHORDS (two-sided unsoundness):
- False positives: two distinct chords sharing a first key, e.g. `K C` and `K D`, both collapse to Keystroke `K` via `keys.first()`, so the map counts 2 for `K` and `has_conflict` flags BOTH even though they never collide at runtime.
- False negatives / wrong framing for true shadowing: the runtime matcher is first-match-wins with a prefix early-return — `push_keystroke` iterates ALL bindings, and on `keystrokes.starts_with(&pending.keystrokes) && binding.context_predicate.eval(ctx)` it returns the action immediately when `keystrokes.len() == pending.keystrokes.len()` (matcher.rs:324-345; starts_with at 326; equal-length return at 329-331). I traced this: because the loop visits every binding and only a complete-length match returns, a complete short binding `K` is always reached and returns Action(K), so it ALWAYS wins over a longer chord `K C` regardless of iteration order, leaving `K C` unreachable (silent prefix-shadow). The current ConflictMap cannot represent or label this prefix relationship.

NO ENGINE-LEVEL HELP EXISTS: there is no cross-binding conflict or prefix detector anywhere. `validate_bindings` (matcher.rs:181-203) only runs per-binding validators registered via `register_binding_validator`/`set_default_binding_validator` (matcher.rs:160-177), all `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` and panic-on-fail (panic at 201). The two real validators, `is_binding_pty_compliant` (bindings.rs:894-907) and `is_binding_cross_platform` (bindings.rs:918-933), each take a single `BindingLens`, call `trigger_to_keystroke` (lines 896, 924 — they too see only the first keystroke), and have zero awareness of any other binding. My independent grep for `conflict` across crates/warpui_core/src and app/src (excluding settings_view/keybindings.rs) returns only unrelated uses (cloud_object sync ConflictStatus, presenter position naming, borrow-conflict comments) — confirming the settings ConflictMap is the only keybinding conflict logic.

DESIGN IMPLICATIONS (code-verifiable shape of the fix):
1. Rekey the map off the full trigger, not a head Keystroke: `HashMap<Vec<Keystroke>, usize>` (or a normalized `Trigger`), and stop routing chords through `trigger_to_keystroke`/`Option<Keystroke>` in CommandBinding for conflict purposes.
2. Add an explicit prefix index. Exact-duplicate trigger (same Vec) = the existing count>1 hard conflict. Prefix-shadow = binding A is a STRICT prefix of binding B; detect with the same `starts_with` predicate the matcher already uses (matcher.rs:326), and surface it because it is precisely the silent override this axis targets.
3. Correctness also requires context-predicate awareness, which the map entirely lacks today (it keys on keystroke only, ignoring `context_predicate`; the matcher already gates on `context_predicate.eval(ctx)` at matcher.rs:327). That is the Axis-2/R6 dependency (see A5-Q11).

No code-editor vs terminal-editor divergence here: both share the same settings ConflictMap and the same `push_keystroke` matcher.

_Evidence:_
- ConflictMap is keyed on a single Keystroke (HashMap<Keystroke, usize>); update() takes one Option<Keystroke> and decrements old / increments new; has_conflict is count>1 for that exact key (predicate at :128) — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-132` _(code)_
- ConflictMap is rebuilt at page open from binding.trigger (Option<Keystroke>) via FromIterator (:135-146 def, :794-800 rebuild), and rendered via has_conflict(&row.binding.trigger) (:1064) into a warn border (:270-276) and bold conflict text (:313-324) — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:135-146,270-276,313-324,794-800,1064` _(code)_
- CommandBinding.trigger is a single Option<Keystroke> (:534), set from trigger_to_keystroke(lens.trigger) in from_editable_lens (:765) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:534,765` _(code)_
- trigger_to_keystroke collapses a chord Trigger::Keystrokes(keys) to keys.first().cloned() — dropping all but the first keystroke — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:236` _(code)_
- The settings UI can only create single-keystroke triggers today: set_custom_keybinding always wraps a 1-element vec Trigger::Keystrokes(vec![keystroke.clone()]) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-495` _(code)_
- A chord is Trigger::Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>) (:45) and Keystroke is one key + 5 modifier bools (:320-328) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:44-49,320-328` _(both)_
- Matcher is first-match-wins: push_keystroke iterates ALL bindings, on starts_with (:326) + context_predicate.eval (:327) returns the action immediately when lengths are equal (:329-331); traced to confirm a complete short binding always shadows a longer chord sharing its prefix regardless of iteration order — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:307-346` _(both)_
- No cross-binding conflict/prefix detection in the engine: validate_bindings only runs per-binding validators registered via register/set_default_binding_validator, all #[cfg(debug_assertions)], panic on fail (:201) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:159-203` _(code)_
- Both registered validators take a single BindingLens and route through trigger_to_keystroke (:896, :924), so they see only the first keystroke and have no cross-binding awareness — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:894-933` _(code)_
- Independent grep for 'conflict' across crates/warpui_core/src and app/src excluding settings_view/keybindings.rs returns only unrelated uses (cloud_object ConflictStatus, presenter naming, borrow comments) — confirming the settings ConflictMap is the only keybinding conflict logic; starts_with appears exactly once in matcher.rs (line 326) — `crates/warpui_core/src + app/src (grep)` _(code)_
- Uncited cross-check: filter_bindings_including_keystroke also reads only the single binding.trigger keystroke, reinforcing the single-keystroke model pervasiveness (not a conflict detector) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:625-687` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc specify the chord-era conflict model concretely: rekey ConflictMap from HashMap<Keystroke,usize> to the full Vec<Keystroke> trigger, add a strict-prefix index mirroring the matcher's starts_with (matcher.rs:326), stop flattening chords through trigger_to_keystroke for conflict purposes, and declare prefix-shadow surfacing (conflict vs warning) plus its dependence on Axis-2 context scoping.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Two product/UX decisions are not settleable from code: (a) whether prefix-shadow is presented as a hard conflict (same warn border / blocking as exact duplicates) or a softer 'this longer chord is unreachable' warning; and (b) user-facing copy distinguishing exact-duplicate vs prefix-shadow. There is also a sequencing dependency: a fully truthful chord conflict map needs context-predicate awareness (the current ConflictMap ignores context entirely, while the matcher already gates on context_predicate.eval at matcher.rs:327), which is the Axis-2/R6 work tracked in A5-Q11; chord rekeying alone will still over/under-report across distinct contexts until that lands. Not investigated: whether any built-in/default (non-editable) bindings are already multi-keystroke chords today — if so, the false-positive collapse could already be latent rather than purely future, but no such default was surfaced and the settings creation path emits only single-keystroke triggers.
- _Verification:_ Attempted to refute on every axis the mandate flagged; the answer survived all checks. (1) Line refs: each citation is accurate — ConflictMap struct/update/has_conflict at 104-132, FromIterator 135-146, rebuild 794-800, render 1064, warn border 270-276, conflict text 313-324; trigger_to_keystroke keys.first() at 236; CommandBinding.trigger at 534; from_editable_lens at 765; set_custom_keybinding 1-elem vec at 492-494; Trigger::Keystrokes at keymap.rs:45; Keystroke struct 320-328; matcher push_keystroke 307-346 with starts_with at 326 and equal-length return 329-331; validate_bindings 181-203; validators 160-177; bindings validators 894-933. (2) Overgeneralization: the 'always shadows regardless of iteration order' claim is the strongest assertion, so I hand-traced push_keystroke — the loop visits every binding and only an equal-length match returns, so a complete short binding is always reached and returns Action; a longer chord only sets retain_pending. The claim holds. (3) app/-vs-crates scope: correctly attributed — ConflictMap and trigger_to_keystroke/CommandBinding live in app/, matcher/Trigger/Keystroke in crates/warpui_core. No confusion. (4) Counts/grep: re-ran the conflict grep myself; outside settings_view/keybindings.rs there is no keybinding conflict logic, only unrelated 'conflict' tokens. starts_with occurs exactly once in matcher.rs. (5) Graph-vs-code: the answer notes the graph covers only warpui_core/editor/vim and reads app/ directly; I verified app/ claims by direct read. The only soft framing (false-positive K-C/K-D scenario) is explicitly conditioned on 'once real chords exist', which is correct since set_custom_keybinding currently emits only 1-element vecs. No material error found.

### A5-Q8 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** How does the catalog/search represent one action NAME bound in multiple contexts? A name maps to several bindings with distinct ContextPredicates (editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&str, Vec<usize>>, keymap.rs:30); the editor's dedup_by collapses same-name+same-description rows but NOT same-name/different-description ones (keybindings.rs:782, comment 766-781). Is each (name, context) a separate catalog entry, or one entry with N scopes?

**A.** Neither. The catalog has NO scope/context dimension today — CommandBinding drops the ContextPredicate. The de-facto entry key is (name, description): the settings view dedup_by collapses same-name+same-description context variants into ONE scope-less row, while same-name/different-description variants survive as N separate scope-less rows (linked by name). The resource-center catalog dedups by description only. Search matches on description text and keystroke, never on name.

CONFIRMED against the code. ENGINE (warpui_core/keymap.rs) models name->many-contexts, but the UI flattens it before display:\n- `editable_bindings_by_name: HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>` (keymap.rs:30, exact) maps one name to a Vec of indices of context-distinct editable bindings, populated per-registration at keymap.rs:414-417.\n- `get_binding_by_name` (keymap.rs:379-386) returns only the earliest-registered currently-enabled binding for a name (find_map over the index Vec), not the set.\n- `update_custom_trigger` (keymap.rs:422-433) writes the new trigger to ALL editable bindings whose name matches (filters both editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings by `b.name == name`). With `set_custom_keybinding` (bindings.rs:491-506) persisting `Trigger::Keystrokes(vec![keystroke])` (single-element), remapping is name-keyed and uniform across every context variant — one keystroke per name, not per (name, context).\n\nTHE CATALOG ROW HAS NO SCOPE FIELD. `CommandBinding` (bindings.rs:531-541) carries only name, description, trigger, action, group, id — no ContextPredicate. Both constructors drop the predicate: `from_lens` (bindings.rs:749-758) and `from_editable_lens` (bindings.rs:762-771) read description/trigger/action/name/group/id only. So one-entry-with-N-scopes is not representable today.\n\nSETTINGS-VIEW ENTRY GRANULARITY = (name, description). `on_page_selected` (keybindings.rs:754-784) builds one CommandBinding per editable lens, sorts by description then name (759-765), then `dedup_by(|a,b| a.name == b.name && a.description == b.description)` (782). Consequence (comment 766-781): context variants sharing both name AND description collapse to ONE row; variants sharing a name but differing in description (e.g. find in block vs find in terminal) remain SEPARATE rows. editable_bindings() iterates reverse-registration order (keymap.rs:441-447, `.rev()`).\n\nCONFLICT DETECTION is keyed purely on the keystroke, context-blind: `ConflictMap { map: HashMap<Keystroke, usize> }` (keybindings.rs:104-133); a row is flagged via `conflict_map.has_conflict(&row.binding.trigger)` (keybindings.rs:1064).\n\nSEARCH IS BY DESCRIPTION + KEYSTROKE, NOT NAME. `filter_bindings_including_keystroke` (bindings.rs:625-687) scores against `binding.description.in_context(...)` fuzzy (651) and `binding.trigger` keystroke (638); it never reads `binding.name`.\n\nRESOURCE-CENTER CATALOG is coarser: `build_bindings` (keybindings_page.rs:136-153) filters `trigger.is_some() && non-empty description` (140-146), sorts by description (147-151), then `dedup_by(|a,b| a.description == b.description)` (152) — description-ONLY, so even two different names with the same description collapse. `get_bindings_by_section` lives in keybindings_page.rs:229-284 (NOT utils.rs) and filters by `binding.name` membership (line 248) in static name-only arrays. Those arrays are at utils.rs:8-114 (BLOCKS/INPUT_EDITOR/TERMINAL/FUNDAMENTALS); the 5th, PRIORITIZED_KEYBINDINGS used for the Essentials section, is imported from command_palette (keybindings_page.rs:26), not utils.rs.\n\nNet: there is currently no (name, context) entry and no one-entry-with-N-scopes entry. Entries are (name, description) tuples (description-only in the resource center) with scope dropped; multiple contexts either silently merge (same description) or appear as parallel scope-less rows (different description) that all remap together by name.

_Evidence:_
- Engine maps one name to a Vec of context-distinct editable bindings (HashMap<&'static str, Vec<usize>>) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:30` _(code)_
- Name index populated per-registration (idx pushed onto by-name Vec via entry().or_default().push) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:414-417` _(code)_
- get_binding_by_name returns only the earliest-registered enabled binding for a name (find_map over indices), not the set — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:379-386` _(code)_
- Remapping is name-keyed: update_custom_trigger sets the trigger on ALL editable bindings with b.name == name (both vectors) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:422-433` _(code)_
- editable_bindings() iterates reverse-registration order (.rev()), most-recent highest precedence — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:441-447` _(code)_
- set_custom_keybinding persists one keystroke per name via Trigger::Keystrokes(vec![keystroke]) (single element) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-506` _(code)_
- CommandBinding has no context/scope field (name, description, trigger, action, group, id only) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:531-541` _(code)_
- from_lens and from_editable_lens drop the ContextPredicate; read only desc/trigger/action/name/group/id — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:749-771` _(code)_
- Settings catalog dedup key is (name, description): sort by description then name, dedup_by(name==name && description==description) at :782 — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:754-784` _(code)_
- Comment documents that different-description same-name rows intentionally survive (find in block vs find in terminal) — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:766-781` _(code)_
- Conflict map is keyed only on Keystroke (HashMap<Keystroke,usize>), context-blind — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-133` _(code)_
- Conflict flag computed from row.binding.trigger keystroke only — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:1064` _(code)_
- Search scores on binding.trigger keystroke (:638) and binding.description.in_context fuzzy (:651), never reads binding.name — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:625-687` _(code)_
- Resource-center catalog dedups by description ONLY (:152) and filters trigger.is_some() && non-empty description — `app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:136-153` _(code)_
- Resource-center sectioning filters by binding.name membership (line 248) over static name-only arrays; arrays at utils.rs:8-114 (function get_bindings_by_section itself is at keybindings_page.rs:229-284, not utils.rs) — `app/src/resource_center/utils.rs:8-114` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc state the catalog/search entry model truthfully: today an action name renders as scope-less (name, description) rows (description-only in the resource center), context variants either silently merge or appear as parallel name-linked rows, and conflicts plus search are context-blind. This grounds the R6/L7 decision on whether to add a scope field to CommandBinding and thread ContextPredicate through (Axis-2 dependency) so the catalog can honestly show "its real binding + scope" and so search-by-name returns the right 1-or-N hits.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The question's "How to answer" asks to DECIDE entry granularity and align it with Q1's scope field — a design choice, not a code fact. Code fact: today granularity is (name, description) with no scope (description-only in the resource center). The design must choose among: (a) keep name-as-unit with N scope sub-rows (requires adding a ContextPredicate/scope field to CommandBinding, which from_lens/from_editable_lens currently discard, plus surfacing ContextPredicate from the engine — the Axis-2/R6 dependency); (b) make each (name, context) a first-class entry (breaks name-keyed save/load and the uniform remap in update_custom_trigger); or (c) keep (name, description) tuples but add a human-readable scope label. Whether search should index `name` (it does not today) is also a UX decision. I verified only the keybindings-catalog search path (filter_bindings_including_keystroke); a separate command-palette search path could differ but is out of scope for this question. None settleable from current code.

### A5-Q9 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** How are actions with no keyboard trigger represented in the catalog, search, conflict, and the Unbound filter? CommandBinding.trigger is Option<Keystroke> and trigger_to_keystroke (bindings.rs:234) returns None for Standard/Custom/Empty triggers, so Standard-action and currently-unbound editable bindings have trigger=None. Do they appear in the catalog, are they searchable, and do they (correctly) never conflict?

**A.** Triggerless bindings (trigger=None) behave differently per surface. They are INCLUDED in the settings keybindings catalog (built from editable_bindings, no trigger filter) and remain searchable there by description text, and they correctly never conflict (None is flattened out of the keystroke-keyed ConflictMap and has_conflict(&None) is false). But the cheatsheet/resource-center catalog (built from get_key_bindings = fixed+editable) explicitly drops them via `.filter(|a| a.trigger.is_some() && !desc.is_empty())` at keybindings_page.rs:140-146. There is NO "Unbound" filter today; it is an unimplemented R7 feature, though the data (trigger.is_none()) exists to build it. Verified independently — answer is accurate.

VERIFIED CONFIRMED. I opened every cited file and confirmed each evidence claim at the stated line; I also read two locations the answer did not cite (get_key_bindings/editable_bindings definitions in warpui_core, and from_lens) and they corroborate rather than contradict.\n\nTwo distinct catalogs, opposite policies on triggerless/unbound actions:\n\nSETTINGS catalog (app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:748-784): built from `ctx.editable_bindings()` -> `CommandBinding::from_editable_lens` where `trigger = trigger_to_keystroke(lens.trigger)` (bindings.rs:762-771) and may be None. NO trigger filter in on_page_selected; sort/dedup keys on description+name only (759-782). So currently-unbound editable bindings (trigger=None) and Standard-action bindings (None off-Mac) ARE included, rendering with an empty keystroke cell (render_summary `None => Empty::new().finish()`, keybindings.rs:265-266). Confirmed this catalog enumerates ONLY editable bindings: editable_bindings() (warpui_core core/app.rs:1788) returns editable-only, and its doc at editable_bindings_for_view (1810-1813) explicitly says it returns bindings 'regardless of whether or not they have associated key bindings' — i.e. unbound editables are first-class. It never enumerates FixedBindings.\n\nCHEATSHEET / resource center (app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:136-154): built from `ctx.get_key_bindings()` chained with get_additional_keybindings(), then `.filter(|a| a.trigger.is_some() && !description.is_empty())` (140-146). get_key_bindings (warpui_core core/app.rs:1699, doc 1697) returns 'Editable bindings ... followed by any fixed bindings' = fixed+editable. So every triggerless action vanishes from the cheatsheet. Additional nuance the answer did not state but which only reinforces it: from_lens (bindings.rs:749-758) ALSO returns None for any binding with no description, so description-less fixed bindings are dropped even before the explicit filter.\n\nSEARCH (filter_bindings_including_keystroke, bindings.rs:625-687): empty query yields every binding including trigger=None (634-635); non-empty computes keystroke score via `binding.trigger.as_ref().and_then(...)` (638) so None never scores on keystroke, and fuzzy-matches `binding.description.in_context(...)` (650-653). I confirmed the function never reads `binding.name` (only description and trigger fields are referenced) — this also answers A5-Q15. Net: triggerless bindings remain searchable by description text only. (Edge case not affecting the conclusion: an unbound editable binding with an empty description would be included in the settings catalog but unfindable by text, since the settings path has no description-empty filter.)\n\nCONFLICT (ConflictMap, keybindings.rs:104-145): keyed on Keystroke. FromIterator<Option<Keystroke>> uses `iter.into_iter().flatten()` (139) so every None is dropped; has_conflict(&None) short-circuits to false (130); update (110-121) only mutates Some entries. Populated from `bindings.iter().flatten().map(|b| b.trigger.clone()).collect()` (795-800). So triggerless bindings never conflict and never get the warn border — confirming the question's hypothesis. Corollary holds: multiple distinct unbound actions (all None) do not collide with each other, because None is excluded entirely rather than collapsed to one shared key.\n\nUNBOUND FILTER: none exists. Settings view's only filtering is the free-text query (filter_bindings, keybindings.rs:536-549). I re-ran the greps: `trigger.is_some()` in app/src returns exactly 4 hits — keybindings_page.rs:141 (the cheatsheet catalog filter), keybindings.rs:397 (get_edit_button_row button-state, NOT a catalog filter), and two debug logs (controller.rs:1467, terminal/view.rs:9813). `grep -rni unbound app/src crates` returns only unrelated async unbounded channels and new_unbound editor models — no keybinding filter. R7 Unbound chip is unimplemented; data exists (trigger.is_none()), so it is a small additive filter, not an engine change.\n\nDESIGN GOTCHA holds: no single surface lists ALL bindable actions. Settings includes unbound editables but excludes all fixed; cheatsheet includes fixed+editable but excludes all triggerless. A triggerless FIXED binding (e.g. Standard actions on Linux) appears in neither. So catalog-v1 reusing the cheatsheet build path inherits the trigger.is_some() incompleteness; reusing the settings path misses all fixed bindings (A5-Q4 nameless-fixed problem).

_Evidence:_
- Settings catalog built from editable_bindings via from_editable_lens with no trigger filter (sort/dedup on description+name); rows include triggerless editables, rendered with empty keystroke cell (None => Empty::new().finish()) — `app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:748-784, 265-266` _(code)_
- editable_bindings() returns editable-only and includes bindings regardless of whether they have an associated keystroke (so unbound editables are first-class); settings catalog never enumerates fixed bindings — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1788, 1810-1813` _(code)_

- Cheatsheet catalog built from get_key_bindings (which returns editable then fixed) chained with get_additional_keybindings, then explicitly drops triggerless/empty-desc actions via .filter(|a| a.trigger.is_some() && !desc.is_empty()) — `app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs:136-154` _(code)_

### A5-Q12 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** What stable key and snapshot format does the persisted action manifest (R9, for the change-keybinding agent) use, and how does it reference fixed bindings? Is the key the editable `name`, and are nameless fixed bindings simply unreferenceable until Q4 is resolved?

**A.** Confirmed. The only content-stable key in the code today is the editable binding `name: &'static str` (a compile-time `namespace:action` string), which is already the persistence key for the existing keybindings.yaml manifest (CustomKeybindings = HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>, value a normalized keystroke string). BindingId (process-global atomic counter) and description (dynamically resolvable against live AppContext) are both volatile and unusable as durable keys. Fixed bindings carry no name field; their lens name is Default::default() (empty &str), so they are unreferenceable by any name-keyed manifest and unrebindable today — keying them is gated on Q4. No R9 "action manifest" concept exists in code yet (grep for manifest/snapshot/action_id/action_catalog returns nothing); its schema/regeneration trigger/on-disk location are unbuilt design decisions.

I re-grepped the question block (line 843 of the spec) and independently opened every cited file plus uncited corroborating locations. All ten evidence claims hold at the exact line numbers given; no shifts, no overgeneralization, no app/-vs-crates confusion, and the one count (4 name arrays) is correct.

Verified facts:

1) BindingId is NOT durable. crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:262 `pub struct BindingId(pub usize)`; :264 `static NEXT_BINDING_ID: AtomicUsize = AtomicUsize::new(0)`; :266-272 `BindingId::new()` does `fetch_add(1, Relaxed)`. Counter restarts at 0 each process and depends on registration order, so it cannot survive rebuilds/versions. (Answer cited 261-272 — accurate, slightly wider to include the struct.)

2) The editable `name` is the only stable key and is ALREADY the manifest key. EditableBinding.name: &'static str (keymap.rs:294); lens exposes it (keymap.rs:308; from_editable_lens carries `name: lens.name.into()` at app/src/util/bindings.rs:767). On-disk: app/src/keyboard.rs:167-169 `struct CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>)`, doc-commented (155-166) as "top-level YAML map of (Editable Binding Name) -> Keybinding" with examples `"editor:delete_all_left": cmd-shift-A`; PersistedTrigger is a normalized keystroke string (171-187). Writes go by name: write_custom_keybinding(name,...) does `map.0.insert(name, ...)` (keyboard.rs:64-74); set_custom_keybinding(binding_name,...) -> write_custom_keybinding(binding_name.into(),...) (bindings.rs:491-499). Name lookup/override is editable-only: editable_bindings_by_name index (keymap.rs:30, built 414-417), get_binding_by_name (379-386), update_custom_trigger filters `b.name == name` over editable_custom_action_bindings and editable_bindings only (422-433).

3) Fixed bindings have NO name. struct FixedBinding (keymap.rs:277-286) has fields trigger/action/command_description/context_predicate/enabled_predicate/group/id — no `name` (only EditableBinding has it). FixedBinding::as_lens() sets `name: Default::default()` at keymap.rs:633 (impl block 630-642, distinguishable from EditableBinding's impl which starts at 645; it reads self.command_description, a FixedBinding-only field). So a fixed binding is neither name-keyable nor reboundable today (type doc: "This action can't be reconfigured with a custom key binding trigger", keymap.rs:275).

4) Description can go stale. DynamicDescriptionResolver = Arc<dyn Fn(&AppContext) -> Option<String> + Send + Sync> (keymap.rs:69); BindingDescription.dynamic_override (89); with_dynamic_override (148-154); resolve() applies the live resolver against &AppContext (164-172); also per-context `custom` overrides (83). materialize_description only resolves when has_dynamic_override() else clones (app/src/util/bindings.rs:785-791). A frozen description string would capture a point-in-time label — confirming it must not be the key.

5) No manifest exists yet. grep -niE "manifest|snapshot|action_id|action_catalog" over app/src/util/bindings.rs, app/src/keyboard.rs, app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs, app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs returns nothing. The only persisted artifact is keybindings.yaml (name -> trigger string).

6) Static discoverability catalog is editable-NAMES only. app/src/resource_center/utils.rs has exactly 4 `pub const ... : &[&str]` arrays — BLOCKS_KEYBINDINGS (8-25), INPUT_EDITOR_KEYBINDINGS (27-78), TERMINAL_KEYBINDINGS (80-106), FUNDAMENTALS_KEYBINDINGS (108-114) — all editable names; fixed bindings absent. (There is also a get_additional_keybindings() helper at 118+ for mac-menu-only bindings, which the answer didn't mention but which does not contradict the claim.)

Net: stable key = editable `name` (already proven durable as the keybindings.yaml key, value a normalized PersistedTrigger string); BindingId and description are volatile; fixed bindings are nameless and thus out of scope for a name-keyed manifest until Q4 assigns them stable names. The R9 manifest schema/regeneration trigger/location are genuine design residue, unbuilt in code.

_Evidence:_
- BindingId is a process-global atomic counter minted at registration (fetch_add Relaxed, starts at 0 each process); non-deterministic across runs/versions — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:262-272` _(code)_
- FixedBinding (struct fields trigger/action/command_description/context_predicate/enabled_predicate/group/id) has no name field; only EditableBinding has name: &'static str — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:277-286,294` _(both)_
- FixedBinding::as_lens() sets name: Default::default() (empty &str); the impl is FixedBinding's (reads self.command_description, a FixedBinding-only field), distinct from EditableBinding's impl starting at :645 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:630-642` _(code)_
- Name-based lookup/override covers editable bindings only: editable_bindings_by_name index, get_binding_by_name, update_custom_trigger filter b.name==name over editable collections — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:30,379-386,414-417,422-433` _(code)_
- Existing persisted manifest keybindings.yaml is HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> keyed by editable binding Name, value a normalized keystroke string — `app/src/keyboard.rs:155-187` _(code)_
- set_custom_keybinding persists by binding name via write_custom_keybinding, which does map.0.insert(name, ...) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:491-499; app/src/keyboard.rs:64-74` _(code)_
- Descriptions can be dynamic (Arc<dyn Fn(&AppContext)->Option<String>>) and are resolved against live ctx via resolve(); also per-context custom overrides — a frozen description string can go stale — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:69,83,89,148-172` _(code)_
- materialize_description only resolves when has_dynamic_override(), else clones — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:785-791` _(code)_
- Static discoverability catalog is exactly 4 &[&str] arrays of editable NAMES only (BLOCKS/INPUT_EDITOR/TERMINAL/FUNDAMENTALS); fixed bindings absent — `app/src/resource_center/utils.rs:8-114` _(code)_
- No manifest/snapshot/action_id/action_catalog concept exists in the keybinding code today (grep over bindings.rs, keyboard.rs, settings_view/keybindings.rs, resource_center/keybindings_page.rs returns nothing) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs + app/src/keyboard.rs + app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs + app/src/resource_center/keybindings_page.rs` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design commit to keying the R9 action manifest on the editable binding `name` (the same key already proven durable by keybindings.yaml), store the keystroke as a normalized PersistedTrigger string, treat description as a regenerated non-key label, and explicitly scope fixed bindings OUT of the manifest until Q4 assigns them stable names — avoiding a BindingId- or description-keyed schema that would break across rebuilds.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The concrete R9 manifest is a design decision absent from code: its schema (e.g. name -> {description-snapshot, keystroke, group, platform}), regeneration trigger (build-time codegen vs runtime dump on keymap build), and on-disk location (alongside keybindings.yaml in config_local_dir vs a generated source artifact) are unspecified. Referencing fixed bindings is blocked on Q4 (whether FixedBinding gains a stable &'static str name + a by-name index); until then a name-keyed manifest can only cover editable bindings. Whether to store the (volatile) description as a non-authoritative human label vs omit it is a product call. I did not exhaustively grep the entire app/ tree for an alternate stable-id mechanism beyond the four keybinding-relevant files, but those are the canonical persistence/discoverability sites.
- _Verification:_ I opened every cited file at the exact offsets and confirmed each line reference (no shifts). Specifically re-verified: (a) BindingId atomic at keymap.rs:262-272; (b) FixedBinding struct 277-286 genuinely lacks a name field while EditableBinding has name at 294; (c) the as_lens at 630-642 setting name: Default::default() belongs to FixedBinding's impl (it reads self.command_description, EditableBinding's impl starts at 645) — so the "fixed lenses are nameless" claim is correctly scoped, not an overgeneralization; (d) editable_bindings_by_name field at :30 and update_custom_trigger's b.name==name filter at 422-433 operate on editable collections only; (e) CustomKeybindings = HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> at keyboard.rs:169 with the doc comment confirming Name->Keybinding format; (f) materialize_description at 785-791 only resolves on has_dynamic_override. I independently RE-RAN the grep for manifest/snapshot/action_id/action_catalog across the four keybinding files — it returns nothing (confirming claim 5), and counted the name arrays in utils.rs — exactly 4 (matching the answer). I also checked an uncited corroborating location: from_editable_lens (bindings.rs:762-771) carries name: lens.name.into() into CommandBinding, reinforcing that the name is the through-line identifier for editable bindings, whereas CommandBinding::placeholder and the fixed lens both use Default::default() for name. The code anchor's question "does filter_bindings_including_keystroke read name?" — verified NO: it matches on description.in_context and trigger keystroke (bindings.rs:625-687), which does not contradict any answer claim. ### A5-Q13 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Regression strategy for conflict truthfulness and catalog completeness vs ~265 imperative register sites: a golden test that non-overlapping contexts (id!(Vim) vs !id!(Vim), actions.rs:51-60) produce NO conflict while a same-context collision DOES, and a test that every get_key_bindings() binding appears in the catalog with a resolvable id. How to keep these from rotting across 109 files?

**A.** CONFIRMED. No conflict-truthfulness test and no catalog-completeness test exist today. The settings conflict detector (ConflictMap, app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-145) is context-blind: it is a HashMap<Keystroke, usize> keyed solely on the trigger and never reads the context predicate, so the proposed non-overlapping-contexts golden test (id!("Vim") vs !id!("Vim")) would FAIL against today's code until detection is made context-aware. The catalog is split between an auto-derived Resource Center list (which filters out no-trigger / empty-description bindings and dedups by description) and hand-maintained static name-only lists with no resolvability test; the only existing debug-time net is per-binding validators (PTY-compliance / cross-platform), not conflict or completeness checks. Every evidence claim's file:line was independently verified and every count re-ran exactly.

I independently opened every cited file plus uncited corroborating locations (matcher.rs validate_bindings call path in core/app.rs, get_key_bindings delegation, validator cfg-gating). All material claims hold.

(1) CONTEXT-BLIND CONFLICT DETECTION — CONFIRMED. app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs:104-145: ConflictMap { map: HashMap<Keystroke, usize> }. update (110-121), has_conflict (123-132, true when count > 1) and FromIterator (135-145) all key exclusively on Keystroke; the context_predicate is never consulted. Populated at 794-800 from binding.trigger.clone(); consumed at 1064 via view.conflict_map.has_conflict(&row.binding.trigger). Two editable bindings on the same keystroke in non-overlapping contexts would be a false-positive conflict. The golden non-overlap assertion therefore requires making detection context-aware first; it is not merely a missing test. Verified by reading lines 104-145, 748-800, 1050-1074.

(2) LIST BUILT FROM EDITABLE ONLY, DEDUPED BY NAME+DESC — CONFIRMED. keybindings.rs:754 builds from ctx.editable_bindings().collect(); 782 dedup_by(|a,b| a.name==b.name && a.description==b.description). So fixed bindings never enter the conflict map.

(3) actions.rs VIM FORK IS FIXED, NOT EDITABLE — CONFIRMED. app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:50-110 is app.register_fixed_bindings([FixedBinding::new(...)]) with `& !id!("Vim")` vs `& id!("Vim")` forks (lines 54/59, 64/69, 74/79, 84/89, 104/109). Being fixed, the Vim fork is excluded from the editable-only settings conflict map — the answer's nuance (these specific example bindings escape incidentally; the real false-positive risk is editable same-keystroke pairs) is correct.

Corroborated by core/app.rs: register_binding_validator and set_default_binding_validator each have a debug def + a release stub (1568/1578, 1588/1600), and validate_bindings is debug-real (1609) vs release no-op (1616), invoked from platform/app.rs:85. These check individual-binding properties, never pairwise conflicts or catalog membership.

(7) CLOSEST COMPLETENESS ANALOG — CONFIRMED. app/src/util/bindings_tests.rs:76-116: test_orchestration_cycle_bindings_are_editable and test_terminal_page_scroll_bindings_are_editable each use App::test + ctx.editable_bindings().find(name) on two hard-coded names apiece (4 names total), spot-checks not live-set coverage.

## Axis 6 — Cross-cutting foundation (platform-in-data-model)

### A6-Q4 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** What consumes Keystroke's derived serde beyond the normalized()-string paths? Specifically: does settings cloud-sync serialize QuakeModeSettings.keybinding: Option<Keystroke> via the struct form or via to_file_value() (the normalized string)? The keymap.rs:333 comment claims serde uses the default struct form for cloud sync and other in-memory consumers.

**A.** Confirmed. Two persisted/synced consumers use Keystroke's STRUCT serde form (not the normalized string): the platform-native store (UserDefaults/registry) and cloud sync when settings.toml is disabled. The settings.toml file, keybindings.yaml, and telemetry all use the normalized string and are insulated. The keymap.rs:333 comment is correct: a struct-shape change to Keystroke is a breaking change to a synced/persisted format — QuakeModeSettings.keybinding and ActivationHotkeyKeybinding are both cloud-synced (Globally) — not just to keybindings.yaml. The read path is dual-tolerant (parses string OR struct), but Keystroke has NO serde(default), so an old struct-form payload missing a newly added field fails to deserialize and the setting silently resets to default unless the new field is #[serde(default)]/Option.

All 15 evidence claims verified at their cited file:line ranges, plus one independent cross-check the answer did not cite.

TWO serialization paths for Keystroke, selected by storage backend, plus a dual-tolerant read path:

1. SETTINGS FILE PATH (insulated, normalized string): Keystroke::SettingsValue.to_file_value = serde_json::Value::String(self.normalized()), from_file_value = Keystroke::parse (keymap.rs:345-354). derive(SettingsValue) on QuakeModeSettings recursively calls to_file_value per field and bypasses serde (settings_value/src/lib.rs:44-49; settings_value_derive/src/lib.rs:282-291 — verified: `SettingsValue::to_file_value(&self.#ident)` per non-skipped field). write_to_preferences for the settings file uses to_file_value (settings/src/lib.rs:486-492); read uses from_file_value (lib.rs:421-451). On disk in settings.toml the keybinding is the string "cmd-shift-a".

2. STRUCT SERDE PATH (NOT insulated): For the platform-native store, write_to_preferences uses serde_json::to_string(new_value) — the full struct form with per-modifier booleans (lib.rs:493-498), and reads with serde_json::from_str (lib.rs:454-470). Matches the crate doc "Cloud sync and platform-native stores (UserDefaults, registry) continue using serde directly" (settings_value/src/lib.rs:11-13) and the keymap.rs:330-333 comment.

CLOUD SYNC: cloud_preferences_syncer reads the locally-stored serialized string via read_local_setting_value and pushes it as-is (cloud_preferences_syncer.rs:677, 710-716, 751 Preference::new(storage_key, local_value, ...)). read_local_setting_value (manager.rs:224-250) reads the settings file (toml_key) when FeatureFlag::SettingsFile is enabled, else the platform/private store (storage_key). So the cloud receives the normalized string when SettingsFile is ON and the struct form when SettingsFile is OFF (or for private settings). Either way the struct serde form is a live persisted format in the platform-native store and reaches the cloud whenever the file feature is disabled.

WHO IS CLOUD-SYNCED (exactly two Keystroke-bearing settings): QuakeModeSettings { keybinding: Option<Keystroke>, ... } derives Serialize+Deserialize+JsonSchema+SettingsValue (mod.rs:324-338) and is registered SyncToCloud::Globally(RespectUserSyncSetting::Yes), private:false (keys_settings.rs:14-23). ActivationHotkeyKeybinding: Option<Keystroke> is also Globally synced, private:false (keys_settings.rs:42-50). Independent check: app/src/settings/ai.rs only constructs a Keystroke at runtime from the VoiceInputToggleKey enum (the registered/synced type is the enum, not Keystroke), so it is NOT a third synced Keystroke consumer — the "exactly two" claim stands.

INSULATED CONSUMERS: keybindings.yaml stores PersistedTrigger(String) built from Keystroke::normalized() (keyboard.rs:167-187), unaffected by struct changes. Telemetry KeybindingChanged serializes keystroke.normalized() (events.rs:3148-3149), also insulated. The other keystroke: Option<Keystroke> fields (modal.rs:41, toast_stack.rs:366, status_bar.rs:103-107, etc.) are in-memory UI render state, never serialized.

RECEIVE/PARSE IS DUAL-TOLERANT: update_fn, load_fn, and equals_fn all try from_file_value (string) first, then fall back to serde_json::from_str (struct) (macros.rs:864-938). Registration stores BOTH a serde-struct default and a file-form default (macros.rs:845-859).

MIGRATION HAZARD (sound): Keystroke's derive (keymap.rs:320-328) has NO serde container attributes — no #[serde(default)], no deny_unknown_fields. Adding a field (e.g. cmd_or_ctrl) without #[serde(default)] makes every previously-persisted struct-form payload (UserDefaults values, and cloud values written by SettingsFile-disabled clients) fail to deserialize: from_file_value(string parse) returns None on a JSON object, the serde fallback then errors on the missing field, and the setting silently resets to default. Adding it as #[serde(default)]/Option is forward/backward compatible (serde ignores unknown fields by default; default supplies the missing field).

_Evidence:_
- Keystroke derives Clone/Debug/Eq/PartialEq/Hash/Default/Serialize/Deserialize with bool modifier fields + String key; NO serde container attrs (no default/deny_unknown_fields) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:320-328` _(code)_
- Comment: 'Serde continues to use the default struct form for cloud sync and other in-memory consumers' — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:330-333` _(code)_
- Keystroke SettingsValue impl: to_file_value = normalized() String, from_file_value = Keystroke::parse — the file-only path — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:345-354` _(code)_
- Crate doc: cloud sync and platform-native stores (UserDefaults, registry) continue using serde directly; SettingsValue consulted only for the settings file — `crates/settings_value/src/lib.rs:11-13` _(code)_

- settings_value derive emits SettingsValue::to_file_value(&self.#ident) per non-skipped field (confirms recursion, not serde) — `crates/settings_value_derive/src/lib.rs:282-291` _(code)_
- write_to_preferences: settings file uses to_file_value (string); otherwise serde_json::to_string(new_value) — struct form — `crates/settings/src/lib.rs:476-498` _(code)_
- read_from_preferences mirrors the split: from_file_value for file, serde_json::from_str otherwise — `crates/settings/src/lib.rs:411-471` _(code)_
- QuakeModeSettings derives Serialize+Deserialize+JsonSchema+SettingsValue; field keybinding: Option<Keystroke> — `app/src/settings/mod.rs:324-338` _(code)_
- QuakeModeSettings registered SyncToCloud::Globally(RespectUserSyncSetting::Yes), private:false — IS cloud-synced — `app/src/terminal/keys_settings.rs:14-23` _(code)_
- ActivationHotkeyKeybinding: Option<Keystroke> also Globally cloud-synced, private:false — `app/src/terminal/keys_settings.rs:42-50` _(code)_

- read_local_setting_value reads settings file (toml_key) when SettingsFile enabled, else platform/private store (storage_key) — `crates/settings/src/manager.rs:224-250` _(code)_
- Registration stores BOTH serde-struct default and file-form default; update/load/equals parse from_file_value first then serde fallback (dual-tolerant) — `crates/settings/src/macros.rs:845-938` _(code)_
- keybindings.yaml stores PersistedTrigger(String) via Keystroke::normalized() — insulated — `app/src/keyboard.rs:167-187` _(code)_
- Telemetry KeybindingChanged serializes keystroke.normalized() (string) — insulated — `app/src/server/telemetry/events.rs:3148-3149` _(code)_
- ai.rs uses Keystroke only via VoiceInputToggleKey::keystroke() (runtime construct); registered/synced type is the enum, not Keystroke — no third synced Keystroke consumer — `app/src/settings/ai.rs:216-240,709-883` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets A6-Q2 decide the canonical Keystroke representation knowing the true blast radius: any struct-shape change to Keystroke is a change to a cloud-synced + UserDefaults-persisted format (QuakeModeSettings.keybinding and ActivationHotkeyKeybinding), so it must be additive (#[serde(default)] or Option) or carry an explicit migration, and cannot be treated as safe merely because keybindings.yaml is string-based.

I confirmed the code branch (manager.rs:224-250) but did not determine the flag's current rollout stage, so I cannot state whether most production clients currently emit struct-form or string-form to Warp Drive. Regardless, the struct form is unconditionally written to/read from the platform-native store, and historical/legacy synced values may already be struct-form, so a struct change is migration-sensitive. Whether to add cmd_or_ctrl (A6-Q2 option a) and how to gate it (#[serde(default)] vs versioned migration) is a design choice; the code only mandates that any new struct field be #[serde(default)]/Option-shaped to avoid breaking previously-persisted struct-form values.

### A6-Q5 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** Four platform-divergence mechanisms: how many are pure cmd-ctrl swaps vs genuine key/chord divergence; pick the canonical model and how the other three lower in.

**A.** cmdorctrl is the only pure cmd-ctrl swap mechanism; the large majority of platform-divergence sites are genuine key/chord divergence, so the canonical model must be a full per-platform chord pair.

All five mechanism/behavior claims verified against code. (1) cmdorctrl (keymap.rs:914-919) sets cmd=true on Mac else ctrl=true, rest of the keystroke identical -> pure cmd<->ctrl swap by construction; 140 raw grep hits = 139 sites excluding the parser match arm. The Keystroke struct (keymap.rs:320-328) has only ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta/key, NO cmdorctrl field, so it is resolved at parse time and the inactive platform's keystroke is never stored. (2) cmd_or_ctrl_shift (bindings.rs:863-888) = cmd-{key} on Mac vs ctrl-shift-{UPPER(key)} elsewhere -> genuine full-keystroke divergence (adds shift, uppercases key, debug-asserts on non-lowercase). 100pct genuine. (3) with_mac_key_binding / with_linux_or_windows_key_binding (keymap.rs:688-713) NOOP on the inactive OS; counts verified exactly at 101 + 56 = 157. (4) new_per_platform (keymap.rs:497-508) selects one of {mac, linux_and_windows} at construction; 13 sites verified. OperatingSystem::get() (platform/mod.rs:668-683) is compile-time cfg(target_os/family) on native and a wasm OnceLock initialized from the browser user agent via get_or_init (wasm.rs:55-71) with NO runtime setter, so only one platform branch ever runs and the inactive side is never constructed/parsed/stored. SlashCommandTrigger::cmd_or_ctrl_enter (slash_commands/mod.rs:90-103) is a bool flag, correctly excluded from the census. CORRECTION: the cmd_or_ctrl_shift count of 181 (taken from the spec block) is inflated -- 182 raw occurrences minus the definition includes ~17 use-imports and ~80 references inside crates/integration test code; actual binding-registration call sites (cmd_or_ctrl_shift( minus the definition) number ~140. This drops the aggregate from ~490 to ~449 sites and the pure/genuine split from 31/69 to roughly 34/66, but does NOT change the conclusion: cmdorctrl is the only pure cmd-ctrl swap, the majority of sites are genuine divergence, and because the inactive platform side is never constructed the editor must store a full per-platform chord pair (mac + other Keystroke lists) as the canonical model, with cmdorctrl lowering in as cmd/ctrl sugar and cmd_or_ctrl_shift lowering in as (cmd-X, ctrl-shift-Xupper).

_Evidence:_
- cmdorctrl is a pure cmd<->ctrl swap by construction (cmd=true on Mac else ctrl=true, rest of keystroke unchanged); 140 raw hits = 139 sites excluding the parser match arm — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:914-919` _(both)_
- Keystroke struct has only ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta/key and no cmdorctrl field, so cmdorctrl is resolved at parse time and the inactive platform keystroke is never stored — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:320-328` _(code)_
- cmd_or_ctrl_shift is genuine full-keystroke divergence: cmd-{key} on Mac vs ctrl-shift-{UPPER(key)} elsewhere (adds shift, uppercases). Count: 182 raw occurrences, ~140 actual call sites (NOT 181 binding sites; 181 includes use-imports and crates/integration test references) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:863-888` _(both)_
- with_mac/with_linux_or_windows key binding NOOP the inactive OS; new_per_platform picks one string at construction. Counts: with_mac 101 + with_linux 56 = 157; new_per_platform 13 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:497-508,688-713` _(both)_
- OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time cfg on native and a wasm OnceLock from user-agent via get_or_init with NO runtime setter; only one platform branch runs and the inactive side is never constructed — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/mod.rs:668-683 and crates/warpui_core/src/platform/wasm.rs:55-71` _(both)_
- SlashCommandTrigger::cmd_or_ctrl_enter is a bool flag/constructor, not a Keystroke builder, correctly outside the census — `app/src/terminal/input/slash_commands/mod.rs:90-103` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Rejects a cmdorctrl-only canonical model (only ~31pct of sites). R5 must store a full per-platform chord pair for the ~69pct genuine divergence; since the inactive side is never constructed, the editor needs both platform sides stored explicitly.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ The pure-vs-genuine split inside with_mac/with_linux (~19pct pure) is from sampling paired sites, not exhaustive enumeration -- unverified here but flagged honestly in the original. The exact aggregate percentages depend on whether one counts grep occurrences (181) or true binding-registration call sites (~140) for cmd_or_ctrl_shift; I used the latter. The canonical-model choice and codemod plan are A6-Q2/Q9/Q10 design decisions, not code facts.
- _Verification:_ Opened all cited files and one extra location (slash_commands/mod.rs, wasm.rs) the answer referenced but did not fully cite. Re-ran every count via grep: with_mac=101, with_linux=56, new_per_platform=13 match exactly; cmdorctrl=140 raw hits reconciles to the answer's 139 sites once the keymap.rs:914 parser match arm is excluded. The one real discrepancy: cmd_or_ctrl_shift has 182 raw occurrences (181 minus definition), but breaking it down by file shows ~80 occurrences live in crates/integration test code and ~17 are use-import statements; actual binding-registration call sites (grep 'cmd_or_ctrl_shift(' minus the pub fn definition) = 140. The spec block and the answer report 181 as binding sites, which over-counts. I verified the qualitative divergence semantics of all four mechanisms directly in source -- cmdorctrl truly only swaps cmd/ctrl per token (pure), the other three perform whole-keystroke/whole-chord substitution (genuine). The Keystroke struct having no cmdorctrl field and the wasm OnceLock having no setter both independently confirm the 'inactive side never constructed' claim that drives the unblocks. Corrected the count; the conclusion stands.

### A6-Q6 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Can the data model express a binding whose mac and non-mac sides differ in the KEY itself (not just the primary modifier) — the cmd_or_ctrl_shift pattern 'cmd-[' (mac) vs 'ctrl-shift-{' (other) — and can such a pair be displayed and conflict-checked on a platform where only one side was ever constructed at registration?

**A.** No on both counts. The runtime data model (Keystroke/Trigger) stores only a single, already-resolved chord for the CURRENT platform — no field for a second platform's chord and no cmdorctrl marker. cmd_or_ctrl_shift and the with_mac/with_linux noop builders only ever construct the ACTIVE platform's side; the inactive side's string is never formatted, never parsed into a Keystroke, so it cannot be displayed or conflict-checked.

VERIFIED AGAINST CODE (every cited file:line opened; all six grep counts re-run and reproduced exactly).

1) The divergence is real and is the key, not just the modifier. cmd_or_ctrl_shift(key) (app/src/util/bindings.rs:863-888) returns a single String: on mac format!(\"cmd-{key}\"), otherwise ctrl-shift-{KEY} with the key uppercased (and a debug panic if a non-letter key is passed). So the non-mac side differs from the mac side by (a) primary modifier (cmd vs ctrl), (b) an ADDED shift, and (c) a transformed key (f -> F; special chars must diverge entirely, the documented cmd-[ vs ctrl-shift-{ at bindings.rs:854-861, which the function cannot auto-derive and forces a hand-written mac/non-mac if/else). Confirmed: 181 call sites. Sampled sites are predominantly single lowercase letters (d=25, t=23, c=21, f=15, w=14, ...) plus specials (\"enter\" x4, \"alt-o\" x1) — all produce a non-mac chord whose key/shift differs from the mac chord.

2) The data model cannot hold both sides. Keystroke (keymap.rs:320-328) = {ctrl, alt, shift, cmd, meta, key: String} — resolved booleans + one key string; no cmdorctrl field, no platform field. cmdorctrl is collapsed at parse time into cmd or ctrl (keymap.rs:914-919). Trigger (keymap.rs:44-49) is Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>) — a single resolved sequence, no {mac, other} variant. A full-keystroke {mac:'cmd-[', other:'ctrl-shift-{'} pair has no home in the runtime model.

3) Only the active platform's side is ever constructed. OperatingSystem::get() (platform/mod.rs:668-683) is a cfg_if: compile-time on native (linux/freebsd -> Linux, macos -> Mac, windows -> Windows, else Other(None)) and runtime-once on wasm (wasm::current_platform, wasm.rs:55-60, reading PLATFORM: OnceLock<OperatingSystem> at wasm.rs:8 from the user agent). One platform at runtime. Consequences: cmd_or_ctrl_shift only formats the taken branch; with_mac_key_binding / with_linux_or_windows_key_binding (keymap.rs:688-713) NOOP on the wrong platform so the inactive string is never passed to with_key_binding and Keystroke::parse (keymap.rs:719-723) is never run on it (101 with_mac sites, 56 with_linux_or_windows); FixedBinding::new_per_platform (keymap.rs:497-508) selects keystroke.mac OR keystroke.linux_and_windows and discards the other (PerPlatformKeystroke, keymap.rs:487-493, carries both raw &'static str only at the constructor boundary; 13 new_per_platform sites).

4) Therefore display and conflict-check of the inactive side are impossible. normalized()/displayed() (keymap.rs:970-1014) operate on the single resolved self Keystroke; is_binding_cross_platform (bindings.rs:918-933) and is_binding_pty_compliant (bindings.rs:894-907) inspect only the one active/registered Keystroke (via trigger_to_keystroke / original_trigger). The other platform's chord was never parsed into a Keystroke, so there is no object to render or feed into conflict detection. Confirms the adversarial angle: it is not a cmd<->ctrl swap; the whole chord (key + shift) changes, and the noop/branch builders mean the inactive chord never becomes a Keystroke.

REPRODUCED COUNTS (from repo root): cmd_or_ctrl_shift=181; with_mac_key_binding=101; with_linux_or_windows_key_binding=56; new_per_platform=13; cmdorctrl (excl keymap.rs)=138; PerPlatformKeystroke=31 — all match the original answer exactly.

Minor imprecision found (non-material): the original's parenthetical describes OperatingSystem::get() as cfg on \"linux/macos/windows\"; the actual cfg_if also groups freebsd with Linux and has an Other(None) fallback, and checks target_family=\"wasm\" first. This does not change the conclusion (still exactly one platform at runtime).

_Evidence:_
- Keystroke stores resolved modifier booleans + one key string; no cmdorctrl field, no platform field — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:320-328` _(code)_
- Trigger::Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>) holds a single resolved sequence; no {mac,other} variant — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:43-49` _(code)_
- cmd_or_ctrl_shift returns a single String resolved at call time: cmd-{key} on mac else ctrl-shift-{KEY} uppercased, debug-panic for non-letter; documented manual cmd-[ vs ctrl-shift-{ example — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:840-888` _(both)_
- cmdorctrl token resolved to cmd or ctrl at parse time via OperatingSystem::get(); never retained on Keystroke — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:914-920` _(code)_
- OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time on native (cfg_if linux/freebsd->Linux, macos->Mac, windows, else Other) and wasm runtime; one platform at runtime — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/mod.rs:668-683` _(code)_
- On wasm PLATFORM is a OnceLock resolved once from the user agent via get_or_init — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/wasm.rs:8,55-60` _(code)_
- with_mac_key_binding / with_linux_or_windows_key_binding NOOP on the wrong platform; only with_key_binding runs Keystroke::parse, so the inactive string is never parsed — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:688-726` _(code)_
- FixedBinding::new_per_platform picks mac or linux_and_windows and discards the other; PerPlatformKeystroke holds both raw &'static str only at the constructor boundary — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:487-508` _(code)_
- normalized()/displayed() operate on the single resolved self Keystroke — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:970-1014` _(code)_
- is_binding_pty_compliant / is_binding_cross_platform inspect only the single resolved/active Keystroke — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:894-933` _(code)_
- 181 cmd_or_ctrl_shift call sites; sampled sites are mostly single lowercase letters (d/t/c/f...) plus specials enter, alt-o — `grep -rn cmd_or_ctrl_shift --include=*.rs app crates | grep -v 'pub fn cmd_or_ctrl_shift' | wc -l = 181` _(code)_
- 101 with_mac_key_binding, 56 with_linux_or_windows_key_binding, 13 new_per_platform, 138 cmdorctrl (excl keymap.rs), 31 PerPlatformKeystroke call sites — all reproduced — `grep counts across app+crates` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Confirms R5's expressive (full-keystroke) form cannot ride the current flat-string model or the cmd<->ctrl substitution assumption: the inactive platform's chord is never materialized, so editing/displaying/conflict-checking BOTH platforms requires a structured per-platform record (pulling Axis 6 partly downstream of Axis 2's schema), while a cmdorctrl-modifier-only slice can ship on the flat string in Phase 1. This sizes the 181-site cmd_or_ctrl_shift mechanism as the decisive case for the split-vs-monolith call (A6-Q10).
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Whether to model the divergent pair as a structured {mac, other} record (which A6-Q9 indicates needs Axis 2's record schema, since the flat HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger> file can carry a cmdorctrl token but not a full-keystroke pair) versus synthesizing the inactive side on demand, and how a synthesized side would compute the non-uniform cases (bracket->brace, added shift, uppercasing) — these are A6-Q2/A6-Q9/A6-Q10 design/cross-team decisions, not code-verifiable here. Also (A6-Q7): cmd has a separate meta field and cmdorctrl never maps to meta/super, so any cmd->ctrl-only synthesis would be lossy for real Linux meta bindings. I verified the runtime model claims directly; I did not exhaustively audit every persisted/settings serialization path, but the SettingsValue impl (keymap.rs:345-354) round-trips a single normalized() string, consistent with the single-chord conclusion.
- _Verification:_ Opened and read every cited location: keymap.rs:320-328 (Keystroke struct), :43-49 (Trigger), :487-508 (PerPlatformKeystroke/new_per_platform), :688-726 (with_mac/with_linux_or_windows/with_key_binding+parse), :905-920 (cmdorctrl parse), :970-1014 (normalized/displayed); bindings.rs:840-933 (cmd_or_ctrl_shift doc+body, is_binding_pty_compliant, is_binding_cross_platform); platform/mod.rs:668-683 (get()); wasm.rs:8,55-60 (PLATFORM OnceLock + current_platform). Independently re-ran all six greps: 181/101/56/13/138/31 — every count matches the original exactly. Also checked locations the answer did not cite: normalized()/displayed() (confirmed single-self), the sibling cmd_or_ctrl_enter() in slash_commands/mod.rs:95 (a slash-command modifier returning Self, unrelated to keybinding key divergence — does not contradict), and sampled the actual cmd_or_ctrl_shift argument distribution (confirms lowercase letters that get uppercased+shift on non-mac, plus specials enter/alt-o). No shifted line refs, no app-vs-crates scope confusion, no overgeneralization beyond the runtime model (the original honestly scopes the runtime model and flags the persisted flat-string nuance in residual_unknowns). Only nit: the cfg description of get() slightly simplifies the real cfg_if (freebsd grouped with Linux, Other(None) fallback, wasm checked first) — immaterial to the conclusion.

### A6-Q7 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** Keystroke carries SEPARATE cmd AND meta fields (keymap.rs:325-326); normalized() emits 'meta-' and displayed() renders cmd as 'Logo' on non-mac, yet cmdorctrl maps only to cmd-or-ctrl, never meta/super. When we synthesize the linux/windows view of a mac cmd-X binding, do we produce ctrl-X even though real Linux bindings legitimately use meta/super — and is that mapping lossy?

**A.** Confirmed in substance. Yes — `cmdorctrl` is the only cross-platform primary-modifier token and is hard-wired to cmd(mac)/ctrl(non-mac) (keymap.rs:914-919), so a mac `cmd-X` synthesized for Linux/Windows always becomes `ctrl-X`, never meta/super; no `cmdorsuper`/`cmdormeta` token exists (0 sites). It is an expressiveness gap, NOT a round-trip data loss: `meta` is an orthogonal, losslessly parsed/normalized field never conflated with cmd or ctrl. The adversarial premise is partly false for THIS codebase: the debug validator `is_binding_cross_platform` (app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933) forbids cmd=true on non-mac, so there is no real Linux Super/Logo binding to lose today, and `meta` is the mac Option-as-Meta key (derived from Alt at app/src/lib.rs:566-567), authored explicitly, never produced by cmd→ctrl synthesis. Only correction: the "53 meta- sites" figure is imprecise — raw grep over app/+crates = 58, and the three named editor files sum to 35; the substantive point (real un-gated bindings use the meta token in those editors) is verified.

VERDICT: corrected (every substantive claim holds; one evidence count is imprecise and is fixed below).

INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED CODE FACTS:

1. Keystroke has separate cmd and meta bools — keymap.rs:322-327 (ctrl 322, alt 323, shift 324, cmd 325, meta 326, key 327). parse() recognizes ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta tokens at keymap.rs:909-913 and cmdorctrl at 914-920. CONFIRMED.

2. cmdorctrl is the ONLY cross-platform primary-modifier primitive and resolves to exactly cmd-or-ctrl: keymap.rs:914-919 `if OperatingSystem::get()==Mac { cmd=true } else { ctrl=true }`. No meta/super branch. Grep for cmdormeta|cmdorsuper|cmdorlogo|superorcmd = 0. So synthesizing the Linux/Windows view of a mac `cmd-X` via this token ALWAYS yields `ctrl-X`, never meta and never a distinct Logo/super. CONFIRMED.

3. normalized() emits `meta-` at keymap.rs:984-986; displayed() renders cmd as ⌘(mac)/`Logo`(non-mac) at 1024-1031 and meta always as `Meta` at 1032-1034. CONFIRMED (answer's corrected line refs 1024-1034 are exact; the spec question's own ":984-1030" is loose but the answer fixed it).

4. OperatingSystem::get() is cfg_if compile-time-fixed per target_os; only the wasm arm reads it dynamically — platform/mod.rs:669-683 calls wasm::current_platform(), and wasm.rs:38/55-68 reads navigator().user_agent(). No runtime platform override for native builds. CONFIRMED.

5. No runtime "render this binding for the other platform" function exists: grep for for_platform/to_platform/resolve_for_os/render_for/displayed_for/for_os/with_platform returns only unrelated render_form*/render_fork* helpers and warp_completer's for_os() (completer context, not keystrokes). The cross-platform editor the question anticipates does not exist yet. CONFIRMED.

6. Lossiness — META: orthogonal, not a cmd→ctrl conflation. Set only by the explicit `meta` token (keymap.rs:913) or at runtime by reinterpreting Alt/Option as Meta when "Option as Meta" is enabled (app/src/lib.rs:566-567 `keystroke.alt=false; keystroke.meta=true`). Round-trips losslessly via parse↔normalized. cmdorctrl never reads/writes meta. CONFIRMED.

7. Lossiness — SUPER/Logo: live ModifiersState has only alt/cmd/shift/ctrl/func, no meta (event.rs:72-82); physical KeyCode::SuperLeft/SuperRight = "The Windows, ⌘, Command, or other OS symbol key" (platform/keyboard.rs:220-223) — so on Linux/Windows the Super key feeds cmd. But is_binding_cross_platform (app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933) returns IsBindingValid::No for ANY keystroke.cmd==true on non-mac, so real Linux/Windows Super/Logo bindings are deliberately disallowed; the `Logo` arm of displayed() is a defensive edge render path. No real Linux super binding exists to silently lose today. CONFIRMED.

8. Per-platform builders are the only way to author divergent full keystrokes and CAN carry meta losslessly on the non-mac side: new_per_platform (keymap.rs:497-508), with_mac_key_binding (688-697), with_linux_or_windows_key_binding (701-713), all parse via with_key_binding (715-726). Real un-gated examples: terminal `meta-f/meta-b/meta-d` FixedBindings (app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:420-457) and `cmdorctrl-meta-{left,right,up,down}` in app/src/pane_group/mod.rs:325-344 (comment at 323-324). CONFIRMED.

REPRODUCED COUNTS (2026-06-13, app/ + crates/, --include=*.rs): cmdormeta|cmdorsuper|cmdorlogo|superorcmd=0 (match), cmdorctrl=140 (match), cmd_or_ctrl=228 (match), with_mac_key_binding=102 (match), with_linux_or_windows_key_binding=57 (match), new_per_platform=14 (match).

THE ONE CORRECTION — meta- literal count: the answer claims "53 literal meta- sites across the terminal editor app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:420-457, the code editor app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:180-418, and notebooks app/src/notebooks/editor/view.rs." Raw grep `meta-` over app/+crates = 58 (includes tests, comments, and the normalized() emitter). The three NAMED files sum to 19+8+8 = 35, not 53. (58 minus the 4 pane_group cmdorctrl-meta sites and the 1 normalized() emitter = 53, which is the likely derivation, but it is mis-attributed to the three editor files which total 35.) All 19 hits in editor/view/mod.rs are real un-gated bindings (meta-f at 420, meta-b at 430, meta-d at 445, plus meta-f/b/a/e via with_key_binding), so the substantive claim — real bindings author meta explicitly — stands; only the number/attribution is imprecise.

CONCLUSION (unchanged): cmd→ctrl synthesis is decisive and one-directional (cmd↔ctrl only). It is NOT lossy in the data-model round-trip sense (meta is a distinct, lossless, never-conflated field). It IS an expressiveness gap: no portable single-token way to say "cmd on mac, super(Logo) on Linux" or "cmd on mac, meta on Linux." The forward risk is purely about a future cross-platform editor — today non-mac cmd is treated as invalid (is_binding_cross_platform) rather than rendered as Logo/super, and any meta-bearing non-mac side must be authored via the explicit per-platform builders.

_Evidence:_
- cmdorctrl resolves only to cmd(mac) or ctrl(non-mac); no meta/super branch — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:914-919` _(code)_
- Keystroke has separate cmd(325) and meta(326) bool fields; parse handles ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta tokens at 909-913 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:322-327,909-913` _(code)_
- normalized() emits 'meta-' (984-986); displayed() renders cmd as Logo on non-mac (1024-1031) and meta always as 'Meta' (1032-1034) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:984-986,1024-1034` _(code)_
- No cmdorsuper/cmdormeta/cmdorlogo/superorcmd combinator token exists anywhere — `grep -rn 'cmdormeta|cmdorsuper|cmdorlogo|superorcmd' app/ crates/ --include=*.rs | wc -l => 0` _(code)_
- OperatingSystem::get() is compile-time-fixed via cfg_if per target_os; only the wasm arm reads navigator().user_agent() — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/mod.rs:669-683; crates/warpui_core/src/platform/wasm.rs:38,55-68` _(code)_
- meta modifier at runtime is Alt/Option reinterpreted under 'Option as Meta' (keystroke.alt=false; keystroke.meta=true), not the Super key — `app/src/lib.rs:556-568` _(code)_
- meta documented as 'meta in place of alt' for the mac 'Left/Right Option is Meta' settings; cmdorctrl-meta-{left..down} FixedBindings follow — `app/src/pane_group/mod.rs:323-344` _(code)_
- Live ModifiersState has alt/cmd/shift/ctrl/func only — no meta field; physical Super/Windows/Command key maps to cmd — `crates/warpui_core/src/event.rs:72-82; crates/warpui_core/src/platform/keyboard.rs:220-223` _(code)_
- is_binding_cross_platform (debug validator) returns IsBindingValid::No for any keystroke.cmd==true on non-mac, so non-mac Super/Logo bindings are disallowed — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933` _(code)_
- Per-platform builders are the only way to author divergent full keystrokes: new_per_platform(497-508), with_mac(688-697), with_linux_or_windows(701-713); all parse via with_key_binding(715-726) so carry meta losslessly. Counts: with_mac=102, with_linux=57, new_per_platform=14 — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:497-508,688-726; grep counts` _(code)_
- CORRECTED COUNT: raw 'meta-' over app/+crates = 58, not 53; the three named editor files sum to 35 (editor/view/mod.rs=19, code/editor/view/actions.rs=8, notebooks/editor/view.rs=8). All 19 editor/view/mod.rs hits are real un-gated bindings (meta-f@420, meta-b@430, meta-d@445) — `grep -rn 'meta-' app/ crates/ --include=*.rs | wc -l => 58; per-file: app/src/editor/view/mod.rs=19` _(code)_
- cmdorctrl token appears at 140 string-literal sites; cmd_or_ctrl underscore builders at 228 sites — `grep -rn 'cmdorctrl' => 140; grep -rn 'cmd_or_ctrl' => 228` _(code)_
- No runtime per-platform/other-OS keystroke render function exists (matches are render_form*/render_fork* and warp_completer for_os(), all unrelated) — `grep -rniE 'fn (for_platform|to_platform|resolve_for_os|render_for|displayed_for|for_os|with_platform)' app/ crates/ --include=*.rs` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Confirms R5's unresolved model can keep meta as an orthogonal, lossless modifier (no cmd↔meta conflation needed) and that the cmdorctrl token covers ONLY cmd↔ctrl. So a Phase-1 cmdorctrl slice can ship without touching meta semantics; expressing "cmd on mac, super/meta on Linux" is out of scope for the single-token form and must be deferred to the explicit per-platform/record schema. It also tells the design that the cross-platform editor must decide the policy for the currently-forbidden non-mac cmd (Logo/super) case before claiming 'edit both platforms'.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Whether a future cross-platform editor SHOULD let users bind the Linux Super/Logo key independently (currently forbidden by is_binding_cross_platform) or expose meta as a distinct primary modifier is a product/architecture decision, not code-determined. If yes, the model needs a new combinator token (cmdorsuper/cmdormeta) or a structured {mac,other} record plus relaxing the cmd-on-non-mac validator. Exact 'real binding' vs test/comment breakdown of the 58 meta- sites was spot-checked (editor/view/mod.rs:19 all real) but not exhaustively classified across all files. All reproducible counts are 2026-06-13 snapshots and will drift as the tree changes.

Confirmed OperatingSystem::get()@669-683 is cfg_if compile-time and only wasm reads user agent (wasm.rs:38/55-68). Confirmed app/src/lib.rs:566-567 (alt→meta), pane_group/mod.rs:323-344, event.rs:72-82 (no meta in ModifiersState), keyboard.rs:220-223 (Super = OS symbol key → cmd), and is_binding_cross_platform@918-933 (No for cmd on non-mac). The ONLY discrepancy: the "53 meta- sites" figure is not reproducible — raw grep = 58 over app/+crates, and the three named editor files total 35 (19+8+8); 53 ≈ 58 minus the 4 pane_group cmdorctrl-meta and 1 normalized emitter, so it is a defensible-but-mis-attributed number. This is non-load-bearing: the substantive claim (real un-gated bindings author the meta token explicitly in those editors, e.g. meta-f/meta-b/meta-d at editor/view/mod.rs:420-457) is fully verified.
