### A6-Q8 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** For multi-keystroke chords, can platform divergence occur PER-KEYSTROKE within the chord, or only as a whole-chord swap? How does the chosen unresolved model and the on-disk string (PersistedTrigger space-join, keyboard.rs:180) represent a chord where only the SECOND keystroke differs across platforms?

**A.** Confirmed. Two independent divergence mechanisms exist at different levels: the `cmdorctrl` token diverges PER-KEYSTROKE (each chord token resolves independently at parse time) but only for the cmd↔ctrl swap; the `with_mac`/`with_linux_or_windows`/`new_per_platform` family is a WHOLE-CHORD source-string swap that can express arbitrary divergence (incl. only-second-keystroke-differs). There is NO unresolved model in the data: `Keystroke` has no cmdorctrl field (resolved eagerly via OperatingSystem::get()), and `normalized()`/PersistedTrigger emit only resolved ctrl/cmd — so a chord whose 2nd keystroke differs across platforms cannot be persisted as a single unresolved entry; it collapses to the running OS's resolved string. Every code anchor and the audit counts (102/57/14 whole-chord, 140 cmdorctrl) verified exactly. Only nuance: those grep totals each include the one definition line, so true call sites are ~1 fewer per family (~170 vs the stated 173) — does not change the conclusion.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently opened every cited file:line and re-ran every grep. The answer's central thesis and all evidence claims hold; the line references are accurate (at most off by the closing brace).

VERIFICATION OF EACH CLAIM:

1) Keystroke struct (keymap.rs:320-328) — VERIFIED EXACTLY. Fields are `ctrl, alt, shift, cmd, meta, key` (lines 322-327). No `cmdorctrl`/`cmd_or_ctrl` field. Confirms eager resolution: nothing unresolved is stored in-memory.

2) cmdorctrl per-token resolution (keymap.rs:914-920) — VERIFIED. The parser branch `"cmdorctrl" => { if OperatingSystem::get() == OperatingSystem::Mac { cmd = true } else { ctrl = true } }` resolves at parse time. (Substance is 914-919; closing brace at 920 — immaterial.) Doc at :871 confirms semantics ("cmd on Mac, ctrl on Linux/Windows").

3) Chord built per-token (keymap.rs:519-523 `new`, 715-726 `with_key_binding`) — VERIFIED. Both do `.split_whitespace().map(|key| Keystroke::parse(key)...).collect()` into `Trigger::Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>)`. Because each token is parsed independently, `cmdorctrl` composes per chord element — so `cmd-k cmdorctrl-d` → `[cmd-k, cmd-d]` (Mac) / `[cmd-k, ctrl-d]` (Linux), divergence on ONLY the 2nd keystroke. Per-keystroke divergence IS expressible in the SOURCE string, limited to cmd↔ctrl.

4) Whole-chord swap (keymap.rs:688-713 with_mac/with_linux_or_windows; 497-508 new_per_platform; 488-493 PerPlatformKeystroke{mac, linux_and_windows}) — VERIFIED. All three select one complete space-separated string by `OperatingSystem::get()`; the inactive platform's string is never parsed. Expresses arbitrary divergence (key or non-cmd/ctrl modifier in the 2nd keystroke) that cmdorctrl cannot — but compile-time only, not in the Keystroke/Trigger data model, not persistable.

5) Matcher platform-blind, per-element (matcher.rs:307-346) — VERIFIED. `push_keystroke` pushes one already-resolved Keystroke into `pending.keystrokes` (321) and prefix-matches via `keystrokes.starts_with(&pending.keystrokes)` (326). No cmdorctrl/platform notion; resolution is entirely upstream.

6) normalized() drops cmdorctrl (keymap.rs:970-993) — VERIFIED EXACTLY. Emits only ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta then the key; NO branch re-emits `cmdorctrl`. So serialization is lossy w.r.t. the unresolved token.

7) On-disk round-trip (app/src/keyboard.rs:176-213) — VERIFIED. Write: `PersistedTrigger(keystrokes.iter().map(Keystroke::normalized).join(" "))` (180) → space-joined RESOLVED per-OS strings. Read: `TryFrom` splits whitespace (198) and `Keystroke::parse`s each token (199) — it COULD parse a `cmdorctrl-d` token if present (parse handles it), but the writer never emits one. Net: a chord whose 2nd keystroke diverges is stored only in running-OS resolved form, indistinguishable from a deliberate single-platform binding; cannot store a {mac,other} pair. Confirmed.

8) OperatingSystem::get() fixed (platform/mod.rs:668-683; wasm.rs:55-71) — VERIFIED. Native: compile-time via cfg(target_os/target_family). Wasm: `current_platform()` memoizes in `PLATFORM.get_or_init(...)` (a OnceCell) reading the user agent once. Not runtime-switchable; the inactive platform cannot be rendered by flipping get(). Reinforces A6-Q11 coupling.

9) AUDIT COUNTS — RE-RAN, MATCH EXACTLY: with_mac_key_binding=102, with_linux_or_windows_key_binding=57, new_per_platform=14, cmdorctrl=140. cmdorctrl definitional occurrences confirmed = exactly 2 in keymap.rs (:871 doc, :914 parser) → 138 binding usages. Whole-chord family raw total = 173.

ONE PRECISION NUANCE (does not change conclusion): the raw grep totals 102/57/14 each include the single function/struct DEFINITION line as a match, so true CALL sites are ~1 fewer per family → ~170 whole-chord call sites, not 173. The answer subtracted definitional occurrences for cmdorctrl (140→138) but labeled the whole-chord numbers "sites" without the same subtraction. Even corrected (~170 whole-chord call sites vs 138 cmdorctrl usages), the conclusion stands: full-keystroke/whole-chord divergence is comparable-to-larger than the cmd/ctrl-only token mechanism — a first-class, heavily-used case, not an edge case.

ANSWER STANDS: Per-keystroke divergence is possible (cmdorctrl resolves per token; whole-chord swap also possible). But the data model stores nothing unresolved, so a chord where only the 2nd keystroke differs cannot be persisted as a single unresolved entry: (a) the cmdorctrl token expresses it in a SOURCE string for cmd/ctrl only, yet normalized()/PersistedTrigger collapse it to the resolved OS form on write; (b) any non-cmd/ctrl 2nd-keystroke difference is expressible only via the compile-time whole-chord {mac,other} swap, which is not in the data model and not persistable. Faithful on-disk representation therefore requires either a normalize-surviving per-token cmdorctrl (cmd/ctrl-only) or a structured per-keystroke / {mac,other} record (the Axis 2 schema of A6-Q9). The flat resolved string carries neither.

_Evidence:_
- Keystroke struct has fields ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta/key and NO cmdorctrl field — no unresolved divergence stored in-memory — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:320-328` _(code)_
- cmdorctrl token resolves PER-TOKEN eagerly at parse time to cmd (Mac) else ctrl, via OperatingSystem::get(); doc at :871 confirms semantics — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:865-920` _(code)_
- A chord is built by split_whitespace + Keystroke::parse each token into Trigger::Keystrokes(Vec<Keystroke>), so per-token cmdorctrl composes per chord element (2nd-keystroke-only divergence expressible in source) — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:519-525, 715-726` _(code)_
- with_mac/with_linux_or_windows swap the ENTIRE source string by OS; inactive platform's string never parsed — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:688-713` _(code)_
- new_per_platform picks one whole string from PerPlatformKeystroke{mac, linux_and_windows} by OS — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:488-508` _(code)_
- normalized() emits only ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta then key — no cmdorctrl branch, so the unresolved token cannot be re-emitted — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:970-993` _(code)_
- PersistedTrigger write = map(Keystroke::normalized).join(" ") (resolved per-OS strings); read-back TryFrom splits whitespace and Keystroke::parses each (could parse cmdorctrl token but writer never emits one) — `app/src/keyboard.rs:176-213` _(code)_
- Matcher push_keystroke pushes one resolved Keystroke into pending.keystrokes and prefix-matches via starts_with — platform-blind, per-element — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:307-346` _(code)_
- OperatingSystem::get() compile-time fixed on native via cfg(target_os/target_family); wasm current_platform() memoizes user-agent OS in PLATFORM.get_or_init OnceCell — not runtime-switchable — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/mod.rs:668-683; crates/warpui_core/src/platform/wasm.rs:55-71` _(code)_
- Audit re-run matches exactly: with_mac_key_binding=102, with_linux_or_windows_key_binding=57, new_per_platform=14 (whole-chord raw total=173, ~170 true call sites after subtracting the 3 definition lines); cmdorctrl=140 total, exactly 2 definitional in keymap.rs (:871 doc, :914 parser) -> 138 usages — `grep -rn over repo --include=*.rs for each symbol; cmdorctrl defs confirmed at keymap.rs:871,914` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design pick a chord-divergence representation: the cmdorctrl token already gives per-keystroke cmd/ctrl divergence but does NOT round-trip through normalize/disk and cannot express non-cmd/ctrl differences; any chord whose individual keystrokes diverge beyond cmd↔ctrl (or must persist faithfully) requires a structured per-keystroke or {mac,other} record — confirming R5's expressive form is coupled to the Axis 2 file schema rather than shippable on the flat resolved string.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Which unresolved model to CHOOSE (per-token cmdorctrl that survives normalize, vs a structured per-keystroke {mac,other}, vs a whole-chord {mac,other} record) is a design decision, not a code fact. cmd→ctrl is also lossy on Linux w.r.t. the separate meta/super modifier (A6-Q7 angle), and the on-disk read-back cannot distinguish a resolved 'cmd-d' from a deliberate mac-only override (A6-Q12) — both product/schema decisions downstream of and coupled to the Axis 2 record schema (A6-Q9). I did not exhaustively enumerate every one of the ~170 with_mac call sites to confirm how many actually differ beyond cmd/ctrl (the answer's claim that full-keystroke divergence is heavily used is supported by the magnitude of the counts, not by per-site inspection).
- _Verification:_ I opened all cited files at the exact line ranges and re-ran all four audit greps myself. Findings: (1) Keystroke struct 320-328 — exact, no cmdorctrl field. (2) cmdorctrl parser branch — substance at 914-919, closing brace 920 (immaterial). (3) split_whitespace+parse in both `new` (519-523) and `with_key_binding` (719-723) — exact. (4) with_mac 688-697 / with_linux_or_windows 701-713, new_per_platform 497-508, PerPlatformKeystroke 488-493 — all exact. (5) normalized() 970-993 — confirmed only ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta, no cmdorctrl re-emit. (6) keyboard.rs PersistedTrigger 176-187 write via Keystroke::normalized join, 189-213 read via split_whitespace+parse — exact; I additionally confirmed the read path WOULD parse a cmdorctrl token if present (parse handles it) but the writer never emits one, so the round-trip is lossy as claimed. (7) matcher 307-346 — confirmed per-element starts_with prefix match, platform-blind. (8) OperatingSystem::get() 668-683 cfg-based; wasm.rs current_platform 55-71 uses PLATFORM.get_or_init (OnceCell) — confirmed compile-time/memoized, not runtime-switchable. (9) Grep counts reproduced EXACTLY: 102/57/14/140; cmdorctrl has exactly 2 definitional lines in keymap.rs. One nuance worth flagging, not a refutation: the whole-chord grep totals each include the symbol's own definition line, so true call sites are ~1 fewer per family (~170 not 173); the answer subtracted definitional occurrences for cmdorctrl but not for the with_mac family. The sizing conclusion (whole-chord/full-keystroke divergence comparable-to-larger than cmd/ctrl-only) is unaffected. ### A6-Q9 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Can platform-in-data-model ship on the current flat HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> file (keyboard.rs:169, single normalized string), or is it gated behind Axis 2's record-based schema (R3b {name, trigger, context?, platform?}) because a flat string can carry a 'cmdorctrl-X' token but cannot express a full {mac:'cmd-[', other:'ctrl-shift-{'} pair?

**A.** The expressive full {mac,other} pair form CANNOT ship on the flat file: PersistedTrigger is a single string keyed by binding name (app/src/keyboard.rs:169,174), so one name has exactly one value slot — it cannot hold two distinct platform keystrokes. Only a narrow cmdorctrl-modifier slice could ride the flat string, and even that is read-only today: Keystroke::parse accepts the token but resolves it to cmd/ctrl at parse time via OperatingSystem::get() (keymap.rs:914-919), the Keystroke struct has no cmdorctrl/platform field (keymap.rs:320-328), and the sole write path PersistedTrigger::from → Keystroke::normalized() emits only resolved ctrl-/cmd- (keyboard.rs:180; keymap.rs:970-993). So the full-pair form is gated behind Axis 2's record-based schema (R3b); only the cmdorctrl slice is near-independent, and it still needs a write-path/representation change to round-trip. Verified independently; verdict confirmed.

I independently opened every cited file plus the uncited read path (keyboard.rs TryFrom) and re-ran all audit greps. Every structural claim, line reference, and example holds.

CONFIRMED CODE FACTS\n\n1. Flat file = single-string-per-name. app/src/keyboard.rs:169 `struct CustomKeybindings(std::collections::HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>)`; :174 `struct PersistedTrigger(String)`; doc comment :171-172 ("The normalized version of a keystroke or series of keystrokes ... each is separated by a space"). One value slot per binding name — confirmed. A {mac:'cmd-[', other:'ctrl-shift-{'} pair needs two distinct sequences; the flat string has nowhere for the second.\n\n2. cmdorctrl is read-only, not round-trippable. keymap.rs:914-920 — the `\"cmdorctrl\"` parse arm immediately resolves to cmd (if OperatingSystem::get()==Mac) else ctrl. Keystroke struct (keymap.rs:320-328) has exactly {ctrl, alt, shift, cmd, meta, key} and NO cmdorctrl/platform field — intent is discarded at parse. The ONLY write/serialize path is `impl From<UserDefinedKeybinding> for PersistedTrigger` (keyboard.rs:176-187), which at :180 maps via `Keystroke::normalized`. `normalized()` (keymap.rs:970-993) emits only resolved ctrl-/alt-/shift-/cmd-/meta- with no cmdorctrl branch. I also opened the uncited READ path `TryFrom<PersistedTrigger>` (keyboard.rs:189-199), which calls `Keystroke::parse` per token — corroborating that the app can READ a hand-authored cmdorctrl-x but cannot WRITE/round-trip one.\n\n3. Runtime sees one platform. OperatingSystem::get() (platform/mod.rs:669-683) is compile-time fixed per native build via cfg_if (linux/freebsd→Linux, macos→Mac, windows→Windows); on wasm it is a OnceLock<OperatingSystem> (platform/wasm.rs:8) fixed once per run. The inactive side cannot be resolved at runtime.\n\n4. The paired shape exists only as a compile-time builder. PerPlatformKeystroke {mac, linux_and_windows} (keymap.rs:488-493) and FixedBinding::new_per_platform (keymap.rs:497-508) hold a full pair but collapse to one keystroke at construction via OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() (:502-507); never persisted paired. This is the R3b record shape the on-disk schema lacks.\n\nAUDIT (re-run from /home/mhb/warp, exact):\n- with_mac_key_binding (excl keymap.rs def): 101\n- with_linux_or_windows_key_binding (excl keymap.rs def): 56\n- with_key_binding(\"cmdorctrl ... : 45 binding-definition sites\n- total cmdorctrl token usages: 140 (the answer said 138 — minor, immaterial; the difference is 2 occurrences such as the parse match-arm/comment; the load-bearing 45 binding-def count is exact).\nbuilder defs at keymap.rs:688-697 (with_mac) and :701-713 (with_linux_or_windows). The pairs genuinely differ by key AND modifier, which cmdorctrl (cmd<->ctrl swap on same key) cannot express: app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:304-305 `alt-left`/`ctrl-left`; :370-371 `cmd-left`/`home`; :568-569 `cmd-backspace`/`ctrl-y`. All three verified verbatim.\n\nCONCLUSION (unchanged from original). The flat HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger> can carry a cmdorctrl-X token on read but cannot (a) round-trip-write it today, nor (b) express a full {mac,other} pair at all (one string slot per name). Platform-in-data-model in its expressive form is therefore gated behind Axis 2's record-based schema (R3b {name, trigger, context?, platform?}); only the narrow cmdorctrl-modifier-swap slice is near-independent, and the audit shows that slice covers the minority (45 sites) while the full-pair case dominates (101 + 56 sites). Axis 6's "independent" label holds only for the cmdorctrl slice, not for the expressive majority.

### A6-Q12 — CORRECTED · confidence: high

**Q.** keybindings.yaml persists normalized RESOLVED strings ('cmd-d' on mac, 'ctrl-d' on linux; keyboard.rs:176-187, round-tripped via Keystroke::normalized). When platform-in-data-model lands, how do we (a) emit the unresolved form to disk ('cmdorctrl-d' token vs a structured {mac,other} record), and (b) read existing resolved files WITHOUT misinterpreting a mac-written 'cmd-d' as a deliberate mac-only override?

**A.** The answer is substantively correct and I confirmed every code fact. The on-disk round-trip is LOSSY for cmdorctrl: Keystroke::parse can READ "cmdorctrl" (keymap.rs:914-919, resolving via OperatingSystem::get()) but there is no inverse — the Keystroke struct has no cmdorctrl field (keymap.rs:320-328) and normalized() only emits resolved ctrl-/alt-/shift-/cmd-/meta- (keymap.rs:970-993). So (a) emitting the unresolved form is NOT possible by reusing today's serializer; it requires a new Keystroke field or a structured {mac,other} record on the persistence layer. And (b) a mac-written "cmd-d" parses to a fully-resolved Keystroke{cmd:true} byte-identical to a deliberate mac-only cmd binding, and the flat HashMap<String,PersistedTrigger(String)> file (keyboard.rs:169,176-211) carries no platform/provenance tag — so the two cases are indistinguishable from stored data and can only be separated by an imposed back-compat convention plus migration. ONE CORRECTION: the R3b schema-evolution citation should be report:256 (line 252 is R2, not R3b); R5 at report:146 is exact.

VERDICT: CORRECTED. All substantive code claims independently verified as accurate; all four grep counts reproduced exactly; core thesis holds. The single defect is a 4-line shifted reference to R3b in the architecture report (252 -> 256).

INDEPENDENT VERIFICATION OF EACH CLAIM:

1) Keystroke struct (keymap.rs:320-328): fields are exactly {ctrl, alt, shift, cmd, meta, key} — NO cmdorctrl/cmd_or_ctrl field. CONFIRMED verbatim.

2) cmdorctrl parse (keymap.rs:914-919): the "cmdorctrl" match arm sets cmd=true when OperatingSystem::get()==Mac else ctrl=true. CONFIRMED. I also confirmed there are only TWO occurrences of the literal "cmdorctrl" in keymap.rs — line 871 (a doc comment describing the token) and line 914 (the parse arm) — and ZERO emitters anywhere in the tree (grep for push_str("cmdorctrl") returns 0). The answer said "exactly one occurrence" counting only the read site; there is also a doc-comment mention at line 871, but this does not affect the substance (still no inverse emitter). 

3) normalized() (keymap.rs:970-993): emits only "ctrl-"/"alt-"/"shift-"/"cmd-"/"meta-" + key (with " "->"space"). No cmdorctrl branch. CONFIRMED. Therefore parse->normalized is LOSSY for cmdorctrl: "cmdorctrl-d" -> (mac) Keystroke{cmd:true,key:d} -> "cmd-d", never back to cmdorctrl. CONFIRMED.

4) PersistedTrigger serialization (keyboard.rs:176-187): From<UserDefinedKeybinding> maps keystrokes through Keystroke::normalized joined by spaces; Removed -> REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION. CONFIRMED. I additionally verified REMOVED_KEYBINDING_SERIALIZATION = "none" (keyboard.rs:15), so the answer's "literal 'none' meaning remove" is correct.

5) On-disk schema (keyboard.rs:169): struct CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>) — flat, no platform/context/provenance field. CONFIRMED (note it is #[cfg(not(test))], which does not affect the claim). PersistedTrigger is a newtype over String (keyboard.rs:173-174). CONFIRMED.

6) Legacy read (keyboard.rs:189-211): TryFrom<PersistedTrigger> splits on whitespace and calls Keystroke::parse on each token, producing fully-resolved Keystrokes. A mac-written "cmd-d" is indistinguishable from a deliberate mac-only cmd binding. CONFIRMED.

7) OperatingSystem::get() (platform/mod.rs:668-683 — answer cited 669-682, essentially exact): cfg_if dispatching on target_family=wasm -> wasm::current_platform(), else compile-time target_os branches. Native is compile-time fixed; a migrator cannot know the original writing OS. CONFIRMED.

8) wasm runtime resolution (wasm.rs:7-8): static PLATFORM: OnceLock<OperatingSystem>; set lazily via get_or_init at wasm.rs:56 from the parsed user agent. No external/explicit setter. CONFIRMED.

9) Build-time platform helpers: with_mac_key_binding / with_linux_or_windows_key_binding (keymap.rs:688-713) return self unchanged off-platform; new_per_platform (keymap.rs:497-508) picks keystroke.mac or .linux_and_windows via OperatingSystem::get().is_mac(). CONFIRMED — these DROP the non-matching platform's binding at build time.

10) Reproducible counts (re-run from /home/mhb/warp): cmdorctrl=140, with_mac=102, with_linux_or_windows=57, combined cmd_or_ctrl|cmdorctrl=368. ALL FOUR REPRODUCED EXACTLY.

11) Report citations: R5 "Represent platform in the data model... unresolved CmdOrCtrl modifier or {mac, other} pair... Update Keystroke::parse/normalized/displayed" is at report:146 — EXACT. R3b "evolve CustomKeybindings to records {name, trigger, context?, platform?}" is at report:256, NOT 252. Line 252 is the Phase-2 R2 item ("Binding layers + explicit precedence + Unbound state"). This is the one correction. (R3 master entry is at report:142; R3b is the Phase-2 sequencing item at report:256.)

12) UNCITED SUPPORTING CHECK (detail #5): is_binding_cross_platform lives at app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933 (the answer referenced it generically without a line). Confirmed: on non-mac, if keystroke.cmd is set it returns IsBindingValid::No (lines 928-929) — exactly substantiating the claim that a mac config's "cmd-d" dropped on Linux is flagged invalid. (Minor nit: the answer characterized cmd-on-linux as "the Logo/super key"; the code simply rejects any cmd modifier on non-mac, which is the load-bearing fact.)

BOTTOM LINE (unchanged from original): The existing format does NOT round-trip unresolved intent — platform-in-data-model is a genuine Keystroke/PersistedTrigger schema change (R5), not a free reuse of the cmdorctrl token — and legacy reads are inherently ambiguous between "resolved cmdorctrl" and "intentional single-platform," so the design doc must specify (i) a new on-disk encoding (token vs {mac,other} record) and (ii) a default-interpretation rule for bare resolved cmd-/ctrl- strings plus a one-time migration.

### A6-Q13 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** is_binding_cross_platform (bindings.rs:918-933) currently runs only as the DEBUG default validator and merely flags cmd==true on non-mac (returns Yes immediately on mac). Once keystrokes are unresolved, what is the validator's new contract — is a bare cmdorctrl/{mac,other} pair auto-valid, and does a mac-side cmd with NO explicit other-platform mapping become a hard failure rather than today's silent pass-on-mac?

**A.** Code-verified premise: today's is_binding_cross_platform validator is debug-only (#[cfg(debug_assertions)]), runs once at app-init over every registered binding (not the settings edit path), short-circuits to Yes on mac, and on non-mac fails only a resolved keystroke whose cmd==true. Because cmdorctrl is collapsed to cmd (mac) / ctrl (non-mac) at parse time, and {mac,other} pairs (with_mac_key_binding / with_linux_or_windows_key_binding / new_per_platform) only ever materialize the current build's own OS side, a bare cmdorctrl and a full {mac,other} pair already always pass, and a mac-only cmd binding with no non-mac mapping is structurally invisible (Yes on mac, never registered on non-mac) — so it cannot fail today. Whether the unresolved model makes such forms auto-valid vs a hard failure, and whether the check moves to the release/edit path, is a design decision not settleable from code. All evidence file:line refs and grep counts reproduce exactly.

VERIFICATION RESULT: every factual claim in the colleague's answer holds against the source. I opened each cited file plus uncited locations (OperatingSystem::get, full is_binding_cross_platform reference set) and re-ran all greps.

1) Wiring & scope — CONFIRMED. `is_binding_cross_platform` is referenced in exactly three places and nowhere else: import at app/src/lib.rs:303, install at app/src/lib.rs:1395 (`ctx.set_default_binding_validator(is_binding_cross_platform)`), and its definition at app/src/util/bindings.rs:918. A repo-wide grep over app/ + crates/ returns only those three. It is NOT referenced in the settings edit path.

2) Debug-only — CONFIRMED. `set_default_binding_validator` is `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` in both the public wrapper (core/app.rs:1587-1594) with a `#[cfg(not(debug_assertions))]` empty no-op (1599-1604), and in the matcher impl (matcher.rs:171-177). `validate_bindings` is likewise `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` (matcher.rs:182; core/app.rs:1609) with a release no-op (core/app.rs:1615-1616).

3) Registration-time, runs over ALL bindings — CONFIRMED. `validate_bindings` (matcher.rs:182-203) iterates the default validator over `self.get_bindings()` (line 193) and `panic!`s if any returns `IsBindingValid::No` (lines 200-201). It is invoked once after app init at platform/app.rs:85 (`ctx.validate_bindings()`). Note (precision, not a correction): the default validator runs over every registered binding via get_bindings(), not only editable ones — the answer states this correctly and does not overgeneralize.

4) One-directional logic — CONFIRMED verbatim at bindings.rs:918-933. `if OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() { return IsBindingValid::Yes }` (919-921); else `trigger = binding.original_trigger.unwrap_or(binding.trigger)` (923) → `trigger_to_keystroke` (924, returns Yes if None) → returns `No` iff `keystroke.cmd` (928-932). Only a stray cmd modifier on a non-mac build is caught.

5) Keystroke has no cmdorctrl field — CONFIRMED. keymap.rs:320-328 fields are ctrl, alt, shift, cmd, meta, key. cmdorctrl is resolved at parse time (keymap.rs:914-920: cmd on Mac else ctrl, via `OperatingSystem::get()`). So a bare cmdorctrl-* can never fail (non-mac→ctrl→cmd=false→Yes; mac→short-circuit Yes).

6) {mac,other} pairs materialize only one OS side — CONFIRMED. `with_mac_key_binding` (688-697) and `with_linux_or_windows_key_binding` (701-713) apply the binding only when the current OS matches, else `self` unchanged (no-op). `FixedBinding::new_per_platform` (497-508) picks mac vs linux_and_windows at build time. UNCITED CHECK that strengthens this: `OperatingSystem::get()` (platform/mod.rs:669-683) is compile-time-fixed via cfg_if per target_os (wasm calls wasm::current_platform()), so on a native non-mac build the mac side is a genuine compile-time no-op — confirming a mac-only cmd binding is registered NOWHERE for the non-mac validator to see, and is short-circuited to Yes on mac. Hence the 'silent pass-on-mac'.

7) Edit path has only duplicate-conflict detection — CONFIRMED. app/src/settings_view/keybindings.rs holds a `ConflictMap` (struct 105; impl 109-133; FromIterator 135-144) keyed on Option<Keystroke> (current-platform-resolved), surfaced via `SHORTCUT_CONFLICT_WARNING_TEXT` (defined line 48, rendered at 315). The mutating paths — remove_keystroke (590), reset_to_default_keystroke (617), cancel_keystroke_editing (643), confirm_keystroke_editing (669), set_temporary_keystroke_state (701) — only call `conflict_map.update`; none call `is_binding_cross_platform` or produce `IsBindingValid`. It is a same-platform duplicate check only.

8) Divergence sizing — RE-RUN AND CONFIRMED EXACTLY: 102 `with_mac_key_binding` + 57 `with_linux_or_windows_key_binding` = 159 full-keystroke divergence sites; 13 `new_per_platform` call sites (excluding the fn def); 368 `cmdorctrl`/`cmd_or_ctrl` references across app/ + crates/.

MAPPING TO THE QUESTION (unchanged from the answer, and code-supported): a bare cmdorctrl and a full {mac,other} pair are precisely the forms that can never fail today (cmdorctrl resolved pre-validation; pairs only materialize the covered OS side). A mac-side cmd with no other-platform mapping CANNOT be a failure today (Yes on mac; unregistered on non-mac). Making it a hard failure under the unresolved model requires three net-new changes: (a) drop the is_mac() early-return, (b) validate the UNRESOLVED {mac,other} pair (today's resolved Keystroke field set cannot represent an absent other-platform side), and (c) move the check out of debug-only init into the release/settings-edit path (R10). The carryover assumption that the validator works unchanged is false. The actual new policy (auto-valid vs hard-fail vs auto-default) is a design decision, not settleable from code.

_Evidence:_
- is_binding_cross_platform is referenced in exactly three places: import (lib.rs:303), install via set_default_binding_validator (lib.rs:1395), and definition (bindings.rs:918); a repo-wide grep over app/+crates/ returns nothing else, so it is NOT in the settings edit path — `app/src/lib.rs:303,1395; app/src/util/bindings.rs:918` _(code)_
- set_default_binding_validator is #[cfg(debug_assertions)] with a #[cfg(not(debug_assertions))] empty no-op in the release build — `crates/warpui_core/src/core/app.rs:1587-1604` _(code)_
- validate_bindings is #[cfg(debug_assertions)], iterates the default validator over get_bindings() and panic!s on any IsBindingValid::No — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap/matcher.rs:171-203` _(code)_
- validation runs exactly once after app init via ctx.validate_bindings(), i.e. registration-time not edit-time — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/app.rs:85` _(code)_
- Validator short-circuits to Yes on mac (lines 919-921), resolves original_trigger.unwrap_or(trigger) via trigger_to_keystroke, and returns No only if keystroke.cmd (928-932) — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:918-933` _(code)_
- Keystroke struct has fields ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta/key and NO cmdorctrl field — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:320-328` _(code)_

### A6-Q15 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** displayed() (keymap.rs:998-1030) hardcodes ⌘/⌃/⇧ vs Ctrl/Alt/Shift/Logo by OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() and is called across 21 files. Will the cross-platform editor add a separate displayed_for(target_os) used only by the settings/catalog surfaces, leaving all other call sites on the current-OS path — or will displayed() itself gain a target-OS parameter (touching all 21 callers)?

**A.** Confirmed. Today displayed() (crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:998-1065) takes only &self and reads the OS globally via OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() at five points (ctrl 1001, alt 1009, shift 1017, cmd 1025, and the final join 1058); it has no target-OS parameter and no parallel displayed_for variant exists anywhere. It is called in exactly 21 files / 41 sites, ALL under app/; ~3 are settings-adjacent (settings_view/mod.rs, settings/ai.rs, util/bindings.rs) and the other ~18 are current-OS hint surfaces. Whether to add a parallel displayed_for(os) (additive, ~3 callers) vs. thread a target-OS param through all 21 callers (mechanical wide churn) is an unimplemented design decision; the code only bounds the blast radius. Independently re-verified every cited line.

I independently opened every cited file plus uncited locations (action_button.rs:190-204, util/bindings.rs:478, a repo-wide OperatingSystem-setter search) and the answer holds on every verifiable point.

displayed() (keymap.rs:998-1065): signature is `pub fn displayed(&self) -> String` — no target-OS param (998). It branches on `OperatingSystem::get().is_mac()` FIVE times: ctrl→⌃/Ctrl (1001), alt→⌥/Alt (1009), shift→⇧/Shift (1017), cmd→⌘/Logo (1024-1031, confirming cmd renders as 'Logo' on non-mac), and the final join compact ⌘I on mac vs space-separated on non-mac (1058-1064). Exact.

Call-site inventory: `grep -rln "\.displayed()" --include=*.rs | wc -l` → 21 files; `grep -rn ... | wc -l` → 41 occurrences. Reproduced exactly. All 21 files are under app/ (none in crates/ besides the keymap.rs definition). Highest-density callers re-counted: terminal/view.rs (5), terminal/keys.rs (5), editor/accept_autosuggestion_keybinding_view.rs (5), workspace/view.rs (4) — all match. KeystrokeSource::displayed (action_button.rs:196-203) merely delegates: `KeystrokeSource::Fixed(kb) => Some(kb.displayed())` and the Binding arm maps to `keybinding.displayed()`.

No parameterized variant: `grep "displayed_for\|fn displayed"` returns only keymap.rs:998 (this fn) and action_button.rs:196 (the delegating wrapper); all other displayed_* (displayed_text, displayed_lines, displayed_chip_kinds, displayed_root_directories, etc.) are unrelated. So no platform-parameterized display function exists today.

OperatingSystem::get() (platform/mod.rs:669-683): a compile-time cfg_if over target_family/target_os with no parameter; the only runtime path is wasm::current_platform() (OnceLock in wasm.rs). A repo-wide setter search found no native setter — only the wasm OnceLock and an unrelated OperatingSystemInfo OnceLock in warp_core. Therefore a displayed_for path must thread an explicit OperatingSystem; get() cannot yield the other platform.

Keystroke struct (keymap.rs:320-328): fields ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta/key — NO cmd_or_ctrl field. The `cmdorctrl` token is resolved at PARSE time to cmd (mac) or ctrl (else) via OperatingSystem::get() (keymap.rs:914-919). So a binding's cmd-vs-ctrl identity is already OS-baked before displayed() runs; a faithful cross-platform settings/catalog display needs the unresolved binding plus a target-OS resolver, not glyph rendering alone — the answer's adversarial sharpening is correct.

One minor classification nuance (does not change the verdict): the answer labels util/bindings.rs as one of the 3 'settings/catalog surfaces'. In reality util/bindings.rs:478 is a generic binding-name→keystroke display helper, not a settings view per se; settings_view/mod.rs and settings/ai.rs are the only true settings surfaces. This doesn't affect the substance — the dominant population (~18 files) are tooltips/menus/buttons/onboarding hint surfaces that should stay on the current OS, and only a small number are settings-adjacent. The blast-radius framing (parallel displayed_for(os) = additive/low-risk vs. target-OS param = 21-file mechanical churn) is accurate, and the path choice remains an unimplemented design decision.

_Evidence:_
- displayed() takes only &self with no target-OS parameter and branches on OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() five times (ctrl 1001, alt 1009, shift 1017, cmd 1024-1031, and the final join 1058); cmd renders ⌘ on mac and 'Logo' on non-mac — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:998-1065` _(code)_
- Exactly 21 files call .displayed() (41 total occurrences), all under app/; reproduced with grep -rln "\.displayed()" --include=*.rs | wc -l (21) and grep -rn ... | wc -l (41). High-density callers: terminal/view.rs(5), terminal/keys.rs(5), editor/accept_autosuggestion_keybinding_view.rs(5), workspace/view.rs(4) — `app/ (21 files; per-file counts re-run)` _(code)_
- No platform-parameterized display variant exists; grep displayed_for/fn displayed returns only keymap.rs:998 and action_button.rs:196 (KeystrokeSource::displayed) which delegates to keybinding.displayed(); other displayed_* methods are unrelated — `app/src/view_components/action_button.rs:196-203` _(code)_
- OperatingSystem::get() is a compile-time cfg_if with no parameter and no native setter (only wasm OnceLock path), so a displayed_for path must thread an explicit OperatingSystem rather than reuse get() — `crates/warpui_core/src/platform/mod.rs:669-683 (and wasm.rs OnceLock; repo-wide setter search found no native setter)` _(both)_
- Keystroke has fields ctrl/alt/shift/cmd/meta/key and NO cmd_or_ctrl field; the cmdorctrl token is resolved to cmd-or-ctrl at parse time per current OS, so the stored binding is already OS-resolved before displayed() runs — `crates/warpui_core/src/keymap.rs:320-328 and :914-919` _(code)_
- Classification nuance: util/bindings.rs:478 is a generic binding-name display helper, not strictly a settings view; only settings_view/mod.rs and settings/ai.rs are true settings surfaces, but the dominant ~18 callers are current-OS hint surfaces — the answer's count/framing remains substantively correct — `app/src/util/bindings.rs:478` _(code)_

- _Unblocks:_ Lets the design doc size the display half of R5: choose the parallel displayed_for(os) confined to settings/catalog (additive, ~3 callers, low blast radius) over threading a target-OS param through all 21 callers, and flag that the cmdorctrl parse-time resolution (no cmd_or_ctrl field on Keystroke) must be solved alongside display for cross-platform correctness.
- _Residual / needs decision:_ Which path to take (parallel displayed_for(os) vs. target-OS param on displayed()) is an unimplemented product/architecture decision the code cannot settle — neither exists today. The code bounds the trade-off: parameterizing displayed() = mechanical 21-file churn across all shortcut-hint surfaces (higher regression/review risk); a parallel displayed_for(os) = additive, confined to the few settings-adjacent callers. Two constraints any chosen path must address regardless: (1) it must pass an explicit OperatingSystem because get() is compile-time-fixed on native builds; (2) cmdorctrl bindings are resolved to cmd-vs-ctrl at parse time (no cmd_or_ctrl field on Keystroke), so faithful cross-platform settings/catalog display needs the unresolved binding + a target-OS resolver, not glyph rendering alone. Soft point: the exact membership of the 'settings/catalog' subset (the answer says 3, including the generic util/bindings.rs helper) is a judgment call rather than a hard code fact.
- _Verification:_ Re-ran both greps myself: 21 files (sorted list matches the answer's enumerated files) and 41 total occurrences — exact. Per-file occurrence count reproduced the claimed 5/5/5/4 high-density callers. Opened keymap.rs:998-1065 and counted the OperatingSystem::get().is_mac() branches independently: exactly five (lines 1001, 1009, 1017, 1025, 1058), matching the answer including the often-missed join branch. Opened the Keystroke struct (320-328) and confirmed there is NO cmd_or_ctrl field. Opened the parse path (914-919) and confirmed cmdorctrl is resolved at parse time via OperatingSystem::get(). Opened platform/mod.rs:669-683 and confirmed get() is compile-time cfg_if; ran an extra repo-wide search for any OperatingSystem setter/static and found only the wasm OnceLock (already acknowledged) and an unrelated OperatingSystemInfo OnceLock — so the 'no native setter' claim holds. Opened action_button.rs:196-203 (uncited-detail check) and confirmed KeystrokeSource::displayed only delegates to Keystroke::displayed. The only weakness found: util/bindings.rs is characterized as a 'settings/catalog surface' but is actually a generic helper (line 478) — a soft classification call, not a material error; the 21/41 counts and the blast-radius/decision framing are all correct. No shifted line refs, no app/-vs-crates confusion, no overgeneralization (the answer correctly scopes the cmdorctrl resolution to parse time and notes displayed() is only the glyph half).

## Cross-axis — Cross-axis / sequencing

### X5 — CONFIRMED · confidence: high

**Q.** Should the FixedBinding magnitude audit (how many of the 397 FixedBinding::new sites, all keystroke triggers per keymap.rs:525, sit in a user-reachable context) be done ONCE as initiative-level groundwork before any axis is scoped — given it simultaneously gates Axis 3 (contained Unbound refactor vs ~400-site identity project), Axis 4 (flagship per-mode enter/backspace-in-Vim forks are FixedBindings update_custom_trigger never touches), and Axis 5 (catalog promises 'read-only fixed bindings' and truthful conflict must scan them)?

**A.** The audit is mechanically doable in one pass and decisively settles all three axes' scope. There are exactly 397 FixedBinding::new sites across 86 files (verified by grep), all producing Trigger::Keystrokes (keymap.rs:525). Of these, 167 (160 from the top-4 core files alone) sit in the four core persistent, user-reachable text-editing surfaces (terminal editor / code editor / notebook / terminal), and 24 are non-shipped examples/tests — both exact. This empirically refutes the report's premise (L134/L234) that Fixed is "reserved for internal/transient." Because that same count is the input to Axis 3 scope, Axis 4 feasibility, and Axis 5 completeness, doing it once is code-justified; whether to formally gate Phase scoping on it is a process call.

VERDICT: CONFIRMED. I independently re-ran every grep and opened every cited file plus several the answer did not cite. All load-bearing CODE facts hold.

1. MAGNITUDE (verified). `grep -rn 'FixedBinding::new' --include='*.rs' | wc -l` => 397; file count `grep -rln ... | wc -l` => 86. FixedBinding::new is at keymap.rs:511 inside `impl FixedBinding {` (opens at :495), and unconditionally sets `trigger: Trigger::Keystrokes(keys)` at keymap.rs:525. So "397 sites, all keystroke triggers per keymap.rs:525" is exact and reproducible. (Minor, immaterial nuance: the substring grep also catches the 13 `FixedBinding::new_per_platform` calls; those route through Self::new and also produce Keystrokes, so the "all keystroke" claim is unaffected. The answer's own reproduced command yields 397, so the figure is internally consistent.)

2. DISTRIBUTION — the hard numbers check out exactly. Per-file count (`grep -rc ... | sort -t: -k2 -rn`) gives the top four as app/src/editor/view/mod.rs:49, app/src/terminal/view/init.rs:38, app/src/code/editor/view/actions.rs:38, app/src/notebooks/editor/view.rs:35 => sum 160. Adding terminal/input.rs (5), app/src/code/local_code_editor.rs (1), app/src/ai/blocklist/agent_view/agent_input_footer/editor.rs (1) => 167 core. Verified those two small files = 1 each. examples+tests tally (`grep -rn 'FixedBinding::new' | grep -iE 'example|test' | wc -l`) = 24, exact. The non-core buckets (66 persistent panels / 45 modal / 45 cards / 25 onboarding / 25 misc) are file-as-proxy classifications the answer already flags as graded; bucket totals sum to 397. The headline 167-core and 24-noshipped are the exact, decisive numbers.

3. CONTEXTS ARE PERSISTENT, NOT MODAL-ESCAPE (verified). terminal editor: id!("EditorView") (editor/view/mod.rs:192-224, e.g. :195/:200/:205); code editor: text_entry = id!("CodeEditorView") & !id!("IMEOpen") (actions.rs:47) plus id!("VimNormalMode")/id!("VimVisualMode") (actions.rs:293,506,514,521); notebook: id!("RichTextEditorView") (view.rs:97,110+); terminal: id!("Terminal") (init.rs:75-283, dozens). This directly contradicts report L9 (line 134, verbatim: "FixedBinding appears reserved for internal/transient bindings; user-facing shortcuts are EditableBinding") and report Recommendations (line 234, verbatim: "the only FixedBinding sites observed are internal/transient (modal escape, lightbox nav, onboarding callouts) ... Before promising 'make everything remappable,' audit how many user-visible shortcuts are actually Fixed"). The report itself frames this exact audit as X5's groundwork — confirmed.

4. AXIS 4 KILLER (verified). code editor: "enter" -> CodeEditorViewAction::VimEnter under text_entry & id!("Vim") (actions.rs:56-60); "backspace" -> VimBackspace under text_entry & id!("Vim") (actions.rs:86-90). terminal editor: "backspace" -> EditorAction::VimBackspace under id!("EditorView") & !id!("IMEOpen") & id!("Vim") (editor/view/mod.rs:202-206). Both are inside register_fixed_bindings blocks. update_custom_trigger (keymap.rs:422-433) mutates ONLY editable_custom_action_bindings (:423-429) and editable_bindings (:430-432) — it never touches fixed_bindings/fixed_custom_action_bindings. Confirmed: these per-mode forks cannot be rebound by the existing mechanism.

5. AXIS 5 BLINDNESS (verified). get_binding_by_name (keymap.rs:379-386) reads only editable_bindings_by_name (:380); FixedBinding has no name. I grepped every use of `fixed_bindings`/`fixed_custom_action_bindings` in keymap.rs: the fields are private and read only in bindings() (:458) and custom_action_bindings() (:478) — confirming the ONLY scan paths that surface fixed bindings are bindings() (:454-464, chains editable then fixed) and custom_action_bindings() (:474-484). Also verified the Phase-1 persisted schema is name-keyed: CustomKeybindings(HashMap<String, PersistedTrigger>) at keyboard.rs:168-169, whose keys are editable binding names (e.g. "editor:delete_all_left") — so fixed bindings are structurally outside the persisted remap surface, reinforcing the answer.

CONCLUSION: The audit is a single mechanical grep-and-classify whose one output (167 core / 24 non-shipped of 397) simultaneously feeds Axis 3 blast-radius, Axis 4 feasibility (the Vim forks among the 167 are unreachable by update_custom_trigger), and Axis 5 completeness (catalog/conflict must use bindings(), not get_binding_by_name). Running it once is strongly code-supported. Whether to formally freeze axis scoping on it is a process decision, not code-verifiable — the answer correctly fences this off.
